RIVENKEEP — THE ADMIRAL

The fleet’s commander and its stratagems · Blind · Deterministic · Draws no random numbers
· 2026-09-26 · Draft · Module of the GDD · Owns the fleet’s intent — the next-sortie plan Fleet Memory names but never defines: one stratagem per sortie from what the fleet believes, each formation’s carving, the links that tie them in time, and the book of forty-six stratagems in ten growth lines across five Tides and the Thrones. 0WHAT THIS DOC OWNS

Module of the GDD. It specifies the “next-sortie plan” that Fleet Memory names but never defines (Fleet Memory, Build/Deploy Is Simultaneous Planning, and §12 ledger row Between-sortie planning). It rides Fleet Memory (the three channels), Ships (lineages, flagship, formations, resolve) and Victory (queue, composition law, refusal law). Campaign stages it, the Fight shows it, and The Why governs it. Its companion fiction is the Legends (The Book of the Riven Stone), built from fiction_hooks.md: the tales this doc cites by leaf (Legends V.1, V.2 and so on) are told there, and the delivery map in Legends §3–§4 says when each is laid. The enemy people are the Mystaeri; the defenders, once the Shorelanders, are reborn as the Rivenmen.

The Admiral layer is the fleet’s intent. Between sorties, while the player builds and deploys, it chooses one stratagem for the coming sortie from what the fleet believes. It hands each sailing formation its part of that stratagem, and it ties those parts together in time. Everything below the Admiral already exists. The captains are the lineage movement policies, resolve, crazy-Ivan dodging and the orphan revenge swap (Ships §4, §5–§9). The soldiers are the three channels (Fleet Memory §2–§7).

The review found that the fleet “has good soldiers but no commander” (verified_brief, enemy-mind-legibility-9). This doc is the commander.

OwnsDoes not own
The plan object: one carving per formation per sortie (§3)Which formations sail, and in what order. The queue owns that (Victory §4b: Q is fixed in order; survivors sail first)
The timing links between formations (§4)Whether a core refuses to sail. Resolve owns that (Victory §4a refusal law)
The selection rule and its inputs (§5–§7)Movement inside a fear cell, dodging, rout and revenge. The captains own these (Ships §4, §9)
The player-habit register, the Shore-Reading (§6)What the fleet perceives. Fleet Memory §2–§7 owns that
The stratagem book: 46 stratagems in 10 growth lines across five Tides and the Thrones (§13, §16–§22)Lineage stats and axis expressions (Ships §5–§8)
The recognition loop: signature, Knowing and dispatch (§9)Debrief UI layout (Deploy, Victory)
How Command Time interacts with the fleet (§12). The ramp itself is Jack’s ruling, and the Fight doc owns its presentationBoss set-piece mechanics (the later boss pass)
LAW · THE ADMIRAL IS A PURE FUNCTION

carving(sortie n+1) = f(fleet memory at the end of sortie n, fleet state, era, grain). It is computed once, in Build/Deploy, off the real-time path (Fleet Memory §12). It reads no wall-clock, no RNG stream (not even the fleet stream) and no ground truth. Same memory → same carving.

1JACK’S VISION TESTS

Every choice below is checked against these, in Jack’s words (2026-09-25).

  1. Determinism with sensitivity. “I want a purely strategic game that is deterministic. If two people play EXACTLY the same, they would get the same outcome. But people are not the same, an action 1 second earlier or later could change the course of the game — very much like real life.” → §5 (no RNG), §8 (stable doctrine, sensitive detail), §12 (orders stamped in ticks).
  2. A human on the other side. “I want the ships to feel like there is another human running the enemy. I want it to feel like the ships are working together but also are independent. I don’t want the player to know exactly what the ships are doing at first, but they recognize the strategy and they can counter it.” → §2 (Admiral, captains, links), §6 (the fleet reads your habits), §9 (signature, then Knowing), §10 (counters).
  3. Strategy, not skill. “Strategy not skill (how fast or accurately you click). It is like Brick n Balls: I fire the balls and they do their work. I don’t have a paddle at the bottom that I have to be skillful with.” → §12 (Command Time), §10 (no counter needs a trick).
  4. Many stratagems that grow. “A lot more than 6-8 stratagems… 6-8 in the early game that are very basic, 6-8 early mid, 6-8 mid, 6-8 late mid, and 6-8 late in the game. It doesn’t have to only be 5 game periods. The stratagems can overlap and a simple stratagem could build to a complex one late in the game.” → §13 and §16–§22: five Tides plus the Thrones, with 6–8 stratagems per tier, 46 in all, in 10 growth lines that grow from Cam 1 words into Judgements (§16).
  5. Story that shapes the world, with the Ender’s Game ordering. “The less tech-advanced ships were sent out first… the first are not organized, but they get better.” → §13 (each era unlocks one capability family; the Hasty Tide has no links at all), §23c (each Tide’s characteristic error) and the companion fiction (the Legends, Book Five: the growings told from both sides).
  6. Halyna, as one. → §9b (the Knowing of the Wood is Halyna’s: only one of them need touch the wood, for what one knows, the other knows, and every Carving line in §17–§22 is told by the two of them, by turns).
  7. Command Time. Time ramps down to 18 real seconds per game-hour when an order starts, dwells there a moment after the order commits, then ramps back. → §12.
THE TEST THIS DOC HOLDS ITSELF TO

A player who has met a stratagem three times can name it from its opening signature before the engagement band, and can name one blunt and one clever counter. A player meeting it for the first time cannot name it, but it is explained to them, by name, afterwards. (Greybox bar: §15.)

2THE THREE LAYERS

LayerFiction (fiction_hooks.md; the Legends)DecidesCadence (in sim ticks)Reads
ADMIRALthe Heartwood: the mind in the Standard-Bearer’s heartwood that carves the morrow’s orders (the wood’s words: the heart is the Saenvael, its hull the Sethvaren, “the bearer of the carving”; Legends V.6). No one is aboard, and there is no admiral in the fiction: the ship is the commander, and “the Admiral” is this doc’s name for its heartthe stratagem; role assignment; each formation’s carving (§3); the links (§4)once per sortie boundary, plus ≤1 authored hinge per stratagem (§4). Through Cam 8, once per formation, at that formation’s first boundary only (§17 LAW · GREEN WOOD TAKES ONE CUT)believed state only: fear grid at sortie end, the Contact ledger, the Shore-Reading, its own fleet state, and the history of its stratagems this battle
CAPTAINSeach formation’s lead hull (a hull, never a person), obeying the carving “as a hand obeys the mind”how to execute its part: grid-scoring movement inside a fear cell, crazy-Ivan, screen backfill, when to balk, and any self-trigger its carving holds (§3)every tick (Ships §4, §9; Fleet Memory §12)live LOS, the fear grid, and its own hulls’ resolve
ORDERS BETWEEN THEMthe root-bond (the wood’s Aelralen): the wood obeys the wood. A carved link wakes when its event reaches the hull through the propagation netwhen a part starts, holds or brancheswhen the triggering event arrives at the receiving formation over the propagation net, or on a carved tick (WITH)propagated observations only

What makes it feel human (research P1–P3, P18).

  • Commitment. The Admiral commits once per sortie and holds. It never re-plans a sortie mid-flight. It branches only at a hinge it carved in advance (§4). People commit, and commitment is what makes a strategy recognisable (research P2).
  • Independence. A captain weighs its carving against its own fear and nerve (research P3, the F.E.A.R. model). A formation that is ordered through a cell that went SCALDING during this sortie balks. It stalls at the cell boundary, loosens, and takes its fallback. A holding formation that takes fire breaks its hold early, onto its colder branch or its fallback: that is a balk too, the flinch (§19 E3-01, E3-06; CALL-OUT 22). A Wavering formation abandons a strike role for its fallback. An orphaned screen turns wolfpack (Ships §9, revenge swap). The formations are “working together but also independent” because the carving says what while resolve and fear decide whether.
  • Characteristic error. Each grain (§7) and each era (§13) errs in a nameable way: recency, sunk cost, fixation, overcommitment, caution. It does not err at random (research P18; the Maia and Halo findings). §23 gathers them.
LAW · CAPTAINS MAY DISOBEY; THEY MAY NEVER LIE

A deviation from the carving is always one of four visible events: a balk (a lateral stall at a cell edge, or a hold broken by fire), a Wavering sag aft, a rout, or a revenge swap (Committed forward motion). Each already has a tell in the motion signature (Ships §4). No deviation is silent. At the Thrones fanatical nerve removes the balk and the sag, and independence lives in the lineage policies alone (§22 T-rule 6).

LAW · INTENSITY TOUCHES NERVE, NEVER THE PLAN (every era; brief -6; CALL-OUT 17)
  • At every intensity the Admiral plans approaches, lanes and objectives from believed fear (§3, §5). No intensity makes the carving blind to the fear haze, so a hold-feint written before the sortie routes a fanatical fleet as surely as a bluffable one.
  • Intensity (Ships §4) acts on the captains alone, and only on fear written during the sortie:
    • bluffable: balks at a lane cell written HOT or worse this sortie;
    • default: balks at a lane cell written SCALDING this sortie (§2 above);
    • fanatical: never balks and never sags aft. It holds its carved lane through fear written this sortie, and that is Ships §4’s “ignores the fear grid → drives straight into kill-zones”: a kill-zone the fleet did not believe in at the boundary, sprung on a lane it cannot be frightened off.
  • In E1–E5 intensity is the battle’s resting intensity and the flag lineage’s lean (Ships §4: the Heartoak lineages lean fanatical; the Provocateur is fanatical). The Admiral reads only the resolve band (§8), never intensity, so the choice of stratagem is unchanged; what changes is how many balks the player can provoke. §24 Ships §4 carries the split.

The boundary between plan and live steering (answers enemy-mind-legibility-9). The carving works at fear-cell grain: waypoints are cells about 13×8 grids (Fleet Memory §3). Everything finer is the captain’s job, in live grid-scoring: the exact grid path, spacing, jinks and interposition. So the plan never needs precision the fear grid cannot supply (brief finding -7).

3THE CARVING — THE PLAN OBJECT

One carving per sailing formation per sortie. Every field is chosen by the Admiral from believed state. Default rules are given here, and each carving in the catalogue (§17–§22) overrides only what it names.

FieldWhat it isDefault rule (believed state only)Range and provenance
roleThis formation’s part in the stratagem: strike · cargo · screen · probe · pin · lure (a real formation sent to draw fire or attention; E2+) · decoy (a formation that is not what it looks like: projections, real ships in false dress, a conditioned routine; E5+) · reserve · hunt · siegeRoles are filled in stratagem priority order. Each role takes the lowest-queue-index sailing formation that passes the role’s lineage and kind filter (§5 step 4)Role vocabulary follows research P3 (Halo 3 task tree) and P12 (primitive verbs)
sailsails at a launch offset; or loiters in the launch zone until a link or its latest tick; or sails to a station (a fear edge, a fork, a fringe, a hot cell) and holds there until a link or its latest tickOffset 0 unless the stratagem names oneOffset ∈ [0, L_max]. L_max starts at 18 ticks (one reading band, Fight §3), range 10–24. Settled in greybox so that a loitering reserve still clears the 70-tick backstop with a full passage (Fight §3: 54 + 18 ≈ 70; CALL-OUT 6). A hold’s latest tick is its arrival at station + L_max (conventions below)
approachWhich live approach, and which authored spawn on itAmong the spawns authored for this formation (Enemy Overlay), the one whose first lane cell has the lowest believed fear. Ties → lower spawn index≤3 live approaches (Victory §3); flanks at the Thrones (§22 T-rule 4). CALL-OUT 5
laneAn ordered list of fear cells from spawn to commit pointThe lowest summed believed fear (COLD 0, WARM 1, HOT 3, SCALDING 9). Ties → the lane ending nearer the objective’s Contact → lower cell index, row-major (Fleet Memory §12)Weights are ordinal, starting 0/1/3/9 (a geometric step, so one SCALDING outweighs any two HOT). Settled by legibility: a greybox tester must predict the lane from the fear haze ≥70% of the time
objectiveA Contact (a wall, castle or glinted gun), a believed breach, a fear cell to test, or draw-fireBy role. cargo/strike: the Contact on the lowest-fear lane. probe: the WARM cell nearest the hottest cell. hunt: the freshest glinted gun. siege: the wall the fleet has hit mostOnly things the Contact ledger, the fear grid or the fleet’s own record hold (Fleet Memory §4). Never a true position
commit pointThe cell where the formation stops lane-following and drives at its objectiveThe first lane cell inside the engagement bandThe engagement band is 16 grids, about 1–2 cells (Fight §3)
aspectThe bearing its shields faceToward the hottest cell adjacent to the cargo’s laneShields only. The tightness read is unchanged (Fleet Memory §7: tight = believes it knows)
self-triggerA carved rule its own captain evaluates on the formation’s own perception: a hit on its own hulls, its own first loss, the fear in its own grid, the newest glint it knows. It may start, stop, turn or re-aim the formation’s one partnoneE1 onward. It never names another formation’s deed, so it is not a link, and it never chooses between two carved parts, so it is not a hinge (§4 red line). It is the Hasty Tide’s only coordination (§17)
hinge≤1 branch condition and its tick window (§4)noneWindow ≥ 6 ticks (see §11)
fallbackWhat the formation does when its link breaks, it balks, or its latest tick comesIts lineage’s own movement policy toward its own objective (Ships §5–§6), carrying the local beliefs it holds—
linksTiming ties to other formations’ events or to a carved tick (§4)noneThe link budget per era, counted per carved condition (§4 LAW · COUNTING LINKS; §13)

Parts. A part is what a role is given. It is one of three things:

  • a formation;
  • a convoy’s shield element, stationed up to one cell ahead of its core. A cell ahead it reads as its own object, so it counts as its own contact (the power Cold Shadow needs, §18 E2-03; Victory §3 already treats a formation that breaks up mid-approach as authorable);
  • a lone hull the carving detaches: a rider or screen hull, or the lead hull of a dispersed Recon picket (1 contact per hull, Victory §3). It counts as its own contact from the moment it leaves, which is Victory §3’s “1 → 1 per hull” made deliberate (CALL-OUT 21). The Sounding line’s probes (E2-02 on) and the Bleed line’s bricks are lone hulls. In the Hasty Tide the probe is a Recon pair in close file (1 contact), because at C = 2 a scattered pair would fill the sortie alone (§17 E1-03).

The Admiral assigns parts. It never moves hulls or riders between formations (§5 step 4).

Timing conventions (every entry uses these; Fight §3; Victory §3).

  • Ticks count from the end of the 3-2-1. 1 tick = 1 real s at full speed, which is 1⅓ game-hours (§12). Sea speed is ≈1 grid/tick; Recon, Corsair and Sower are faster, and Ships owns their speeds.
  • The standard approach is ≈39 grids deep: launch zone 0–5, reading band 5–23, engagement band 23–39. A fear cell is ≈13 grids across, so an approach is about 3 cells deep. Withdrawal is 12 ticks, the natural sortie ≈54, the backstop 70.
  • The fear edge is the last COLD or WARM cell on a lane before its first HOT cell. On a road with no HOT cell it is the inner edge of the reading band, just outside believed reach.
  • AFTER(e) from the launch zone. The part loiters and sails when e arrives, or at t18 (L_max) at the latest.
  • A hold’s latest tick. A part holding at a station keeps it until its event arrives, or until its arrival there + L_max: ≈ 18 + p, where p is the station’s distance from spawn in grids. At p = 0 this is L_max itself. At its latest tick the part takes its fallback. The formula keeps the remaining passage plus the withdrawal inside the 70-tick backstop; greybox confirms that every branch of every chain clears 70. From Cam 16, with H9 active, a waiter whose event is fire on a lure, brick, probe or decoy has its latest tick re-cut to the tick that event is predicted to arrive (§6 LAW · THE PATIENT SHORE).
  • Spacing by geometry. Where it can, a carving meets the spacing floor (§11) with hold positions, not delays: one fear cell of separation gives ≈13 ticks.
LAW · A HOLD IS A PRESENCE

A formation carved to wait for a moment holds at the fear edge, full-screened, in shape, bow-on to its objective.

  • It is never confusable with a refusal. A refusing core is visibly thin and sits in the launch zone (Victory §4a). A holding core is full and forward.
  • It is not the Hunt line’s Reaver wait (a wolfpack scattered at the edge) or the Bleed line’s rest-wait (the payload behind a lone brick). Those are wolfpacks and followers. A hold is a core, alone, pointed.
  • Why the fear edge and not the launch zone: a launch-zone wait costs a full passage (≈39 ticks: 5 + 18 + 16) before the waiter arrives, so a moment that falls at tick 30–45 would land its answer past the backstop. From the Second Tide’s station-waits on, waiters hold close. The few parts that still wait at home do so on purpose, and each entry says why: E2-02’s convoy (checked against the refusal tell by §15 test 8; CALL-OUT 20); carriers, which never approach (Ships §5) unless a carving names their station (E5-06; CALL-OUT 37); the held host of any era (§11); and T-03’s ram and hands. The held host and T-03’s parts wait at the launch zone’s forward edge, full-screened and bow-on, never in its depth, so none of them reads as a refusal (§15 test 8).

Worked example: Cold Shadow (E2-03) with two parts.

  • A Runner convoy gets cargo. Its lane is the lowest-fear lane to the believed-softest coast, which crosses one HOT cell. Its objective is the coast cell.
  • The convoy’s own Bulwark-led shield element takes screen, stationed one cell ahead as its own part, with its aspect toward that HOT cell.
  • One link: HOLD-UNTIL(cargo committed). The screen may not peel off to backfill elsewhere until the cargo commits.
  • Fallback: if the Bulwark dies, the Runner beelines the softest COLD coast (Ships, Runner baseline).

4ORDERS BETWEEN THEM — LINKS, HINGES AND THE FLAG

Four link types. Every link is carved before the sortie.

LinkMeaningExample
AFTER(e)Start this part when event e is known to this formationThe strike sails after the feint draws fire
HOLD-UNTIL(e)Keep station and role until eScreens hold until the cargo commits
WITH(t)Start at carved tick t, synchronised with a partner. A clock, not an event: lag does not apply while the heart lives (after it falls, see LAW · DECAPITATION)Three roads, one hour (E4-01)
IF(e, window) → A | BA hinge: if e is observed inside the tick window, take branch A, otherwise branch BThe stimulus test (E4-02): fire drawn → hunt; silence → flood through

Events are only things a hull perceives:

  • fire drawn (Effect written on this formation), volley observed (the tick of an Effect event; the breath is read from two of them, §19 E3-07);
  • fire drawn from bearing B: every Effect carries its shell’s bearing (Fleet Memory §3), so an event may name where the fire came from. The core-hunter’s bearing is the bearing of the shells that sank the fleet’s cores last sortie (H7’s ground, §6; E4-08);
  • a re-task observed: fire on a formation arriving, from a bearing, in a cell the fleet believed at the boundary lay outside that bearing’s reach. It is H4’s own event (§6) read live: the reach walked (E4-05, E5-06);
  • flash seen (Contact written), and seen(X): line of sight re-observes ledger entry X and confirms or contradicts it (the look, E3 onward, §19);
  • crossed cell, including probe crossed cell X;
  • hull sunk, cargo committed, breach observed, bombers released;
  • hazard laid (smoke laid, fire planted: the Sower perceives its own act);
  • own-state events the fleet keeps of itself, such as a head leads in hits (T-01).
LAW · COUNTING LINKS

A link is one carved condition that one or more formations wait on: an event (AFTER, HOLD-UNTIL), a carved tick (WITH), or a hinge (IF).

  • A hinge obeyed by two formations is one link. A WITH tick shared by three formations is one link. One AFTER(e) may wake several formations, each at its own carved offset (E3-05, E5-06).
  • The link budget includes the hinge: E2 1, E3 2, E4 on 3, of which at most 1 is a hinge (§13).
  • Decapitation lowers the budget (below), so the budget is the lever the flag kill pulls.

A link fires on arrival, not on occurrence. The event must reach the receiving formation over the propagation net (Fleet Memory §7). So propagation lag is visible as timing, and coordination quality has a number.

The lag reads the propagation net’s own batching model (Fleet Memory §7), not a flat number. It is an integer computed per link from state at the event’s tick: lag = max(1, base − R) + T + D, or the twist’s value where a Throne names one.

TermValueProvenance
base, E2 (Second Tide)3 ticksStarting value, range 2–4. Set so that early links visibly straggle (research §3.16: Midway’s piecemeal waves, the 1281 rendezvous failure). Settled when greybox testers can see “the strike came late”
base, E32 ticksCreep step (Campaign §4)
base, E4 and later1 tickThe floor, reached by creep across Cam 24–25, not by an unlock. It holds at 2 through Cam 23, which opens the third approach (§13). “Fast, coordinated” (Fleet Memory §9)
R, a relay−1 while a fleet Recon (a picket hull or a rider) is within its sight range of the receiving formation; never below 1Fleet Memory §7: scouts are the premium accelerant. So sinking the Recons slows every link they were carrying
T, a thin net+1 while the fleet’s hulls on the board number fewer than half its launch count this sortieFleet Memory §7: fewer hulls, a thinner net that spreads slower. Threshold start ½, range ⅓–⅔, settled when testers who thin a fleet can see its links straggle
D, flag sunk+3 ticks, and the link type changes (LAW below)Fleet Memory §7: “slow and local”
Special twists0 ticksNamed per Throne (§22: T-01, T-02, T-03)

Entries quote the era’s base lag (“3 ticks later”, “+2”, “lag 1”); R and T always apply on top.

The answering sail (the link’s tell). When a link wakes, the receiving formation’s grey-green sails blanch pale for one tick and its wake lengthens. It is a sight, not a sound: the wood answers the wood without words. This is research P4’s “signal, answer, act” in a form that needs no audio and survives zoom. It is presentation only and never read back by the sim.

The blanch never comes alone. Each Tide pairs it with a behavioural beat, so the tell reads as ships obeying an order, and its growing crispness is itself the enemy getting better:

  • Second Tide: a visible hesitation. The formation’s bows swing a few degrees and settle before it moves, as if it had heard the order late (it has: the lag is 3).
  • Remembering: a tightening. The escort draws in to its tight spacing on the blanch tick (Fleet Memory §7: tight = believes it knows).
  • Joined Tide and after: an aspect swing on the same tick across roads. Formations on different approaches turn their shields to their objectives together.

These beats are presentation, drawn from the formation’s own state on the waking tick; the sim reads none of them back.

LAW · THE WOOD CANNOT HIDE ITS WAKING

Every link that fires shows the answering sail. There are no silent links, even for the cunning grain. A hinge wakes its formations when it resolves, on either branch, so its sail shows both ways. Deception in this game lives in true orders meant to mislead, never in hidden machinery (fiction_hooks §4; Fleet Memory legibility law).

LAW · PALE SAILS HEARD; GREY SAILS KEPT THE HOUR
  • A part released by its link blanches: the answering sail. So does a part released by a clock link after decapitation (below), because a clocked link still fires.
  • A part released by its latest tick, or by a link that broke, goes with its sails unchanged.
  • So the player can always tell a formation that believed what it heard from one that came because it was time. Presentation only; the sim never reads it back.
LAW · DECAPITATION SIMPLIFIES THE CARVING

Sinking the Standard-Bearer does four things.

  1. Pending links degrade.
    • Before Cam 25 they break. Each formation takes its fallback on local beliefs at once, as soon as the news reaches it over the slowed net.
    • From Cam 25 (the Joined Tide’s capability) they become clock links. Each captain fires at the tick the carving predicted for the event, not on the event, late by the decapitated lag (+3) plus its formation’s batch index (0, 1, 2… in spawn order: Fleet Memory §7’s command-confusion batching). WITH links keep their carved tick and take the same lateness, so a WITH shared by three formations breaks into three sails a tick apart. Every predicted tick an entry gives for its flag-sunk behaviour carries this lateness. The one exception is a clock the heart never kept: T-07’s blizzard is weather, and its links fire on it (§22).
    • An open hinge takes its foretold branch when its window closes (CALL-OUT 32). The rule has two clauses. (a) A chain hinge foretells its parent’s own branch, never the chain: the chain is the fleet’s second thought, and a dead heart has none. (b) Any other hinge foretells the branch that carries the payload; where both branches carry one, the branch keyed to the habit the carving was cut against. Each entry names its foretold branch under these clauses.
    • The fleet “keeps the hour the heart foretold, but not the reason” (fiction_hooks §5; Kael’s fourth count, Legends V.5), and it keeps it raggedly. A decapitated feint-and-strike therefore strikes even if the feint drew nothing, but three ticks late and out of step, and the walking reach gains those ticks. That is the command-by-intent answer to research conflict #6, it keeps the flag kill a lever inside the running sortie, and it amends Fleet Memory §7’s “coordination collapses” only as far as CALL-OUT 39 asks.
  2. The link budget falls by one for every later carving this battle, to a minimum of zero (the ratchet; Ships §4: successors are unbuffed).
    • A carving whose links no longer fit leaves the book for the battle, unless its entry names a reduced form, which is carved in its place.
    • Every E2 carving’s reduced form is its own shape with the link dead: the waiting part runs to its latest tick as if its partner’s event never came (“Second-Tide words with Hasty-Tide timing”, §18). Each E3 entry names the link it drops. A one-link wide carving (E3-05) has none to drop and leaves the book; E3-08’s reduced form is its ancestor’s, the wait in the grey with no look. The E4 ratchet table (§20) lists what survives each decapitation.
    • A standing long carving (below) is exempt: its cash-in keeps the links it was carved with.
  3. The grain changes to the successor’s lineage grain (§7). The player chooses which mind they will face next by choosing what they leave alive.
  4. From Cam 29, two rules more (§21).
    • A standing long carving survives the first fall, unless the successor’s grain weights its line at −2 or lower. Then it breaks at the next boundary (CALL-OUT 27).
    • The home clause. If the standard falls a second time in one sortie, every formation of the running carving withdraws in shape to spawn (the Lure root) instead of following its lineage policy. It is a fallback value, not new machinery, and retreat is still not safe passage (Victory §4; CALL-OUT 26).

Nothing already learned is lost: the Contact ledger, the fear grid and the Shore-Reading all persist (Fleet Memory §7). At the Thrones the Throne is the flag, and T-rule 8 governs its fall (§22).

Long carvings (from Cam 29; §21). One stratagem spread over several sorties, with numbered stages and a cash-in stage.

  • The stratagem history (§5 step 3) records it as one entry: for MEMORY, for half-life, and for Twistbough’s “never twice”.
  • At most one long carving stands at a time. Its standing part is a single formation. At each boundary the Heartwood re-carves that part by its entry’s standing rule, without re-selecting. For a routine or a promise the rule is sameness: same cell, same lane, same offset, same hour, and the sameness is the weapon (E5-03, E5-04, T-07). For E5-05’s straw it is the map: the next road it has not yet run.
  • The sortie’s own carving cannot use the standing formation, and that formation counts against the sortie’s ≤4 decision-bearing (§11). So while a lie stands, the sortie’s own carving must fit into ≤3 decision-bearing roles. The fleet spends its cleverness on the lie.
  • Each entry defines a stage test, read at the boundary. A failed test breaks the long carving, which is graded Countered. On a preparation sortie the standing formation sails idle and the stage count holds.
  • It also breaks when its formation is eliminated, or at any boundary where the current grain weights its line at −2 or lower. A proud or rash heart will not keep another heart’s lie. For long carvings, this is what “choose which heart you leave them” means (fiction_hooks §5).
  • At cash-in the long carving becomes the sortie’s own carving, with all its roles, links and hinge. The standing stages may come before the cash-in (routine or straw, then the real run) or after it (Fortitude’s promise).

Chain hinges (from Cam 29; §21). A hinge’s second branch may be a whole older stratagem, carved in full, for the same formations, before launch (both branches are carved: red line below).

  • When the chain is taken, the parent is graded Countered, because its lie was not believed. The chain is graded in the history on its own objective.
  • So every late carving carries an ancestor inside it, and the ancestor’s old counter still answers it. Each entry names its chain; §16c lists them.
  • A chain carves its ancestor whole where the parent’s formations can fill every ancestral role. Where they cannot, the entry names the chain as the ancestor’s form (E1-06-form, E2-01-form) and says what differs; the ancestor’s counter still answers the part that is carried.
RED LINE · NO MID-SORTIE RE-PLANNING

Outside a carved hinge, the Admiral does nothing during the Fight. A hinge’s branches are both carved before launch, and the window is shown by the signature (§9). This is what makes the fleet predictable enough to bait (Fleet Memory: “you can only deceive an opponent whose inference rule you can predict”) and it implements brief -2 fix (1): “belief changes routing only at sortie boundaries.” A captain evaluating a carved rule on its own perception (a self-trigger, §3) is not re-planning; nor is a long carving’s stage test, which is read at the boundary.

5THE SELECTION RULE

In one sentence, for the player: the Heartwood carves the stratagem that best fits what it believes, bent by its grain, aimed at your habits, and never the one that just failed, unless its grain is stubborn.

Inputs, all believed:

  • the fear grid at sortie end, after that boundary’s decay (Fleet Memory §3);
  • the Contact ledger, including ghosts and stale negatives (Fleet Memory §4);
  • the Shore-Reading (§6);
  • the fleet’s own records: its hits on each wall, the breath of each cell (§19 E3-07), the farthest cell it was struck in (§22 T-02);
  • the formations the queue will send next sortie, with losses, shields and resolve band (Victory §4b; Ships §9);
  • this battle’s stratagem history (§5 step 3);
  • the era’s book (§13) and the battle’s link budget after decapitations (§4);
  • the grain (§7).

No true state, and no seed.

Through Cam 8 the rule is S = FIT, once per formation (§17 LAW · GREEN WOOD TAKES ONE CUT): the Hasty Tide, and the Second Tide’s first two campaigns on their new board (§18). GRAIN, HABIT, MEMORY, LINE and INERTIA are all 0 there. The full rule below runs from Cam 9, when the Second Tide’s capability family turns on (§13).

Step 1 · Candidates. A stratagem is a candidate only if all of these hold:

  • it is in the cumulative book for this era (§13);
  • its links fit the battle’s link budget, or it names a reduced form that does (§4);
  • every required role can be filled by a sailing formation (step 4);
  • its preconditions hold in believed state. For example, a Feint needs ≥2 live approaches (≥2 flanks at the Thrones, §22 T-rule 4), and a Hunt needs ≥1 glinted gun;
  • it fits the sortie kind the composition law allows: payload or preparation (Victory §4b; at most one consecutive all-wolfpack sortie). Preparation sorties carve only W, and only a W whose roles wolfpacks can fill (§11);
  • if it is a long carving, no other long carving is standing (§4);
  • it is not retired (step 3, E5 and later).

If no candidate survives, the sortie carries no stratagem. Every formation takes idle: its own lineage policy on its own lowest-fear lane (step 4), with the held host applied (§11). Most preparation sorties end here, because a wolfpack-only sortie can fill few carvings’ roles: E3-05 Make an Example and the E1 wolfpack glyphs (E1-03, E1-05, and E1-06 for Recons). That is honest, and it is the fiction: the preparation sortie is the fleet’s crude arm, sent to wear the fort down, and it speaks Hasty-Tide words in every Tide.

Step 2 · Score. All arithmetic is integer.

S = FIT + GRAIN + HABIT + MEMORY + LINE + INERTIA

TermRangeHow it is computed
FIT0…4One point per satisfied gain condition. Every entry lists up to four, over believed state (e.g. “a HOT cell sits on the shortest lane to the softest believed coast”).
GRAIN−2…+3The grain’s weight for this stratagem’s line (§7 table). A Throne adds +3 on its master line (§22 T-rule 3).
HABIT0…+3+1 for each active Shore-Reading habit this stratagem is keyed to exploit (§6). Maximum 3. Silverbark doubles it.
MEMORY−5…+1This stratagem’s last outcome this battle: Succeeded +1 · Failed −3 · Countered −2 · untried 0. Grains modify it (§7).
LINE−2…+1+1 if an earlier form of the same line was already carved this battle (the Tide grows within the battle: the ratchet at strategy scale). −1 for each era of age when a later form of the same line is also a candidate (supersession, so the crude form fades once its heir exists).
INERTIA0 or +1+1 to last sortie’s stratagem if it was not Failed. This is hysteresis (brief -2 fix 3), so a one-tick difference in play rarely flips the doctrine. It does not apply after a Twistbough success, so “never twice” bites (CALL-OUT 23).

Outcome grading uses perceived events only. Each entry names its own three grades.

  • Succeeded: the stratagem’s objective event was observed (a breach made, cargo landed, bombers released over a castle, a hunted fix destroyed).
  • Failed: every formation carrying an objective-bearing role (cargo, strike, hunt, siege) was eliminated or routed before its commit point. The test reads objective-bearing roles, not cores, so a carving with no core (E2-05, E3-05) can still fail.
  • Countered: the hinge took the branch its entry names as Countered; or a lure, or any part whose objective is draw-fire, drew no fire (errand void, Victory §4b); or a long carving’s stage test failed. A decoy meant to pass unshot (E5-03’s host, E5-04’s routine, E5-07’s first ship) is graded by its entry, not by this clause. When a chain hinge takes its chain, the parent is graded Countered and the chain on its own objective (§4).

Step 3 · History and half-life.

  • From Cam 9 the Admiral remembers every stratagem carved this battle and its grade. A long carving is one entry (§4). Through Cam 8 there is no history: each formation is carved once (§17).
  • No immediate repeat of a Failed stratagem, except for the Ironbark and Heartoak grains (§7). This is research P13’s “no immediate repeats” deal (L4D).
  • Half-life (E5 and later only): a stratagem Countered twice is retired for the battle (research §3.14, the Q-ship decay; CALL-OUT 8).
  • Before E5 nothing retires. Early fleets repeat what fails, and that is part of the Ender’s Game feel: “the first are not organized.”

Step 4 · Roles.

  • Roles are filled in the stratagem’s priority order.
  • Each role takes the lowest-queue-index sailing formation whose kind (convoy / wolfpack / solo) and lineage pass the role filter.
  • An unfilled optional role is dropped. An unfilled required role removes the candidate (step 1).
  • Formations with no role take the stratagem’s idle role. The default idle role is pin: the formation’s own lineage policy on the lowest-fear lane to its own objective. Through Cam 8 there is no idle role: every formation carries its own word (§17).
  • When the carving’s decision-bearing parts plus the sortie’s core-bearing idle formations would exceed four, the surplus is held (§11 The held host).
  • With H10 active (§6), the command convoy is never given a part that commits first (probe, lure, or the first strike to arrive). Where the carving has a part that commits last and the command convoy’s kind fits it, the command convoy takes that part (E4-03’s kept hand, E4-08’s thin convoy); otherwise it sails idle. It never baits with its standard: only Esthaer spends its heart as bait (§22 T-05).
  • The Admiral never moves hulls or riders between formations. A carving that needs a particular pair, such as E4-08’s thick-and-light and thin-and-laden convoys, finds it in the queue or is not a candidate.
  • A Throne may fill a role with its own hull: Esthaer is the only flag in the book given a lure (§22 T-05).

Step 5 · Tie-break.

  1. Higher FIT.
  2. Then the lower era (the simpler carving).
  3. Then the lower id.

The rule never needs a seed. Two battles with identical believed states carve the same stratagem, and that sameness is the doctrine the player learns.

Step 6 · Fill the carving (§3 defaults, then the stratagem’s overrides). A standing part is re-carved by its standing rule and never re-selected: a long carving’s (§4), and a Throne’s across its groan sorties (§22 T-rule 3).

LAW · THE ADMIRAL DRAWS NO RANDOM NUMBERS

Variety between battles comes from the authored book, grain, spawns, queue and composition per battle (Enemy Overlay), and above all from the player’s own play. It does not come from dice. This is stronger than the fleet-stream rule (Why §3), and it makes the counterfactual-invariance test (§15) trivially true for the Admiral layer (CALL-OUT 16).

LAW · THE ADMIRAL NEVER OVERRIDES RESOLVE OR THE QUEUE
  • A refusing core stays refused. The Admiral may give it only the loiter part it is already playing (Victory §4a: the refusal tell is a presence).
  • A queue slot cannot be skipped.
  • Composition and refusal decide which formations sail. The Admiral decides how.

6THE SHORE-READING — WHAT THE FLEET BELIEVES ABOUT YOU

This is the key to “feels like a human” (research §1.11, poker opponent modelling; §1.13 MGSV). The Admiral keeps a small register of the player’s habits, built only from perceived events across the sorties of this battle.

LAW · A HABIT IS AN OBSERVATION COUNT, NEVER AN INFERENCE FROM TRUTH
  • Every habit below names the channel it is counted from.
  • A habit becomes active at count ≥ 2: “seen twice.” That is a starting value, range 2–3. It is settled by greybox, targeting the point where testers can feed a false habit on purpose within one battle.
  • A habit deactivates when it is contradicted twice in a row.
#Habit (the fleet’s words)Counted fromWhat a stratagem keyed to it does
H1“That quarter holds its fire.” Silent QuarterEffect: a fear cell read COLD on the approach turned HOT or SCALDING in the same sortie after a formation committed (a hold-feint observed)Sends a probe into that quarter first (the Sounding line), looks at the silent gun before trusting the silence (E3-08), or glints its gun by sight in passing (E4-07). A routine avoids it: H1 is E5-04’s gate
H2“They guard the ___ road.” Favoured BearingEffect: the approach carrying the largest share of fear mass at sortie endFeints toward it and strikes elsewhere (the Feint line); names the held road and the middle (E4-01, E5-01)
H3“They strike first and far.” Early FireEffect: the median lane-row of first incoming fire, per sortie, is in the reading bandLeads with bricks and decoys to spend the first volley (Bleed, Lure lines)
H4“Their reach moves when pressed.” Re-taskerEffect: new fear written on cells the formation had already passed through as COLD, inside one sortie (an AoR crawl observed)Times a second threat to the vacated arc (Feint, Lure lines; Corsair Wolfpack Lead), reads the walk live and comes home by the far road (E4-05), tempts the reach out with a prize and strikes where it stood (E5-06), and from Cam 25 carves the tick your reach will have walked (E4-01)
H5“They move the guns we find.” RelocatorContact: a glinted gun absent at its ledger position when re-observed next sortieFrom Cam 16, spares the known gun so the unknown stays unknown: no hunt role may take a glinted gun re-seen at its ledger position (the Hunt line’s H5 answer, E3-05 on). Also hunts inside the hour before a Deploy can move the gun (E5-02), or throws only at glints re-verified by sight (T-02)
H6“They mend what we break.” MenderContact: a breach recorded, then re-observed as wall the next sortie. It is the only mend the fleet can see: it reads blows, never HP, so a cracked wall healed unbreached is invisible (§19 LAW · THE LOOK IS A PRESENCE)Stops re-hitting the mended wall and strikes a fresh one (E3-02, E4-04), goes beneath the mending (T-03), or rots what you mend (T-06)
H7“They kill our bearers first.” Core-HunterOwn state: the order in which the fleet’s hulls sank, cores before screens. Its ground is the fear area and the Effect bearing the cores died to (the core-hunter’s bearing, §4)Thickens screens and carries decoy cores (Screen, Mask lines), draws the core-hunter’s bearing with a thick lure (E4-08), or parades a thin prize at the edge of its reach (E5-06)
H8“They are ready for the sky.” Sky-ReadyEffect: bombers hit within 4 ticks of release, over two sortiesReverses the domain strike: sky first, then sea while the Air-ready group reloads (E4-06, Nagumo’s dilemma)
H9“They let the first ship pass.” Patient ShoreEffect: a lure, probe, decoy or brick drew no fire while a later part of the same carving was fired on; or a waiting part was fired on after it sailed at its latest tick. One count per sortieFrom Cam 16, re-cuts every waiter’s latest tick (LAW below). Keyed forms: the hidden march goes with the siege (E3-06), the thin convoy goes with the thick (E4-08), and the first ship carries (E5-07)
H10“They strike at our standard.” Standard-HunterOwn state: the flag sank, or took the fleet’s first incoming fire of the sortieShields and hides the standard: the command convoy is never given a part that commits first, and takes the part that commits last where it fits (§5 step 4; E4-03’s kept hand, E4-08’s thin convoy). It never baits with it (§22 T-05)
H11“Behind their wall, another.” Kill-BoxOwn state: the fleet lost ≥2 hulls, or a troop stack, inside a ring it had breachedLooks before it pours: the ram holds at the breach until a look has gone into the ring (E4-04), and the Same Stone moves to its sister (E3-02). Ironbark counts it and comes anyway (T-03)
LAW · THE PATIENT SHORE (from Cam 16, with H9 active; CALL-OUT 40)

A waiting part whose event is fire drawn on a lure, probe, decoy or brick has its latest tick re-cut at the boundary: it goes at the tick its event is predicted to arrive (the lure’s predicted engagement, plus the lag), not at its station arrival + L_max. If they will not answer the loud, go with the loud.

  • Silence no longer buys a lone straggler into loaded guns. The waiter comes at the hour it would have come had you answered, grey-sailed: it came because it was time (§4).
  • It is a latest-tick value carved at the boundary, not a link: no budget changes, and no mid-sortie re-planning (§4 red line). The spacing the entry carved for the answered case therefore holds (§11).
  • Before Cam 16 the waiter still goes at arrival + L_max: the Second Tide straggles by nature (§18).
  • Every silence counter in §17–§22 is written for a fleet without H9. Two such sorties teach the fleet H9, and from then on the player must vary the answer: answer the lure from enclosed guns, answer it on a tick of their choosing, or shoot the first ship one sortie in two (§10).

Each habit crossing into active earns one dispatch line (§9c): H9 “They marked that we let the first ship pass.” · H10 “They marked that we strike at their standard.” · H11 “They marked that behind our wall stands another.”

Capacity grows with the era (§13). E1 has none, and the register opens at Cam 9. E2 has 1 habit active at a time, the one with the highest count. E3 has 2. E4 has 2 at Cam 23–24 and 3 from Cam 25. E5 has 3 at Cam 27–28 and 4 from Cam 29. The Thrones have 6. Ties go to the lower habit number. At the Thrones, H2 counts the flank that carries the most fear mass (§22 T-rule 4).

Over-fitting is the feature (research P15). The fleet believes what it has counted. A player who holds the north silent twice, on purpose, has taught the fleet H1 and can then spring the south. That is baiting raised to the level of strategy: Fleet Memory §6’s bait window, one layer up.

Habits are per battle. They clear on replay (Fleet Memory: the fleet forgets between battles). They never carry from one battle to the next, because that would make battle n’s outcome depend on how battle n−1 was played. Two players playing battle n identically would then get different results, which breaks vision test 1 and the leaderboard (Why §3). The fiction’s “the wood remembers across the war” is the authored era and creep, not the player’s record (fiction_hooks §3). The Legends tell it so: “The groves learn the wall, and not the hand” (Legends V.3). CALL-OUT 7.

Every adaptation opens a counter-counter (research P16). A habit-keyed stratagem commits hulls to the habit’s answer, and each entry says what it leaves thin. Massing against your silent north thins the south. Answering your early fire with bricks means the bricks carry no cargo.

7GRAINS — ADMIRAL PERSONALITIES

A grain is the Heartwood’s temper: how it weights the lines, and how it reacts to failure. It is set per battle for the first Standard-Bearer (Enemy Overlay). On field promotion it becomes the successor’s lineage grain (Ships §4: “the fleet takes on its personality”).

GrainTemper (the characteristic error, research P18)Line weights (+ favoured / − avoided)Failure reaction (MEMORY term)Lineages that promote to it
GreenwoodNone yet: the Hasty Tide’s unformed wood. Carves by fit alone and repeatsall 0None: each formation is carved once and keeps its word until it is gone (§17)every lineage through Cam 8
IronbarkStubborn, sunk-cost. Re-carves a failing plan, one form heavierSiege +3, Flood +2, Screen +1, Mask −2Failed → +1 and prefers the line’s next formBombard
WillowCautious. Switches at the first failure and prefers to look before it leapsSounding +3, Patience +2, Flood −2Failed → −5; Countered → −3Recon, Chirurgeon-Fleet
BlackthornVengeful, fixated. Aims at whatever hurt it mostHunt +3, Lure +1, Patience −2Failed → −3, but +2 to any stratagem whose objective is the fear cell or Contact where it lost most hulls last sortieReaver
HeartoakProud. Never turns aside, never feints, and retries on the same roadFlood +2, Screen +3, Feint −2, Mask −2Failed → −1, and +1 to re-carving the same stratagem on the same approachBulwark, Breacher, Leviathan
TwistboughCunning. Prefers deception and never runs the same trick twiceFeint +3, Mask +2, Lure +2, Flood −2Succeeded → −2 (“never twice”); Failed → −3Corsair, Wraith, Mimic
YewPatient. Delays, conditions, and waits for the openingPatience +3, Bleed +2, Siege +1, Flood −1Failed → −3; Countered → 0 (it waits and retries later)Skyfall, Sower
AshwoodRash. Commits early and throws the reserve inFlood +3, Hunt +1, Patience −2Failed → −2; its reserve roles launch at offset 0Runner, Provocateur
SilverbarkWise, the council-elder grain. Reads you best and bends leastall ±0; HABIT term ×2Countered → −4 (it learns fastest)Quartermaster; the Crystal Lich
LAW · A GRAIN IS LEGIBLE BEFORE IT IS FELT
  • The first Standard-Bearer’s lineage is visible from sortie 1 (its flag icon, Ships §4), and each lineage maps to one grain. So a player learns “a Bombard flag digs in; a Corsair flag never repeats a trick.”
  • The grain is named in the Knowing after the battle (§9).
  • Its tell in play is its reaction to failure. It either repeats (Ironbark, Heartoak) or switches (Willow, Twistbough), and that is visible from the second sortie.
LAW · SUCCESSION IS A CHOICE THE PLAYER MAKES

The anchor hierarchy decides who inherits (Ships §4: flagship > Herald/Rally > Quartermaster > capitals > peers). Leave a Quartermaster alive and you will face Silverbark. Leave only Runners and you will face Ashwood. The next-in-line anchor carries a pennant (brief -6 fix), so the succession, and the grain it brings, is predictable.

Mystarchs are the ten eldest hearts. They are the last of the black pillars, the Mystholders, grown into moving thrones (Legends III.2). Each Throne has a fixed grain and a master line worth an extra +3 (§22 T-rule 3; the roster is §23b). This is research P14: a repertoire, a signature and a published weakness.

A grain can also end a lie. A standing long carving breaks at any boundary where the current grain weights its line at −2 or lower (§4). How each grain plays across the whole book, and how to beat it, is the character sheet of §23a.

8STABLE DOCTRINE, SENSITIVE DETAIL

Vision test 1 asks for two things that pull apart: determinism that can be learned, and a world where “an action 1 second earlier or later could change the course of the game.” They sit on two different layers of the plan.

LayerExamplesSensitivityWhy
Doctrine: which stratagemCold Shadow this sortieStable. A one-tick change in player input should leave it unchanged in ≥95% of cases (greybox bar, §15)Its inputs are quantised: named fear tiers, Contact presence, habit counts ≥2, formation roster. INERTIA adds hysteresis (§5)
Detail: how it landsWhich hull fills the screen, which of two near-equal lanes, which tick the hinge resolves on, which branchSensitive by design. A held alpha one tick earlier sinks the probe before it reports, so the hinge takes branch B and the landing goes north instead of southHinges and links fire on perceived events, and those are pure functions of the exact tick of the player’s orders (§12)

The player therefore learns the doctrine and plays the detail. “It’s a Sounding” is recognition. “If I kill the sounder before tick 22, the main body never learns the south is open” is the counter, and it lives on a tick.

LAW · HINGES ARE QUANTISED; OUTCOMES ARE NOT

Hinge conditions test events that are true or false (was fire observed in this window?). They never test graded magnitudes, so small perturbations produce small, explainable changes (brief -2). Resolve, with its thresholds and cascades, is kept off the Admiral’s inputs except through the coarse resolve band per formation: steady, wavering or breaking.

9THE RECOGNITION LOOP

Research P6 says to telegraph tactics and conceal strategy. Research P7 says to name the strategy afterwards. The loop has four surfaces, and none of them labels a stratagem live.

9a · Signature: the opening, in the reading band (Fight §3, 18 ticks)

  • Every stratagem opens with a fixed, visible signature built only from tells that already exist:
    • launch-zone presence;
    • launch order and offset;
    • shape and tightness (Ships §9);
    • lateral versus fore-aft motion (Ships §4);
    • payload versus preparation silhouette, and ride height (a laden hull rides low; §24 Ships);
    • the answering sail (§4).
  • Each line has one root sign, and every stratagem in the line keeps it. A late player who sees the root sign asks “which Sounding is this?” and that question is the recognition Jack asked for (research §4: “you know the words, now read the sentence”).
  • Seren names each root sign for the stroke it makes in the wood (fiction_hooks §6; the Book of Knowings, the Legends’ appendix), and the codex uses her names.
LineRoot sign (Seren’s name)What every form keeps
Floodthe WaveEvery formation leaves the launch zone at offset 0, on straight lanes
Soundingthe Lone StrokeOne lone hull leaves first, ≥6 ticks ahead of anything else
Screenthe Bar BeforeShields lead the cargo by one cell, tight, their aspect locked on one hot cell
Huntthe SparkFormations bend toward a glinted gun. A Reaver pack waits at the fear edge
Siegethe Mute SquareBombards line abreast on the same wall, sortie after sortie
Feintthe Two MouthsOne formation moves laterally with no fore-aft commitment while another approach is quiet
Lurethe Turned SternA formation turns its stern whole. In the Hasty Tide it scatters once clear of your reach (a real flight); from the Tide of Remembering it keeps its shape all the way (a false one: a true rout scatters, Ships §4)
Bleedthe BreathA cheap hull goes in alone and turns after drawing fire, while the rest wait at the fear edge
Patiencethe KnotFormations loiter in the launch zone or a COLD fringe and launch late (offset ≥12)
Maskthe Smoothed CutSmoke before the sails, or silhouettes that do not jink when shells land near them (brief -8), or a thick formation riding high
LAW · A SIGNATURE IS A PRESENCE AND NEVER LIES BY ACCIDENT

Victory §4a: the tell is a presence, not an absence. Ships §9: a shape never lies by accident.

  • Deceptive lines (Feint, Lure, Mask) are licensed liars, and each carries a counter-tell in its entry. Examples: the feint has no fore-aft commitment; the feigned rout keeps its spacing; phantoms leave no wreck and do not jink.
  • From Cam 29 the decoy role may also make a shape lie, and only with its seam (§21 RED LINE · A SEAM NEVER LIES).
  • The Mimic may lie about ships and never about the exposure glint. It provokes real glints and never forges one (brief, skeptic note; Fleet Memory §8: one hard rail). This strikes Ships §6’s forged glints and fear-haze from a permanent lineage, so it is Jack’s ruling to make (CALL-OUT 35).

9b · The Knowing of the Wood: after the battle

In the debrief, Halyna, the Bonded of the Keep, know the carving from wreck-wood (Legends V.1 tells the first Knowing). The source is the Standard-Bearer if it sank; otherwise the formation that hurt the fort most; otherwise any sunk hull.

  • The Knowing is generated from the sim’s own record of the carving that ran, so it is always true.
  • It explains this battle and never hints at the next (story_bible C3).
  • Halyna know the wood; no one reads it. Halvard and Rhyna are Aetherbonded, husband and wife, sealed as Halyna, and the bond is why the wood gives itself to them at all: it gives its knowing whole to no single mind. There are no words in the grain. One of them touches the wood and its carving is known, whole, as a memory is known; and what one knows, the other knows, so only one of them need touch it. The knowing is not the hard thing. The telling is, for a knowing cannot be turned the way words can. So they tell it by turns in the fewest, plainest words they can find: one begins the line and the other finishes it, and a longer telling goes a paragraph each. Seren Two-Inks, their unbonded third, cannot know the wood herself; she sets down what Halyna tell, in two inks, marks every break, and does not mend it with guesses. This is Jack’s vision 6: the Knowing of the Wood is one act done by two, and every Carving line in §17–§22 is told as the two of them tell it.
  • Fidelity rises with the war, the Ender’s Game ordering turned on the tellers: at first a shore-mind receives only the shape of a knowing, and over the Tides Halyna learn to hold more of it and to tell it (Legends §4):
EraWhat the Knowing showsExample (fiction_hooks §6 has more)
E1 · Hasty TideUntold glyph. One carved sign, drawn. Halyna know only its shape and can tell one word of it, and the codex gives the plain gloss of what the formations did (from the sim). The carven name stays blank until the Night of the Naming (Legends V.2)᛫ “…shore…” · Codex: “Both formations sailed straight for the south coast and did not wait for each other.”
E2 · Second TideNamed. The two-sign phrase is told, with gaps. Near the end of the Tide, when Cam 13 is complete, the E1 glyphs are known again, whole, and named in the codex (the Night of the Naming, Legends V.2), so the first carvings are understood at last“Screen … bearer.” → Cold Shadow
E3 · RememberingSentences, with doubtful words marked and the memory clause clear“Where the stone fell [silent?], look before you trust.”
E4 · Joined TideFluent, with the links. It says who waited for whom“The west waited for the east to be struck.”
E5 · Learned DeceitFluent, with its aim. It names the habit the carving was cut against, and tells the chain when the fleet took it“Cut against: they hold the north silent.”
Last Tide (Cam 32)Whole plans. The carving that ran, the branch it did not take, and each formation’s fallback, the home clause included. Still this battle only, so it explains and never forecasts (CALL-OUT 28)“When the bearer falls, the second takes the standard; when the second falls, go home.” (Legends V.7, Part III)
ThronesThe Mystarch’s true name, the haven it holds, its Judgement, and the wrong it avenges (§22 T-rule 9)—
  • Partial live readings (E5 and the Thrones). When a hull carrying the running stratagem sinks during the sortie, its wreck floats a single glyph for the rest of the sortie: the line’s root sign, never the form. Seren has learned to read the first stroke by eye: a root sign is read, and only the rest of a carving must be known (Legends, the Book of Knowings). It is earned by a sink, it is partial, and it names the family, not the plan. That is enough to ask “which Feint?” early, and never enough to be told (CALL-OUT 14).

9c · The dispatch: between sorties (Build/Deploy)

This is a short “what they learned” note, in the voice of the lookouts or Halyna:

  • new glints: the Contacts gained;
  • fear-tier changes at sortie end (the Deploy intel layer, brief -1 fix a/b);
  • from Cam 9, at most one habit line when a habit crosses into active (“They marked that the north holds its fire”; §6 gives each habit’s line).
RED LINE · THE DISPATCH NEVER CARRIES INTENT
  • It reports observations, the inputs to the next carving, and never the carving itself.
  • It never names the coming stratagem, lane or objective.
  • Brief -1’s option (d), “intercepted orders”, is rejected for the default game. It would put Into the Breach’s telegraph on the fleet level, which vision test 2 forbids (“I don’t want the player to know exactly what the ships are doing at first”). It is kept only as a possible assist mode (CALL-OUT 13).

9d · The Book of Knowings (codex)

  • Every stratagem met is logged by its signature. Once known, it is logged by name, with its plain gloss and its counters.
  • Each growth line is a page that fills from root to capstone, so the codex shows the player the ladder they are climbing (§16b).
  • An entry is earned when the stratagem is met and survived by any route, never only by a particular counter (story_bible C5; no forced playstyle).

10COUNTERS LAW

LAW · EVERY STRATAGEM HAS ≥2 COUNTERS, ONE BLUNT, AND NONE REQUIRES A SPECIFIC TRICK (Why §4 No Forced Playstyle; Ships §11)
  • Blunt means coverage, enclosure, a cannon or tower from the locked kit, or simply not taking the bait. It needs no timing and no reading of the fleet.
  • Clever means reading the signature and exploiting the stratagem’s commitment: a hinge, a link or its thin side. Clever is cheaper, never required. The board audit checks that the clever line is cheaper (brief -5 fix).
  • Counters use only the locked kit: 8 cannons (Standard, Piercer, Marksman, Interdictor, Suppressor, Breaker, Splasher, Saturation), 7 towers (Spotter, Powder Store, Stonewright, Camouflage, Rally Flag, Flare, Jammer) (Cannon §11); 3 Fight verbs (re-task, domain switch, fire-control); and Build/Deploy (walls, relocation, enclosure).
  • The Powder Store adds damage, not rate of fire. No counter may cite a tower for shortening a reload; the fire-rate towers are not in the locked kit (§19 E3-07).
  • No counter needs two orders closer together than Command Time’s post-commit dwell allows (§12). A counter that chains two groups is written so the second order falls inside the dwell or on a later, announced tick.
  • A counter that works by silence (holding fire, not taking the bait) is true until the fleet counts it. From Cam 16 two such sorties make H9 active, and the waiter’s latest tick is re-cut (§6 LAW · THE PATIENT SHORE); the same holds for a standard struck first (H10) and a kill-box behind the breach (H11). Every entry’s counters are written for a fleet that has not yet counted them, and each keyed form says what the counted habit changes. The player who varies wins; the player who repeats is read, as a human opponent would read them.

Every entry in §17–§22 lists its blunt (B) and clever (C) counters.

11ATTENTION-LAW COMPLIANCE

Victory §3, Why §6, Campaign §4.

LawHow the Admiral obeys it
≤8 contacts; the Campaign ramp is 2 / 4–6 / 6–8Each entry declares C, its contacts. The book for an era contains only stratagems whose C fits that era’s band. The Admiral cannot add hulls, only assign parts. A released bomber cloud and a landed troop stack are contacts too (Victory §3): each is sequenced ≥12 ticks after the arrival before it, so two decisions never land together (§21 E5-01)
≤4 decision-bearing contactsDecision-bearing roles are strike, cargo, hunt, lure, decoy, probe (it asks a question), a siege that is itself the question (a line parked or working a wall), and any role carrying a hinge. An idle or pin formation that carries a core counts too: an idle Runner convoy is a landing in all but name. screen, and wolfpacks in pin or idle, are glanceable. A standing long-carving part counts against the sortie’s ≤4 (§4). A decoy counts (it asks a question). The limit binds the whole sortie, not only the carving: any core-bearing formation beyond it is held (below)

Wide and simple, or narrow and clever, never both (Ships §9; Victory §4b)Clever (P) means ≥1 hinge, ≥2 links, or any decoy or lure role; it fields ≤4 contacts. Wide (W) carries ≤1 link, no hinge, no decoy and no lure; only a W carving may field 5–8 contacts. Spacing law (Fight §6; Campaign §4)Any two linked arrivals that demand a costed verb (a re-task or a domain switch) of the same player group are carved ≥ 12 ticks apart: crawl ≈8 ticks (Victory: the AoR crawl is 1 grid/tick, and a typical re-task is ~8 grids) + reload 4 ticks (Cannon §4). Starting value, range 10–16. Settled in greybox so a planned single-group walk always fits. Arrivals that ask no costed verb may come closer: a convergence on one gun whose blunt answer is made in Deploy (E3-05), or a strike inside a reload whose blunt answer is free fire-control (the Bleed line’s licence, E2-06, E3-07). Both keep the red line against forced simultaneity (Fight §2; Victory §3; CALL-OUT 24). From Cam 23, wherever one group may answer two approaches, the floor widens to Δ = max(12, d + 4 + s), with a slack s of 4 ticks so a planned walk arrives a reload early (§20 LAW · THE THIRD ROAD WIDENS EVERY GAP)
Hinge windows≥ 6 ticks wide. A third of the reading band (Fight §3). The choice must be makeable at full speed by a player who planned it, and Command Time (§12) makes it deliberate
Two eye-seizing events (Fight §5)Signatures are built so the only sudden events are the two the Fight allows: a valuable target committing into a bright core, and a flank going wrong. Link wakes (the answering sail) are deliberately quiet
No punishment for an unwatched, unsignalled contact (red line)Every decision-bearing role carries its line’s root sign during the reading band. A contact with no sign may only be a wolfpack pin or idle, or a held formation (below)

The held host (every era). When the carving’s decision-bearing parts plus the sortie’s core-bearing idle or pin formations would exceed four, the surplus core-bearing formations, highest queue index first, are held. The Admiral may not skip a queue slot or keep a formation from sailing (§5 LAW), so a held formation sails and waits:

  • it waits at the launch zone’s forward edge, full-screened, in shape and bow-on: a presence, never a refusal (§3 LAW · A HOLD IS A PRESENCE; §15 test 8);
  • it sails as pin on its lowest-fear lane on the tick the sortie’s live decision-bearing count falls below four (a part eliminated, routed or withdrawn, or a landing whose stack has itself been cleared), lowest queue index first, and its arrival is spaced ≥12 ticks after the last decision-bearing arrival;
  • a held formation still at the forward edge at t24 (the latest start that completes a passage inside the backstop: 18 ticks to the band, 16 through it, 12 home) goes home unengaged and sails first next sortie (Victory §4b: survivors sail first).

Wolfpacks are never held: a wolfpack in pin or idle is glanceable. At the Thrones this is T-rule 5’s held host (§22).

12COMMAND TIME (JACK’S SLOW-TIME RULING) AND THE FLEET

The ruling, in numbers (Jack, 2026-09-26; CALL-OUT 2, resolved).

  • The normal siege clock is 1 siege day = 18 real s = 24 game-hours of 60 minutes, so 1 game-hour = 0.75 real s. The racing hour is meant: it is the urgency of the holding.
  • The sim tick stays 1 real s at the siege pace. It is the unit the crawl, the reloads and the carvings are written in, so 1 tick = 1⅓ game-hours (80 game-minutes).
  • When the player starts an order, time ramps down (it does not snap) to 18 real s per game-hour. That is 1/24 speed: “an hour lasts as long as a day.” Game-minutes tick every 0.3 real s, and one sim tick lasts 24 real s.
  • When the order is committed or cancelled, time dwells at the floor for a moment, then ramps back up.
  • Orders are stamped in sim ticks.
ParameterStarting valueRangeHow it will be settled
Trigger (“starting an order”)Touch-down on a group (the order begins when you pick up the group)touch-down / drag-start / commitCALL-OUT 3. Greybox A/B: does touch-down turn every sortie into a slow-motion study session?
Ramp-down duration0.75 real s0.5–1.5Feel number (CALL-OUT 4). The target is “a breath, not a brake”: testers should not be able to name the moment it stopped easing
Post-commit dwell1.0 real s at the floor after the order commits, before the ramp-up begins0.5–2Feel number (CALL-OUT 4). Touching another group inside the dwell keeps time at 1/24 and starts that group’s order. Settled so a player who planned two orders (volley the west head, then alpha the east) places the second without racing the ramp: no counter in the book needs two orders faster than the dwell (§10)
Ramp-up duration1.5 real s1–3Feel number. Slower than the ramp-down, so the return to speed is never a jolt
Floor1/24 speed: an hour takes 18 real s, and a game-minute 0.3 s—Jack’s ruling (2026-09-26)
CurveEase in log(speed) (smoothstep)—Perceptual: speed changes are felt as ratios

What the fleet does in Command Time: nothing different.

  • The sim steps the same fixed ticks and the renderer interpolates.
  • The fleet’s cadences are all in ticks: captains every tick, links on arrival, hinges on tick windows, and the Admiral only at the boundary. So a slower wall-clock never gives the fleet “more thinking”, and never gives it less.
  • The backstop clock (70 ticks) and the time bank (Victory §5) run on sim ticks, so Command Time never burns the bank. The Build and Deploy clocks stay real-time (they have no fleet to wait for).
LAW · WALL-CLOCK SPEED NEVER CHANGES AN OUTCOME

An order stamped at tick T produces the identical battle whether it was placed at full speed or at 1/24. Greybox test: inject the same orders with randomised wall-clock delays and fixed tick stamps, then assert identical state hashes (§15).

How Command Time serves reading a stratagem.

  • Hinges become choices. From the Tide of Remembering almost every stratagem has a hinge tick where your action or silence picks the branch (§4; research conflict #9); in the Second Tide the same choice picks the tick a link wakes. In Command Time you watch the probe cross the silent cell, and you decide on this tick whether your north battery speaks. “A second earlier or later” becomes a deliberate choice of tick, never a reflex (vision test 1 meets vision test 3).
  • Signatures stay alive. Tells, including the answering sail, keep animating at 1/24. A player composing a re-task can watch the root sign resolve and ask “which Sounding?” This is why touch-down is the recommended trigger: picking up a group is how a commander studies the board.
  • The held alpha moves into tick-space. Fight §2’s “half-second early or late” becomes “which tick.” Dexterity no longer substitutes for the read, and the lack of it no longer punishes a good read.
CALL-OUT · COMMAND TIME AND THE FELT TARGET

Why §4 wants the late war “always just barely overwhelming.” The ≤4 / ≤8 / ≤3 ceilings were derived for real-time tracking. If touch-down Command Time is free and unlimited, a player can slow every hinge and the late war may go slack. Recommendation:

  • Accept it. The ceilings were attention ceilings, and Command Time helps only the group being ordered, one at a time (there is no multi-select, Fight §6). The post-commit dwell lets a second planned order follow the first at the floor, so two-group counters test the plan, not the hand.
  • Do not raise any ceiling to compensate.
  • Measure it in greybox. If slack appears, the fix is to move the trigger to drag-start, never to add a budget or a cost.

Fight §3 L40 and §10 L231 (“no in-Fight pause or slow-motion”) are superseded and need revising. The fiction may call it “the Captain’s long breath” (story_bible C10; Legends I.3, The Captain’s Long Breath).

The Twelve: one battery at a time, in the fiction (Legends I.1, I.3). Each battery (a player gun group) is commanded by one of the Captain’s twelve True Men, who knows the Captain’s mind and fights his battery by his own judgment. That is why the groups act independently between orders, and why the Captain calls to one battery at a time, by horn, with three calls every True Man knows: shift your ground, change your shot, hold, or loose (the three verbs of §10: re-task, domain switch and fire-control; there is no multi-select, Fight §6). The runners carry what is seen, never commands. When the Captain calls, the hour stretches: that is Command Time.

Hour arithmetic (resolved; CALL-OUT 2). Build §2 displays 24 hour-values per 18 s day, 0.75 s an hour, and that is Jack’s ruling: the day has 24 hours, and the racing hour is the intended urgency. No tick count changes. 1 tick = 1 real s at the siege pace = 1⅓ game-hours, so the AoR crawl of 1 grid per tick and the 1 grid/s sea speed (Victory §3) stand, and so does every tick in this doc. Only the old statement that a tick is an hour is withdrawn.

13THE ERAS — THE ENDER’S GAME RAMP, ONE CAPABILITY FAMILY AT A TIME

“The first are not organized, but they get better.” Each era unlocks one capability family (research P13; Plants vs. Zombies): one idea whose parts make sense only together, turned on in one campaign. The book is cumulative: older stratagems stay available and fade by supersession (the LINE term, §5). The era boundaries follow the Whisper bands (GDD) and the Campaign contact ramp. The fiction names each era a Tide (fiction_hooks §2).

These steps are discrete in kind and plan-level: links, hinges, the look, WITH, anticipation, decoys, long carvings, chain hinges. They supersede Campaign §4’s “no tiers” for plan-level capabilities only; estimator quality (fear decay, propagation, the lag’s creep, the cognition apexes that are not plan-level) keeps creeping per battle as Campaign §4 and Why §5 say (CALL-OUT 38). No step carries a banner. Each is shown first by one carving and read afterwards.

Why each family is one idea.

  • E2, the phrase and the temper (Cam 9): a mind that re-carves every hull for every morrow must carve by something, and the temper (grain, MEMORY, the failure reaction, one habit) is what it carves by; the one link and the lure role are the first thing it carves.
  • E3, the memory clause (Cam 16): the look, the hinge and a history with branches. The wood remembers, so it checks, and a check is worth nothing unless it can change the road.
  • E4, one hour on three roads (Cam 25): the heart carves a tick (WITH across roads); a tick carved for where your reach will be is anticipation; a tick that outlives the heart is the clock fallback; the third link and the third habit are what three roads need.
  • E5, true orders meant to mislead (Cam 29): a lie needs a liar with a seam (the decoy), a lie told over sorties is the long carving, the second thought when the lie is not believed is the chain hinge, the fleet’s answer to its own lies being read is half-life, and the fourth habit is what the lies aim at.
LAW · DISCRETE EVENTS TURN SINGLY (Campaign §4 choreography law, restated in discrete events per the brief)
  • A capability family never shares a campaign with a new approach (Cam 7, 23) or with piece-layer C (Build §5: pieces and cognition never turn together). That gives unlocks at Cam 9, 16, 25 and 29. The lag’s creep is estimator quality, not a family: it holds at 2 through Cam 23 and creeps to 1 across Cam 24–25.
  • A family debuts carried by one carving, on a battle that introduces nothing else. The carrier may be a new carving that exists to show it (E2-03 at Cam 9, the first link; E3-01 at Cam 16, the first hinge and the first look; E5-02 at Cam 29, the first decoy), or a known carving re-cut in the new grammar (E3-04 re-cut WITH at Cam 25 battle 2, the first hour on two roads). Where a family holds a part no single sortie can show, a second carrier shows it on a later battle of the same campaign: the long carving, which needs several sorties, is carried by E5-05.
  • A stratagem debut happens on a battle, not a campaign: one new carving per battle, never on a battle that also introduces a lineage or an approach, and never on a campaign’s first battle. With ~6 battles per campaign (190 ÷ 32), the calendar in §16a is satisfiable.
  • Exemptions. The two baseline words, Landfall and Break the First Wall, are live from Cam 1’s first battle: they are the Runner’s and the Bombard’s own policies given an objective rule. Each Throne battle introduces its Throne, chassis and Judgement together (CALL-OUT 31).
  • A debut is real only if the debut board makes it win. Enemy Overlay authors the queue so that the formation meant to carry the new carving first sails after a sortie that writes its conditions (§24).
LAW · BREADTH ENTERS WITH LAYER B (Campaign §5; GDD matrix; CALL-OUT 11)
  • Layer B is the advanced-enemy layer, the breadth of new lineages (B at Cam 3, 7, 11–13, 17–19, 23–25, 27–29, 31, 32). Every lineage after Cam 1’s baseline debuts on a campaign that carries B.
  • A campaign without B debuts no lineage, and fields every lineage already met. “Basic enemies” (Cam 30) means no new hull, not a smaller roster. So a carving may debut on a no-B campaign with any lineage the player has already faced (Cam 14, 16, 20–22, 26, 30).
  • Two base-lineage exceptions, both put to Jack: the Recon at Cam 2 (A), the fleet’s eyes, which bring no hull the player must answer with new kit and without which the Sounding root has nothing to carry it; and the Sower at Cam 9 (AD), whose smoke and fire are the fleet’s own weather and arrive with D.
