The early war is genuinely crude. In E1 there is no plan above the formation. Two formations may carry two unrelated glyphs, arrive piecemeal, and fixate on the first wall they touch. They repeat a failing carving until the hull carrying it is gone. That is Jack’s “not organized”: the Viking raids of 787–793 and the first Sea Peoples raids (research §3.7, §3.21). It is not merely weaker.

It gets better in kind, never in HP (Why §4). Each step is visible:

  • the first answering sail (E2, Cam 9);
  • the first formation that waits for another (E2);
  • the first formation that comes back saying something new, because from Cam 9 the Heartwood re-carves every hull for every morrow (E2);
  • the first probe of a gone-quiet cell (E3, Cam 16);
  • three roads arriving on one hour (E4, Cam 25);
  • the first lie told in true wood (E5, Cam 29), after a prize at Cam 28 in which nothing is false;
  • the first plan that plans for losing (the Last Tide, Cam 32).

These are the “they’re checking now” moments Campaign §4 requires to be legible in kind, and the discrete steps CALL-OUT 38 asks Jack to rule on. Each Tide’s characteristic error is gathered in §23c.

14DETERMINISM LEDGER ADDITIONS (FOR FLEET MEMORY §12)

MechanismDeterministic because…
Stratagem selection (§5)Integer score over quantised believed state; tie-break by FIT, era, id. No RNG draws at all.
Carved once (through Cam 8, §17)Each formation’s glyph is a pure function of believed state at its first boundary; its fields are refilled by the glyph’s own rule at every later boundary.
Role assignmentQueue order plus role priority order, with H10’s filter (§5 step 4). The Admiral never moves hulls or riders.
The held host (§11)Surplus core-bearing formations held by highest queue index; each sails on the tick the live decision-bearing count falls below four, lowest queue index first; t24 cut-off.
Every selection’s tie-breakAny rule that picks “the nearest”, “the freshest”, “the most” or “the lowest” breaks ties by lower cell index (row-major), then lower queue index (Fleet Memory §12). Two glints written on the same tick: the lower cell index is the freshest.
Lane and approach choiceOrdinal fear weights; ties by distance to the objective’s Contact, then row-major cell index (the existing grid tie-break).
Link lag (§4)Integer: max(1, base − R) + T + D, from era, Recon presence, hull count and flag state at the event’s tick.
Links and hingesFire on perceived events arriving over the (deterministic, batched) propagation net, or on carved ticks; windows in ticks. Clock links after decapitation fire on the tick the carving predicted, +3, plus the formation’s batch index in spawn order.
Self-triggersEvaluated by the captain on its own formation’s channel events, every tick.
Link budget and reduced formsAn integer per battle, lowered by one per decapitation; each entry names its reduced form.
Shore-ReadingInteger observation counts from channel events, H1–H11. Per battle; cleared on replay. H9’s re-cut latest tick is a boundary value (§6).
The breath (§19 E3-07)Per fear cell, the most recently measured interval in whole ticks between two volleys observed striking one formation from that cell this battle. Derived from Effect tick stamps; holds no new information.
Blow counts and the most-hit wallThe fleet’s own record of connecting shots per wall section (Contact channel). It never reads wall HP.
The farthest cell struck (§22 T-02)Own-state Effect: the farthest fear cell in which a hull of the fleet was struck this battle.
Long carvings (§4)Stage counts are integers read at boundaries; the standing part is re-carved identically.
The rings (§22 T-rule 2)Count sortie boundaries; no wall-clock, no RNG. The Judgement’s sortie is known a whole Build ahead.
Grain on successionLineage → grain lookup; successor from the published anchor hierarchy.
Command TimePresentation only. The sim steps fixed ticks, orders stamp in ticks, and the backstop and bank run in ticks.
Knowings and dispatchesGenerated from the sim’s record after the fact; never read back by the sim (research P4, P5).
THE INVARIANT, EXTENDED

Carving = pure function of (believed state at sortie end). Believed state = pure function of the player’s tick-stamped inputs. So: the same play gives the same stratagems, the same links and the same branches. Play one tick differently and you may get the same doctrine with a different detail (§8), still fully reproducible.

15GREYBOX ACCEPTANCE TESTS (PASS BARS FIXED IN ADVANCE)

These follow the brief’s instruction that the prototype verifies perception as well as tuning feel (enemy-mind-legibility-3). Each entry’s feel numbers carry their own settle bars; the tests below are the doc-wide ones.

  1. Stability. In a headless sim, perturb one player input by ±1 tick across 1,000 saved sorties. The chosen stratagem must be unchanged in ≥95% of cases. Detail (lanes, branches) may differ. If it fails, add hysteresis or coarsen a precondition. Never add noise.
  2. Counterfactual invariance (research P1). Change any player state the fleet has not observed. The carving must be byte-identical until the tick that state enters a channel. Run in CI.
  3. Command Time invariance. Inject identical orders with randomised wall-clock delays and fixed tick stamps. The state hashes must be identical on iOS and Android.
  4. Recognition (research P6). After a sortie, testers pick the stratagem from three options. Target: chance on the first encounter, ≥70% by the third encounter of the same line.
  5. Explicability (research P7). After a debrief, ≥70% of testers can state one thing they will change to exploit the fleet’s belief. E3-07’s dash must read as “they moved on my silence”, not as luck; this test is its bar.
  6. Deviation read (research P3). About one visible captain deviation per sortie, and testers can say why it happened ≥70% of the time. Too rare reads as robotic, and too common reads as chaos. Measure the Thrones separately: fanatical nerve removes balks and sags there, so the expected rate is lower (§22 T-rule 6).
  7. Blunt-line audit (brief -5). A headless bot with no feints and no core-hunting must find a winning line against every stratagem at every era. The clever line must cost fewer castle-damage points than the blunt line.
  8. Stillness is read correctly. Testers tell apart, at a glance by t8: a convoy waiting in the launch zone (E2-02) and a refusing core (Victory §4a); a tight convoy halted at a fork (E3-01) and a refusing core; a hold at the fear edge (§3) and a refusal; a held formation at the launch zone’s forward edge (§11; §22 T-rule 5) and a refusal; T-03’s ram and hands at the forward edge and a refusal. If the E2-02 pair fails, that convoy waits at the launch zone’s forward edge instead (CALL-OUT 20). If a forward-edge pair fails, the held part also closes to tight spacing, the waiter’s tell (Fleet Memory §7).
  9. Seams are visible at overview zoom. The look (a line slowing at the edge of sight, E3-02, E3-08), the draught (a laden hull riding low, E4-08, E5-04, E5-07, T-08; verified at ≈27 pt, brief -4), the late sail (a mirror blanching a tick after its twin, T-08) and the non-jinking projection (E5-03, E5-05) are each identified by ≥70% of testers without zooming.

16THE STRATAGEM TREE

Forty-six carvings in ten lines across five Tides and the Thrones: 7 / 8 / 8 / 8 / 7 / 8. A line is a family of carvings that keeps one root sign (§9a) and grows by adding one primitive at a time (research P12: “each late stratagem must be describable as a combination of earlier ones in one sentence”). Lines overlap eras, skip eras, and borrow from each other. A simple word in Cam 1 is the root of a Judgement at the Thrones.

The grid: every line against every Tide. A dash is a Tide the line skips; its older form stays in the book (§13).

Line · root signE1 Hasty (1–6)E2 Second (7–14)E3 Remembering (15–22)E4 Joined (23–26)E5 Deceit + Last (27–32)Thrones

E1 carries seven of the ten roots. Feint, Bleed and Mask need two roads, a cadence or smoke, so their roots are Second-Tide words.

16a · All forty-six, with the calendar

C/A is the contacts and approaches the carving’s own roles use. Kind P is clever (≤4 contacts; links, a hinge, a lure or a decoy), W is wide (≤1 link, no hinge, no lure or decoy), §11. Links counts carved conditions, hinge included (§4). ← / → are the direct growth links; each is reciprocal, and each entry’s header states the same pair.

16b · The ten lines, root to capstone

FLOOD · root sign the Wave (every formation leaves at offset 0 on straight lanes) · master Thaesaen (the Leviathan) · carried by Ashwood +3, Ironbark +2, Heartoak +2; shunned by Willow and Twistbough (−2).

  1. E1-01 Landfall (Cam 1). All sail at once for the least-feared coast. Mass.
  2. E2-01 Run the Batteries (Cam 11). A screened column straight through the fire, with a cover line at the band’s edge. Speed through fire.
  3. E4-01 Three Roads, One Hour (Cam 25). Synchronised arrivals on three approaches, the last road carved against your walk. Sync across roads.
  4. E5-01 The Closing Hand (Cam 32). Cannae: the middle yields in shape and the hand closes one finger at a time; its chain is E2-01. Envelopment.
  5. Thaesaen’s Judgement carves E5-01 whole at the Thrones: the Throne goes under at the hinge and rises behind the coldest rear face (§21 E5-01, At the Throne).
  • Borrowed in: E3-01 → E4-01 (the sounder replaced by memory); E3-03 → E5-01 (the middle’s feigned flight). Lent out: E2-01 → E3-04 (Screen’s converging columns); E1-01 → E5-02 (as dress).

SOUNDING · root sign the Lone Stroke (one lone hull leaves first, ≥6 ticks ahead) · master Naelthar (the Storm King) · carried by Willow +3; Silverbark doubles its habit point.

  1. E1-03 Go Until Struck (Cam 2). A blind lone advance; nobody follows. Blind advance.
  2. E2-02 The Sounding (Cam 10). The rest follow the quiet water, 3 ticks late. The rest follow.
  3. E3-01 Sounding and Mass (Cam 16). Hold at the fork, then choose the road at a hinge. Hold and choose.
  4. E4-02 Ask the Silent Water (Cam 24). A stimulus test on one suspected silent battery, its answer used both ways. A stimulus test.
  5. E5-02 Come as the Young Wood Came (Cam 29). A probe dressed as a Hasty-Tide blunder; its chain is E1-01. A probe disguised as a blunder.
  6. Naelthar’s Judgement carves E5-02 whole: everything at once, and the Throne itself is the hunter (§21 E5-02, At the Throne).
  • Lent out: E3-01 → E4-01 (Flood).

SCREEN · root sign the Bar Before (shields lead the cargo by one cell, tight, aspect locked on one hot cell) · master Leavaren (the Hydra) · carried by Heartoak +3, Ironbark +1.

  1. E1-04 Cheapest First (Cam 1). The cheap hulls lead, ordered by worth, not task. Cheap lead.
  2. E2-03 Cold Shadow (Cam 9). A screen that stops in the fire and holds until the cargo commits: the war’s first answering sail. Held screen.
  3. E3-04 Two Roads to One Stone (Cam 18). Converging screened columns on one sector. Converging columns.
  4. E4-03 The Great Hull and the Kept Hand (Cam 26). A forward heavy that drinks fire, and a held reserve that chooses its lane at the hinge. Forward heavy + reserve.
  5. T-01 The Heads That Grow (Leavaren). A reserve that flows to whichever head is struck, as often as it is struck. A flowing reserve.
  • Borrowed in: E2-01 → E3-04 (the column that runs the fire). Lent out: E1-04 → E5-02 (as dress).

HUNT · root sign the Spark (formations bend toward a glinted gun; a Reaver pack waits at the fear edge) · master Rhenvael (the Colossus) · carried by Blackthorn +3, Ashwood +1; no grain weighs against it.

  1. E1-05 Strike the Flash (Cam 3). Chase the newest glint. Chase the glint.
  2. E2-05 Make Them Speak (Cam 11). A goad forces the glint. Force the glint.
  3. E3-05 Make an Example (Cam 16). Every hunter punishes the first gun to speak; with H5 active it spares the gun it has seen standing, so the unknown stays unknown. Punish the glint.
  4. E4-07 Borrow the Road (Cam 24). Glint the silent gun by sight in passing, and take it after the landing. Take it in passing.
  5. E5-06 The Prize Too Easy (Cam 28). A real, thin prize tempts the core-hunter’s reach to walk, and the hunters strike where it stood. Tempt the reach.
  6. T-02 The Thrown Stone (Rhenvael). Every glint answered from beyond the edge of known reach. Hunt every glint.

SIEGE · root sign the Mute Square (Bombards line abreast on the same wall, sortie after sortie) · master Ralensaen (the Ancient Treant) · carried by Ironbark +3, Yew +1. It skips the Second Tide.

  1. E1-02 Break the First Wall (Cam 1). Fixation on the first wall its own shells struck. Fixation.
  2. E3-02 The Same Stone (Cam 15). A standing objective across sorties, counted in its own blows; once E3-01 has shown the look (Cam 16), it looks before it strikes. A persistent objective.
  3. E4-04 When the Stone Gives (Cam 24). Breach, then the ram, then the host. Breach + ram.
  4. T-03 The Roots Beneath the Wall (Ralensaen). Go under the wall the young wood first touched. Under the wall.
  • Lent out: E1-02 → E5-02 (as dress).

FEINT · root sign the Two Mouths (one formation moves laterally with no fore-aft commitment while another approach is quiet) · master Senneir (the Sandworm) · carried by Twistbough +3; Heartoak −2 will not feint. Its root is a Second-Tide word: a feint needs a second ship that waits.

  1. E2-04 Noise in the East (Cam 12). A short demonstration, and a landing elsewhere. Short noise.
  2. E3-06 Open Roads, Hidden March (Cam 19). A credible siege as cover for a hidden route. A credible siege as cover.
  3. E5-03 Fortitude (Cam 31). A phantom host that persists across sorties after the real landing. A phantom host that persists.
  4. T-04 The Road Under the Sand (Senneir). The host above, the Worm below. The host above, the worm below.
  • Borrowed in: E2-08 → E5-03 (the smoke that promises sails). Lent out: E2-04 → E4-06 (Bleed: the noise draws one domain while another falls).

LURE · root sign the Turned Stern (a formation turns its stern whole: it scatters in the Hasty Tide, keeps its shape after) · master Esthaer (the Magma Titan) · carried by Twistbough +2, Blackthorn +1. It skips the Second Tide.

  1. E1-07 Home When Wounded (Cam 2). Real flight, and it scatters. Real flight.
  2. E3-03 Feigned Flight (Cam 17). False flight in shape, drawing pursuit into glinting. False flight.
  3. E4-05 Along the Shore, Home by the Far Road (Cam 25). A long false flight across approaches: Kalka. A long false flight across roads.
  4. T-05 The Burning Retreat (Esthaer). False weakness at throne scale, with the heart itself as the bait. False weakness at throne scale.
  • Lent out: E3-03 → E5-01 (Flood: the bending middle).

BLEED · root sign the Breath (a cheap hull goes in alone and turns after drawing fire while the rest wait at the fear edge) · master Vaelress (the Plague Herald) · carried by Yew +2. Its root is a Second-Tide word: it needs a cadence to spend.

  1. E2-06 Toss a Brick (Cam 12). Spend one volley on worthless wood. Spend one volley.
  2. E3-07 Between Their Breaths (Cam 20). Strike on the reload read from the rhythm of your shells. Time the reload.
  3. E4-06 Sea Below, Sky Above (Cam 26). Strike on the domain-switch window. Time the domain switch.
  4. E5-07 The First Ship Carries (Cam 30). The brick is the bearer: against a shore that lets the first ship pass, the first ship lands. Bear in the brick’s place.
  5. T-06 The Slow Rot (Vaelress). Outpace the mending, and strike between two breaths; with H9 active its brick carries, as E5-07’s did. Outpace the mending.
  • Borrowed in: E2-04 → E4-06 (the noise that draws one domain); E4-08 → E5-07 (the draught seam). Reach-back: E2-06 → E5-07 (the brick, carved as the bearer).

PATIENCE · root sign the Knot (formations loiter in the launch zone or a COLD fringe and launch late) · master Eirlenth (the Frost Wyrm) · carried by Yew +3, Willow +2; Blackthorn and Ashwood −2 will not wait.

  1. E1-06 Wait in the Grey (Cam 4). Loiter in the fringe until the shore falls quiet. Loiter.
  2. E2-07 Let the Fire Work (Cam 10). Let a hazard do the work, and go when the wall gives. Let a hazard work.
  3. E3-08 Spent or Waiting (Cam 21). Wait in the grey as the young wood did, then look at the gun that fell silent before trusting the silence. Look at the silence.
  4. E5-04 The Fourth Time (Cam 30). Conditioning over three sorties, then the real run, if its eye finds your coast unchanged; its chain is E1-06’s form. Condition.
  5. T-07 The Long Winter (Eirlenth). Conditioning plus a wall that cannot mend. Condition + a frozen wall.
  • Reach-back: E1-06 → E5-04 directly (the routine is Wait in the Grey carved three times over).

MASK · root sign the Smoothed Cut (smoke before the sails, silhouettes that do not jink, or a thick formation riding high) · master Neivaere (the Crystal Lich) · carried by Twistbough +2; Ironbark and Heartoak −2 will not hide. Its root is a Second-Tide word: it needs smoke.

  1. E2-08 Grey Before the Sails (Cam 13). Smoke before the landing. Smoke.
  2. E4-08 Thick and Hollow (Cam 26). A convoy that looks heavier than it is. False thickness.
  3. E5-05 Straw, Then Men (Cam 29). Straw down every road to learn which one you leave silent, then men down it. A map drawn in false silhouettes.
  4. T-08 The Mirror Host (Neivaere). A host that mirrors your own habits. A host that mirrors you.
  • Lent out: E2-08 → E5-03 (Feint: the smoke that promises sails); E4-08 → E5-07 (Bleed: the draught seam).

16c · Borrowings, dress and chains

Beyond the in-line steps of §16b, six relations tie the tree together. None is a separate carving, and each is named at both of its ends.

RelationFrom → toWhat passes
Borrowing (a growth link across lines)E2-01 → E3-04 · E3-01 → E4-01 · E2-04 → E4-06 · E3-03 → E5-01 · E2-08 → E5-03 · E4-08 → E5-07One primitive from a neighbouring line: the column that runs the fire; the counted walk in place of the eye; the noise that draws one domain; the bending middle; the smoke that promises sails; the draught that tells the laden from the light
Reach-back (a growth link that skips its own line’s middle)E1-06 → E5-04 · E2-06 → E5-07The Hasty Tide’s wait, carved three times over; the Second Tide’s brick, carved as the bearer

A -form chain carries the ancestor’s behaviour on formations its filter never took (a Runner convoy waiting in the grey; a column that runs the fire with no cover line), and each entry says what differs

16d · How to read an entry

Every entry in §17–§22 has the same bones.

  • Header. Two lines: era and debut, line and root sign, kind, contacts and approaches, links, habit key; then Grows from and Grows into, the direct links of §16a (and Carried whole where a later chain carves it, or carried in form where a chain carries it on other formations).
  • Intent is the order as the Heartwood means it. Chosen when gives preconditions and the gain conditions that make FIT (§5), the habit key (§6) and the grains that favour it (§7).
  • Composition gives the parts, roles, contacts and links. Choreography gives the ticks (§3 conventions).
  • Signature gives what a first-timer sees and what a returning player reads in the reading band (§9a).
  • Captains’ independence gives the balks, sags, routs, forks and the flag-sunk behaviour (§2, §4).
  • Counters gives at least one blunt (B) and one clever (C) answer, all from the locked kit (§10).
  • If it fails gives the grades (§5), what the fleet learns, and each grain’s reaction.
  • The Carving is the line Halyna know from the wreck-wood and tell together: one of them touches the wood, what one knows the other knows, and one begins the line and the other finishes it (§9b). Real-world root is the historical source (research_stratagems.md by section).

17E1 · THE HASTY TIDE (Leathae)

Cam 1–6 · no links, no hinges, Greenwood grain · “the first are not organized”

What this growing is. The groves sent their youngest wood first, because it was ready first. Each hull was cut in a single season and carried a single sign, and green wood heals shut over a cut, so it said that sign until the sea took it (fiction_hooks §2, the Law of the Rings; Legends V.6). This is the last of the green wood, sent to finish a shore the council believed already won, and Rivenkeep is the first place it meets anyone who fights back. Kael the Counter’s first count holds the whole era (Legends V.5): “Eight sails, and not one keeping station with another.”

How organised it is: not at all, and in kind rather than degree (§13).

  • There is no stratagem above the formation. Each formation carries one glyph, chosen alone from what the fleet believes when that formation first sails, and it keeps that glyph for the battle (LAW below).
  • No formation waits for another. There are no links, no hinges, and no lure or decoy roles. There is no idle role either: every formation carries its own word. Two formations in one sortie may carry unrelated glyphs and arrive piecemeal, and what looks like a plan is only the sum of their words.
  • The grain is Greenwood for every lineage, so a field promotion changes nothing (§7). The Shore-Reading holds no habits (§6). Nothing retires, and no history is kept (§5 step 3).
  • The mind under the words is fully live from Cam 1: the three channels, fear decay, propagation, resolve and cascades all run (Victory §3; Fleet Memory §7). The young wood sees, remembers and fears. It cannot plan.
LAW · GREEN WOOD TAKES ONE CUT

In E1 the Heartwood carves each formation once, at the boundary before that formation’s first sortie of the battle, and never again. The law holds through Cam 8: the Second Tide’s first two campaigns carve the Hasty Tide’s way on their two-road board, and the re-carving arrives with the rest of the Second Tide’s family at Cam 9 (§13, §18).

  • The glyph is fixed. Its fields (spawn, lane, objective, loiter cell, commit point) are refilled at every boundary from believed state, by the glyph’s own rule (§3). A survivor comes back with the same word on a new road.
  • The only way to change what a formation says is to sink it. The next formation in the queue is carved against whatever the fort has taught since (Victory §4b: Q is fixed in order; survivors sail first).
  • It remains a pure function: glyph = f(believed state at the formation’s first boundary), and each sortie’s fields = f(glyph, believed state at that boundary) (§0).
LAW · THE YOUNG WOOD WAITS ON THE SEA, NEVER ON ITS KIN

Four E1 glyphs carry a self-trigger (§3): a carved rule its captain evaluates on the formation’s own perception (a hit on its own hulls, its own first loss, the fear in its own grid, the newest glint it knows). A self-trigger may start, stop, turn or re-aim the formation’s one part. It never names another formation’s deed, so it is not a link, and it never chooses between two carved parts, so it is not a hinge. Captains evaluating a carved rule is not mid-sortie re-planning (§4 red line).

RED LINE · NO SAIL BLANCHES IN THE HASTY TIDE

E1 has no links, so it has no answering sail (§4), and nothing in E1 may imitate one. Cam 9’s first answering sail must be the first time a player ever sees canvas blanch, and E5-02 Come as the Young Wood Came depends on it: its seam is a “Hasty Tide” wave whose sails blanch once.

CALL-OUT 18 · CARVED ONCE (§25)

The LAW above makes the young wood’s repeat structural rather than scored. It makes the Hasty Tide the most predictable fleet in the war, and it gives the Second Tide a jump in kind the player can see: from Cam 9 the Heartwood re-carves every hull for every morrow, and a formation that comes back saying something new is the first sign that the wood over there is thinking. Recommend: accept.

The budget.

DialE1 valueSource
Contacts C2 board-wide. A sortie sails two formations, each 1 contact; ≤2 decision-bearingCampaign §4; Victory §4b (a sortie fields min(C, formations remaining))
Approaches A1, with ≤2 authored spawns per formationCampaign §4; CALL-OUT 5
Hulls · pool · Q · sorties6–8 on board · 17 · 4 formations · 3 sortiesVictory §4b roster
Guns · groups2 → 4 guns · 1 group. Every counter below is playable with one group, except one marked from Cam 7 (E1-05’s recency, which needs a second group)Campaign §4; Deploy §2
Links · hinges · habits0 · 0 · 0§13
KindEvery E1 glyph is W: no link, no hinge, no decoy, 1 contact§11
Castles required1/4 (Cam 1–3) · 2/4 (Cam 4–6)GDD campaign matrix
Win routeAnnihilation or the wall clock; no core huntingCampaign §4

Lineages in the water, by debut (CALL-OUT 11; §13 LAW · BREADTH ENTERS WITH LAYER B): Bombard and Runner with Swarm troops (Cam 1) · Recon (2) · then Cam 3, B’s isolation campaign, one lineage per battle: the Reaver (arriving with Camouflage), Sapper troops aboard the Runner, and the Bulwark in the shield slots (arriving with Piercer). Before the Bulwark’s battle, shield slots hold the plainest hulls of the fleet’s own lineages, because a shield is a role and not a class (Ships §9). Cam 4–6 debut no lineage.

The counter kit in E1. Standard (Cam 1); Stonewright, the first tower; Suppressor, early; Splasher, with the first troop clump; Spotter; Camouflage (Cam 3); Piercer (Cam 3) (Campaign §6). The order card arrives one field per battle through Cam 1: placement, then the AoR nudge, then domain, then fire-control, then the tower stack (Campaign §4 on-ramp). A clever counter that needs hold-fire is marked as such, and the on-ramp teaches fire-control on Cam 1’s fourth battle. There are no hinges in E1, so Command Time serves the held alpha only: picking up the group to hold or release slows the sortie to 1/24 speed, and the release lands on a tick the player chose (§12).

Decapitation is not an E1 counter. Sinking the flag breaks no carving, because there is nothing to break and every lineage promotes to Greenwood. Its whole E1 value is the resolve crater and a slower, more local net. That fits Campaign §4, which stages no core hunting before Cam 7. The player learns here that the young fleet’s plan does not live in its flag, because it has no plan. In the Second Tide, sinking the flag silences the answering sails, and that is a discovery.

The E1 selection rule

At a formation’s first boundary, S = FIT. GRAIN, HABIT, MEMORY, LINE and INERTIA are all 0: Greenwood weighs nothing, no habits exist, wood that has never sailed has no history, and no earlier forms exist. The candidates are the glyphs whose filter the formation passes and whose preconditions hold. FIT counts the gain conditions listed in each entry (at most four). Ties go to the lower id, since every E1 glyph shares an era (§5 step 5).

The resolve band enters only as a precondition. Four glyphs need a steady formation, one needs a shaken one, and two take either. That keeps §8’s rule that resolve reaches the Admiral only as a coarse band. Every predicate is quantised, so a one-tick difference in play rarely changes a word (§8):

PredicateChannelMeaning
RoadEffectTier of the worst cell on the formation’s least-feared lane to a coast (COLD / WARM / HOT / SCALDING). “The road burns” means HOT or worse
FringeEffectA COLD cell in the reading band, beside a HOT or SCALDING cell, reachable on an all-COLD lane
Unknown seaEffect≥3/4 of the approach’s fear cells read COLD
Glint / fresh glintContact≥1 exposure glint in the ledger / one written last sortie
BreachContactA breach observed this battle
Lossown stateThe fleet lost ≥1 hull last sortie
Kin lostown stateA formation of the fleet was eliminated last sortie
Steady / shakenown stateThe formation’s resolve band at the boundary. Resolve damage spans unsailed reserves, so fresh wood can sail shaken (Ships §4; Fleet Memory §7)

The first words: the rule in plain speech (what a returning player learns; the gain conditions in the entries are the rule itself).

Formation, at its first sortieSteadyShaken
Runner convoyRoad COLD/WARM → Landfall · road burns, after losses → Cheapest FirstRoad burns → Home When Wounded · otherwise Landfall
Bombard line (two cores abreast)Road COLD/WARM → Break the First Wall · road burns with a fresh glint → Strike the Flash · road burns with a cold fringe beside it → Wait in the Grey · otherwise Break the First WallA fresh glint → Strike the Flash · otherwise Home When Wounded
Convoy with one Bombard core (a command convoy)As the line, but Landfall where the line would Break: one core’s breach clock is too slow to open a wall in a battle (Build §11). It carries no troops, so it runs Landfall’s road to the coast and shells the wall it finds there (E1-01)As the line, but Landfall on a cold road
Recon wolfpackRoad burns with a cold fringe → Wait in the Grey · otherwise Go Until StruckHome When Wounded
Reaver packStrike the FlashStrike the Flash

Three consequences follow, and the campaign should lean on them.

  1. The first sortie of every battle speaks the blind words: Landfall, Break the First Wall, Go Until Struck, Strike the Flash. All water is COLD, no one has died, and no gun has flashed.
  2. The burned words belong to later wood. Cheapest First, Wait in the Grey and Home When Wounded appear only on formations that first sail after the fort has written fear and killed hulls. With C = 2 and survivors first, that means formations Q3 and Q4. Each battle therefore replays the Viking arc in miniature: blind raiders first, burned ones after (research §3.7).
  3. The young fleet is the most predictable fleet in the war, so it is the drill ground for seven of the ten root signs (§9a).

The E1 sortie, in ticks (Fight §3; 1 tick = 1 s at full speed, 1⅓ game-hours; sea speed ≈1 grid/tick; a fear cell is ≈13×8 grids, so an approach crosses 2–3 cells, Fleet Memory §3):

Tick (from launch)Beat
t0–5Safe launch zone (5 grids; no hull can be hit)
t5–23Reading band (18 grids)
t23–39Engagement band (16 grids)
t39–51Withdrawal (12 ticks; retreat is not safe passage, Victory §4)
t30The latest a Hasty Tide formation may hold short and still complete one passage inside the backstop (≈8 ticks to the band edge + 16 + 12 ≈ 66). Start value; range 24–34; settled in greybox against the 70-tick backstop
t70Backstop clock (sim ticks, never wall-clock, §12)

What the player is learning to read.

  • The fear haze predicts the road (Landfall). §3’s bar, a tester predicting the lane from the haze ≥70% of the time, is first tested here.
  • A glint promises a hunt (Strike the Flash).
  • The first wall is the only wall (Break the First Wall).
  • A lone fast hull is asking a question (Go Until Struck).
  • Small hulls in front mean something precious behind (Cheapest First).
  • Idle hulls in cold water are waiting for the fort to fall silent (Wait in the Grey).
  • A real flight breaks apart once it is out of reach (Home When Wounded).

There is also one absence to remember: no sail ever blanches. It is not an E1 tell (tells are presences, Victory §4a). It is the baseline against which Cam 9’s answering sail reads as new. Every E1 signature below is written to be remembered, because the late war will forge it.

The Knowing and the dispatch in E1.

  • The Knowing comes from the Standard-Bearer’s formation if it sank, else from the formation that hurt the fort most, else from any sunk hull (§9b).
  • In E1 it is an untold glyph. One of Halyna touches the wreck-wood and both know it, but a shore-mind receives only the shape of a forest’s thought, and they can tell one word of it. Seren draws the sign and sets the word down, and does not mend it with guesses. The codex adds the sim’s plain gloss of what that formation did.
  • The whole carven lines below are what Halyna knew and told at the Night of the Naming (Legends V.2, laid when Cam 13 is complete, near the end of the Second Tide), when every kept wreck in the vault is known again, whole. The codex fills the lines in then.
  • The first battle with a sunk hull gives the war’s first Knowing. Its tale, The First Glyph (Legends V.1), is laid when Cam 1 is complete (Legends §3).
  • The dispatch reports only new glints and fear-tier changes. E1 has no habits to report (§9c).
