E1-01

Landfall

“To the shore.”

Each formation that carries it sails at t0 on the straightest road to the least-feared coast, and none waits for another.

LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Flood · the Wave (root)W1 / 10—
Era & debut

E1 · the Hasty Tide · Cam 1, from its first battle: a baseline word, exempt from the rule against debuts on a campaign’s first battle (§13)

Grows from

— (root of the Flood)

Grows into

E2-01 Run the Batteries (Cam 11) · E5-02 Come as the Young Wood Came, whose green landing wears it as dress

Carried whole

as the chain of E5-02

Line onward

E2-01 → E4-01 Three Roads, One Hour → E5-01 The Closing Hand (§16b)

Intent

The shore is soft, so go to it. Put the troops on the coast that has hurt no one, by the straightest cold road, before anything can stop them. The word has no thought of the fire, of the other ships, or of what comes after the touch.

Chosen when

Carved once, at the formation’s first boundary. Filter: a Runner-cored formation, or a convoy with a single Bombard core; any resolve band. Gain conditions:

  1. the road is COLD or WARM;
  2. the formation carries troops;
  3. the fleet lost no hull last sortie;
  4. no SCALDING cell lies on the approach.

Every Runner convoy that sails in sortie 1 carries it (all water COLD: 4 of 4). A Runner keeps choosing it while any road stays cold or warm; on a merely WARM road it wins the tie against Cheapest First. It is also the first word of a convoy with a single Bombard core (3 of 4: it carries no troops, so gain 2 fails). Ghosts and stale negatives do not enter; the road is a judgement of fear alone.

Greenwood carves it by fit. In later Tides it stays in the book. Ashwood (Flood +3, the Runner’s own grain) and Heartoak (+2) keep reaching for it when Run the Batteries has no HOT band to run. Willow and Twistbough (−2) all but never carve it. From Cam 11 it takes LINE −1 whenever Run the Batteries is also a candidate (§5).

Composition

One formation · role cargo · C 1 · A 1 · W.

  • Runner convoy. The Runner is the core (purpose-bearing) and carries Swarm troops, with Sappers aboard from Cam 3. Four shields (purpose-serving) sail in a column. Alternatively this is the command convoy, with the flag hull in its core slot.
  • Convoy with a single Bombard core. It carries no troops. It runs the same road to the same coast, and at its commit point takes its Bombard baseline, shelling the wall it faces; its objective event is the touch, not a landing.
  • Carving.
    • sail: offset 0.
    • approach: the authored spawn whose first lane cell is least feared (ties: lower index).
    • lane: least summed fear to a coast cell; among equals the fewest cells, which is the “straight” road; then row-major (Fleet Memory §12).
    • objective: the coast cell the lane ends on.
    • commit point: the first lane cell inside the engagement band.
    • No aspect, no links, no hinge.
    • fallback: the Runner baseline, which beelines the softest COLD coast on local beliefs.
  • Contacts. The convoy at sea is 1. Once it docks it stops moving (Ships §7), and its landed stack (1, Victory §3) takes over as the decision.
Choreography

Within the sortie:

  • t0. It leaves the launch zone at speed, bow already on the coast.
  • t5–23. It crosses the reading band without pausing at any cell edge. On WARM it skirts inside its lane; it never leaves the lane.
  • t≈23. It commits and drives for the beach. It docks and disgorges at its cadence, and the Swarm fans out by weighted A* to the nearest structure (Ships §7).
  • It keeps time with no one. If a Bombard line sails the same sortie, each arrives when its own speed and road say.

Across sorties:

  • Survivors keep the word and sail first (Victory §4b). Only the road moves.
  • A beach you punished reads HOT at the next boundary, so the next road runs to the next-coldest coast.
  • When every road burns, the formation still carries Landfall and takes the least-feared road through the fire. That is the price of green wood.
Signature
  • First sight. A heavy line runs for one point of the coast. It does not shoot, it does not wait for the other ships, and it does not slow as it enters the fire.
  • Returning player’s tell, from the launch zone. The bow is on the beach before the convoy clears the zone and never swings, and the convoy leaves at t0 with no loiter. That is the Flood’s root sign in its first form.
Captains’ independence
  • Balk. If a lane cell went SCALDING during this sortie, the convoy stalls at the cell edge, loosens, and takes its fallback on local beliefs.
  • Nerve. The Runner has no resolve of its own (Ships §5); its shields carry the nerve. A Wavering screen sags aft and the column strings out.
  • Orphan fork. If the Runner sinks before it docks, no land wave spawns. Steady shields turn wolfpack and drive for the beach in revenge (Committed motion); shaken ones go home (Ships §9).
  • Flag sunk. No carving changes. The resolve crater and a slow, local net are the whole effect. If the flag hull is this Runner, its death is also the last core’s, and the orphan fork runs.
Counters
  • B · Cover the beach the road ends at. Standard for the hulls; Splasher for the landed stack once it is in the kit; Stonewright on whatever the Swarm chews. Triage: at Cam 1–3 only one castle in four is required, so let the landing beach front a castle you can spare.
  • C · Predict the road.
    • In sortie 1 every cell is COLD, so the road is the fewest-cell lane from the spawn to the coast: straight ahead.
    • Lay the AoR across the last cell before that beach and load Sea. A Runner sunk before it docks lands nothing (Ships §5).
    • From the battle fire-control is taught: hold fire while it crosses, so the coast stays COLD and the next Landfall returns to it. Then spend the first volley, the clean one before the crazy-Ivan, on the tick it commits. Command Time makes that tick a choice rather than a reflex (§12).
  • C · The Delta. Leave one coast cold on purpose and ring it with your group. The young wood sails into the channel you chose (research §3.21).
If it fails
  • Succeeded: troops ashore (a Bombard-cored convoy: the coast reached). Failed: the core sunk or routed before its commit point.
  • Greenwood reads no grade and re-carves nothing. A surviving convoy says Landfall again, on the next-coldest road.
  • What the fleet has learned lives in the channels: fear on the cells where it bled, and a Contact for every wall its sight or its troops touched.
  • The next Runner convoy from the queue is carved against that fear. It carries Cheapest First if it sails steady, Home When Wounded if shaken.
  • In later Tides, Ashwood re-carves Landfall at once, Ironbark re-carves it heavier as Run the Batteries, and Willow drops the line.
The Carving

On the shingle below the wall, the war’s first knowing: Rhyna’s palms on the grain and Halvard’s over hers, for they did not yet know that one pair of hands was enough. The sign, the Wave, came as a shape; Halvard knew it as she knew it, and could tell only “…shore…” (Legends V.1). Known whole at the Night of the Naming, when Rhyna held this plank and Halvard, across the vault, the plank that had given nothing (Legends V.2): “To shore. As root to water, go. Wait on no bough.”

Real-world root

Lindisfarne, 793: raiders came straight to the richest, softest point of the coast, unscouted and uncoordinated. #5 Loot a burning house, crude form (research §1, §3.7).

E1-02

Break the First Wall

“Break the wall.”

Bombards line abreast at the first wall their own shells ever struck, and shell it until it breaks or they do.

LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Siege · the Mute Square (root)W1 / 10—
Era & debut

E1 · the Hasty Tide · Cam 1, from its first battle: a baseline word, exempt from the rule against debuts on a campaign’s first battle (§13)

Grows from

— (root of the Siege)

Grows into

E3-02 The Same Stone (Cam 15; the Siege line has no Second-Tide form) · E5-02 Come as the Young Wood Came, whose green line wears it as dress

Carried whole

inside E5-02’s chain, by a dressed Bombard line

Line onward

E3-02 → E4-04 When the Stone Gives → T-03 The Roots Beneath the Wall (§16b)

Intent

Stone is what stands between the wood and the shore, so break it. The line does not pick the weakest stone or the one that matters. The first stone its own shells strike becomes the only stone, and it comes back to that stone until it opens.

Chosen when

Filter: a Bombard line (two Bombard cores abreast); steady only. Gain conditions:

  1. the line is whole;
  2. the road is COLD or WARM;
  3. no exposure glint is known;
  4. no breach has been observed this battle.

Every Bombard line that sails in sortie 1 carries it (4 of 4). A line first sailing later still carries it, unless the road burns and either a fresh glint (Strike the Flash) or a cold fringe (Wait in the Grey) outscores it.

Ghosts feed it. A wall Contact is revised only by contradiction, so a line keeps shelling a wall you let fall until a shell finds the gap (Fleet Memory §4). Greenwood carves by fit. Later, Ironbark (Siege +3, the Bombard’s own grain) keeps it alive against supersession, which costs it LINE −2 once The Same Stone is a candidate. Yew gives it +1.

Composition

One formation · role siege · C 1 · A 1 · W.

  • Bombard line. Two Bombard cores (purpose-bearing) and two shields, line abreast (Ships §9). Wall damage is per formation, from filled wall-breaking core slots. Three Bombards abreast break the ring now (Build §11); the Hasty Tide’s line has two, so its breach clock is slow, a wall opened over sorties, not in one.
  • Carving.
    • sail: offset 0.
    • lane: least summed fear to the objective.
    • objective: the first wall this line’s own shells connected with. Before any hit, it is the first wall its sight brings into range on the least-feared road.
    • commit point: its effective standoff from that wall (Bombard baseline, Ships §5).
    • fallback: the Bombard baseline, which shells the wall it faces.
Choreography
  • Sortie 1. It leaves at t0 on the least-feared road and crosses the reading band like a metronome. At the first wall its sight confirms in range, it forms abreast at standoff and shells. The first shell that connects fixes the objective for the battle.
  • Sortie 2 onward. It sails for that wall on whatever road the fear allows and passes nearer, weaker walls without a shot. It forms abreast at the same standoff and shells until the wall opens, the line breaks, or the magazine is spent (Held).
  • When the wall opens, the word is spent, and green wood has no second word. The line takes its fallback and shells whatever it now faces through the gap, usually the next ring.
  • It works alone. The Swarm (and from Cam 3 the Sappers) of a Landfall go for the structure nearest their own beach. A Hasty Tide battle therefore leaves two unrelated wounds rather than one breach (Build §11: piecemeal troops leave a wide ruin).
Signature
  • First sight. Bombarding hulls side by side at standoff, methodical, shelling one stretch of wall.
  • Returning player’s tell, at the start of sortie 2. The line steers past a nearer, weaker wall to form abreast on the stretch it hit last time. That is the Siege root sign, “the same wall, sortie after sortie”, in its bluntest form.
Captains’ independence
  • Bombards are the backbone, with middling resolve (Ships §5). A lost shield does not turn the line.
  • A lost core halves its wall damage and drains its neighbours. The line keeps shelling at half weight.
  • Balk. If the road to the wall runs through a cell that went SCALDING this sortie, the line stalls at the cell edge and shells from there, which is its fallback anyway.
  • Orphan fork on the last core (Ships §9).
  • Flag sunk. The Bombard line is not the command convoy (Victory §4b), so it loses nothing but nerve. A Wavering line sags aft and its fire slackens.
Counters
  • B · Harden that face. Stonewright and Suppressor on it: the Suppressor lowers the line’s accuracy in its zone, and the Stonewright mends between volleys. Thicken the wall in Build. A line parked at standoff is the stillest target on the board, so plain Standard coverage over its standoff cell pays.
  • C · Build a St Elmo. Put an outwork on the least-feared road, in sight range before the ring, so it is the first stone their shells find. Mend it just enough each Build that it never opens. The word is then never finished, and the line never moves on (research §3.19). Triage variant: let the fixation fall on the wall of a castle the board does not require.
  • C · Kill one core of the two. One core’s clock is too slow to open the wall inside the battle’s sorties (Build §11), and the fixation becomes a slow tax rather than a breach.
If it fails
  • Succeeded: the wall breached. Failed: routed or eliminated before its commit point. The common case, a wall hit but not opened, is ungraded.
  • Survivors keep the word and the wall; only the road bends around new fear. The wall never leaves the ledger, so a burning road never lures them off it.
  • A fresh line from the queue fixes on the first wall its shells find. One battle can therefore hold two lines on two walls, neither helping the other.
  • Later, Ironbark (Failed → +1, and it prefers the line’s next form) re-carves the word as The Same Stone. Willow switches away at the first failure.
The Carving

Known by Halvard, his hand on the grain; Rhyna knew it as he knew it. One sign, the Mute Square, how the wood cuts stone, and of it she could tell one word: “…wall…”. Known whole at the Night of the Naming: “Break wall. First stone, only stone. Strike till it opens or wood is ash.”

Real-world root

The Great Siege of Malta, 1565: the Ottomans spent a month and thousands of men on the small outwork of St Elmo, their first objective, while the main forts stood (research §3.19).

E1-03

Go Until Struck

“Go until struck.”

A lone scout runs straight in until the first shell lands, then swerves; nobody follows it.

LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Sounding · the Lone Stroke (root)W1 / 10—
Era & debut

E1 · the Hasty Tide · Cam 2, on a battle after the Recon’s first

Grows from

— (root of the Sounding)

Grows into

E2-02 The Sounding (Cam 10) · E5-02 Come as the Young Wood Came, whose lone stroke wears it as dress

Carried whole

inside E5-02’s chain, by its lone stroke

Line onward

E2-02 → E3-01 Sounding and Mass → E4-02 Ask the Silent Water → E5-02 (§16b)

Intent

The sea is unknown, so go into it until it answers. The scouts run at the fort to learn where the shore bites, and they turn aside at the first bite. They ask for no one: no ship is waiting on the answer.

Chosen when

Filter: a Recon wolfpack; steady only. Gain conditions:

  1. at least three quarters of the approach’s fear cells read COLD (unknown sea);
  2. no exposure glint is known;
  3. the fleet lost no hull last sortie;
  4. the road is COLD or WARM.

A Recon that sails in sortie 1 always carries it (4 of 4). A steady Recon first sailing into a burned sea carries Wait in the Grey if cold water waits beside the road, and this word otherwise. A shaken one goes Home When Wounded. Greenwood carves by fit. Later, Willow (Sounding +3, the Recon’s own grain) keeps the line alive and reaches past it for The Sounding. The root takes LINE −1 once that heir is a candidate.

Composition

One formation · role probe · C 1 · A 1 · W.

  • The Recon wolfpack. In the Hasty Tide it is two Recons in close file, one contact (Victory §3: a formation holding shape is 1). At C = 2 the pair must hold that shape to sail beside another formation. A scattered pair would be the whole sortie, and there would be no one left to not follow it (§3 Parts).
  • Carving.
    • sail: offset 0.
    • lane: the fewest-cell lane from the spawn to the nearest coast, whatever its fear. The road is the test, not a way through.
    • objective: the cells of that lane, tested in order.
    • No commit point: it never commits into a core (Recon baseline).
    • self-trigger: on the tick a shell first hits it, it swerves into the adjacent cell of least believed fear (ties: row-major) and takes up the Recon baseline, skirting the perimeter at the edge of its sight range and watching.
    • fallback: the Recon baseline.
Choreography
  • t0. It leaves with everything else, because the young wood cannot wait. It is faster than any convoy (Ships §5), so by the middle of the reading band it is alone out front. The Sounding’s root sign, one small contact out first, arrives here by speed, not by waiting. To lead a convoy by §9a’s ≥6 ticks at the engagement band, the Recon must make at least 23/17 ≈ 1.35 grids/tick (a Ships §5 debt, §24).
  • It runs dead straight, through WARM and HOT alike.
  • At the first hit: the crazy-Ivan, then a hard lateral turn. It never re-enters. From the edge it watches: every wall and exposed gun inside its sight range becomes a Contact, and what it felt enters the net at the Hasty Tide’s slow rate.
  • If nothing hits it, it runs on into the engagement band and turns along the coast at the DMZ edge, skirting and looking.
  • Nobody follows it. No formation’s road or timing depends on it. Its fear still reaches the others through the net, and a convoy whose lane cell turns SCALDING on what the scouts felt will balk there. That is an accident of propagation, not a plan.
  • Across sorties, survivors keep the word and run the same straight road, and they are struck where they were struck before.
Signature
  • First sight. A small, fast ship runs straight at the fort ahead of everything. Hit once, it jinks, veers off sideways, and hangs at the edge, looking in. The convoy behind pays it no heed.
  • Returning player’s tell. A lone small contact pulls ahead in the reading band on a dead-straight line that never skirts a cell edge, even a warm one. Nothing else in the Hasty Tide ignores the haze.
Captains’ independence
  • Recons are skittish (Ships §4). A second hit often routs them, and skittish wood cascades fast.
  • The swerve is carved, not a deviation. What can deviate is the run home: a routed scout carries its unpropagated sightings with it, and if it sinks they sink too (Fleet Memory §7).
  • Flag sunk. Its sightings crawl hull to hull instead of passing through the hub, so what it saw may not reach the convoy inside the sortie. The carving is unchanged.
Counters
  • B · Shoot it. Standard on the straight road. The first shell turns it and the second often routs it.
  • B · Shut its eyes. Enclosure breaks sightlines, so it confirms only what stands in the open (Fleet Memory §5).
  • C · Let it run blind. Needs fire-control. Hold fire and let it run into the engagement band: it writes no fear, only what is exposed. Then kill it in one volley deep in your AoR before its sightings propagate (Fleet Memory §7: kill the knower before it speaks).
  • C · Choose where it is struck. Open fire on the tick it enters the cell you want it to believe is HOT. The swerve, and the fear it carries back into the net, are yours to place. Command Time makes the tick deliberate.
If it fails
  • Succeeded: struck, turned, and still watching at sortie end, so it wrote both fear and sight. Countered: never struck, so it tested nothing. Failed: sunk before its sightings left it.
  • Greenwood reads no grade. A surviving pair runs the same road next sortie.
  • What the fleet learns is exactly what you let it feel and see.
  • Later, Willow re-carves the line as The Sounding, where the convoy waits for the scout.
The Carving

Known by Rhyna, her hand on the grain; Halvard knew it as she knew it. One sign, the Lone Stroke, a short cut running ahead of a long one, and of it he could tell one word: “…go…”. Known whole at the Night of the Naming: “Go until struck. Where bark splits, turn aside.”

Real-world root

#13 Beat the grass to startle the snake, crude form: advance until something fires (research §1).

E1-04

Cheapest First

“Before the bearer.”

The cheapest hulls lead the column into the fire; the cargo keeps its spacing and does not slow when the lead dies.

LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Screen · the Bar Before (root)W1 / 10—
Era & debut

E1 · the Hasty Tide · Cam 1, on a later battle than the first (§13)

Grows from

— (root of the Screen)

Grows into

E2-03 Cold Shadow (Cam 9) · E5-02 Come as the Young Wood Came, whose green landing leads with its cheapest hulls

Line onward

E2-03 → E3-04 Two Roads to One Stone → E4-03 The Great Hull and the Kept Hand → T-01 The Heads That Grow (§16b)

Intent

The road burns, so let the thin wood burn first. Send the hulls of least worth in front so the fire spends itself on them, and keep the bearer coming behind at the same pace. The word orders who dies first. It says nothing about how the bearer lives.

Chosen when

Filter: a Runner-cored formation with at least one shield; steady only. Gain conditions:

  1. the road is HOT or SCALDING;
  2. at least two shields sail;
  3. the fleet lost a hull last sortie;
  4. the fewest-cell lane to a coast also crosses a HOT or SCALDING cell (the fire is on the straight road too).

It is the word of a steady Runner convoy that first sails after the fort has burned the road and killed wood (4 of 4). Where the road is only WARM, Landfall wins the tie. A shaken convoy on a burned road goes Home When Wounded instead. Later, Heartoak (Screen +3, the Bulwark’s grain) and Ironbark (+1) carry the line. The root takes LINE −1 once Cold Shadow is a candidate (Cam 9).

Composition

One formation · role cargo, with its own screen · C 1 · A 1 · W.

  • Runner convoy. The Runner core (purpose-bearing) and its shields (purpose-serving). Before the Bulwark’s battle (Cam 3) the shields are plain hulls standing in the role. From then one may be a Bulwark (Ships §9).
  • Carving.
    • sail: offset 0.
    • lane: the road, through the fire, since every road crosses it.
    • Column order, front to back, by hull cost: cheapest first, the Runner last.
    • The column’s authored spacing is held rigidly.
    • No backfill. A healthier shield never moves up; this word suspends the Ships §9 rule that escorts reallocate under fire.
    • No aspect lock.
    • commit point: the first lane cell inside the engagement band.
    • fallback: the Runner baseline.
Choreography
  • t0. It leaves in file, small hulls first. No link and no waiting.
  • It enters the HOT cell at the Runner’s speed and does not slow.
  • The lead sinks and the next hull is simply the lead. No gap closes and no hull comes up from behind; the column shortens from the front.
  • From Cam 3, the Bulwark rides mid-column, placed by its price. It does nothing a shield-hull is for until the cheap hulls ahead of it are gone, and then it leads. The young wood orders its screen by worth, not by task, and that single error is the one Cold Shadow (E2) corrects.
  • At its commit point the Runner drives for the beach.
  • Across sorties, survivors keep the word, with a shorter column each time. When the screen falls to the refusal threshold, the Runner refuses and loiters visibly in the launch zone (Victory §4a). The word has spent its own wood.
Signature
  • First sight. A transport follows a file of small hulls into the fire. The small ones die first, and the transport keeps coming at the same speed.
  • Returning player’s tell, in the launch zone. The smallest silhouettes lead the column and the heaviest brings up the rear, the reverse of the Bulwark-led convoy (Ships §9). From Cam 3 a Bulwark rides in the middle. That is the Screen’s root sign, shields before the cargo, before it learned to aim.
Captains’ independence
  • A Wavering shield sags aft down the file, and the column bunches behind it. That is a visible deviation.
  • A shield that routs breaks out of the file and scatters off the lane. The column does not close the gap.
  • The Runner’s death forks its shields: steady ones drive for the beach in revenge, shaken ones go home (Ships §9).
  • Flag sunk. No carving changes. The resolve crater hits the front first, because the hulls in front are the ones already bleeding.
Counters
  • B · Fire through the file, and keep firing. Standard works. The screen cannot backfill, so every kill brings the Runner one hull nearer the head of the file, and each crew takes the target with the highest hit probability (Cannon §5), which is usually the lead. From Cam 3, Piercer for the Bulwark when it takes the head. Splasher at the beach for whatever lands.
  • C · Place the reach at the beach end. A triage placement, not a hunt (Campaign §4 stages no core-hunting before Cam 7). In Deploy, centre the AoR on the road’s last cell before the beach rather than its mouth. The crews still take whatever the file offers first (Cannon §5), but a file that never slows and never backfills carries its Runner through the centre of your reach on the way to the touch, and every lead you sink there moves the Runner up into the fire. It costs you the approach.
  • C · Time the alpha. Because the column never slows, the Runner’s commit tick is fixed by its speed and known in advance. That tick is when the held alpha goes.
If it fails
  • Succeeded: troops ashore. Failed: the Runner sunk before its commit point. Its shields fork, and the word dies with the bearer.
  • Greenwood repeats: a Runner that lives sails again behind whatever shields remain, cheapest first, or it refuses.
  • What the fleet learns is the fear on the cells where the file died.
  • Later, Heartoak re-carves on the same approach, and Ironbark carves the word heavier as Cold Shadow.
The Carving

Known by Halvard, his hand on the grain; Rhyna knew it as he knew it. One sign, the Bar Before, and of it she could tell one word: “…before…”. Known whole at the Night of the Naming: “Before bearer, thin wood. Thin wood falls; bearer keeps pace.”

Real-world root

#11 Sacrifice the plum to save the peach, first form: the sacrificial van, the cheapest thrown in front (research §1).

E1-05

Strike the Flash

“Where it flashed.”

Hulls bend toward the newest exposure glint and ignore everything else.

LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Hunt · the Spark (root)W1 / 10—
Era & debut

E1 · the Hasty Tide · Cam 3, on a battle after the Reaver’s first (Camouflage arrives with the Reaver)

Grows from

— (root of the Hunt)

Grows into

E2-05 Make Them Speak (Cam 11)

Line onward

E2-05 → E3-05 Make an Example → E4-07 Borrow the Road → E5-06 The Prize Too Easy → T-02 The Thrown Stone (§16b)

Intent

It showed itself, so kill it. Go where the last gun flashed and destroy it; nothing else matters. The young wood can hold one flash, and it holds the newest.

Chosen when

Filter: a Reaver pack, always; or a Bombard-cored formation once an exposure glint is known. Any resolve band. Gain conditions:

  1. an exposure glint is known;
  2. one was written last sortie;
  3. the formation is a Reaver pack;
  4. the least-feared road to the freshest glint has no SCALDING cell.

It is a Reaver pack’s only E1 word. A steady Bombard line takes it over Break the First Wall when the road burns and a fresh glint is known. A shaken line takes it rather than going home, if the glint is fresh. Ghosts feed it: a glinted gun you moved stays in the ledger until something sees the empty grid (Fleet Memory §4). Later, Blackthorn (Hunt +3, the Reaver’s own grain) keeps the line alive. The root takes LINE −1 once Make Them Speak is a candidate (Cam 11).

Composition

One formation · role hunt · C 1 · A 1 · W.

  • Reaver pack. This is brief -8’s early bait arc: a small pack against one exposed gun. It holds one clump on one glint (1 contact). It never splits its fixes across two guns; the Reaver Pack scheme of later Tides does that (Ships §9). If a board authors brief -8’s scattered two-hull pack instead (2 contacts), it sails alone.
  • Carving.
    • sail: offset 0.
    • objective: the newest glint this formation knows. Its captain re-reads it as the net brings news, and never goes back to an older one (self-trigger).
    • lane: least summed fear to sight of that gun.
    • commit point: the first lane cell inside the engagement band.
    • With no glint at launch, it runs to the inner edge of the reading band and holds there until one is known, or until t30, then takes its fallback.
    • It ignores walls and castles on the way (Reaver baseline). A Bombard formation carrying the word stops shelling walls.
    • fallback: the Reaver baseline, nearly inert against an enclosed fort (Ships §5).
Choreography
  • t0. It leaves. With no glint known, it waits at the edge of the fear.
  • The tick a glint is known, whether its own sight wrote it or the net carried it, every bow swings to the flash and the pack runs for sight of the gun. It lines up and hammers, and it rages when the fix is a good one (Ships §5).
  • If a newer flash is known mid-run, it swings to that one, once per flash.
  • The errand is the gun it is hunting. When that gun is destroyed, or re-observed enclosed, the errand voids and the pack withdraws, eliminated (Victory §4b).
  • Across sorties, survivors keep the word and go to the newest glint in the ledger, even the ghost of a gun you moved. They shell dead ground until they see that it is gone.
Signature
  • First sight. Your exposed gun fires, and a pack turns on it and hammers it, ignoring walls, castles and everything else.
  • Returning player’s tell. At the start of the sortie a pack idles at the edge of the fear, going nowhere, until something flashes. Then every bow swings at once toward the newest flash. That is the Hunt’s root sign (§9a).
Captains’ independence
  • Middling nerve that over-commits on a juicy fix (Ships §5). A raging pack drives Committed through fire it would otherwise skirt, and that is the deviation the player can bait.
  • Balk at a lane cell gone SCALDING this sortie.
  • Flag sunk. The net slows, so a pack waiting at the fear’s edge hears of distant glints late, or not at all this sortie. The carving is unchanged.
Counters
  • B · Enclose the gun. Walls hide the flash; no glint, no hunt (Fleet Memory §5). Re-enclose a hunted gun in Build and the errand voids (Victory §4b).
  • B · Camouflage. Arriving with the Reaver at Cam 3, it hides the group so it cannot be fixed.
  • C · Bait and relocate. Fire exposed, let the gun glint, and move it in Deploy. A Contact is revised only by contradiction, so next sortie the pack shells dead ground (Fleet Memory §6; brief -8’s arc: exposed → fixed → relocated → it shells nothing).
  • C · Play the recency (from Cam 7, with a second group: fire-control is per group, so the far gun must be in a group of its own). With two exposed guns, flash the far one after the pack commits to the near one. It swings broadside across your group’s fire.
  • Triage. Let an exposed gun on a flank you do not need take the pack.
If it fails
  • Succeeded: the hunted gun destroyed. Countered: the glint re-observed absent or enclosed, so the errand voids. Failed: eliminated or routed before its commit point.
  • Greenwood repeats: a surviving pack goes to the newest glint again.
  • The fleet learns only the empty grids its eyes reach, one gun at a time.
  • Later, Blackthorn (Failed → −3, but +2 toward the Contact where it lost most hulls) comes back for the gun that hurt it most. That is the seed of Make Them Speak.
The Carving

Known by Rhyna, her hand on the grain; Halvard knew it as she knew it. One sign, the Spark, a cut that ends in a star-notch, and of it he could tell one word: “…flash…”. Known whole at the Night of the Naming: “Where it flashed, strike. Last flash, only flash.”

Real-world root

Flash-spotting, 1915–18: observers took crossed bearings on muzzle flashes to fix and counter-shell the guns that fired.

E1-06

Wait in the Grey

“Wait in the grey.”

Formations loiter in COLD fringe cells and drift in only when no new fear has been written for one reload.

LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Patience · the Knot (root)W1 / 10—
Era & debut

E1 · the Hasty Tide · Cam 4, not on its first battle. It is the one-group hold-feint board brief -8 asks for

Grows from

— (root of the Patience)

Grows into

E2-07 Let the Fire Work (Cam 10) · E5-04 The Fourth Time, which is this word carved three times over

Carried in form

as the chain of E5-03 and E5-04, on a Runner convoy this filter never took (§16c)

Line onward

E2-07 → E3-08 Spent or Waiting (this word with a look) → E5-04 → T-07 The Long Winter (§16b)

Intent

The shore burns, so stand where it does not reach until it tires. Wait in the cold water at the edge of the fire, and go in when the shore falls quiet. The young wood cannot tell a gun that is spent from a gun that is waiting.

Chosen when

Filter: a Bombard-cored formation or a Recon wolfpack; steady only. Precondition: a cold fringe exists. Gain conditions:

  1. the road is HOT or SCALDING;
  2. the fringe lies beside the least-feared road;
  3. the fleet lost a hull last sortie;
  4. no fresh glint is known.

It is the word of a steady standoff formation that first sails into a sea the fort has burned, with cold water to stand in (4 of 4). No formation can carry it in sortie 1, because there is no fear yet; it belongs to the second and third wood of a battle. A fresh glint beats it (Strike the Flash wins the tie). Later, Yew (Patience +3, the Skyfall and Sower grain) and Willow (+2) carry the line. The root takes LINE −1 once Let the Fire Work is a candidate (Cam 10).

Composition

One formation · its lineage’s role (siege for a Bombard formation, probe for Recons) · C 1 · A 1 · W.

  • Carving.
    • sail: offset 0, to the loiter cell: the cold fringe cell beside the road, nearest the spawn (ties: row-major).
    • self-trigger QUIET: it loiters until no new fear has been written in this formation’s own grid for 4 ticks, or until t30.
    • Then it drifts in along the road to its lineage objective.
    • commit point: the first lane cell inside the engagement band.
    • fallback: its lineage baseline.
    • If no cold fringe is left at a later boundary, it waits in the launch zone instead: the other form of the Patience root sign (§9a).
ValueStartRangeProvenance and how it is settled
QUIET window4 ticks3–6One reload (Cannon §4, ~4 s): a battery in action writes fear at least once a reload. Settled on the Cam 4 hold-feint board: a one-group player who stops firing on purpose must see the drift begin inside the reading band. A longer window lets a group that is still firing keep it parked by accident
Latest releaset3024–34The shared E1 hold-short limit (intro table): a loiterer released at the latest tick must still complete a passage inside the 70-tick backstop (Fight §3)
Choreography
  • t0. It leaves and crosses the cold water of the reading band.
  • It stops in the fringe cell. The hulls idle and drift.
  • It stays as long as fear is being written, whether on its own hulls or carried by the net from any other formation. When the other formation of the sortie is under fire, it waits for that fight to end.
  • Four quiet ticks later it comes in, alone, late, against guns that have reloaded. It is the piecemeal wave by construction (research §3.20, the missed rendezvous of 1281; §3.16, Midway’s straggling waves).
  • Hit while it waits, it makes the crazy-Ivan, shifts to the nearest COLD cell, and restarts the quiet clock. With no COLD cell left, it drifts in at once.
  • Across sorties, survivors keep the word. The fringe moves outward as fear builds, and inward as the young wood’s fast decay forgets.
Signature
  • First sight. A formation stops short in open water at the edge of your fire and sits there doing nothing while the rest of the attack goes on. A little after your guns fall silent, it comes in.
  • Returning player’s tell. A formation halts in a cold cell before the engagement band, bows not quite on the fort, idling. It moves only on your silence.
Captains’ independence
  • Waiting drains nerve slowly, because there is no progress (Ships §4). A formation parked until its latest tick may already be sagging aft when it goes in.
  • The waiting is carved; it is never a balk. A balk is a lateral stall at a cell edge that went SCALDING (§2).
  • Flag sunk. The net turns slow and local, less news of fire reaches the formation, and it hears the quiet sooner. Decapitation makes the waiter come early.
Counters
  • B · Do not feed the fringe; cover where it must come. Spend no fire on the fringe. It has to go in by t30 anyway, into a group that is loaded and waiting.
  • B · Spotter. It grows the group’s AoR over the fringe, so next sortie no cold water is left to wait in.
  • C · Hold-feint. Needs fire-control. Fall silent on purpose. Four ticks later it drifts in, on a tick you chose; spend the first volley as it commits.
  • C · Re-task onto the parked formation. It sits still and does not jink until the first hit, so the first volley is clean, at the price of the crawl and your coverage elsewhere.
If it fails
  • Succeeded: it reached its commit point. Countered: released at t30 without ever hearing quiet. Failed: eliminated or routed before its commit point, usually by the alpha on the drift-in.
  • Greenwood repeats: survivors wait again at the new fringe.
  • The fleet learns the fear where the alpha fell, so next time the grey it stands in is farther out.
  • Later, Yew (Countered → 0) waits and retries. Blackthorn and Ashwood (Patience −2) will not carve the line.
The Carving

Known by Halvard, his hand on the grain; Rhyna knew it as he knew it. One sign, the Knot, a stroke that stops in a knot of the grain, and of it she could tell one word: “…grey…”. Known whole at the Night of the Naming: “Wait in grey, as seed waits out frost. When stone is still, come.”

Real-world root

#4 Wait at leisure while the enemy exhausts himself (Sun Tzu, ch. 7), crude form: hang at the fringe and drift in when the fire slackens (research §1).

E1-07

Home When Wounded

“Home when wounded.”

The whole formation turns for spawn as one at its first hull lost, runs in a body through your reach, and breaks apart once clear of it: the real flight that later becomes a feigned one.

LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Lure · the Turned Stern (root)W1 / 10—
Era & debut

E1 · the Hasty Tide · Cam 2, on a battle before the Recon’s (shaken Runner and Bombard formations can carry it)

Grows from

— (root of the Lure)

Grows into

E3-03 Feigned Flight (Cam 17; the Lure line has no Second-Tide form)

Line onward

E3-03 → E4-05 Along the Shore, Home by the Far Road → T-05 The Burning Retreat (§16b)

Intent

Go, but do not die here. Sail at the shore, and at the first hull lost turn every hull for home. Wood that has seen its kin sink will not give the shore a second.

Chosen when

Filter: a Runner-cored or Bombard-cored formation, or a Recon wolfpack; shaken only. Resolve damage accumulates across the battle and spans unsailed reserves, so fresh wood can sail already shaken (Ships §4; Fleet Memory §7). Gain conditions:

  1. the road is HOT or SCALDING;
  2. the fleet lost a hull last sortie;
  3. a formation of the fleet was eliminated last sortie;
  4. the formation is skittish (a Recon).

A shaken Recon always carries it. A shaken Runner carries it once the road burns (otherwise Landfall). A shaken Bombard line carries it unless a fresh glint is known (then Strike the Flash). It belongs to the last wood of a bad battle. The Lure grains (Twistbough +2, Blackthorn +1) carry its heir, Feigned Flight. The root takes LINE −2 once that heir is a candidate.

Composition

One formation in its lineage’s own role (cargo, siege or probe), with a carved low commit · C 1 · A 1 · W.

  • Carving.
    • sail: offset 0.
    • lane, objective and commit point: by its lineage.
    • self-trigger: on the tick this formation loses its first hull, the whole formation turns for its spawn together, in shape, on its least-feared lane home. The tick its rearmost hull clears the engagement band outward, every hull breaks for spawn on its own least-feared lane from where it is (ties: row-major), so the formation fans out.
    • Hulls running home do not stop to shoot.
    • A docked Runner cannot turn (Ships §7), but its shields do.
    • fallback: its lineage baseline.
  • Contacts. 1 through every tick the player’s guns can reach it: it holds its shape until it is clear of the engagement band, so the Hasty Tide’s contact band of 2 holds (Campaign §4). Only beyond the band, going home out of believed reach, does it break into its hulls (Victory §3: a formation that breaks apart). By then the scattered hulls ask nothing of the player: they are leaving, out of reach, and do not fire. The scatter is the tell, not a decision (Victory §3 red line).
Choreography
  • t0. It leaves on its lineage’s road, indistinguishable from any Hasty Tide formation except for the lean of shaken hulls.
  • The tick its first hull sinks, the whole formation turns stern-to at once and runs for home in a body.
  • Clear of the engagement band, it breaks apart, every hull by its own water.
  • It is under fire all the way out of the band. The withdrawal is 12 ticks, and retreat is not safe passage (Victory §4).
  • Across sorties, survivors sail first next time (Victory §4b), carrying the same word, and turn again at the first loss. Each flight drains more nerve, and the formation ends in refusal or in the fleet’s Retreat.
Signature
  • First sight. You sink one ship and the whole group turns and runs. Once it is out of your reach, it comes apart, every ship its own way.
  • Returning player’s tell. In the reading band, before anything has hit it, the formation already sags aft with its lines loose. Then comes the simultaneous turn at the first sink.
  • Why it is not an ordinary rout. A rout by nerve takes hulls one at a time and spreads over ~2–3 s (Ships §4). This turn takes every hull on the same tick, the steady with the shaken, and then, past your reach, it scatters.
  • That is the Lure’s root sign, the Turned Stern, in its true form: a real flight breaks shape once it is safe to. From the Tide of Remembering on, a flight that keeps its shape all the way home, or turns back in, is a lie (§9a).
Captains’ independence
  • The turn is carved, so even steady hulls take it.
  • The one exception is the orphan fork. If the first hull lost is the formation’s last core, the fork runs instead, and steady orphans turn wolfpack and drive in: one hull coming at you while the rest go home (Committed motion, Ships §9).
  • Flag sunk elsewhere. That is not this formation’s loss, so it does not turn on it. The resolve crater may still rout its hulls one by one, and the difference between that cascade and the carved scatter is visible.
Counters
  • B · Keep firing. The fleeing hulls cannot hurt the fort, and every one you sink never sails again.
  • B · Sink its first hull early. Take the cheapest shot in the lead, and the formation turns before it reaches the engagement band: a Repel for one shell.
  • C · The golden bridge. Let it go and hold your fire for what follows. One sink is certain to turn it, so a single early kill buys the whole formation’s retreat and frees your only group for the other formation.
  • C · Choose the tick of that kill. Use Command Time: the earlier it turns, the less of your fire, and the fewer of your glints, it carries home.
If it fails
  • Succeeded: home with half its hulls or more. Failed: eliminated.
  • Greenwood repeats: a formation that lives comes back first and turns again, so one shot a sortie turns it until it is gone or refuses.
  • What the fleet learns is where it was hit, and that its nerve is lower each time.
  • Later, the Lure grains carve the heir: the flight that keeps its shape, made from this one.
The Carving

Known by Rhyna, her hand on the grain; Halvard knew it as she knew it. One sign, the Turned Stern, a stroke that hooks back on itself, and of it he could tell one word: “…home…”. Known whole at the Night of the Naming: “Home when wounded. One bough falls; all turn, each by its own water.”

Real-world root

Hastings, 1066: the genuine rout of the Breton wing came before any feigned flight, and the Normans then learned to repeat it on purpose. #36 Retreat is best, first form (research §3.5).

18E2 · THE SECOND TIDE (Vaelthae)

Cam 7–14 · one link, the lure role, lineage grains, one habit · “the first answering sail”

They came in pairs now. The front one took the shot and the back one took the ground. And in the third month of that Tide a lookout on the east tower called me up to see a thing I will not forget. A hull was struck off the north point, and a mile south of it another hull’s sail went pale, grey-green blanching to white like a man’s face, and it turned. It had not seen. It had felt. That was the first answering sail.

— Kael the Counter, the second count of The Tides, as the Wall Counted Them (Legends V.5)

What this growing is. The Second Tide is the first fleet with a plan above the formation. From Cam 9, each sortie the Heartwood carves one stratagem and gives it to two parts, tied by one link (§4). Everything the carving does not name still sails as the Hasty Tide sailed. An idle formation runs its own lineage policy on its own lowest-fear lane (§5 step 4). So an E2 sortie reads as a pair that works together inside a crowd that does not.

The board turns first and the mind after it, one screw at a time (§13; Campaign §4):

  • Cam 7 (AB). The second approach opens. The Skyfall and the Provocateur arrive, each on a battle of its own (§13 LAW · BREADTH ENTERS WITH LAYER B). Nothing in the Admiral turns: the fleet still carves each formation once, in Greenwood grain, by fit alone (§17 LAW · GREEN WOOD TAKES ONE CUT), now with unrelated glyphs on two bearings, and the Hasty Tide never looks more disordered than it does here.
  • Cam 8 (AC). A piece-layer campaign. Nothing in the Admiral turns, and no lineage debuts (no Layer B).
  • Cam 9 (AD). The Second Tide’s family turns on (§13): from the first battle the Heartwood re-carves every hull for every morrow, lineage grains arrive with their whole temper (§7), and one habit may become active (§6). Links and the lure role unlock, and Cold Shadow carries them on a battle of its own: the first linked carving of the war, and the first answering sail. The Sower arrives on another battle (CALL-OUT 11).
  • Cam 10–13. One new carving per battle, never on the same battle as a new lineage (table below).
  • Cam 14 (CD). Nothing new is added; the whole book is combined. It closes the Tide, and Kael’s second count is laid (Legends V.5). The vault of Hasty-Tide wrecks has been known at last the campaign before: The Night of the Naming (Legends V.2) is laid when Cam 13 is complete, and the codex fills the E1 lines that night (§9b).

How organised it is. It is crude, and the crudeness is easy to see.

  • One link. Two parts wait on each other, and any third part keeps its own hour. The Second Tide can coordinate a pair, never a fleet.
  • The link straggles. An event reaches its partner 3 ticks late while the flag lives (§4; range 2–4). So partners arrive a beat behind the moment they were waiting for, which is the Midway pattern of piecemeal waves (research §3.16).
  • No hinge. The plan cannot branch. If the partner’s event never comes, the waiting part goes anyway at its latest tick, into whatever is there.
  • Nothing retires (§5 step 3). Only the rule against repeating a Failed carving immediately, and the grain, stop a carving that is failing.
  • Decapitation drops it back a Tide. A sunk flag breaks every pending link. Each part takes its fallback on local beliefs, and the net slows by +3 ticks. Every later carving this battle loses its link (1 → 0). A carving that has lost its link runs as if its partner’s event never arrived: an AFTER part sails at its latest tick, and a HOLD-UNTIL part holds to its latest tick. For the rest of the battle the fleet speaks Second-Tide words with Hasty-Tide timing.
LAW · TWO PARTS, ONE LINK (E2)
  • An E2 carving ties exactly two parts with exactly one link.
  • A part is a formation, or a convoy’s shield element stationed ahead of its core (Cold Shadow; §3 Parts).
  • A third formation in the carving may be given a lane, an objective and a launch offset, but never a link. It keeps its own hour and shows no answering sail.

Timing follows §3’s conventions (ticks, the fear edge, a hold’s latest tick at arrival + L_max). Every E2 link fires on an event its partner perceives, normally between t15 and t30, and none needs precision finer than a fear cell (§2).

Pale sails heard; grey sails kept the hour (§4). From Cam 9 a part released by its link blanches, and one released by its latest tick or by a broken link goes grey. So the player can always tell a formation that believed what it heard from one that came because it was time.

What the player is learning to read.

  1. Who waits on whom. Every E2 carving has one part that goes first and one that waits. The waiting part shows the link twice: first by waiting in plain sight, then by its sails.
  2. Three new root signs. Feint (the Two Mouths), Bleed (the Breath) and Mask (the Smoothed Cut) appear for the first time. Flood, Sounding, Screen, Hunt and Patience each gain their second form. Siege and Lure skip this Tide, so their E1 forms stay in use.
  3. The heart’s temper. From Cam 9, and from each battle’s sortie 2, the grain shows in how the fleet answers a failure. Ironbark and Heartoak come again; Willow and Twistbough switch (§7). The first Blackthorn heart met from Cam 11 unlocks The Axe in the Grain, in fragments (Legends IV.2; its stone leaf, IV.1, is laid at Cam 10).
  4. The first thing the fleet believes about you. One habit at a time. The dispatch reports it in one line when it crosses into active: “They marked that the north holds its fire.” (§9c). No E2 carving is keyed to H5 or H8, and that is deliberate: H5 can still be counted and dispatched here, so the fleet is seen to learn that you move its guns a Tide before it can use it (the Hunt line’s H5 answer, from Cam 16, §6).

Reading the Second Tide at a glance. Every E2 opening is one goes, one waits. The carvings are told apart by what waits, where it waits, and what the other part is doing. All of it is visible inside the reading band (§9a).

Lineages available.

  • From E1: Bombard, Runner with Swarm troops, Recon, Reaver, Sapper troops, Bulwark.
  • New this Tide: Skyfall with bombers and the Provocateur (Cam 7), Sower (9), Corsair and Quartermaster (11), and the common Leviathan pair (13). These are the CALL-OUT 11 proposals.

The kit the counters may use. Each counter arrives with its threat (Campaign §6).

  • Cannons: Standard, Suppressor, Splasher, Piercer (Cam 3), Saturation (with the first bomber cloud, Cam 7), Marksman (Cam 11).
  • Towers: Stonewright, Spotter, Camouflage (Cam 3), Flare (Cam 9), Jammer (Cam 11), Powder Store (Cam 13, with the common Leviathan: the first hull whose soak your output must beat; proposal, CALL-OUT 11).
  • Not used here. No E2 counter uses the Interdictor or Breaker, which arrive with the Breacher at Cam 23, or the Rally Flag, which arrives at Cam 15 (as verb-speed begins to decide hinges; proposal, CALL-OUT 11).

Budget (Campaign §2 contact ramp for Cam 7–22; §11).

  • Contacts and approaches. C 4–5 board-wide, ≤4 decision-bearing. A = 2 live approaches.
  • Hulls and sorties. 12–18 hulls on the board, Q ≈ 9 formations, 5–6 sorties.
  • Guns and groups. 5→8 guns in 2 groups, and a third as the stage fills out (Campaign §4: “a third group appears, so one can be spared to hunt”).
  • Per carving. Each carving’s own parts spend 2–3 contacts (≤3 of them decision-bearing) and 1–2 approaches. The rest of the sortie is idle.
  • Idle cores still count. An idle formation that carries a core counts as decision-bearing, so the sortie total must still fit ≤4; any surplus core is held at the launch zone’s forward edge (§11 The held host).
  • The book. 15 cumulative carvings (7 + 8). An E1 form fades by supersession (LINE −1) whenever its E2 heir is also a candidate. It returns whenever the heir cannot be carved: no Bulwark sails, no Sower sails, or the link has been lost to decapitation.
