The Knowings. In this Tide the wreck-wood gives Halyna two signs and the gap between them.

  • One of Halyna touches the grain, Rhyna or Halvard, and what one knows the other knows. They tell it by turns, one beginning and the other finishing.
  • Seren Two-Inks sets the phrase down in two inks, without marking whose is whose, and marks every break in it (§9b; fiction_hooks §7).
  • The Carvings below are exemplars. The generator writes the real line from the record of what ran, with the true bearings, and with gaps where the wood broke.
E2-01

Run the Batteries

“Through, not against.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Flood · the WaveW (one link, no hinge, no lure)2 / 1–2, 2 decision-bearing1H3
Era & debut

E2 · the Second Tide · Cam 11 (BC)

Grows from

E1-01 Landfall: Landfall given a road through the fire, and a second ship to keep the gunners busy while it runs

Grows into

E3-04 Two Roads to One Stone (two such columns converge) · E4-01 Three Roads, One Hour (three roads on one hour)

Carried whole

as the chain of E5-01; carried in form as the chain of E5-05 (its column, without the cover line, §16c)

Intent

“The shore behind their fire is cold, and their fire is a band, not a wall: a column that does not stop spends few breaths inside it. Keep the stone busy at the edge, and trade no shot for shot.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions (believed state):
    • A Runner convoy sails at or above half its screen, so it is not refusing (Victory §4a).
    • A band of ≥2 adjacent HOT cells lies across every lane from its spawn to a COLD coast cell behind the band.
  • Gain conditions (FIT):
    1. The straight lane crosses ≤2 HOT cells.
      • Starting value 2, range 1–3.
      • Settled in greybox so that a three-cell run loses its cargo more often than not against a default fort. The fleet should be right to believe “thin”.
    2. The lowest-fear lane around the band is ≥2 cells longer than the straight one.
    3. No cell on the straight lane is SCALDING.
    4. A Bombard line sails, and a wall Contact lies within one cell of the crossing.
  • Habit: H3 “They strike first and far.” When the first fire comes far out, the shore behind it is thin.
  • Grains: Ashwood +3 (a Runner flag carves its own charge), Ironbark +2, Heartoak +2. Against it: Willow −2, Twistbough −2, Yew −1.
  • Score sketch (Ashwood, H3 active, FIT 3): 3 + 3 + 1 = 7. Landfall scores 2 + 3 − 1 (LINE) = 4.
Composition
  • cargo: the Runner convoy in column. 1–2 Runner cores and 4 shields, with a Bulwark in the van, which is its default shape (Ships §9). Decision-bearing.
  • siege, the cover: a Bombard line (core and 2 shields, line abreast), or from Cam 13 a Leviathan pair.
    • It takes station at the near edge of the band and shells the wall Contact nearest the crossing.
    • It holds there HOLD-UNTIL(cargo committed).
    • Decision-bearing, because it asks the player whether to answer the line or the column.
  • idle: everything else.
  • Approaches: A 1, or 2 when the cover’s best station is on the other road. The link is the cover’s hold.
Choreography

Single sortie.

  • t0. Every formation leaves at once on a straight lane: the Flood root sign. The column’s lane points into the haze. Every other Runner lane in the game points around it.
  • t≈10–15. The cover reaches the band edge, stops line abreast and opens on the wall. Part of the band’s fire goes to it: each crew takes the target with the highest hit probability (Cannon §5), and for the guns on the cover’s side a line parked at the band edge is the nearest and stillest target.
  • t≈10–28. The column crosses the band at full speed. Its captains’ grid-scoring is limited to spacing: no lateral fear avoidance and no standoff. When the van Bulwark dies, the next shield backfills the lead.
  • t≈23–28. The column reaches its commit point and drives for the touch.
  • 3 ticks later. The cover’s sails blanch. It reverts to its own policy: it slides laterally to cooler water and keeps shelling.
  • If the column never commits, the cover goes grey at its hour, about 18 ticks after taking station.
  • Across sorties. A success adds MEMORY +1 and INERTIA +1, so the fleet runs the same band again until the coast behind it stops reading COLD.
Signature
  • First time. Everything comes at once. The landing ships come in single file, ruler-straight, and do not swerve when your guns open. A line of warships stops at the edge of your fire and shells the wall. When the landing touches, the warships’ sails flash pale and they slide away.
  • Returning tell (by t≈12). The Flood root sign, plus a column whose lane points into the haze instead of around it. It is the only Runner behaviour in the game that ignores a HOT cell. With a Bombard line stopping short at the edge, it is this carving and not Landfall.
Captains’ independence
  • Balk. The carving says through HOT, never through SCALDING. If a cell on the lane goes SCALDING this sortie, the column balks at its edge: it stalls laterally, loosens and takes its fallback. The fallback is the Runner beeline to the softest COLD coast on local beliefs. This is Mobile Bay’s falter, made legible.
  • Waver. The cover has middling resolve. Pounded hard, it sags aft before the column is ashore, and the column loses its partner early.
  • Flag sunk. The link breaks. The cover takes its fallback at once and skirts out of the band, leaving the band’s guns only the column to shoot. If the flag rode in the column, the column runs on regardless, because it is the carrying formation. Later carvings lose the link, so the next Run the Batteries has no cover that holds.
Counters
  • B · Depth.
    • Cover the beach, not only the band: put a second group on the coast behind the fire, with Standard on the column and Splasher on the troop stacks it lands.
    • Piercer the van Bulwark.
    • Triage: if that coast’s castle is not required, hold the ring behind it.
  • C · A column cannot turn.
    • Hold fire through the band. Let the column cross cold and spend the alpha where it commits. It will not swerve.
    • Scald the first cell. Concentrate early fire on its first HOT cell until that cell goes SCALDING. The column balks and falls back to the softest COLD coast, which you left cold on purpose over a silent group (the hold-feint, Fleet Memory §6).
    • Crawl onto its axis. In Command Time, pick up the band group as the column enters and crawl its AoR along the column’s axis. The column arrives on a known line at a known tick.
    • Ignore the cover. A Stonewright on its wall answers it.
If it fails

Failed: the column is eliminated or routed before its commit point.

  • Ironbark (+1) re-carves it, heavier. Its line’s next form (E4-01) is not yet in the book, so it runs the same band again.
  • Heartoak (−1, then +1 on the same road) comes by the same road.
  • Ashwood (−2) may not repeat a Failed carving, so it falls back to Landfall, the crude flood. The rash heart, burned once, forgets that it learned to run.
  • Willow (−5) turns to The Sounding.
  • What it learned.
    • The band’s cells take more fear and may go SCALDING.
    • If the column died at the beach, that coast is no longer COLD, so FIT 1 and the precondition both fail there.
    • The run therefore never repeats against a coast you defended. It repeats only when you did all your killing in the band.
The Carving

Through burning, not against. Stone-breakers hold at edge … till bearer touches shore.

One of Halyna said, and the other finished: “They were not sent to fight our guns.” “Only to pass them.”

Real-world root

On 24 April 1862 Farragut ran past Forts Jackson and St Philip below New Orleans while Porter’s mortar schooners kept the forts’ gunners busy. At Mobile Bay in 1864 his column faltered when Tecumseh sank, then pushed through (research §3.22).

E2-02

The Sounding

“One goes; the rest follow the quiet.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Sounding · the Lone StrokeW (one link, no hinge, no lure)2 / 1, 2 decision-bearing1H1
Era & debut

E2 · the Second Tide · Cam 10 (AE)

Grows from

E1-03 Go Until Struck: Go Until Struck with someone listening. The lone hull still runs until something fires, but now a convoy waits on what it learns

Grows into

E3-01 Sounding and Mass, where the waiting convoy learns to choose the other road

Intent

“We do not know this water, and one hull that sees is cheaper than a convoy that guesses. Let the root go before the tree; where the root is not burned, the tree follows.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions:
    • A Recon sails.
    • A cargo convoy sails (a Runner, or from Cam 7 a Skyfall).
    • The lane to the softest believed coast has ≥2 cells that are WARM or never crossed. A mapped sector would void a Recon picket’s errand (Victory §4b).
  • Gain conditions (FIT):
    1. More than half the lane’s cells are WARM or uncrossed.
    2. A second candidate lane lies within one fear step of the first, so the fleet cannot tell which is better.
    3. Last sortie a cargo was struck in a cell the fleet believed COLD. It has been surprised once.
    4. The probe’s lane passes within Recon sight of a wall Contact, so it will see something worth passing on.
  • Habit: H1 “That quarter holds its fire.” The probe’s lane is carved into the silent quarter.
  • Grains: Willow +3 (a Recon flag looks before it leaps). Silverbark doubles the H1 point. No grain is against it.
  • Score sketch (Willow, H1 active, FIT 3): 3 + 3 + 1 = 7. Go Until Struck scores at most 1 + 3 − 1 = 3.
Composition
  • probe: a Recon wolfpack, or what is left of one, sailing as one small contact on the convoy’s own lane. Decision-bearing: you either shoot it or let it look.
  • cargo: the convoy loiters in the launch zone with a full screen, AFTER(probe crossed its first band cell). Decision-bearing.
    • Its carved lane is the probe’s lane.
    • Once it wakes, its captains’ grid-scoring avoids any lane cell where fear was written this sortie.
    • So it follows the probe’s quiet and swerves from the probe’s pain, 3 ticks late.
  • Approaches: A 1. The probe sounds the road the convoy will take; idle formations may use the other road.
Choreography

Single sortie.

  • t0. The probe leaves alone and nothing else on its road moves: the Sounding root sign, a lone hull ≥6 ticks ahead of anything.
  • t≈10–13. The probe clears the first reading-band cell of the lane.
  • 3 ticks later. The convoy’s sails blanch and it goes, about 11 ticks behind the probe and a little before its latest tick (t18).
  • Where the probe is struck, it swerves. It is skittish, and that was its E1 ancestor’s only habit. The fear it wrote reaches the convoy 3 ticks later, and the convoy skirts that cell.
  • If the probe is struck before it clears its first cell, the convoy sails grey at t18. It knows only what the probe passed on before it broke.
Signature
  • First time. One small, quick ship comes out alone and sweeps along your coast, looking rather than shooting. The landing convoy sits in its safe water, fully escorted and loosely spread. Then its sails go pale, it draws in tight and comes, down the very water the little ship crossed unhurt.
  • Returning tell (by t≈8). A lone Recon out first, and a full-screened convoy motionless in the launch zone.
  • The escort’s tightness shows what the convoy believes (Fleet Memory §7).
    • A convoy that wakes and tightens believes the probe has told it where your fire is.
    • A convoy that sails grey and loose is guessing.
CALL-OUT 20 · The waiting convoy and the refusing convoy (§25)

Both sit in the launch zone. Victory §4a makes the refusal tell a presence, so the two can be confused.

  • Screen. A refuser is visibly thin, below half its shields. A waiter is full, and a lone Recon has gone out ahead of it.
  • Timing. A refuser never leaves. A waiter is gone by t18.
  • Test. §15 test 8: testers tell them apart at a glance by t8. If they cannot, the Sounding convoy waits at the launch zone’s forward edge instead.
Captains’ independence
  • The Recon. It is skittish. Struck once, it swerves. Struck twice, it may rout for spawn, still passing on what it saw as it runs; it is the fleet’s premium propagation node (Fleet Memory §7).
  • The convoy. Its captains balk at any lane cell that went SCALDING this sortie, usually the cell where you alpha’d the probe.
  • Flag sunk. The link breaks. The convoy does not wait for the probe: it sails grey on local beliefs as soon as the news reaches it (lag 3 + 3). If the flag dies before the probe clears its cell, the convoy goes blind down last sortie’s map.
Counters
  • B · Kill the knower.
    • The probe is alone, thin and skittish, and a Standard is enough. Whatever it had not yet passed on sinks with it, and its sector re-blinds (Fleet Memory §7).
    • Or cover both candidate lanes (Spotter widens a group’s AoR), so there is no quiet water to find.
  • C · Let it pass.
    • Stay silent on its lane. Keep the guns on the probe’s lane silent and enclosed; its sight then writes walls but no glints. It reports quiet, the convoy blanches, and it follows the probe’s lane into your held alpha. This is the hold-feint worked through the fleet’s own scout.
    • Strike it where you want the lane empty. Hit the probe only from an enclosed group on the lane you want empty. That writes fear with no glint, and the convoy swerves onto your other group.
    • Choose the tick. In Command Time, pick up the group as the probe crosses and choose the tick your first shell lands. Before the probe clears its first cell, the convoy sails grey and blind. After, it knows exactly what you let it learn.
If it fails

Failed: the cargo is eliminated or routed before its commit point.

  • Willow (−5) will not look the same way again. It turns to Let the Fire Work or Wait in the Grey.
  • Ironbark (+1) sounds the same road again.
  • Blackthorn puts its +2 on the cell where the convoy died and carves a Hunt there.
  • What it learned.
    • A convoy that died on water its probe called quiet is a COLD cell that turned HOT after a commit: one count of H1.
    • At two counts the dispatch reads “They marked that the north holds its fire,” and every Sounding-line carving aims its probe at that quarter first.
    • That count is what E3’s Sounding and Mass branches on.
The Carving

Root before tree; tree waits in still water. Where root … not burned, tree follows.

One of Halyna said, and the other finished: “The small ship was their hand in the dark.” “And the rest went where the hand came back unburned.”

Real-world root

Scouting before committing the main body (research §3.16). At Midway in 1942 the Tone’s scout launched about half an hour late, and that delay helped decide who was surprised.

E2-03

Cold Shadow

“Screen the bearer.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Screen · the Bar BeforeW (one link, no hinge, no lure)2 / 1, 1 decision-bearing1H7
Era & debut

E2 · the Second Tide · Cam 9 (AD). The first linked carving of the war and the first answering sail (Kael’s second count, Legends V.5). It is also the stage’s first clearly screened core, which is where core-hunting and purpose collapse become discoverable (Campaign §4)

Grows from

E1-04 Cheapest First: Cheapest First taught to stand still. The lead no longer just goes first into the fire; it stops inside it, faces it, and stays

Grows into

E3-04 Two Roads to One Stone

Intent

“The bearer is what we came to land; the bar is what we came to lose. Put the bar in their fire, facing it, until the bearer is ashore.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions: a cargo convoy with ≥1 Bulwark shield sails, and a HOT cell lies on or beside the cargo’s lowest-fear lane inside the reading band.
  • Gain conditions (FIT):
    1. That is the only HOT cell on the lane: one fire to face.
    2. The objective coast is COLD or WARM.
    3. The convoy’s screen is at full strength.
    4. Last sortie, cargo hulls sank while their shields survived.
  • Habit: H7 “They kill our bearers first.” Gain 4 is the same observation H7 counts.
  • Grains: Heartoak +3 (a Bulwark flag is proud of its shield), Ironbark +1.
  • Score sketch (Heartoak, H7 active, FIT 4): 4 + 3 + 1 = 8, or 9 with LINE +1 if Cheapest First ran earlier this battle. Cheapest First scores 2 + 3 − 1 = 4.
Composition
  • screen: the convoy’s shield element, Bulwark-led. It is stationed one cell ahead of its core, tight, aspect locked on the HOT cell, and holds HOLD-UNTIL(cargo committed).
    • A cell ahead, it reads as its own object, so C = 2.
    • It is glanceable (§11). This is the shield-element power of §3 Parts.
  • cargo: the core, with any riders. Decision-bearing.
    • A Quartermaster rider can ride here from Cam 11, which is why the Marksman arrives then.
    • For a Skyfall battlegroup, committed means bombers released.
  • Approaches: A 1.
Choreography

Single sortie.

  • t0–5. The convoy leaves. The shields pull a cell ahead, since escorts are faster than their core (Ships §9).
  • t≈10–15. The shields enter the HOT cell and stop, bows to the believed fire, drawn in tight.
  • t≈15–25. The cargo passes beside them through the WARM or COLD cells of its lane. While the Bulwarks stand nearer the AoR centre, they draw most of the fire (Cannon §5).
  • t≈23–28. The cargo commits.
  • 3 ticks later. The shields’ sails blanch. They release and close behind the cargo toward the beach, interposing by their own policy.
  • If the cargo is sunk before it commits, the hold ends with its purpose. The last core has died, so the screen meets the orphan fork (Ships §9).
Signature
  • First time. Big, heavy ships race out ahead and stop inside the water your guns cover, all facing the same way, taking every shot. The landing ship slides past behind them. When it reaches the shore, the heavy ships’ sails go pale and they close up behind it.
  • Returning tell (by t≈12). The Screen root sign: shields lead the cargo by a cell, tight, aspect locked on one hot cell, and they stop there.
    • A column that goes through the fire is Run the Batteries.
    • Shields that stop and face the fire are this carving.
Captains’ independence
  • The Bulwarks. They lean fanatical, rarely balk, and hold the hot cell under punishment.
  • The screen reshuffles. A healthier shield backfills the one you are cracking, and you can watch it happen.
  • The cargo. The Runner has no resolve of its own, but its captains still balk at a lane cell gone SCALDING this sortie.
  • The orphan fork. Kill the cargo in the shadow and the fork decides. Fresh Bulwarks turn revenge-wolfpack and drive forward: the Committed read. Worn ones go home.
  • Flag sunk. The link breaks. The screen drops its aspect lock and reverts to its lineage policy: it interposes on the nearest hull and turns to whatever fired last. The shadow wanders, and the cargo is partly bared.
Counters
  • B · Break the bar or reach past it.
    • Piercer through the Bulwark. It is the hull the Piercer exists for, and chip damage on it is wasted.
    • Deep-centre an AoR on the cargo’s lane: about 35% of shots leak to the core per passage, against 10% with a forward centre (Cannon §5). It costs coverage but needs no read.
    • From Cam 11 the Marksman ignores the screen and picks the core outright, at ×2.5 reload.
    • Against a Skyfall cargo, load Air before the deck opens.
  • C · Lock it on nothing.
    • Escort-tightness bait (Fleet Memory §7). In Deploy, move the AoR the fleet saw you fire from. The screen locks its aspect on the old hot cell and faces empty water while the cargo walks into your new centre.
    • Crawl off the parked screen in mid-Fight. It cannot follow until the cargo commits.
    • Choose the orphan fork. Grind the shields first if you want them to go home when you kill the bearer. Kill the bearer clean if you can afford an angry wolfpack.
If it fails

Failed: the cargo is eliminated or routed before its commit point.

  • Heartoak (−1, then +1 on the same road; exempt from the no-repeat rule) sends the shield by the same road again. The proud heart does not turn aside.
  • Ironbark (+1) re-carves it heavier, refilling the screen from the pool if it can (Ships §9).
  • Willow (−5) looks first next sortie, with The Sounding.
  • What it learned.
    • The screen’s hot cell takes the fear the Bulwarks soaked and may go SCALDING. The next Cold Shadow faces the new hottest cell beside the lane, so you can steer it.
    • Each cargo that dies before its shields is one count of H7. At two the dispatch reads “They know we kill the bearers first,” and the Screen and Mask carvings gain their habit point.
The Carving

Bar before bearer; bark to fire. Bar holds … till bearer is ashore.

One of Halyna said, and the other finished: “The great hulls were told to stand in our fire and face it.” “And they stood, until the bearer was ashore.”

Real-world root

#11 Sacrifice the plum tree to save the peach tree. In the convoys of 1917, escorts were set between the U-boats and the merchantmen, and losses fell (research §1, §4.2).

E2-04

Noise in the East

“Loud in one place; land in another.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Feint · the Two Mouths (root)P2 / 2, 2 decision-bearing1H2, H4
Era & debut

E2 · the Second Tide · Cam 12 (BD)

Grows from

— (root of the Feint). This is the first feint of the war. The Hasty Tide could not have made it, because a feint needs a second ship that waits

Grows into

E3-06 Open Roads, Hidden March (the noise becomes a credible siege) · E4-06 Sea Below, Sky Above (the noise draws one domain while another falls)

Intent

“They turn their reach to the loudest ship. Be loud on the road they guard and never close; when they answer, land on the other road.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions:
    • Two live approaches.
    • A lure-capable formation sails: a Provocateur wolfpack, or failing that a Bombard line.
    • A cargo convoy sails, with a spawn authored on the other approach (CALL-OUT 5).
  • Gain conditions (FIT):
    1. One approach carries most of the fear mass at sortie end, and the lure goes there.
    2. The other approach’s coast is COLD or WARM.
    3. The favoured approach holds glinted guns, so a demonstration there will draw fire.
    4. Last sortie, the fleet saw fear written on cells it had already passed as COLD: your reach moved while it sailed.
  • Habit: H2 “They guard the east road” or H4 “Their reach moves when pressed.” E2 holds one active habit, so the bonus is at most +1.
  • Grains: Twistbough +3 (a Corsair flag loves a trick). Heartoak −2, because it never feints.
  • Score sketch (Twistbough, H2 active, FIT 3): 3 + 3 + 1 = 7.
Composition
  • lure: the Provocateur (or Bombard) on the guarded approach, A. Decision-bearing.
    • Its objective is draw-fire.
    • Its carved commit point is the fear edge, where it paces laterally and never closes.
  • cargo: the convoy on approach B. Decision-bearing. It sails at t0 to B’s fear edge, just outside believed reach, and holds there HOLD-UNTIL(fire drawn on lure).
  • Approaches: A 2, one part per road.
Choreography

Single sortie.

  • t0. Both parts leave.
  • t≈12–15. The cargo parks at B’s fear edge and sits.
  • t≈15. The lure reaches A’s fear edge and turns along it, pacing sideways with its bows never pointing in.
  • First shell on the lure. That is fire drawn. Three ticks later the cargo’s sails blanch and it runs for B’s softest coast, about 10–20 ticks from its station.
  • If nobody shoots the lure, the cargo goes grey at its hour, about 18 ticks after parking.
  • The lure paces until the Fight’s withdrawal. A Provocateur never fired upon has voided its errand, and it leaves the queue (Victory §4b).
  • Spacing. The lure never arrives, so no single group is asked to answer two arrivals, and the spacing law (§11) is met trivially. What the feint buys is the ticks your re-tasked group spends crawling back: about 8 (Victory §3).
Signature
  • First time. One tough ship comes at your strong side and drifts along the edge of your fire, sideways, daring you, never coming in. On your other road a convoy is parked at the edge of your reach, doing nothing. The moment you shoot the loud one, the convoy’s sails go pale and it comes, a little late.
  • Returning tell (by t≈15). The Feint root sign: one formation moves laterally with no fore-aft commitment while another approach is quiet. Here a convoy is parked on the quiet road.
  • The seam. A demonstration never closes (§9a). Watch the bows: a real attack points in.
  • Not to be confused with Make Them Speak. There the pusher closes, and a Reaver pack waits behind it.
Captains’ independence
  • A Provocateur lure is fanatical. It neither balks nor sags, and soaks for a long time (its Goad expression).
  • A Bombard lure has middling resolve. Under heavy fire it wavers, sagging aft, and the demonstration ends early.
  • The cargo’s captains balk at a SCALDING cell on B.
  • Flag sunk. The link breaks. The cargo leaves its station at once, grey, on its own policy for B’s softest coast, as soon as the news reaches it (6 ticks). A decapitation while the lure paces therefore releases the landing early. Take the flag only if B is covered.
Counters
  • B · A group on each road, and no re-task to noise.
    • The demonstration cannot breach; it only asks for your fire.
    • Answer it, if at all, from enclosed guns, so it banks no glints. Camouflage on the group does the same.
  • C · Silence.
    • Never fire on the demonstration. The cargo holds until its hour, then comes grey and alone onto the road your group never left. A Provocateur that drew no fire all sortie also leaves the queue for good.
    • Delay the first shell. In Command Time, pick up the A group as the lure arrives and choose the tick your first shell lands. The later it lands, the later the cargo wakes, up to its hour.
    • Triage. If B’s castle is not required, let the landing come to a ring you have already given up.
If it fails

Failed: the cargo is eliminated or routed before its commit point. Countered: the lure drew no fire.

  • Twistbough (Failed −3; Succeeded −2, “never twice”) moves to Grey Before the Sails either way.
  • Willow (−5 Failed, −3 Countered) turns to The Sounding.
  • Silverbark (Countered −4) drops it fastest.
  • What it learned.
    • The lure writes fear on approach A and banks every un-enclosed gun that shot at it. An answered lure therefore makes A look more guarded, and the next feint aims there again.
    • If the cargo died at B, B’s coast is no longer COLD, so gain 2 fails.
    • A player who never re-tasks gives H4 nothing to count.
The Carving

Loud on guarded road; touch no shore. When stone answers loud … bearer to quiet road.

One of Halyna said, and the other finished: “The loud ship was never meant to reach us.” “It was meant to be heard.”

Real-world root

#6 Make a sound in the east, strike in the west (聲東擊西): a short, noisy feint draws the defence while the blow falls elsewhere (research §1).

E2-05

Make Them Speak

“Make them speak; mark who speaks.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Hunt · the SparkP2 / 1, 2 decision-bearing1H3
Era & debut

E2 · the Second Tide · Cam 11 (BC)

Grows from

E1-05 Strike the Flash: Strike the Flash that no longer waits for you to flash first. It makes you flash

Grows into

E3-05 Make an Example, where the whole fleet answers the first gun to speak

Intent

“Their guns hide until they speak. Send one they cannot ignore to make them speak, and keep the hunters close to where they will.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions: a Provocateur and a Reaver pack sail, and one flank is believed to hold un-enclosed guns, because the Contact ledger holds a glint there (live or stale).
  • Gain conditions (FIT):
    1. A glinted gun Contact is on that flank.
    2. That flank’s fear is HOT.
    3. Last sortie, the first fire came from that flank.
    4. The Reaver pack is whole.
  • Habit: H3 “They strike first and far.” A shore that shoots eagerly will shoot the goad.
  • Grains: Blackthorn +3 (a Reaver flag goes where the guns are) and Ashwood +1 (a Provocateur flag). No grain weighs against the Hunt line.
  • Score sketch (Blackthorn, H3 active, FIT 3): 3 + 3 + 1 = 7. Strike the Flash scores 1 + 3 − 1 = 3.
Composition
  • lure: the Provocateur wolfpack. It runs its own policy, driving the flank believed exposed, and it closes. Decision-bearing.
  • hunt: the Reaver pack. Decision-bearing.
    • It sails to the fear edge behind the Provocateur’s lane and holds HOLD-UNTIL(flash seen).
    • Once released, it hunts the freshest glinted gun.
  • Approaches: A 1.
Choreography

Single sortie.

  • t0. Both parts leave.
  • t≈12–15. The Reavers stop at the fear edge and idle. A Reaver without a fix is nearly inert, so waiting is also its nature.
  • t≈15–25. The Provocateur drives in and takes fire.
  • If an un-enclosed gun fires, its muzzle flash is seen and a Contact is written. On your fort, that spot jumps at once from twilight to noon (Fleet Memory §8).
  • 3 ticks later. The pack’s sails blanch. It bends toward that gun and fixates on it, ignoring walls and castles.
  • If only enclosed guns answer, fire is drawn but no flash is seen. The pack holds to its hour (about 18 ticks after parking), then goes grey on its own policy: it hunts the freshest fix in its ledger, which may be a ghost.
Signature
  • First time. One stubborn ship comes straight at a wall, soaking shots and never turning. A pack of gunships waits just outside your reach, doing nothing. An open gun fires, and its spot on your fort lights up. The pack’s sails flash pale and it goes straight for that gun.
  • Returning tell (by t≈15). The Hunt root sign: a Reaver pack waits at the fear edge, with a lone durable pusher closing on one flank.
  • The pusher closes (fore-aft), so holding fire on it is not free. Hold, and it breaches (Ships §6).
  • Noise in the East is different. Its loud ship paces, and it can be ignored.
Captains’ independence
  • The Provocateur is fanatical and eats fire. It sags only when nearly sunk.
  • The Reavers have middling resolve but over-commit on a juicy fix. Once hunting, they may drive into the HOT water around the gun. That Committed read is visible, and punishable.
  • If the hunted gun dies, the pack’s errand voids and it leaves the queue (Victory §4b).
  • Flag sunk. The link breaks. Through the slowed net the new flash never reaches the pack, so it hunts the freshest fix it holds locally. It bends toward an older glint, often dead ground.
Counters
  • B · Give it nothing to mark.
    • Enclose the flank. An enclosed gun’s flash is hidden, so it writes Effect only (Fleet Memory §5).
    • Or Camouflage the group.
    • Re-enclose, in Build, the gun that spoke: its fix goes cold and the pack’s errand voids.
    • Piercer and Standard grind the Provocateur down in time.
  • C · Speak where it cannot see.
    • Answer from enclosed guns only. Answer the goad from enclosed guns and hold the exposed ones. The pack waits out its hour, then hunts a ghost.
    • Fire, then relocate. Fire exposed, then relocate that gun in Deploy. Next sortie the pack hammers the old position until it re-observes, because Contact is revised only by contradiction (Fleet Memory §4).
    • Offer a gun. Let an expendable exposed gun, on a flank you can afford, speak first under a second group’s AoR. The pack fixates and rides into it.
    • Decapitate. Sink the flag while the pack waits. It never hears the flash.
If it fails

Succeeded: a hunted fix is destroyed. Failed: the pack is eliminated or routed before it fires on its fix. Countered: the goad drew no fire. The carving has no core, so it is graded on its objective-bearing role, the hunt (§5).

  • Blackthorn (−3) puts its +2 on the cell where it lost the most hulls and carves a Hunt there. If the pack died at the gun, it comes back for that gun with Strike the Flash at the same fix.
  • Ashwood (−2) floods instead.
  • What it learned.
    • Every exposed gun that fired is now a Contact, and the flank’s fear rises.
    • If only enclosed guns answered, the flank is felt but never found: a ghost the Hunt line cannot use, so the Admiral looks elsewhere.
    • A gun re-observed absent after you moved it is one count of H5. At two the dispatch reads “They know we move the guns they find.” E2 has no answer to that yet. From Cam 16 the Hunt line spares a gun it has seen standing, so the unknown stays unknown (§6 H5).
The Carving

Goad stone until it speaks. Mark mouth that speaks; hunters … to mouth.

One of Halyna said, and the other finished: “The first ship came to make our guns cry out.” “And the others came to the cry.”

Real-world root

#13 Beat the grass to startle the snake, in its aimed form: reconnaissance by fire. The hunters take their fix as flash-spotting did in 1915–18 (research §1). This is the Screen-Baiter→Reaver combo of Ships §6.

E2-06

Toss a Brick

“Spend their fire on wood of no worth.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Bleed · the Breath (root)P2 / 1, 2 decision-bearing1H3
Era & debut

E2 · the Second Tide · Cam 12 (BD)

Grows from

— (root of the Bleed). This is the first time the fleet sends a ship out alone to buy a moment for another. Cheapest First spent its shields, but always in front of the cargo they were shielding

Grows into

E3-07 Between Their Breaths, where the crude wait becomes a strike timed to your observed reload · E5-07 The First Ship Carries (reach-back), where the brick is the bearer

Carried whole

as the chain of E5-07

Intent

“Their first fire is their best. Give it wood of no worth, and send what matters while they reload.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions:
    • A cheap wolfpack sails: the one of lowest value, meaning fewest hulls, then lowest queue order. From Cam 11 this is typically a Corsair pair; otherwise it is a pack worn to 1–2 hulls.
    • The brick is never a core. A Bombard with no shields refuses at default intensity (Victory §4a), and the Admiral never overrides refusal.
    • A cargo convoy sails.
    • The fear cell where the fleet lost the most hulls last sortie lies on the cargo’s shortest lane.
  • Gain conditions (FIT):
    1. That cell sank ≥2 hulls last sortie.
    2. The coast behind it is COLD or WARM.
    3. Last sortie’s losses in that cell came in the first ticks after entering it. The fleet’s own hit record shows the fire was front-loaded.
    4. The brick is undamaged.
  • Habit: H3 “They strike first and far.”
  • Grains: Yew +2 (a Skyfall or Sower flag is patient). Blackthorn’s failure bonus (+2 to anything aimed at the cell where it lost the most) often lands here, because the brick’s objective is that cell.
  • Score sketch (Yew, H3 active, FIT 3): 3 + 2 + 1 = 6.
Composition
  • lure: the brick. Decision-bearing.
    • It runs alone into the killing cell with objective draw-fire, and turns out when struck.
    • The Corsair’s sharp crazy-Ivan is exactly the brick’s turn.
  • cargo: the Runner convoy. It sails to the fear edge of the killing cell and holds HOLD-UNTIL(fire drawn on brick). Decision-bearing.
  • Approaches: A 1.
Choreography

Single sortie.

  • t0. Both parts leave.
  • t≈12–15. The cargo stops at the edge of the cell.
  • t≈15–20. The brick goes on alone into the cell.
  • First shell on the brick. It jinks and runs back out through the same water, still drawing fire as it flees.
  • Three ticks later. The cargo’s sails blanch and it drives through the cell. It starts inside the Standard’s ≈4-tick reload (Cannon §4), and deep inside the Marksman’s ≈10 if the Marksman took the bait.
  • If the brick is never fired on, it passes through and takes its own hit-and-run fallback on a seam. The cargo goes grey at its hour.
  • No carved cadence read. E2’s 3-tick lag happens to land inside one reload; it is not aimed there. Aiming is E3’s step (E3-07).
Signature
  • First time. A small, fast ship runs straight into the spot where you sank the most ships last time, alone. Your guns open. It flinches, jinks and flees. While your guns reload, the landing convoy that was sitting just outside your reach comes through the same water.
  • Returning tell (by t≈15). The Bleed root sign: a cheap hull goes in alone while the rest wait at the fear edge.
  • Not to be confused with The Sounding.
    Toss a BrickThe Sounding
    The lone hullcharges into your hottest cellleaves from the launch zone and sweeps
    Its convoyhas already come to the edge of your reachwaits in the launch zone
Captains’ independence
  • The Corsair is skittish. After the first volley it may rout clean to spawn, which suits the fleet: the brick has done its work.
  • The cargo’s captains balk if the killing cell went SCALDING this sortie. So a volley on the brick heavy enough to scald the cell while the brick is still inside turns the landing away. That is this carving’s knife-edge: spend big and the landing balks; spend small and it comes through on your reload.
  • Flag sunk. The link breaks. The cargo leaves the edge at once, grey, on its Runner policy for the softest COLD coast, and may abandon the killing cell for a colder coast altogether.
Counters
  • B · Price the target.
    • Hold fire on a lone cheap hull, and keep your first volley for the cargo.
    • Or cover the killing cell with two groups so that one reload never empties it, with a Suppressor on the cargo’s lane.
  • C · Refuse them the breath.
    • Split the fire. Let one group take the brick while the other holds (fire-control is per group). Then there is no reload window.
    • Scald the cell. Overkill the brick while it is still inside the cell, so the cell goes SCALDING and the cargo balks.
    • Let it run. Let the brick through and accept a chip on a seam. The cargo comes grey at its hour into an AoR that has not fired.
    • Jammer (Cam 11+) makes the brick linger. It is worth it only with a second group ready for the cargo.
If it fails

Failed: the cargo is eliminated or routed before its commit point. Countered: the brick drew no fire.

  • Yew takes Countered at 0, so it waits and tries again later in the battle. On Failed (−3) it turns to Let the Fire Work.
  • Blackthorn aims its next Hunt at the killing cell.
  • What it learned. If you ignored the brick and killed the cargo late, your first fire came in the engagement band, not the reading band. That contradicts H3. Two contradictions in a row deactivate H3 (§6). Patience teaches the fleet that you do not strike first, and this carving loses its habit point.
The Carving

Spend their fire on wood of no worth. When fire falls on worthless … bearer goes.

One of Halyna said, and the other finished: “They sent the least of their ships to take our first fire.” “And the best of them came while we reloaded.”

Real-world root

#17 Toss out a brick to lure a jade (拋磚引玉). The inverse lesson is Drøbak Sound, 9 April 1940: the Blücher led into a silent narrows and was sunk by the fort’s old guns and torpedoes. A fleet that has learned sends a brick first (research §3.23).

E2-07

Let the Fire Work

“Let the fire work.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Patience · the KnotW2 / 1, 2 decision-bearing1H6
Era & debut

E2 · the Second Tide · Cam 10 (AE)

Grows from

E1-06 Wait in the Grey: Wait in the Grey given something to wait for

Grows into

E3-08 Spent or Waiting, where the wait is given a question it can check: is the silent stone spent, or waiting?

Intent

“The stone we struck most is the stone most ready to fall, and fire costs us one small ship where their guns cost us many. Set it burning, stand where they cannot reach, and go when it gives.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions:
    • A Sower sails.
    • One wall Contact has taken the fleet’s hits on ≥2 sorties (the fleet’s own record).
    • A COLD fringe cell lies within one cell of that wall’s approach.
    • A Bombard line sails to stand off (from Cam 13, a Leviathan pair may do this instead).
  • Gain conditions (FIT):
    1. The most-hit wall was hit last sortie too.
    2. The fringe cell has never been struck.
    3. This sortie’s Runner convoys are refusing or thin. With nothing to land, the fleet wears down the wall.
    4. Last sortie the fleet lost hulls and made no breach.
  • Habit: H6 “They mend what we break.” A breach the fleet has watched you mend between sorties is better opened by fire inside the sortie.
  • Grains: Yew +3 (a Sower or Skyfall flag), Willow +2. Against it: Blackthorn −2, Ashwood −2.
  • Score sketch (Yew, H6 active, FIT 3): 3 + 3 + 1 = 7. Wait in the Grey scores 2 + 3 − 1 = 4.
Composition
  • siege, the planter: the Sower. Decision-bearing, because you can shoot it before it plants.
    • It darts to the most-hit wall, plants a burn, and peels off. This is its Firebrand expression, a burn zone that chips walls (Ships §5).
    • A Sower wolfpack that has laid its field has spent its errand and leaves the queue (Victory §4b). Each Sower pack buys one fire.
  • siege, the stand-off: the Bombard line (or Leviathan pair). Decision-bearing, because a parked line is a question: answer it or leave it.
    • It sails to the COLD fringe cell nearest that wall and parks there, silent, facing the wall.
    • It holds HOLD-UNTIL(breach observed on the burning wall).
    • On release it closes to standoff and shells that wall.
  • Approaches: A 1.
Choreography

Single sortie. The objective carries across sorties, because the most-hit wall is the fleet’s running record.

  • t0. Both parts leave.
  • t≈8–12. The Sower is fast and reaches the wall first. It plants and flees.
  • t≈12–15. The Bombards reach the fringe and stop. They do not fire.
  • If the wall is seen to break, the stand-off’s sails blanch 3 ticks later and it closes on the breach.
  • If the wall holds, the stand-off goes grey at its hour (about 18 ticks after parking) and shells the burned wall anyway.
  • If it is fired on while parked, it sidesteps along the fringe to the nearest unstruck cell and keeps the hold. The motion is lateral, so it reads as fear, not nerve.
  • Succeeded: a breach was made. Next sortie, the breach in the ledger draws the Siege and Flood carvings to it. That is where the overcommit-punish bait lives (Fleet Memory §6).
Signature
  • First time. A small, fast ship darts to the wall you have been losing, leaves it burning, and runs. A line of warships comes to the edge of your reach and stops, doing nothing, watching the fire. If the wall breaks, their sails go pale and they come.
  • Returning tell (by t≈15). The Patience root sign: a formation parks in a cold fringe and does not fire, and a fire is left burning on your most-struck wall.
  • Not to be confused with Wait in the Grey. There the loiterers wait for your guns to go quiet. Here they wait for your wall, and they face it.
Captains’ independence
  • The Sower is skittish. Struck on its dart, it may rout without planting. The stand-off then waits for a breach nothing is making, and goes grey at its hour.
  • The stand-off balks out of its fringe only if you turn that cell SCALDING.
  • Flag sunk. The link breaks. The stand-off drops its patience and closes on the burning wall at once, on its own policy. The fire and the guns then come together, sooner than the carving meant.
Counters
  • B · Mend and shield.
    • Put a Stonewright on the burning wall, so it is mended as it burns.
    • Put a Suppressor on that face, so the line that closes cannot finish the wall.
    • Or shoot the Sower on its dart: it is alone and skittish, and a Standard is enough.
  • C · Use its stillness.
    • Crawl onto the parked line. Crawl a group toward it. It believes its cell is out of your reach, and it will not move while it waits. Hold fire until the AoR has arrived, then alpha the stillest target of the sortie.
    • Triage. Let a wall you do not need burn. The fleet spends a sortie watching a wall that does not matter, and if that wall breaks, the ring behind it is a kill-box.
    • Hold-feint the fringe. In Deploy, grow a silent group’s AoR over the fringe with a Spotter. The fleet still reads the fringe COLD, since it has never been struck there, and parks inside your hold-feint.
If it fails

Failed: the stand-off is eliminated or routed. A sortie with no breach and no loss is ungraded (MEMORY 0).

  • Yew (−3) turns to Toss a Brick or Wait in the Grey.
  • Willow (−5) turns to The Sounding.
  • What it learned.
    • A fringe where the parked line was struck is no longer COLD, so gain 2 and the precondition both fail there.
    • A breach it made and then sees walled over next sortie is one count of H6. A burned wall healed before it opened is invisible: the fleet reads blows, not HP (§6).
    • The most-hit wall stays the most-hit wall. The fleet keeps coming back to it until you give it a better one (Fleet Memory §4).
The Carving

Set fire on stone most struck. Stand in grey; let fire work … go when stone gives.

One of Halyna said, and the other finished: “They did not come to break the wall that day.” “They came to watch it burn.”

Real-world root

#9 Watch the fire burning across the river (隔岸觀火). In 207 Cao Cao withdrew rather than press the Yuan brothers. They fled to Gongsun Kang, and he killed them (research §1).

E2-08

Grey Before the Sails

“Grey before the landing.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Mask · the Smoothed Cut (root)W3 / 1, 3 decision-bearing1H7
Era & debut

E2 · the Second Tide · Cam 13 (BE)

Grows from

— (root of the Mask). This is the first time the fleet hides anything

Grows into

E4-08 Thick and Hollow (hide the real cargo by looking thin) · E5-03 Fortitude (smoke that promises sails that never come)

Intent

“They kill what they can see. Lay grey across the road for the bearer to walk in, and send the swift one at the same hour to be seen beside it.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions:
    • A Sower sails.
    • A cargo convoy sails.
    • The cargo’s lowest-fear lane crosses or skirts one HOT cell in the reading band.
  • Gain conditions (FIT):
    1. That HOT cell is the only one on the lane.
    2. The objective coast is COLD or WARM.
    3. A Corsair wolfpack sails: a second thing for the player to look at.
    4. The fear edge before the HOT cell lies inside the Sower’s dart, so it can lay before it is struck.
  • Habit: H7 “They kill our bearers first.” The carving hides the bearer.
  • Grains: Twistbough +2 (a Corsair flag). Against it: Ironbark −2 and Heartoak −2, which will not hide.
  • Score sketch (Twistbough, H7 active, FIT 3): 3 + 2 + 1 = 6.
Composition
  • screen: the Sower, laying smoke. Its Fogbank expression (Ships §5): smoke that occludes LOS and drops your accuracy through it.
    • It darts across the lane at the HOT cell’s edge, lays grey, and peels off.
    • Counted decision-bearing: you have to kill it before it lays.
    • A Sower wolfpack that has laid its field leaves the queue (Victory §4b). A Sower riding inside a convoy stays with it.
  • cargo: the convoy. It sails to the fear edge before the HOT cell and holds HOLD-UNTIL(smoke laid). Decision-bearing.
  • strike: a Corsair wolfpack, with no link. Decision-bearing.
    • It sails on a carved launch offset, timed to reach the smoke’s edge at the predicted laying tick + 3.
    • The prediction is the lane’s length at Sower speed, worked out from believed fear only.
    • It then darts at the wall beside the cargo’s landing.
    • This is the Second Tide’s guess at an hour. By the Joined Tide the guess becomes a true WITH (E4-01).
  • Approaches: A 1.
Choreography

Single sortie.

  • t0. The Sower and the convoy leave. The Corsair leaves at its offset.
  • t≈8–12. Grey lands across the lane before any sail is near it: the Mask root sign.
  • t≈12–15. The convoy is at the edge.
  • 3 ticks after the smoke. The convoy’s sails blanch and it goes into the grey.
    • The smoke must outlast the convoy’s crossing of one cell (8–13 ticks) plus the lag.
    • Smoke duration is a Sower stat that Ships owns. Starting value 16 ticks, range 12–24, settled in greybox so that a punctual convoy stays in the grey for the whole HOT cell and a convoy 4 ticks late exits into clear water (§24).
  • The Corsair arrives at its given hour beside the grey, darting at the wall.
    • If the smoke was on time, the two come together.
    • If the smoke was late or never came, the raider is early and alone, in clear water.
Signature
  • First time. A small ship races ahead and throws a bank of grey smoke across the approach before anything else arrives. A convoy vanishes into the grey, and in the same moment a fast raider appears beside it, darting at your wall.
  • Returning tell (by t≈12). The Mask root sign: smoke before the sails.
    • The convoy that waited for the smoke blanches.
    • The raider does not, because it came on its own hour. So if the smoke is late, the raider is early.
Captains’ independence
  • The Sower is skittish. Struck on its dart, it may rout before laying, and then there is no grey: the convoy goes grey-sailed at its hour into clear water.
  • The Corsair is the independent part. It keeps its hour whatever happens to the smoke, and it jinks harder than any other lineage.
  • The cargo’s captains still balk at SCALDING. Grey does not cool a cell the fleet already fears.
  • Flag sunk before the smoke. The link breaks. The convoy leaves the edge at once on its Runner policy for the softest COLD coast, out of the grey lane entirely.
Counters
  • B · See through it, or shoot through it.
    • Flare the smoke: the pulse strips the grey.
    • Saturation on the smoke’s exit: light damage to everything in a sub-zone, with no sight needed.
    • Pre-task an AoR onto the exit at Deploy, so nothing needs deciding in the Fight. The cargo must come out of the grey where the coast is.
  • C · Kill the grey, or ignore the raider.
    • Kill the Sower on its dart. It is alone, early and skittish. The convoy waits for grey that never comes, and the raider arrives alone on its own hour.
    • Ignore the raider. A wolfpack carries no payload and cannot win alone (Cannon §5, the counter-chain). Put your fire into the grey, where the bearer must be.
    • Decapitate early. Sinking the flag before the smoke lands pulls the convoy off the grey lane.
If it fails

Failed: the cargo is eliminated or routed before its commit point.

  • Twistbough (Failed −3; Succeeded −2, “never twice”) changes its trick either way, back to Noise in the East.
  • What it learned.
    • Fire poured into the grey writes fear on that lane and turns it HOT, so the next Mask carving needs another lane.
    • A Sower lost on its dart thins the only lineage that makes Mask possible. With no Sower in the queue, there is no grey.
The Carving

Grey before landing. Bearer walks in grey; swift one keeps … same hour.

One of Halyna said, and the other finished: “The grey came first, and the ships came inside it.” “The swift one came at the hour it was told, whether the grey was there or no.”

Real-world root

#20 Muddy the water to catch the fish (混水摸魚). At Zeebrugge, 23 April 1918, smoke covered the blockships while the assault on the Mole drew the defenders’ fire. A wind shift then thinned the smoke, which is why a smoke carving must be able to fail legibly (research §3.24).

19E3 · THE TIDE OF REMEMBERING (Ralenthae / Ra’lenthae)

Cam 15–22 · two links, one hinge (from Cam 16), 2 habits, verification · “they’re checking now”

“The young wood said one word at our wall until it drowned. This wood says a sentence, and in the middle of the sentence it stops, and looks.”

Halyna, one beginning and the other finishing, in the vault of kept wrecks

The growing. Ralenthae is the root-tide. Its wood grew in groves that had known, hull by hull and through the roots, the wrecks of two Tides before it, and it holds a sentence with a memory in it. The Hasty Tide came each hull alone. The Second Tide learned to wait upon one other hull. This wood remembers what it struck, what struck it, and how long the Rivenmen’s guns take to draw breath, and it will not trust a quiet it has not tested. The lookouts’ phrase for it became the Rivenmen’s: they came to see whether the gun was still there.

How organised it is.

E2 · Second TideE3 · RememberingE4 · Joined Tide
Links per carving123
Hinges per carving01 (from Cam 16)1
Link lag, flag alive3 ticks2 ticks1 tick
Verificationnonethe look+ anticipation
Its own historyMEMORY, the grain’s failure reaction, no immediate repeat of a Failed carving (§5)+ a Countered branch named on every hinge; the breath registersame
Habits active123
Flag sunklinks breaklinks break (formations take fallbacks on local beliefs); every later carving this battle carves with one link fewerlinks become clocks

The one new capability family: the memory clause (unlocks Cam 16). §13 counts it as one family. It has three parts, and they turn together because they are one idea: the wood remembers, so it checks.

  1. Verification (“look before you trust”).
    • A belief is doubtful when it rests on memory the present has not confirmed:
      • (a) a fear cell that fell from HOT/SCALDING to COLD/WARM by decay rather than by passage (Fleet Memory §9: “a region gone quiet gets probed, not trusted”);
      • (b) a glinted gun not re-seen since its glint;
      • (c) a recorded breach not re-seen since it was made.
    • From Cam 16 no strike or cargo part commits on a doubtful objective blind. The carving sends a looker ahead (the Sounding line), or it ties the commit to a look with a HOLD-UNTIL or a hinge.
    • A look is line of sight re-observing one ledger entry, by a Recon or a lead hull. It adds one event to the link vocabulary (§4): seen(X), meaning LOS has confirmed or contradicted ledger entry X.
    LAW · THE LOOK IS A PRESENCE

    Every look is a hull slowing and skirting at the edge of its sight range toward the thing it doubts. No look is silent, and no look reads truth. A look at an enclosed gun sees wall. A Stonewright repair is invisible to it, because the fleet reads no HP. It looks only at what it means to use, so hidden breaches and ghosts elsewhere keep working (Fleet Memory §4, §6). The blunt answer to every look is to deny the sightline: walls, Camouflage, or killing the looker.

  2. The hinge. Each carving may hold at most one: IF(e, window) → A | B. The window is at least 6 ticks, both branches are carved before launch, and the signature shows the window. One stratagem runs per sortie, so a sortie holds at most one hinge. That gives one deliberate choice of tick per sortie, the right dose for the first era of choices. It is also where Command Time first pays: a 6-tick window lasts 6 s at full speed and up to 144 s at the 1/24 floor while the player holds a group.
  3. A history with branches. The Heartwood has graded its own carvings since the Second Tide (§5 step 3: the MEMORY term, the grain’s failure reaction, no immediate repeat of a Failed carving; CALL-OUT 23). With the hinge, each carving now names the branch that grades Countered.
    • So each grain’s failure reaction now turns on a choice the player made on a tick (§7): Ironbark comes again heavier, Willow switches (Countered −3), Heartoak returns by the same road, Yew waits (Countered 0), Silverbark learns fastest (Countered −4).
    • The fleet keeps one new integer register of its own measuring, the breath of a cell (E3-07) (§14). What its looks find (E3-01, E3-02, E3-08) lives in the Contact ledger it already keeps.
    • Nothing retires yet. Half-life belongs to the Tide that Learned Deceit.

Two dials turn around the capability. Links rise to two, and habits active rise to two: the two highest counts, with ties going to the lower habit number. The link lag also falls to 2 ticks.

Cam 15 is a threshold, not a turn. Cam 15 (CE) turns piece layer C, and pieces and cognition never turn together (Build §5; §13), so the memory clause waits for Cam 16 (DE). Cam 15 fields the era’s contacts (C 5–6) and debuts E3-02 on the Second Tide’s capability: one link, one habit and no look. The look and the hinge then unlock on E3-01’s debut battle at Cam 16, so nothing looks before E3-01 does. So The Same Stone meets the player twice, blind at Cam 15 and looking once E3-01 has shown the look. That difference is the whole era’s lesson in one carving.

Its characteristic error: it believes what it has measured. E1 repeats what fails. E2 straggles, because its links arrive late. E3 trusts its own counts:

  • its blows on a stone;
  • the breath of a battery;
  • the silence an eye came back through;
  • the silent stone its eye saw standing, or saw gone.

Every count is honest, and every count can be taught. This is the error the player learns to farm. Feed the fleet a walled-over breach, a slow breath, a silence, or a battery its eye reads as wall, and it will build a sentence on it.

What the player is learning to read.

  • The look. A hull sent to see, not to shoot: a lone eye down one lane, a line pausing at the edge of sight before its first shell, a scout drifting past an open gun it does not fire on.
  • The listening hold. Formations stopped in open water, tight and full, waiting on another’s deed. E2 showed the first answering sail. E3 shows chains of them (siege, then raiders, then bearers) and asks who is waiting for whom?
  • The hinge window. At most once a sortie, the fleet will go one of two ways depending on what the player does in a stretch of ticks they can see coming. The player picks the branch.
  • Memory across sorties. The same stone, the same breath, the gun it has seen standing. The dispatch (§9c) still carries at most one habit line per boundary, but now two habits can stand active at once: “They marked that the north holds its fire.”

Lineages on the board.

  • Everything from the first two Tides remains: Bombard, Runner (+Swarm), Recon, Reaver, Sapper troops, Bulwark, Skyfall and bombers, Provocateur, Sower, Corsair, Quartermaster, and the common Leviathan.
  • The era’s own lineages each debut on a battle with nothing else new (CALL-OUT 11):

Chirurgeon-FleetCam 17 (ABC)First sails idle, as a rider on its own lineage policy; first carved into a part by Two Roads to One Stone’s lead column (E3-04, Cam 18)
Aegis and Linebreaker troopsCam 17 (ABC), on a battle of their ownThe Same Stone’s landings (E3-02)

WraithCam 19 (ABE), on a later battle than E3-06’s debutOpen Roads, Hidden March, as the hidden march (E3-06)

Every one debuts on a campaign that carries Layer B (§13 LAW · BREADTH ENTERS WITH LAYER B). Cam 15, 16 and 20–22 debut no lineage.

Budget (Campaign §4; Victory §3).

  • Wide carvings (W) carry ≤1 link and no hinge, and only they may spend 5–6 contacts (E3-05; E3-02 while it is blind).
  • A formation with no part takes idle (pin) and stays glanceable.

Kit that answers them. Every counter below uses only kit the player holds by Cam 15:

  • six of the eight cannons: Standard, Suppressor, Piercer, Marksman, Splasher and Saturation (the Interdictor and Breaker arrive with the Breacher at Cam 23);
  • all seven towers (the Powder Store from Cam 13, the Rally Flag from Cam 15);
  • the three verbs;
  • Build and Deploy: walls, relocation and enclosure.

The book. 23 carvings are now cumulative (7 + 8 + 8). An E3 form that is a candidate pushes its E2 ancestor down by 1 (LINE, §5). One carving debuts per battle, never on a campaign’s first battle and never beside another new thing.

Timing follows §3’s conventions. The forward hold and its latest tick (≈ 18 + p) exist for exactly these chains: every E3 chain uses them to fit the 70-tick backstop. Links are counted per carved condition, not per listener (§4 LAW · COUNTING LINKS), so one AFTER(e) may wake several formations (E3-05). The 12-tick floor (§11) is met by hold geometry, not by delay: one fear cell of separation gives ≈13 ticks.

The Knowing at this Tide (§9b).

  • Knowings are now told in sentences, with doubtful words bracketed and the memory clause clear.
  • Only one of Halyna touches the grain, Rhyna or Halvard, and what one knows, the other knows. They tell it by turns: one begins the sentence and the other finishes it (§9b). The wood gives its knowing whole to no single mind.
  • Seren sets the Knowing down and brackets what she will not swear to.
  • The first verification the player meets, from Cam 16, unlocks The Wreck That Went Home (Legends V.3).
  • One small lexicon runs through the carvings below, so the generator and the tales speak alike:
