E3-01

Sounding and Mass

“Hold; hear the sounding; go where it is quiet.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Sounding · the Lone Stroke (one hull out at least 6 ticks ahead)P3–4 / 22, one a hingeH1, H3
Era & debut

E3 · the Tide of Remembering · Cam 16 (DE). It is the first carving with a hinge and the first look, and nothing else is new on its battle

Grows from

E2-02 The Sounding

Grows into

E4-02 Ask the Silent Water (one cell asked one question) · E4-01 Three Roads, One Hour (the eye replaced by memory: the fleet counts your walks instead of sending a hull)

The growth

This is the Sounding grown a second head. In E2-02 the convoy waited for the eye to cross the reading band, then went on its own policy, having learned only now. Here the body listens: what happens to the eye chooses its road.

Intent

“Two roads look alike to our dread, and one of them went quiet by forgetting, not by passage. Send the eye down the quiet one. If it is struck, that road is watched: go by the other. If it goes free, follow it.” The Heartwood pays one Recon to turn a doubt into a fact before it commits the bearers.

Chosen when

Preconditions:

  • Two lanes share a fork cell (the last cell both pass through) and then part to two coasts. The coasts are on the two approaches when the fork lies in the sea between them, or else on the same approach.
  • A Recon sails.
  • A cargo core sails.

FIT gain conditions:

  1. The two lanes’ summed fear differs by ≤1: the dread cannot choose.
  2. A cell on one lane is doubtful, having fallen from HOT/SCALDING to COLD/WARM by decay. That lane becomes lane A, the eye’s.
  3. The fork cell is COLD.
  4. A mass is worth protecting: the cargo is a shared convoy (two Runner cores), or a Skyfall sails to follow it.

Also:

  • HABIT: H1 (a quarter that holds its fire) and H3 (you strike first and far, so the eye will be answered).
  • Grain: Willow +3. Silverbark doubles the HABIT term.
Composition
PartRoleLineage · formationContacts
eyeprobe; decision-bearing, since its fate is the hingea lone Recon: the convoy’s rider sent ahead, or a Recon picket1
main bodycargo, carries the hingeRunner convoy in column behind a lead Bulwark; a shared convoy (2 Runners, 7 shields) when one sails1
followercargo, optionalSkyfall battlegroup, ring0–1
idlepinBombard line on its own lowest-fear lane0–1
  • Decision-bearing contacts: 3.
  • Links: 2, namely the hinge and the follower’s AFTER(main body crossed out of the fork cell).
  • Approaches: 2.
Choreography

(one sortie).

  • t 0. The eye leaves alone down lane A. The main body leaves at t 6 (the root sign’s gap), sails tight to the fork cell (p ≈ 13) and stops dead. The follower stays at its launch point with its deck shut.
  • The hinge: IF(fire drawn on the eye before the window closes) → lane B : lane A.
    • The window closes 6 ticks after the eye enters the engagement band, at about t 29 at Recon speed. A faster eye is carved to skirt its first band cell until those 6 ticks have run.
    • Fire on the eye earlier, in the reading band, counts: the branch is B.
  • Branch B. “Fire drawn” reaches the main body 2 ticks after the eye is struck. Its sails blanch and it takes lane B.
  • Branch A. If the window closes unfired, the main body wakes at t 31, which is its latest tick (18 + 13), and follows the eye’s wake.
  • The follower opens its deck AFTER(main body crossed out of the fork cell), a crossed cell event on whichever lane it took (§4). Its bombers go for the castle nearest the chosen coast.
  • Spacing. On lane A the main body enters the band about 18 ticks after the eye. On lane B it faces another group, or the same group at ≥12 ticks. A struck eye is fleeing, so it is not a second arrival.
Signature
  • First time. A single grey hull runs ahead down one lane. Behind it a tight convoy sails one cell out and stops in open water, motionless, sails full. Then the eye is struck, or it slips through, and the convoy’s sails blanch all at once and it goes: down the other lane if you hit the eye, after the eye if you did not.
  • Returning tell, reading band. A lone Recon at least 6 ticks ahead, and a tight convoy halting at a fork rather than in the launch zone. The E2 Sounding waited at home and never turned away.
  • Not a refusal. A refusing core also sits still, but it is thin. This one is tight, because it believes it is about to know.
Captains’ independence
  • The eye. It is skittish. Struck, it swerves off and runs for the edge of its sight range. It never commits into a core, so it usually survives and what it saw usually gets home.
  • A main body under fire at the fork does not hold. It balks off its hold early, onto whichever branch lane is colder in its own fear map. The tell is the hold breaking with no answering sail.
  • On the chosen lane, a cell that went SCALDING this sortie makes the column balk at its edge and take its fallback: the softest COLD coast it believes.
  • Flag sunk mid-stratagem. Links break. A main body still at the fork sails at once on its fallback and its own fear map; if the eye’s answer has not yet propagated, it sinks with the heart. The follower stays deck-shut to its latest tick, then launches at the castle its own policy picks.
  • After a decapitation this battle, the carving keeps the hinge and drops the follower.
Counters
  • B Put a group on each lane’s coast and answer whichever road they take. Splasher at both beaches for the landing. Standard or Marksman on the eye is fine too: killing it is fire drawn, so they go to B, and you are there.
  • B Triage. If one coast guards only an optional castle, cover the other and let that one take the landing.
  • C Choose their road.
    • Hold fire on the lane you want them on. The eye goes free, the body follows it into your silent battery, and the alpha falls on a tight column in your AoR centre.
    • Or speak early, with a gun you can spare, on the lane you want them off, and wait for them on the other.
    • In Command Time the choice is a tick. Strike the eye at t 28 and they turn. Strike it at t 30 and the window has shut: they are already coming.
  • C Crawl an AoR onto the fork cell (Rally Flag). A formation that holds is a formation that sits.
  • C Decapitation. If the flag rides in the main body, sink it at the fork and the body goes blind on its own map.
If it fails
  • Grades.
    • Succeeded: the cargo landed.
    • Failed: the cargo was eliminated or routed before it committed.
    • Countered: the lane it chose turned HOT or SCALDING after it woke; the quiet was held fire.
  • A Countered sounding is also an H1 observation of that quarter, so the next carving distrusts the same silence.
  • By grain.
    • Willow switches at the first failure (Failed −5, Countered −3). It falls back to the E2 Sounding or to the Patience line.
    • Ironbark re-carves it heavier.
    • Blackthorn is drawn (+2) to whatever carving aims at the cell where the column died.
  • Learned. The fire the eye drew writes fear, and glints for any exposed gun, on lane A. A held battery that sprang is now HOT in the grid.
The Carving

Known from the column’s wreck-wood, or the Standard-Bearer’s if it sank, by Rhyna, whose hand was on it; Halvard stood at the tide-line and knew it as she knew it. They told it by turns, one beginning and the other finishing.

“Where the dread has [cooled?], trust it not. Send one eye before, and let the body wait where the roads part. If the eye is struck, go by the other road; if the eye goes free, go in its wake.”

Real-world root

Midway 1942: the side that scouted deeper chose where to strike, and the Tone’s late scout cost the Japanese the choice. Reconnaissance in force (Dieppe 1942) is the paid-for look.

E3-02

The Same Stone

“The same stone, each morrow.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Siege · the Mute Square (Bombards line abreast on the same wall, sortie after sortie)W while blind (one link); P once it carries the hinge3–4 / 1–21 while blind; 2, one a hinge, once the look is unlockedH6, H11
Era & debut

E3 · the Tide of Remembering · Cam 15 (CE), without a hinge; it gains its look on E3-01’s debut battle (Cam 16), the war’s first look

Grows from

E1-02 Break the First Wall

Grows into

E4-04 When the Stone Gives

The growth

This is Break the First Wall with a memory. The Hasty Tide fixed on whatever wall it touched first and had forgotten it by the next day. This wood counts its blows and comes back. E4-04 will teach the landing to wait for the breach. Here it only waits for the first blow.

Intent

“We have struck that stone more than any other, and every blow still in it is ours. Go to it again each morrow, with the chisel as well as the hammer, until it gives. If they have laid it new, go to its sister.”

Chosen when

Preconditions:

  • At least one wall Contact carries the fleet’s own blows.
  • The blow count is the number of connecting shots on a wall section, taken from the fleet’s record of its own fire (Contact channel). It never reads wall HP, so a Stonewright repair it cannot see leaves the count unchanged.

FIT gain conditions:

  1. A stone carries blows from at least two earlier sorties.
  2. A Bombard line sails.
  3. A Runner carrying Sappers sails (from Cam 17 it may also carry Aegis and Linebreakers).
  4. The stone’s standoff cell is COLD or WARM.

Also:

  • HABIT: H6 (Mender). With H6 active, it skips any stone it has seen re-laid and carves the most-struck stone it has not. H11 (Kill-Box): with H11 active, it skips any stone whose breach has already sprung a kill-box on the fleet, and carves its sister (the next-most-struck stone on the same face) instead.
  • Grain: Ironbark +3; on failure it re-carves heavier. Yew +1.
Composition
PartRoleLineage · formationContacts
siegesiege; carries the hinge once the look is unlockedBombard line abreast (core: breach; 2 shields)1
second siegesiege, optional (Ironbark heavier, or a 6-contact sortie)a second Bombard line, or a common Leviathan pair, on the same face0–1
landingcargoRunner convoy with a troop wave: Sappers, and from Cam 17 Aegis in front and Linebreakers behind. The troops’ target is the carved stone, not troop A* over true wall HP1
idlepinthe restrest
  • Decision-bearing contacts: 2–3 (the siege, a line working a wall; the landing; and the second siege if it sails). Any core-bearing idle formation beyond four is held (§11).
  • Links: blind, it has 1, the landing’s HOLD-UNTIL(stone struck). Once the look is unlocked it has 2, adding the siege’s hinge.
Choreography
  • Across sorties.
    • Whatever carving runs, the fleet’s shells land somewhere, and the most-struck stone accrues blows.
    • The Same Stone returns there each sortie until a breach is observed (Succeeded).
    • After that, the line’s next form, When the Stone Gives, waits for Cam 24; until then the book offers other carvings.
  • Within the sortie.
    • t 0. The siege sails its lane to standoff before the stone, arriving at about t 23. The landing sails behind it and holds a cell short of the band, at the edge of the fire (p ≈ 14, latest tick ≈32).
    • The first blow. The first shell to connect with the stone (a Contact refresh) reaches the landing 2 ticks later. Its sails blanch, it runs in, and it lands its wave at that very section. It enters the band about 13 ticks after the siege, so the spacing floor is met by the hold’s position.
    • The look (from E3-01’s debut battle). IF(seen(the stone's recorded breach) contradicts it, within 6 ticks of the stone entering the siege lead's sight) → shift fire to its sister (the next-most-struck stone on the same face) : strike the stone.
      • A stone that was only cracked cannot be seen mended, because the fleet reads no HP. So the look fires only on a breach that has been walled over.
      • The landing’s wake keys to whichever stone is struck first, so it follows the switch.
  • Blind (Cam 15, and Cam 16 before E3-01’s battle). With no look, it strikes a re-laid breach anyway. H6 needs two sightings to go active, so the fleet returns at least twice to a stone the player keeps mending before it learns.
Signature
  • First time. The same Bombard line comes back to the wall it cracked last time. It ignores walls that look softer, lines up abreast and shells. A troop convoy waits at the edge of your fire until the first shell lands, then runs in and puts its troops ashore at that very section.
  • Returning tell. You can name the stone before a shot is fired. It is the wall you watched crack most (Fight §3B), and the line’s lane bends toward it from the launch zone.
  • Once the look is unlocked, add it. The line slows at the edge of sight before its first shell, then either opens fire or slides one section along.
Captains’ independence
  • The Bombards are avoiders. A standoff cell that went SCALDING this sortie makes the line balk at the cell edge. It takes its fallback: shell the nearest wall Contact from the coldest standoff it can find, often a neighbour of the stone.
  • Troops ashore do not read fear. Sappers sprint and die on the wall, Linebreakers leave their checkerboard of gaps, and Aegis turn their faces to the heaviest fire.
  • Flag sunk. Links break. A landing still holding goes at once for the softest COLD coast, so hammer and chisel part company. The siege line keeps working the stone on its own policy.
  • After a decapitation, the carving drops the look and keeps the landing’s link.
Counters
  • B Mend and thicken in Build: new pieces behind the stone, and a Stonewright on its group.
    • Suppressor on the line’s standoff, so its first blow comes late and so does the landing.
    • Splasher on the troop clump at the section.
    • Switch the section’s group to Land when the wave beaches (domain).
  • B Triage. If the stone guards an optional castle, let it take the siege and spend your fire elsewhere.
  • C Let it fall into a kill-box: a second ring behind the stone, with an AoR centred on the gap (overcommit-punish, Fleet Memory §6). The wave floods into your centre. Twice sprung, it teaches H11 (§6): from then the Same Stone goes to the sister stone, so build the next box behind the sister, or let the old box stand empty and cheap.
  • C Teach it a mender.
    • Re-lay the breach twice so H6 goes active. From then on it goes to the stone it has not seen mended, so choose which one: make your thickest wall the next-most-struck.
    • Or heal a cracked stone with the Stonewright in silence. It counts blows, not strength, and keeps coming to a wall that is whole.
If it fails
  • Grades.
    • Succeeded: a breach was observed at the stone.
    • Failed: the siege line was eliminated or routed before standoff.
    • Countered: the look found the stone re-laid.
  • By grain.
    • Ironbark (Failed +1) re-carves heavier at the same stone, with two lines or a Leviathan: the characteristic sunk cost.
    • Yew (Countered 0) comes back to it later.
    • Willow and Twistbough switch lines.
  • Learned. Every re-laid breach it sees is an H6 count. Blows persist. A stone that became a kill-box is SCALDING in the grid, so lanes to it cost more next time.
The Carving

Known from a Bombard’s wreck-wood by Halvard, whose hand was on it; Rhyna knew it as he knew it. They told it by turns, one beginning and the other finishing.

“Count our blows on the mute stone. Go each morrow to the stone that has taken most, hammer and chisel together, until it gives. Where we broke it and it stands [new-laid?], go to its sister.”

Real-world root

Rochester 1215: King John’s engines failed against the keep, so he mined one corner until it fell. The corner was rebuilt round, and that was the defender’s answer.

E3-03

Feigned Flight

“Show your stern; turn at the stone.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Lure · the Turned Stern (a formation turning aft while holding its shape)P3 / 1–22, one a hingeH3, H4
Era & debut

E3 · the Tide of Remembering · Cam 17, on a battle apart from the Chirurgeon-Fleet’s and the Aegis and Linebreakers’ debuts

Grows from

E1-07 Home When Wounded

Grows into

E4-05 Along the Shore, Home by the Far Road · E5-01 The Closing Hand (its bending middle, joined to the Flood’s three roads)

The growth

This is Home When Wounded learned as a trick. The Hasty Tide’s flight was real: first loss, scatter, home. At Hastings a real rout drew the defenders down, and the attackers then did the same thing twice on purpose. This wood has known the wrecks of the hulls that were chased. Its sterns lie about its nerve, and only about that. The order in the wood is plain (“show your stern”), and the seam is the spacing. Deceits that make many true orders into one false picture belong to the Tide that Learned Deceit.

Intent

“When our wood runs, their stone speaks after it, and stone that speaks can be found. Run, but keep the line; let them lean out to strike our sterns. Where they lean, the hunters wait. If they will not lean, turn and come again.”

Chosen when

Preconditions:

  • A Bombard line sails.
  • A Reaver pack sails.
  • The lure’s flank has a fear-edge cell for the hunters to wait in.

FIT gain conditions:

  1. The lure’s flank is believed to hold exposed guns: glints on the ledger within one cell of its lane.
  2. Last sortie, withdrawing hulls were struck beyond the engagement band on that flank. The fleet saw you pursue.
  3. The Reaver pack has no standing fix to hunt elsewhere.
  4. The lure’s wall carries fleet blows, so a line that turns back in has a stone to return to.

Also:

  • HABIT: H3 (early fire) and H4 (a reach that chases).
  • Grain: Twistbough +2; after a success it will not run the trick twice in a row. Blackthorn +1.
Composition
PartRoleLineage · formationContacts
the flightlure; carries the hingeBombard line abreast1
huntershuntReaver pack holding at the flank’s fear edge1
second huntershunt, optionalCorsair wolfpack on the same wake: raids the seam beside the glinted gun0–1
idlepinthe restrest
  • Decision-bearing contacts: 3.
  • Links: 2, namely the lure’s hinge and the hunters’ AFTER(glint written). The Corsairs listen to the same condition.
Choreography

(one sortie).

  • t 0. The lure sails its lane toward its wall. The hunters sail to the fear edge on that flank, at the band’s outer edge (p ≈ 23, latest tick ≈41), and wait there, bows toward the fort: the Hunt root sign.
  • The flight. The first of the lure’s hulls to be struck inside the engagement band, at about t 25, turns the whole line aft. It keeps its spacing and sails at line speed, not a rout’s sprint, back one fear cell to the turning cell: “the stone”, the last reading-band cell it crossed.
  • The hinge: IF(a new glint is written on the lure's flank while it flies to the turning cell) → the hunters go, and the lure goes on home : at the turning cell, the lure turns back in.
    • The window is the flight, about 13 ticks and never under 6.
  • Hunters’ wake. A new glint is an exposed gun’s flash seen by any fleeing hull. It reaches the pack 2 ticks later; its sails blanch and it drives on the freshest glinted gun, about one cell of approach. The Corsairs, if any, raid the neighbouring seam on the same wake.
  • Unanswered. If the window closes with no glint, the line turns back in at the stone (the feint’s own confession) and resumes shelling. The hunters hold to their latest tick, then take their fallback.
  • Spacing. A lure in flight is leaving, not arriving. In the unanswered branch, its second arrival at the band (about t 50) comes more than 12 ticks after its first.
Signature
  • First time. A Bombard line takes a few hits and turns for home, and you get a free shot at its sterns. But it does not scatter: it keeps its spacing and stays abreast. And a pack of hunters is sitting still at the edge of your fire, on that same flank.
  • Returning tell. A retreat that keeps its shape (a true rout scatters; E1’s did), plus hunters parked at the fear edge.
  • The moment of truth is the turning cell. If you did not bite, the line turns and comes back. A real rout never does.
Captains’ independence
  • A flight can become real. A Bombard whose resolve falls below its rout threshold leaves the line and sprints for spawn. Heavy enough fire turns the feint into a cascade (Ships §4): the line breaks shape and the tell reverses.
  • Reavers over-commit on a juicy fix and may drive deep.
  • Flag sunk. Links break. The flight ends where it stands, and the line resumes standoff shelling from there. The hunters take only fixes they see themselves.
  • After a decapitation, the carving drops the hunters’ wake. The flight still turns back at the stone: a lure with no hunt behind it.
Counters
  • B Enclose that flank’s guns, or give their group Camouflage. Pursuit fire from them writes no glint, the hunters never wake, and you kill fleeing Bombards for free.
  • B Do not pursue past the band. Let the flight go (the golden bridge); the line comes back at the stone into guns that have reloaded.
  • C Feed the hunters a glint on purpose, from a gun whose AoR centre lies across the pack’s lane in. The hunters commit into a bright core (Fight §5’s first eye-seizing event), and your held alpha falls on them.
  • C Break it for real. Sustained fire from enclosed guns on the fleeing line cascades it, and the feint becomes the rout it pretended to be.
  • C Pursue from the exposed gun, then relocate that gun in the next Deploy. This sortie’s hunters strike it; the next sortie’s strike a ghost. That is also an H5 count, and at two counts the Hunt line starts sparing the guns it has seen standing (§6 H5).
If it fails
  • Grades.
    • Succeeded: a hunted gun was destroyed.
    • Failed: the lure and the hunters were eliminated or routed before they committed.
    • Countered: the window closed unanswered.
  • By grain.
    • Twistbough, after a success, takes −2 against a repeat (“never twice”); Failed is −3. It moves to the Feint line.
    • Blackthorn is drawn (+2) to any carving aimed at the flank that killed its pack.
  • Learned. The glints it gets persist. If you pursued only from enclosed guns, it learns fear but no Contacts: the flank goes HOT, and the next lure picks another flank.
The Carving

Known from a Reaver’s wreck-wood by Rhyna, whose hand was on it; Halvard, at the far end of the vault, knew it as she did. They told it by turns, one beginning and the other finishing.

“They lean after wood that flees; we have seen it. Bend back as the bough bends and keep your line. Where stone speaks in the leaning, loose the hunters; if none speaks, spring back at the [marked?] stone.”

Real-world root

Hastings 1066: a real Breton rout drew the English off the ridge, and the Normans then feigned flight twice on purpose. Salamis 480 BC: the Greek line backed water before it rammed.

E3-04

Two Roads to One Stone

“Two roads to one stone.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Screen · the Bar Before (shields a cell ahead of the cargo, tight, aspect locked on one hot cell)P2 (+0–2 pins) / 22H7, H2
Era & debut

E3 · the Tide of Remembering · Cam 18, on a battle apart from the Infiltrator’s debut

Grows from

E2-03 Cold Shadow · E2-01 Run the Batteries

Grows into

E4-03 The Great Hull and the Kept Hand

Re-cut

at Cam 25, battle 2, with its second column WITH the first instead of AFTER it: the carrier of the Joined Tide’s capability (§13, §20)

The growth

This is Cold Shadow doubled and aimed. The Second Tide screened one bearer on one road, and ran the batteries straight. This wood sends two screened columns by two roads at one stretch of coast, and the second listens for the first. It cannot yet join an hour: that is the Joined Tide’s WITH (E4-01). So it arrives on the first column’s heels. Its lead Bulwark takes the first fire bow-on so the second column can come, and that is the seed of E4-03, which puts the great hull there.

Intent

“The guns that guard that shore face one way. Come at it from two. The first column goes into the fire bow-on and draws it; the second waits at the edge of the fire until the first is in, then comes by the other road, before their reach can turn.”

Chosen when

Preconditions:

  • Two convoys sail.
  • Two lanes, from the two approaches, reach one coastal sector.

FIT gain conditions:

  1. The sector’s covering fire is believed single-bearing: HOT or WARM cells on one side of it only.
  2. Neither lane crosses a SCALDING cell.
  3. A Bulwark can lead column 1.
  4. The sector holds a castle Contact.

Also:

  • HABIT: H7 (core-hunter: its bearers died first, so it screens them hard) and H2 (the second road is the bearing you do not favour).
  • Grain: Heartoak +3: the proud heart sends both columns bow-on and turns neither aside. Ironbark +1.
Composition
PartRoleLineage · formationContacts
column 1cargoRunner convoy in column behind its lead Bulwark. From Cam 18 it may carry a Chirurgeon-Fleet rider inside the screen. No Leviathan leads it: the Leviathan sails as its own pair (Victory §4b) and stands off (Ships §6), and the Admiral moves no hulls between formations (§5 step 4)1
column 2cargoa second Runner convoy (or the shared convoy) in column behind a Bulwark1
idlepinthe rest0–2
  • Decision-bearing contacts: 2.
  • Links: 2.
    • Column 2 has HOLD-UNTIL(column 1 enters the band) at its hold cell.
    • Column 1’s lead has HOLD-UNTIL(column 2 enters the band): the first holds the door. It does not peel off to backfill elsewhere, and its aspect stays locked on the sector until the second is in.
Choreography

(one sortie).

  • t 0. Both columns sail. Column 1 runs straight at the sector, bow-on, at column speed. Column 2 sails to its hold, the fear-edge cell on its own bearing about 12 grids short of the band (p ≈ 11, latest tick ≈29), and stops.
  • About t 23. Column 1 enters the band. The event reaches column 2 at +2; its sails blanch and it drives.
  • About 14 ticks later, column 2 enters the band: 12 grids plus the lag. The spacing floor is met by geometry, not delay.
  • The payoff. Both waves land on one section, one on the other’s heels. Troops that meet stack on the same grids, and the stacking curve (×4 damage at a full stack) is the reward.
  • If column 1 is late reaching the band (its lead killed and backfilled, or a balk), column 2 goes at its latest tick on its fallback, alone.
Signature
  • First time. Two heavy columns on two bearings, both pointed bow-on at the same stretch of your coast. One drives straight into your fire, ignoring it. The other stops at the edge of your fire and waits. The moment the first is in, the waiting column’s sails blanch and it comes.
  • Returning tell, reading band. A column halted at the fear edge on one bearing while another drives in on the other, both with their aspects locked on one sector.
Captains’ independence
  • The lead Bulwark is fanatical-leaning and holds. If it dies, the healthiest shield moves up to the head. Under the hold it backfills forward and does not peel off elsewhere.
  • Column 2’s captains read their own fear. If an AoR crawled onto their road turns a cell SCALDING before they wake, they balk at its edge and take their fallback: the softest COLD coast.
  • Flag sunk. Links break. Column 2 goes at once for the softest coast it believes, and the two roads no longer meet.
  • After a decapitation, the carving drops column 1’s hold (column 1 escorts by default). Column 2 still listens.
Counters
  • B Overlap the sector’s coverage: a second group whose AoR, grown by a Spotter, reaches the sector from the adjacent face.
    • Piercer for the leads.
    • Splasher at the beach where the two waves stack.
    • Switch the beach group to Land as the troops disembark.
  • B Triage. If the sector holds an optional castle, ring its neighbours and let this one take the stack.
  • C Piercer the first lead before it crosses into the band.
    • Column 1 stalls while it backfills. Column 2 hears nothing and comes at its latest tick, alone, into a group that is waiting for it.
    • In Command Time this is a tick: the lead dying at t 22 keeps column 2 at the edge, while at t 24 it is already coming.
  • C Walk a second group toward column 2’s road the moment it halts, during the reading band.
    • The fear you write there makes it balk.
    • Or your crawl arrives within the 14 ticks between the columns; the spacing law guarantees that a planned walk fits.
  • C Marksman the Chirurgeon rider, and the lead stops healing.
If it fails
  • Grades.
    • Succeeded: troops came ashore at the sector.
    • Failed: both columns’ cores were eliminated or routed before they committed.
    • Countered: column 2 ran out its hold (column 1 never reached the band).
  • By grain.
    • Heartoak (Failed −1, and +1 to the same carving on the same approach) comes the same two roads again.
    • Ironbark comes heavier.
    • Willow switches.
  • Learned. Fear written from the adjacent face tells it the sector is two-bearing, so FIT 1 fails there and the next carving picks another sector. An AoR crawl onto column 2’s road is an H4 count.
The Carving

Known from wreck-wood of the first column’s lead by Halvard, whose hand was on it; Rhyna knew it as he knew it. They told it by turns, one beginning and the other finishing.

“Their fire at that shore looks one way. Two roots to one stone: the first goes into the fire, the second waits at its edge [until?] the first is in, and closes.”

Real-world root

Trafalgar 1805: Nelson’s two columns went bow-on at one part of the allied line and accepted the raking approach, while the allied van could not turn back in time.

E3-05

Make an Example

“The first to speak, silence.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Hunt · the SparkW (1 link, no hinge)5 / 1–21H3
Era & debut

E3 · the Tide of Remembering · Cam 16, before E3-01 in the same campaign

Grows from

E2-05 Make Them Speak

Grows into

E4-07 Borrow the Road

The growth

This is Make Them Speak aimed past the gun at the gunner. The Second Tide forced one flank to fire and hunted whoever spoke. This wood hunts the first speaker with everything it has, so the rest of the fort learns silence. Silence, to a fleet that reads fear, is open water.

Intent

“They speak from open stone because it costs them nothing. Make it cost. The first stone to speak this morrow gets all our wood. The others will remember, and their silence will be our road.”

Chosen when

Preconditions:

  • At least three hunt-capable formations sail: Reaver packs, Bombard lines, or the common Leviathan pair.

FIT gain conditions:

  1. Glints were written in at least two sorties so far: the Rivenmen keep speaking from open stone.
  2. Last sortie’s first fire came from a gun the fleet glinted.
  3. At least one exposed gun sits in the ledger on a live approach.
  4. The sortie’s cargo can carry its own part, on a lane that does not need the hunters.

Also:

  • HABIT: H3 (early fire). With H5 active (they move the guns we find), no hunter may take a glinted gun re-seen at its ledger position: the fleet spares the gun it knows, so the unknown stays unknown, and the example falls only on a gun glinted this sortie (§6 H5: the Hunt line’s answer to the Relocator).
  • Grain: Blackthorn +3, Ashwood +1.
Composition
PartRoleLineage · formationContacts
huntershunt after the wake; pin before itReaver packs, Bombard lines and the Leviathan pair: up to three on a payload sortie, choosing the three whose lanes run nearest the ledger’s exposed guns (ties: §14). Up to four only on an all-wolfpack sortie, where every hunter is a Reaver pack and no idle formation carries a core. From Cam 18 a Runner carrying Infiltrator troops may take a slot: its troops land and dash for the example gun, ignoring walls≤3 (≤4)
the restidle (pin)cargo and any other formations on their own lowest-fear lanes≥1
  • Decision-bearing contacts: ≤4. On a payload sortie the idle cargo carries a core and counts (§11), so three hunters plus the cargo make four, before the wake and after it. A Bombard or Leviathan hunter counts even as a pin. On an all-wolfpack sortie four Reaver hunters are four after the wake and none before it. Any other core-bearing formation is held (§11).
  • Links: 1, AFTER(first glint of the sortie). It is one condition, and every hunter listens to it.
Choreography
  • Before the wake, the sortie looks ordinary. Each hunter plays its lineage policy as a pin: Bombards line up on walls, Reavers idle at the fear edge, the Leviathan creeps to standoff.
  • The wake. The first new glint of the sortie propagates. Each hunter’s sails blanch as it arrives, with a 2-tick lag batched by spawn order, so the blanching ripples across the fleet over a few ticks. All the hunters bend toward the one gun.
  • Arrival is spread by distance. The Leviathan never closes: it stands off at the edge of the longest reach it has been hit from and shells the gun from there. The Bombards bend but stay avoiders. The Reavers drive. Infiltrators ashore run for the gun.
  • No wake. If no glint comes all sortie, every hunter plays its pin part to the end.
  • Spacing. The convergence asks one group one question: save the gun, or spend it. It forces no costed verb, because the blunt answers (enclose it, or let it fall) need none. So the 12-tick floor binds between the convergence and any other linked arrival, not between hunters (§11).
  • Across sorties, the lesson pays in the next sortie’s COLD water. Fire the Rivenmen withhold lets fear decay, and the next carving routes through the quiet. The fleet does not model your caution. It reads only the silence it produced.
Signature
  • First time. You fire one open gun early. Within a few ticks sails blanch across the whole fleet, one after another, and every warship on the board turns toward that one gun at once.
  • Returning tell. Before the first glint there is none: the sortie looks ordinary, with a Reaver pack idling. The tell is the violent, fleet-wide answer to the first glint.
  • A returning player learns to ask the question before firing: which of my guns do I want to speak first?
Captains’ independence
  • Reavers rage on the fix and over-commit.
  • Bombards bend but balk at a SCALDING cell rather than die for the lesson, so their bend often stalls at the edge of your AoR centre.
  • The Leviathan keeps its standoff.
  • Flag sunk before the wake. The link breaks and there is no example. Each hull hunts only what it sees itself: the crude Strike the Flash.
  • Flag sunk after the wake. The Reavers keep the fix, because it is their own. The Bombards drift back to their walls, and the Leviathan to its standoff.
  • After a decapitation this 1-link carving is left with no links and cannot be carved again this battle.
Counters
  • B Let an enclosed gun speak first: no flash, no glint, no example. Camouflage on any group that must fire exposed.
  • B Hold fire from open stone until the payload commits. The hunters are only pins until the wake, so the example comes too late to matter.
  • C Choose the first speaker.
    • Open with an exposed gun you can spare, on a flank you do not need: the mulberry. The whole fleet bends off its objectives toward it, across your other AoR centres.
    • Or speak first from a gun whose AoR centre sits on the hunters’ converging lanes, so the example walks into your alpha.
  • C If the example gun survives, relocate it in the next Deploy. The hunters come back for a ghost, and you teach H5: from its second count the example falls only on guns glinted that sortie, so a gun you left standing is spared (Composition).
  • C Switch the nearest group to Land when the Infiltrator Runner beaches. Splasher on the Infiltrators before they reach the gun.
If it fails
  • Grades.
    • Succeeded: the example gun was destroyed.
    • Failed: every hunter was eliminated or routed before it committed.
    • Countered: no glint came all sortie; the wake never woke.
  • By grain.
    • Blackthorn keeps carving it (Hunt +3 offsets Failed −3) and hunts the gun that shamed it.
    • Ashwood repeats it rashly.
    • Willow almost never carves it.
  • Learned.
    • Every glint persists.
    • A destroyed gun leaves the ledger.
    • A relocated survivor is an H5 count.
    • A sortie fired entirely from enclosed guns leaves only fear, and the next carving turns to the Siege or Sounding lines.
The Carving

Known from a Reaver’s wreck-wood by Rhyna, whose palms were on it; Halvard knew it as she knew it. They told it by turns, one beginning and the other finishing.

“They speak from open stone; we have seen it twice. The first stone to speak this morrow, silence with all our wood, that the others [learn?] silence.”

Real-world root

Thirty-Six Stratagems #26, “point at the mulberry, curse the locust”: Sima Rangju had a late-come noble executed to steel an army.

E3-06

Open Roads, Hidden March

“Work the east wall as if it mattered; the west is the road.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Feint · the Two Mouths (one formation with no fore-aft commitment while another approach is quiet)P3–4 / 22H2, H9
Era & debut

E3 · the Tide of Remembering · Cam 19 (ABE), with a Corsair hidden march; the Wraith debuts on a later battle of the same campaign and takes the march from then

Grows from

E2-04 Noise in the East

Grows into

E5-03 Fortitude

The growth

This is Noise in the East made credible. The Second Tide’s lure demonstrated and never closed, and a player learned to ignore the noise. This wood makes the noise a real siege, one that could breach alone in two sorties, so ignoring it costs something. Meanwhile it marches the landing by the road the Rivenmen can see least. Keeping one siege credible, and one more landing always threatened, is exactly what Fortitude grows into.

Intent

“They will not leave a real siege to go and look at a quiet road. Give them a real siege on the road they guard. While they answer it, send the hidden by the road under the hill, and the bearers after the hidden.”

Chosen when

Preconditions:

  • Two live approaches.
  • An occluded lane to approach B: cells the board’s author marks as fog or terrain shadow (Enemy Overlay). It is authored geometry, never the fort’s live sight, which the fleet cannot read. On clear-sea boards the carving is not a candidate (§24, Enemy Overlay).
  • A Bombard line, a Corsair or Wraith wolfpack, and a Runner convoy all sail.

FIT gain conditions:

  1. A wall Contact on approach A carries the fleet’s blows from at least one earlier sortie, so the siege is credible.
  2. The ledger holds a castle Contact behind that stone: the siege threatens something the Rivenmen must answer.
  3. The occluded lane’s cells are COLD.
  4. A Sower sails to ride with the siege.

Also:

  • HABIT: H2 (Favoured Bearing: A is the road they guard). H9 (Patient Shore): with H9 active the march and the bearers do not wait out their hold for a wake that may never come. Their latest tick is re-cut to the siege’s predicted first fire plus the lag (§6 LAW · THE PATIENT SHORE), so they leave cover with the siege’s engagement whether A answers it or not.
  • Grain: Twistbough +3; after a success it will not use that road twice. Heartoak −2: it will not feint.
Composition
PartRoleLineage · formationContacts
the open roadlure (a real siege)Bombard line abreast on A’s most-struck stone, with a Sower riding in it to set the wall burning. It brings no transports1
the hidden marchstrikeCorsair wolfpack, or a Wraith wolfpack from its debut, on B’s occluded lane1
the bearerscargoRunner convoy on the same lane, one cell behind1
idlepinthe rest0–1
  • Decision-bearing contacts: 3. The siege counts, because it is the question.
  • Links: 2.
    • One condition, fire drawn on the siege, wakes both the hidden march and the bearers from cover.
    • The siege has HOLD-UNTIL(bearers commit at B): it works A until the landing is in.
Choreography
  • Across sorties. Often, the stone on A took blows under an earlier carving, so A’s threat is real both in the fleet’s memory and in your walls.
  • t 0.
    • The siege sails to standoff before A’s stone (about t 23) and works it abreast, while the Sower sets the section burning.
    • The strike sails down B’s occluded lane and stops in cover near the band.
    • The bearers stop in cover one fear cell behind the strike (p ≈ 17).
    • Both holds share one latest tick, the rear hold’s: 18 + 17 ≈ 35.
  • The wake. A’s guns fire on the siege; +2 ticks; both parts’ sails blanch in cover. The strike comes out onto the guns covering B’s landing coast, hit-and-run. The bearers run for the coast a cell behind.
  • Spacing. The one-cell gap puts the bearers about 13 ticks behind the strike at B’s coast, in either branch.
  • If A draws no fire, both go at t 35 on their own clock, still a cell apart. With H9 active they go at the siege’s predicted first fire + 2 (≈ t 25), still a cell apart: silence on A no longer buys the late, lone march.
Signature
  • First time. An honest siege on your most-watched wall: line abreast, the wall burning. On that side there is nothing else, no transports. The quiet approach looks empty, but at the fog edge or in the headland’s shadow a silhouette fades in and out. When you open up on the siege, raiders come out of the fog, with a convoy a cell behind them.
  • Returning tell. A siege with no transports (the counter-tell), plus a fading silhouette on the quiet road.
  • The root sign holds. The siege line shuffles along the wall at standoff and never closes, while the other approach is quiet.
Captains’ independence
  • Corsairs are skittish and have the sharpest crazy-Ivan. Lit in cover by a Flare pulse, or struck there, they bolt early: a flinch, with no answering sail. A Spotter grows your reach but lights nothing the Corsairs can perceive. The bearers, still listening for the siege, stay in cover alone.
  • The Wraith hugs occluders and fades. Once revealed, it runs.
  • The siege is honest Bombards. It balks at SCALDING cells and it can rout.
  • Flag sunk. Links break. Strike and bearers leave cover at once on their fallbacks, so the landing comes early, unescorted by the strike. The siege reverts to plain Bombard policy.
  • After a decapitation, the carving drops the siege’s hold, so the siege may withdraw when its magazine is spent.
Counters
  • B Flare over the occluded lane shows the march in cover, and a Spotter grows a group’s AoR so your guns can reach it there. Keep B’s group on B whatever A does. Splasher at B’s beach.
  • B Answer A with the wall, not the gun. Thicken and mend A’s stone in Build (Stonewright); the siege needs two more sorties to breach.
  • C Ask what A cannot do alone: it brings no transports, so hold fire on the siege. The hidden march waits in cover for a wake that never comes, then comes on its own clock into a B group that has been waiting for it. Twice done, this teaches H9, and the march then comes with the siege’s engagement: the answer becomes the blunt one, B’s group already on B.
  • C In Deploy, pre-task an AoR on the mouth of the cover, so nothing needs deciding when they come out.
  • C Answer A late. Let the siege work, then fire on it at the tick you choose. The march wakes then, into a B group whose guns are loaded. In Command Time this is a tick.
If it fails
  • Grades.
    • Succeeded: the cargo landed on B.
    • Failed: the cargo was eliminated or routed before it committed.
    • Countered: the hidden march ran out its hold unwoken, because A drew no fire.
  • By grain.
    • Twistbough, after a success, takes −2 against using that road again; Failed is −3. It moves to the Lure line.
    • Willow and Heartoak rarely carve it at all.
  • Learned.
    • Fear written in the cover (a Flare-lit ambush) makes the occluded lane HOT, so FIT 3 fails there.
    • If you answered A only with walls, your fear mass may have shifted to B. H2 then flips, and the next feint aims at the other road.
The Carving

Known from a Corsair’s wreck-wood, in the shallows under the headland, by Halvard, who waded out to it; Rhyna, on the shingle, knew it as he knew it. They told it by turns, one beginning and the other finishing. The bearings are the generator’s to fill.

“Work the east stone we have struck before, as though it mattered, and let them answer there. When they answer, the hidden go west in the hill’s [shadow?], the bearers a road’s length behind.”

Real-world root

Thirty-Six Stratagems #8: in 206 BC Han Xin openly rebuilt the burned gallery roads while his army marched by the hidden road through Chencang.

E3-07

Between Their Breaths

“Between their breaths.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Bleed · the Breath (a gap left between two cuts)P2–3 / 1–22H3
Era & debut

E3 · the Tide of Remembering · Cam 20 (ACD, no lineage debut), not on its first battle

Grows from

E2-06 Toss a Brick

Grows into

E4-06 Sea Below, Sky Above

The growth

This is Toss a Brick with a memory of rhythm. The Second Tide guessed the reload: it moved four ticks after the first volley, whatever the guns were. This wood measures it. It counts between the blows on the brick, and it remembers the count, cell by cell, for the rest of the siege. E4-06 will turn the same count against the domain switch.

Intent

“Their stone must draw breath between blows, and at the same place the breath is always the same. Let worthless wood take the blows and count between them. Then send the swift in the breath.”

Chosen when

Preconditions:

  • A cheap hull to spend. Prefer the most damaged hull the sortie carries: a reallocated orphan keeps its damage and never heals (Ships §9).
  • A fast gap-runner: a Corsair wolfpack, a Runner convoy of skiffs, or the cloud of a Skyfall.
The breath

For each fear cell, the breath is the most recently measured interval, in whole ticks, between two volleys striking one of its formations from that cell this battle. Each new measurement replaces the one before, so the fleet believes the rhythm it heard last. Effect events carry their tick, and the breath is an integer kept beside the fear grid, like a habit count. It is kept per cell because reload is shell flight time and grows with range (Cannon §4).

FIT gain conditions:

  1. A HOT cell on the runner’s lowest-fear lane has a remembered breath, or the brick can measure one.
  2. That cell sank hulls last sortie.
  3. A WARM cell at that cell’s edge gives the runner somewhere to wait.
  4. A damaged hull is available as the brick.

Also:

  • HABIT: H3 (you strike first and far, so the brick will be answered).
  • Grain: Yew +2; Countered costs it 0, so it waits and tries later.
Composition
PartRoleLineage · formationContacts
the bricklureone cheap, damaged hull. In the Air variant, the Skyfall’s first bomber1
the runnerstrike or cargoCorsair wolfpack, or Runner skiffs. In the Air variant, the rest of the Skyfall’s cloud1
idlepinthe rest0–1
  • Decision-bearing contacts: 2.
  • Links: 2.
    • The runner has AFTER(breath read).
    • The brick has HOLD-UNTIL(runner commits): the worthless wood stays in the fire, drawing part of every volley, until the runner is in.
Choreography
  • Across sorties. Any carving that took fire from the cell may already have taught the fleet its breath. A remembered breath needs only one volley on the brick.
  • t 0. The runner sails to the WARM cell at the fear edge (p ≈ 20, latest tick ≈38) and waits, bows in. The brick sails alone into the HOT cell.
  • The count. The brick takes a volley at tick v1 and stays. It takes the next at v2, and the breath is b = v2 − v1. The new count replaces any breath already remembered for the cell.
  • The dash. The runner’s wake is carved so it crosses the fear edge on the tick of the next volley on the brick (v2 + b, less its lag and its run to the edge). If that offset would be negative, it waits one more breath.
    • It enters your zone while your guns reload, so it never takes your first volley, the cleanest one, fired before the crazy-Ivan starts (Cannon §6).
  • The brick holds in the fire until the runner commits, then turns: the Bleed root sign.
  • Spacing. The runner arrives inside one group’s reload by design; that is the Bleed line’s licence, and E2-06 already times its payload at +4. The brick is not an arrival the group must answer, since the blunt answer is to decline it. The strike asks for no costed verb, so the red line against forced simultaneity holds (§11).
  • Air variant. One bomber flies in first, and the breath is counted on the Air-loaded group that fires on it. The cloud then launches so it crosses the flak edge in the breath.
Signature
  • First time. A single battered hull sails into your fire and just sits there, taking a volley, then another. Far back at the edge of your fire, a pack of fast raiders idles, bows in. Right after your guns fire, the raiders’ sails blanch and they sprint in, and your guns are still reloading as they cross.
  • Returning tell. A brick that stays for the second volley, plus raiders idling at the fear edge. The E2 brick turned after the first volley. This one waits, to count.
Captains’ independence
  • The brick is cheap, damaged, often an orphan. It may rout after the second volley. The runner then goes on the remembered breath, or on the default of 4.
  • Corsairs crazy-Ivan the moment a shell lands near them. A runner struck inside its breath jinks and scatters, because the breath was false.
  • Flag sunk. Links break. The runner goes when its own fear map reads the lane low, the crude Corsair policy, no longer on your breath. The brick routs.
  • After a decapitation, the carving drops the brick’s hold. The brick turns at once after drawing fire, as the E2 brick did.
Counters
  • B Price the target: hold fire on a lone damaged hull. It measures nothing, and the runner goes at its latest tick into loaded guns.
  • B Depth. A second group whose AoR overlaps the runner’s lane (Spotter), so a runner that slips one group’s breath meets another group’s loaded guns. Jammer on the runner’s lane: slowed, it cannot cross in the breath. For the Air variant, split domains at Deploy so an Air group already covers the flak edge.
  • C Break your breath. Fire on the brick from one group and keep the second silent. The gap the runner reads is only one group’s reload; the other’s guns are loaded.
  • C Set their clock. Hold fire on the brick and release at the tick you choose. The breath is measured from your volley and the dash is computed from it, so the runner arrives in your AoR centre just as your reload completes. In Command Time this is a choice of tick.
  • C Teach it a long breath. Fire on the brick slowly on purpose, holding fire between volleys. The fleet remembers the long breath, and its next runner comes too early.
If it fails
  • Grades.
    • Succeeded: the runner reached its objective (cargo ashore, or the strike on the coast guns).
    • Failed: the runner was eliminated or routed before it committed.
    • Countered: the brick drew no volley, or the runner was struck inside its breath.
  • By grain.
    • Yew (Countered 0; Failed −3) tries again later, often at another cell.
    • The other grains move on.
  • Learned.
    • Every volley it observes updates the cell’s breath.
    • Both lies are farmable, because the fleet keeps the newest count. Two groups firing in turn teach it a false short breath: fewer dashes fit, FIT 1 fails at that cell, and it stops carving there. Slow fire on purpose teaches it a false long one: the next runner comes too early, into guns that have reloaded.
The Carving

Known from a raider’s wreck-wood by Rhyna, whose palms were on it; Halvard knew it as she knew it. They told it by turns, one beginning and the other finishing. The number is the fleet’s own count, in ticks.

“Let worthless wood take their blows, and count between. Between breath and breath, the swift go in. At the [low?] stone their breath is four.”

Real-world root

Thirty-Six Stratagems #4, “wait at leisure while the enemy exhausts himself” (Sun Tzu, ch. 7), refined into a strike timed to the defender’s reload.

E3-08

Spent or Waiting

“Is the stone spent, or waiting? Look, then go.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Patience · the Knot (formations loitering in a COLD fringe and launching late)P (a hinge)2–3 / 1–21: the hingeH1, H5
Era & debut

E3 · the Tide of Remembering · Cam 21 (ACE, no lineage debut), not on its first battle; it replaces Leave the Known Gun (CALL-OUT 40)

Grows from

E2-07 Let the Fire Work

Grows into

E5-04 The Fourth Time

The growth

This is Wait in the Grey (E1-06) remembering. The young wood waited in the cold water until the shore fell quiet and then went in, because it could not tell a gun that was spent from a gun that was waiting. Let the Fire Work taught the wood to wait for a thing it could watch. This wood waits as the young wood did, and then it looks: before it trusts a silence, it sends an eye to the stone that fell silent. The Fourth Time takes the next step, and gives the Rivenmen a waiting of the fleet’s own to watch.

Intent

“The stone that spoke has fallen still. Spent, or waiting? The green wood could not tell, and went in on the silence. We can look. Wait in the grey until their shore is quiet; then send an eye to the still stone. If it stands, it is waiting: go round it. If it is gone, go.”

Chosen when

Preconditions:

  • A COLD fringe cell beside a road that crosses HOT cells (E1-06’s fringe, §17).
  • X, the thing the fleet doubts: the glinted gun whose ledger position lies nearest those HOT cells (ties: §14). With no glinted gun there, X is the HOT cell nearest the fringe, and the look asks only whether any gun can be seen in it.
  • A cargo convoy sails, and a looker rides with it or beside it: a Recon rider or picket, or failing one the convoy’s lead hull.

FIT gain conditions:

  1. The road’s HOT cells fell quiet before the end of last sortie: the fire slackened, which is the doubt.
  2. X is a glinted gun not re-seen since its glint, a doubtful belief under the memory clause (§19, part 1(b)).
  3. A Recon sails: the eye with the long sight (Ships §5).
  4. E1-06 or E2-07 was carved this battle (LINE +1).

Also:

  • HABIT: H1 (that quarter holds its fire, so its silence is suspect) and H5 (they move the guns we find, so a silent gun may be gone).
  • Grain: Yew +3, Willow +2: the patient and the cautious hearts both look before they go. Blackthorn −2 and Ashwood −2 will not wait.
Composition
PartRoleLineage · formationContacts
the waiterscargo, carries the hingeRunner convoy behind its lead Bulwark, loitering in the COLD fringe cell beside the road1
the eyeprobethe convoy’s Recon rider sent out, or a Recon picket’s lead hull (§3 Parts)1
idlepinthe rest0–1
  • Decision-bearing contacts: 2, plus any core-bearing idle formation (held beyond four, §11).
  • Links: 1, the hinge.
  • The bend is X’s own cell plus the adjacent cells that read HOT or SCALDING at the boundary: believed state only, because the fleet never resolves one gun’s circle from the smear (Fleet Memory §3). The bend lane is the lowest-fear lane that avoids every bend cell.
Choreography

(one sortie).

  • t 0. The waiters sail to the fringe cell (about t 12–14) and loiter there: the Knot. They keep E1-06’s QUIET self-trigger, waiting until no new fear has been written in their own grid for 4 ticks.
  • The eye goes on the quiet, or at t 22 at the latest (start value; range 18–26; settled so the bend branch still commits inside the backstop). It runs for the edge of its sight range toward X (about 6 ticks at Recon speed), slows and skirts there: the look, a presence (§19 LAW · THE LOOK IS A PRESENCE).
  • The hinge: IF(seen(X) confirms a gun at X, within 6 ticks of the eye reaching sight range) → waiting : spent.
    • Waiting. The gun stands, silent: a held battery. The waiters’ sails blanch (lag 2) and they take the bend lane round X.
    • Spent. The look finds X’s position empty, or sees only wall. The waiters’ sails blanch and they drift in on the road, straight through the cells X was believed to cover.
    • A window that closes with no look (the eye sunk, or turned away by fire) reads waiting. The memory clause trusts no silence it has not seen.
  • Timing. At the latest the hinge resolves at about t 34. The road commits at about t 46–49; the bend, a cell longer, at about t 59–62. Both land inside the 70-tick backstop, and a docked Runner does not withdraw (Ships §7).
  • If it is struck while it waits, it makes the crazy-Ivan, shifts to the nearest COLD cell, and restarts the quiet clock, as its ancestor did.
  • With H9 active the eye still goes at t 22 at the latest; nothing in this carving waits on a lure’s fire, so the patient-shore re-cut does not touch it (§6).
Signature
  • First time. A convoy stops in cold water at the edge of your fire and waits, as the young wood did. When your guns fall quiet, a small ship slips out toward the gun that went silent, slows at the edge of its sight, and looks. Then the convoy’s sails go pale and it comes: straight in if the gun was gone, round it if the gun was there.
  • Returning tell. The Knot (a formation parked in a COLD fringe), plus a lone eye that leaves only on your silence, toward your quietest known gun. Wait in the Grey drifted in on the silence. This one looks first.
Captains’ independence
  • The eye is a skittish Recon. Fired on, it swerves and leaves, and the window closes unlooked: the waiters take the bend.
  • The waiters balk at a lane cell gone SCALDING this sortie, on either lane, and take their fallback: the softest COLD coast.
  • Flag sunk. The link breaks. The waiters drift in on the quiet with no look: Wait in the Grey’s form, on a Runner convoy.
  • After a decapitation the carving drops the look, and its reduced form is its ancestor: E1-06’s wait, on a Runner convoy.
Counters
  • B Cover the road and the bend, a group on each, weapons-free. Whatever the look finds, the convoy comes into guns. Splasher at the beach.
  • B Kill the eye as it leaves. The window closes unlooked, the convoy takes the bend, and the bend is a lane you can name before the sortie.
  • C Let it look at a wall. Enclose the silent battery in Build. The look sees wall, reads the gun spent, and the convoy drifts in down the very road that battery covers. The fleet believes what it measured, and it measured a wall.
  • C Let it see the gun, and wait on the bend. Leave X in the open and silent. The look confirms it, and the convoy takes the bend, which is predictable to the cell: X’s cell and its hot neighbours, plus one. Put a silent enclosed battery there.
  • C Choose the tick of your silence. The eye leaves on your quiet. Fall silent on purpose on the tick you want, and the whole chain runs on your clock. In Command Time it is a chosen tick.
If it fails
  • Grades.
    • Succeeded: the cargo landed.
    • Failed: the cargo was eliminated or routed before it committed.
    • Countered: the look found the gun standing (waiting): the silence was a held battery, and the fleet was made to go the long way.
  • By grain.
    • Yew (Countered 0) waits, and looks again next sortie.
    • Willow (Countered −3) switches to the Sounding line and sends an eye down the road instead.
    • Ironbark re-carves it heavier: a shared convoy waits in the grey.
  • Learned. A gun seen gone is an H5 count. A gun seen standing refreshes its Contact and is no longer doubtful. A bend that turned HOT after the convoy committed is an H1 count on that quarter. The deeper lesson is that the Rivenmen’s silence can be checked, and that the checking itself can be watched. The Fourth Time begins there.
The Carving

Known from the eye’s wreck-wood by Halvard, whose hand was on it; Rhyna knew it as he knew it. They told it by turns, one beginning and the other finishing.

“The stone that spoke is still. Is it spent, or [waiting?]? Wait in the grey, then send an eye. If the stone stands, go round it as water goes round a root; if it is gone, go.”

Real-world root

The Dardanelles, 18 March 1915. As the forts’ fire slackened, the Allied fleet read the silence as guns spent. The forts were short of shells but largely intact, and ships manoeuvring in Erenköy Bay ran onto a line of mines laid ten days before. Three battleships were lost.

20E4 · THE JOINED TIDE (Aelthae)

Cam 23–26 · three links, WITH across roads (from Cam 25), anticipation, the hour that outlives the heart, 3 habits · “one hour on three roads”

On three roads the grey sails went pale in one breath, and the watch on the north tower and the watch on the south cried out together, though neither could see the other. (A hearth-saying of the Joined Tide.)

What this growing is. The Joined Tide is the wood that carves one sentence across many hulls. Every link before it was a hull waking to another hull’s deed once the deed had crossed the root-bond, and it woke late: 3 ticks in the Second Tide, 2 in the Remembering (§4). The lag holds at 2 through Cam 23, which opens the third road, and creeps to its 1-tick floor across Cam 24–25. From Cam 25 the heart carves the hour itself (WITH), and formations in three launch zones blanch on one tick and go, as if one hand had flung them (fiction_hooks §2). It is also the first Tide that acts on a thing before it happens. It keeps a count of how your reach walks (H4, §6) and carves the tick at which it will have walked. And it keeps that hour when the heart that cut it is on the sea floor. This is the root-bond become what the Aetherbond was: wordless, in step, one work from many hands (fiction_hooks §8). The tales let that likeness land without saying it; this doc says it once, here, so every carving below is built to show it.

How organised it is.

CapabilityFromWhat it doesHow the player sees it
Propagation lag 1 tick (flag alive)holds at 2 through Cam 23; creeps to the floor across Cam 24–25 (Campaign §4 cognition creep, not an unlock)Links fire 1 tick after the event reaches the hull (§4), less a Recon relay’s help, more a thin net’s dragThe answering sail blanches almost with the deed. Links no longer visibly straggle
Three links, ≤1 of them a hingeCam 25Carvings may tie three conditions together (counted as §4 LAW · COUNTING LINKS says)Three-part sentences: a draw, a moment, a release
WITH across roadsCam 25A link that fires on a carved tick, not on an arrived event. Lag does not applySeveral answering sails on one tick, on different approaches
AnticipationCam 25A WITH tick may be carved from an active habit alone: the fleet acts on where your reach will be (Ships §6 Wolfpack Lead, raised to fleet level)A formation that moves before the thing it answers has happened
Clock fallbackCam 25When the heart dies, links keep their predicted ticks, three ticks late and a tick apart by spawn order, and hinges take their foretold branch (§4; below)Ships obeying a dead mind, a beat late and out of step, into nothing
Three habits activeCam 25Shore-Reading capacity 3 (§6)A dispatch line when a third habit crosses into active
Stratagem historyunchanged since E2No immediate repeat of a Failed carving, except Ironbark and Heartoak. Nothing retires before E5 (§5 step 3)The fleet will try a failed carving again, a sortie later

The threshold (Cam 23–24). The capability cannot arrive with the third approach (Cam 23) or with a piece-layer campaign (Cam 24, BCE) (§13: discrete events turn singly). So the Joined Tide’s first two campaigns carve in the Remembering’s grammar (2 links, ≤1 hinge, 2 habits) on three roads, and a sunk heart still breaks their links. Cam 23 also holds the lag at 2, so the third road is the only thing that changes there. What changes at the threshold is the board. The third road opens (Cam 23). The Breacher arrives with Interdictor and Breaker (Cam 23). Troops come as one, not piecemeal: the Standard-bearer and the Pioneer (Cam 24). The three carvings that debut at Cam 24 (E4-02, E4-04, E4-07) are written in that grammar. They gain the clock fallback at Cam 25 without changing shape.

What the player is learning to read.

  1. One tick, many sails. Earlier Tides wake piecemeal. When sails blanch on the same tick on different roads, the wood has carved an hour, and it will keep it.
  2. A formation that stops is waiting for a moment. Five of the eight carvings park a formation at the fear edge, full-screened, bow-on to its objective (§3 LAW · A HOLD IS A PRESENCE). The question the player learns to ask is “what is it waiting for?”, and each carving answers it with a different event.
  3. The fleet now plans on your walks. H4 (Re-tasker) is the era’s master habit: three of the eight carvings are keyed to it. The fleet no longer reacts to where your reach went. It carves for where your reach will go. The answers are a reach that does not walk, or a walk the fleet has not counted.
  4. The flag kill changes meaning. From Cam 25, sinking the Standard-Bearer no longer breaks the running plan. The hour stands, raggedly: every clock fires three ticks late, and sails that would have blanched as one blanch a tick apart (§4). The kill buys those ticks now, and the next plan: one link fewer (the ratchet below), and the successor’s grain (§7).

Lineages on the board. All earlier lineages stay (Bombard, Runner + Swarm, Recon, Reaver, Sapper, Bulwark; Skyfall and bombers, Provocateur, Sower, Corsair, Quartermaster, common Leviathan; Aegis, Linebreaker, Chirurgeon-Fleet, Infiltrator, Wraith). New in this era:

  • Breacher (Cam 23, with Interdictor + Breaker; Campaign §6). Fear-immune and unbreakable, so it cannot be baited, only stopped (Ships §6). Its lane is always its own straight line to one wall, fixed at carving, and no hinge ever chooses its road. The Admiral may hold it at a station on that line, read once at carving, and release it by a link or a clock; once it drives it never routes by fear (§24 Ships §6; CALL-OUT 36). It is the Joined Tide’s instrument for the road you leave: it does not need cold water, only an unanswered wall.
  • Standard-bearer and Pioneer troops (Cam 24; Ships §7). The Standard-bearer tightens command-confusion batching, so a landing comes as one weakest-run A* path, not a 5-cell ruin (Build §11). The Pioneer bridges or stabilises fragile ground at a breach.
  • Stage roster additions for Cam 23–32 (Victory §4b): a mixed Skyfall+Runner convoy, a Skyfall pair, the Breacher wedge, 4 wolfpacks.

The budget.

DialCam 23–26Provenance
Contacts C, board-wide6–8. Payload sorties 3–4, all decision-bearing; preparation sorties 6–8 wolfpacksCampaign §4; Victory §4b
Decision-bearing≤4Victory §3; §11

Hulls / Q / sorties18–24 / 11 / 7–10Campaign §4

Every E4 carving is narrow and clever (P): ≤4 contacts, 1–3 links. The era adds no wide form, because a WITH wake across 6–8 wolfpacks is forced simultaneity by construction (§11; CALL-OUT 25). A preparation sortie in the Joined Tide carves the only older W forms wolfpacks can fill: E3-05 Make an Example with Reaver hunters, or the E1 wolfpack glyphs (E1-03, E1-05, and E1-06 for Recons). Otherwise it carries no stratagem, and every pack runs its lineage policy (§5 step 1).

Counting links. A link is one carved condition, hinge included, and the era budget is 3, of which at most 1 is a hinge (§4 LAW · COUNTING LINKS). Each decapitation removes one link from every later carving this battle (§4), so the budget is the lever the flag kill pulls:

After this many decapitations this battleLink budgetE4 carvings still in the book
03all eight
12E4-01, E4-02, E4-04 (without H11’s look), E4-06, E4-07, E4-08 (the kept hand and the long shore leave)
21E4-02, E4-06
30none; the fleet carves E1 forms, and E2 forms with their link dead (§4)
LAW · THE THIRD ROAD WIDENS EVERY GAP

Any two decision-bearing arrivals that one player group could have to answer are carved Δ = max(12, d + 4 + s) ticks apart, where d is the grid distance between the two commit points (a planned crawl at 1 grid/tick, Victory §3), 4 is one reload (Cannon §4), and s is a slack of 4 ticks (start value; range 2–6; settled so a planned walk back arrives a reload early, and a single tick of hesitation never decides it). The 12-tick floor is §11’s spacing value (range 10–16). Campaign §4 requires exactly this widening once a third approach opens. The Admiral computes d from its own commit points, which are fleet state, so it reads no AoR. A wolfpack pin arriving with a decision-bearing part is glanceable by role (§11); a core in pin is decision-bearing and is carved Δ apart like any other decision. So no two decisions are simultaneous whatever the true coverage turns out to be.

The hour outlives the heart from Cam 25: a sunk heart’s pending links become clock links, three ticks late and a tick apart by spawn order, and an open hinge takes its foretold branch at the window’s close (§4 LAW · DECAPITATION SIMPLIFIES THE CARVING; CALL-OUT 39). It is always visible, because a clocked link still shows its answering sail, and the ragged blanching is itself the tell of a dead heart. Before Cam 25, at the threshold, a sunk heart breaks links and each formation takes its fallback on local beliefs, as in E3.

Debuts in the era (§13: one new carving per battle; never on a campaign’s first battle, or on a battle that introduces a lineage or an approach; a capability debuts on a battle of its own, carried by one carving; ≈6 battles per campaign).

The eight carvings at a glance.

The Knowings below are told as Halyna tell them: one of them touches the grain, what one knows the other knows, and they tell it by turns, one beginning each line and the other finishing it (§9b; fiction_hooks §7). Seren sets it down and glosses. The Joined Tide’s Knowings are fluent and they name the ties: who waited for whom (§9b). They keep the wood’s own words from the Remembering’s lexicon (§19): speaking stone, the reach, the swift, the dread. The generator fills bearings and sectors from the sim’s record, so the examples below use east, north and south as placeholders.

E4-01

Three Roads, One Hour

“Three roads, one hour.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Flood · the WaveP3–4 / 32 (both WITH), no hingeH4, H2. Never carved before sortie 3 of a battle, because H4 needs two observations (§6)
Era & debut

E4 · the Joined Tide · Cam 25, battle 3 (the battle after the capability’s debut)

Grows from

E2-01 Run the Batteries (the column that goes through, not against) · E3-01 Sounding and Mass (hold, then go where it is quiet)

Grows into

E5-01 The Closing Hand

The growth

This is Run the Batteries on three roads at once. It is also Sounding and Mass with the sounder replaced by memory: the fleet no longer sends a hull to learn which road your reach will leave, because it has counted your walks.

Intent

“Their one reach walks between two roads. Strike the road it walks to and the road that holds, together. When it has walked, strike the road it left, with the hull that cannot be turned.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions (all required).
    • 3 live approaches.
    • H4 (Re-tasker) active. This is the anticipation form, and without H4 it has nothing to anticipate.
    • Two cores sail: a Runner convoy, and a Breacher wedge (or, failing one, the mixed Skyfall+Runner convoy).
    • A wolfpack sails for the held road. A core would be decision-bearing in pin (§11), so the held road takes only a wolfpack.
    • ≥2 links in budget.
    • The last road’s commit falls ≥10 ticks inside the 70-tick backstop (start value; range 8–14; settled so the wedge’s engagement is watchable before the sortie ends).
  • Gain conditions (FIT, over believed state).
    1. Fear mass at sortie end is unequal across the three roads, and one road carries the most. That is the held road, the bearing believed to be covered by a group that does not move.
    2. On the other two roads, last sortie’s fear was written early and then went quiet on one (the home road, where the walking reach started), and written late on cells passed COLD on the other (the pull road, where it walked to). That is H4’s own observation, placed on the map.
    3. The home road’s coast holds a wall Contact with recorded hits: an objective for the Breacher.
    4. H2 (Favoured Bearing) is active and names the held road.
  • Habit. H4 (+1), H2 (+1).
  • Grains. Ashwood +3 (Runner and Provocateur flags: the rash heart loves one hour), Ironbark +2, Heartoak +2. Willow −2, Twistbough −2, Yew −1. Silverbark weights nothing but reads H4 twice (HABIT ×2), so it carves this the moment the walk is counted.
Composition
PartRoadFormationCore or screenRoleContactsDecision-bearing
Hold the held roadheld roadCorsair wolfpack (else any wolfpack; never a core)wolfpackpin1no (a wolfpack pin is glanceable, §11)
The pullpull roadRunner convoy: a column behind its lead Bulwarkcore (land) + 4 shieldscargo1yes
The last roadhome roadBreacher wedge (3 shields); else the mixed Skyfall+Runner convoycore (breach)strike1 (+1 bomber cloud if Skyfall)yes
  • Links: WITH(t0), shared by all three (1). WITH(t_h + Δ) for the last road’s drive (2).
  • Leaves thin: the held road gets a raid and nothing else. The group there is free to spend its alpha on the pull road’s landing, and that frees the walking reach to stay home.
Choreography

(one sortie; ticks from the 3-2-1; launch zone 5, reading band 18, engagement band 16, Fight §3; 1 grid/tick, Victory §3).

  1. t 0–5. Three formations form up in three launch zones. Spawn batching may bring them in at different ticks. Each one waits for the others. No earlier Tide waits.
  2. t0 = the tick the last of them has formed up (start value: tick 5). WITH(t0): three answering sails blanch on one tick, and all three leave on straight lanes (the Flood’s root sign).
  3. The pin and the pull run in. Their lanes are cut for equal passage (±2 ticks), so both reach their commit points at t_h ≈ t0 + 18.
  4. The last road’s wedge runs its own straight lane toward its wall and heaves to at its station (≈ t0 + 12): the fear edge on that line, read once at carving and never re-read. It stops dead, wedge closed, bow-on to its wall. Its road and station were fixed at the boundary; nothing the player does in the Fight moves them (§24 Ships §6; CALL-OUT 36).
  5. WITH(t_h + Δ). The drive tick is carved so the wedge reaches its commit point at t_h + Δ. Δ = max(12, d + 4 + s), where d is the distance between the pull road’s and the home road’s commit points and s is the 4-tick slack (LAW · THE THIRD ROAD WIDENS EVERY GAP). Example: commit points 20 grids apart give Δ = 28, and the wedge commits at ≈ t 51. The Breacher then drives as it always does: straight, undeviating, fear-immune (Ships §6).

Δ is the one number where the fleet’s intent and the fairness law agree. The fleet wants the wedge to arrive when your reach has walked to the pull road and fired a reload there. The law demands that a planned walk back still fits, with room to spare. The intended clever answer is the read, not the walk: the heave-to names the road, so the player who has read it keeps the roving group home (C · Read the heave-to, below). The walk to the landing and straight back is the blunt fallback, and it is forgiving: with s = 4 the walker is home a reload before the wedge commits, so no single tick of hesitation decides it.

Signature
  • First time. Three formations sit in three launch zones and nothing moves. Then all three go at once. Two run straight in. The third stops at the edge of your reach, bow-on to your wall, and waits. While your roving group deals with the landing on its second road, the wedge on its first road starts to move. What a first-timer remembers is “the third one came late”. The codex tells them it was carved late.
  • Returning tell. Three answering sails on one tick. No earlier Tide can do it: its links straggle by 2–3 ticks. Then the heave-to. The formation that stops names the road the fleet believes you will leave.
Captains’ independence
  • The pin (Corsair, skittish) is the loosest part. If the held road’s fire drives it Wavering, it sags aft and gives up the pin, and the held road’s group is free.
  • The pull (Runner) balks if its lane went SCALDING this sortie (a Piercer through its lead Bulwark writes that fast). It stalls at the cell edge and takes its fallback, the softest COLD coast (Ships §5, Runner baseline). That is often the home road’s coast, where it then arrives early and alone.
  • The wedge is fear-immune and unbreakable. It will keep the hour. Its only deviation is the orphan fork: if the Breacher core dies while heaved-to, its three shields turn wolfpack and drive (revenge swap, Committed) if their resolve is above the rout threshold, or retreat if not (Fleet Memory §7).
  • Flag sunk mid-stratagem. Both links are already clocks, so the hour stands, raggedly: the wedge drives at t_h + Δ + 3, plus its batch index, whether or not your reach walked: the hour the heart foretold, kept late (Kael’s fourth count, Legends V.5; §4). The walking reach gains those ticks. Resolve also craters (the biggest drain, Ships §4), so the skittish pin usually breaks. The next carving also loses a link and takes the successor’s grain (§4). Three Roads needs two links, so after a second decapitation it leaves the book for the battle.
Counters
  • B · A group per road, and no walking. At Cam 23–26 the fort fields 2–3 groups for 3 roads (Campaign §4). With three groups, answer each road with its own group and never re-task. H4 never activates, and the carving is never a candidate.
  • B · Wall the road it will come for. With two groups, put Interdictor + Breaker on the home road’s wall. The wedge then meets guns that fire at the hull nearest the wall and shove it off, whatever the reach is doing (Cannon §11: the breakthrough answer and the only knockback). Rally Flag shortens the crawl, so the Δ gap gains slack.
  • C · Read the heave-to, and stay. The wedge that stops tells you where the fleet thinks you will leave. Answer the pull road’s landing with the held road’s spare alpha (the carving left that road a raid) and a Splasher at the beach. Keep the roving group home, hold its alpha, and spring it when the wedge drives. With Command Time, pick up the roving group as the landing enters the band: the hour slows to 1/24 and you can watch both roads.
  • C · Teach it a false walk. H4 is counted from what the fleet saw. In the sorties before, walk the roving group the other way. The fleet then names the wrong home, and the wedge heaves to on the road your reach is walking to.
  • C · Cut the next sentence. Decapitate earlier in the battle. Each decapitation removes a link from later carvings, and after two this one cannot be carved (the ratchet table).
If it fails
  • Grading. Succeeded if the wedge commits on a breach, or the landing lands. Failed if both cores are eliminated or routed before their commit points.
  • What it learned. Suppose the home road’s guns fired again while the landing was being shot. Then the fleet has seen fear written on the home road from guns that did not walk, and H4 is contradicted. Two contradictions in a row deactivate it (§6). Without H4 the precondition fails, so the fleet stops anticipating walks and falls back to Run the Batteries on the believed-weakest road.
  • By grain.
    • Ironbark (Failed → +1, prefers the line’s next form): E5-01 is not yet in the book, so it re-carves Three Roads with the heaviest formation on the last road.
    • Heartoak (+1 on the same approach) re-carves it on the same three roads.
    • Ashwood (Failed −2 against its +3 line weight) usually carves it again next sortie if the FIT still holds.
    • Willow (−5) turns to Ask the Silent Water or Sounding and Mass: it asks before it goes.
    • Twistbough (−3) moves to the Feint and Lure lines (Open Roads, Hidden March; Along the Shore).
The Carving

Three roads, one hour. Let the east and the north go as one. Let the south stand at the edge of the dread until the reach of the south has walked east; then let the south go.

One of Halyna begins it and the other finishes it, and then neither of them speaks for a long while.

Real-world root
