The Mongol invasion of Japan, 1281: the Eastern and Southern Route fleets failed to meet on time. This is that rendezvous done right, with the decimal drill’s lesson that sub-units keep the drill without new orders (research §3.20, §3.6).

E4-02

Ask the Silent Water

“Ask the silent water; heed its answer.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Sounding · the Lone StrokeP3–4 / 2–31: one hinge shared by two formationsH1
Era & debut

E4 · the Joined Tide · Cam 24, battle 5, in the threshold’s E3 grammar (CALL-OUT 19)

Grows from

E3-01 Sounding and Mass

Grows into

E5-02 Come as the Young Wood Came

The growth

This is the Sounding taught to ask one question. Sounding and Mass asked “which road is quiet?” and chose between roads. This carving asks one cell one question at one hour, and it uses the answer both ways.

Intent

“One water beside their fire is quiet. Is it empty, or is it holding? Ask it with one hull. If it speaks, hunt the voice. If it keeps silence, go through it.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions.
    • A test cell: a fear cell believed COLD, adjacent to a HOT or SCALDING cell, lying on the lowest-fear lane to the believed-softest coast.
    • A probe: the lead hull of a Recon picket, or failing a Recon, the lead hull of a Corsair wolfpack.
    • A Reaver pack sails, and a cargo convoy sails.
    • 1 link (the hinge) in budget.
  • Gain conditions.
    1. H1 (Silent Quarter) is active on the test cell’s quarter: the fleet has seen a hold-feint there.
    2. The Contact ledger holds a glinted gun within one cell of the test cell, so exposed guns have stood in that quarter before.
    3. The flood’s road through the test cell is short: its commit point lies within one cell beyond.
    4. E3-01 was carved this battle (LINE +1).
  • Ghosts and stale negatives. The test cell’s COLD may be a stale negative, a gun moved there after the fleet last wrote the cell. The carving is exactly how a Remembering-grade mind checks one.
  • Habit. H1 (+1).
  • Grains. Willow +3 (Recon and Chirurgeon flags: the cautious heart asks before it goes). Silverbark reads H1 twice. Ashwood and Heartoak, who do not ask, rarely reach it.
Composition
PartFormationRoleContactsDecision-bearing
The questionlead hull of a Recon picket (a dispersed picket is 1 contact per hull, Victory §3). The picket’s second hull holds at the fear edge as its eyesprobe2the lead hull: yes (it asks a question, §11). The eyes: no
The huntersReaver pack at the fear edge, a cell aside from the test cellhunt (obeys the hinge)1yes
The floodRunner convoy, a column behind its Bulwark, holding at the last COLD cell before the test cellcargo (obeys the hinge)1yes
  • C 4, or 3 with a Corsair probe whose pack holds shape. Decision-bearing 3.
  • Leaves thin: the flood and the hunters sit motionless at the fear edge for the whole question. In branch A the flood takes a late fallback lane that you can predict.
Choreography
  1. t 0. The probe leaves the launch zone first, ≥6 ticks ahead of anything else (the Sounding’s root sign), on the flood’s own lane.
  2. t ≈ 6. The hunters and the flood sail and stop at the fear edge: the flood bow-on to the test cell, the hunters scattered a cell aside.
  3. t ≈ 20. The probe enters the test cell and lingers there. Recon “lingers and sweeps” (Ships §5); its dwell is carved at 8 ticks (start value; range 6–12; settled when greybox testers who planned it choose fire or hold inside the window with Command Time ≥70% of the time). A Corsair probe crosses the cell’s width instead, which takes 8–13 ticks at 1 grid/tick. Both clear §11’s 6-tick floor.
  4. The hinge. IF(fire drawn on the probe at any point on its run, up to the tick it clears the test cell) → A : B. The whole run counts, because the probe runs the flood’s own road; fire anywhere on it is an answer. A probe sunk inside the window counts as answered: its eyes see it die. The window closes at the carved tick t_q (≈ t 28), when the probe clears the cell. Lag 1, so both waiting parts wake at t_q + 1.
    • A · the water answered. The hunters go for the freshest glint written during the window. If there is none (the answer came from an enclosed or camouflaged gun), they take their fallback: the freshest fix in the ledger, else pin toward the test cell. The flood takes its fallback lane, the lowest-fear lane that avoids the test cell, and arrives late (≈ +10–16 ticks).
    • B · the water kept silence. The flood drives through the test cell to its coast (≈ 8–13 ticks; commit ≈ t 37–41). The hunters run on its flank on their lineage policy and hunt any gun that glints against it. That needs no extra link.
Signature
  • First time. One hull runs ahead into a patch of quiet water beside the guns that have been busy, and dawdles there. Everything else stops at the edge of your reach. Then the hull leaves, and one tick later two formations blanch and go. Either they go straight through the quiet water, or they go at the gun that spoke.
  • Returning tell. The Sounding’s lone stroke, aimed. The probe goes to the quiet cell; a brick goes to the loud one (E2-06). It lingers. The others wait at the fear edge, not in the launch zone. The waiting column’s bow points at the quiet cell: that is the road it will take if you stay silent.
Captains’ independence
  • The probe (skittish) crazy-Ivans and runs when struck. A struck probe that routs still counts, because its report has already crossed the root-bond.
  • The flood balks at the test cell’s edge if the cell went SCALDING before the hinge resolves. In practice that means the probe drew heavy fire, so the branch is A anyway.
  • The hunters, once released on A, over-commit on a juicy fix (Ships §5: Reavers rage). That is their movement policy, not a deviation.
  • Flag sunk, Cam 24 (threshold). The hinge breaks. The flood beelines the softest COLD coast on local beliefs, and the hunters go for the freshest fix they hold.
  • Flag sunk, from Cam 25. The hinge takes its foretold branch, B (the flood carries the payload, §4 clause (b)), at t_q + 3. The flood goes through the quiet water a beat after the hour, whatever your guns did.
Counters
  • B · Enclose the guns that watch your quiet water. Walls, or Camouflage. Then fire as freely as you like. If you fire on the probe, it draws fire but writes no Contact: the hunters have nothing, and the flood stays out. If you hold, the flood comes into enclosed guns. Neither case needs timing.
  • C · Hold through the window. Put the covering group on hold-fire before the probe enters, let it clear, and alpha the flood as it commits into the test cell. This is the hold-feint answered with a hold-feint. With Command Time, pick up the group as the probe enters: the tick when it clears is the tick you are playing against.
  • C · Answer with a gun you do not need. Fire on the probe’s run from an exposed gun on a flank you can spare. The answer is A. The hunters chase the sacrifice, and the flood takes its fallback lane, which is predictable (the next-lowest-fear lane) and which you have covered.
  • C · Kill the heart first (from Cam 25). Sink the Standard-Bearer before t_q and the hinge takes the flood branch, three ticks after the hour. You may then shoot the probe to pieces and still get the flood into your alpha.
If it fails
  • Grading. Countered if A with no glint: the water answered and gave nothing to hunt. Failed if the flood is eliminated before its commit point in B, because the hold-feint sprang. Succeeded if a hunted fix is destroyed or the flood lands.
  • What it learned. A B-branch flood shot in the test cell is a fresh H1 observation (COLD turned HOT after a commit), so the count rises. The test cell now reads HOT and is no longer a candidate. The quarter’s silence is distrusted, and the next sortie’s lanes bend away from it.
  • By grain.
    • Willow (Failed −5) drops it and carves Sounding and Mass on the other approach.
    • Blackthorn (+2 to any carving aimed where it lost most) comes back to the test cell’s quarter with Make an Example or Borrow the Road.
    • Ironbark (Failed → +1) asks the same water again next sortie.
    • Twistbough never asks twice and turns to the Feint line.
    • Silverbark (Countered −4) learns fastest and does not ask a quarter that answered with nothing.
The Carving

Send one sapling to the still water, at the hour cut here. Heed it. If the water speaks, the hunters go to the voice. If the water keeps silence, the bearers go through.

Halyna tell it by turns. Seren adds a gloss of her own under it: “It asked, and we did not know it was a question.”

Real-world root

Midway, 1942: Midway’s plain-language report that its water plant had failed, sent to learn whether “AF” meant Midway, and confirmed within a day when the Japanese reported “AF is short of water” (research §3.16).

E4-03

The Great Hull and the Kept Hand

“The great hull first; the kept hand last.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Screen · the Bar BeforeP3–4 / 2–33, one a hinge. It needs the full E4 budget, so one decapitation removes it from the bookH4, H3, H10
Era & debut

E4 · the Joined Tide · Cam 26, battle 2

Grows from

E3-04 Two Roads to One Stone

Grows into

T-01 The Heads That Grow (the Hydra’s reserve that flows to whichever head is struck)

The growth

This is Two Roads to One Stone with the second column held back until it knows which road to take.

Intent

“Let the great hull take their first fire and hold it. Let the raiders pull a reach aside. Keep one hand closed until the moment; then open it where the reach is not.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions.
    • A Leviathan pair sails.
    • A Corsair wolfpack sails.
    • A Runner convoy sails to be the kept hand. Never a Breacher: the hinge chooses the hand’s road, and no hinge chooses a Breacher’s road (§24 Ships §6; CALL-OUT 36).
    • Two lanes into the same sector are believed COLD or WARM: L1 on the flank where the wing will dart, and L2 inboard of it.
    • 3 links in budget.
  • Gain conditions.
    1. H4 is active.
    2. A HOT cell sits on the centre lane, so the great hull has fire to drink.
    3. The last sortie’s first fire came from inside the reading band (H3’s evidence: the Rivenmen spend their alpha early).
    4. E3-04 was carved this battle (LINE +1).
  • Losses so far. The Leviathan pair must be whole. A cracked Leviathan (Piercer damage carried between sorties, Victory §4b) fails the FIT test the fleet uses for “can soak”.
  • Habit. H4 (+1), H3 (+1). H10 (+1, Standard-Hunter): when the command convoy is the kept hand, the standard rides in the part that commits last, behind the great hull (§5 step 4).
  • Grains. Heartoak +3 (Bulwark, Breacher and Leviathan flags: the proud heart puts its greatest hull first), Ironbark +1. Under an Ashwood heart the hand is not kept (Captains’ independence).
Composition
PartFormationCore or screenRoleContactsDecision-bearing
The great hullLeviathan pair (1 + 1, screens itself), on the centre lanecore (breach)screen: the galleass1yes. It is a core, and it shells your wall
The wingCorsair wolfpack on the flank seamwolfpackstrike: a real raid on the seam, whose answer is the question1yes
The kept handRunner convoy (column), one cell behind the great hullcore (land)reserve (obeys the hinge)1yes
(optional)a 4th formation—idle / pin1no
  • Links: the wing AFTER(great hull draws fire) (1). The kept hand’s hinge (2). The great hull HOLD-UNTIL(kept hand commits) (3).
  • Leaves thin: the great hull is carved one cell inside your band, the only carving in the book that tells a Leviathan to come closer than it likes (its Grand Battery policy stands off at the edge of your longest reach, Ships §6). It is a Piercer’s best target of the war.
Choreography
  1. t 0. The great hull and the kept hand sail at offset 0 on the centre road. The hand trails one cell behind, the Screen’s root sign: the heavy leads the bearer by a cell, with its aspect locked on the hottest cell. The wing sails on the flank road and holds at the flank’s fear edge.
  2. t ≈ 20–24. The great hull reaches its carved station one cell inside the band and shells the sector’s wall. The kept hand stops at the staging cell: the last cell before the band where L1 and L2 part, about 8–10 ticks from either commit point. It does not choose yet.
  3. First fire on the great hull (≈ t 24). Lag 1, then the wing’s answering sail. It darts for the flank seam (Corsair: hit-and-run, hugs seams). Its dash is cut so it enters the band 12 ticks after the great hull’s first fire (≈ t 36; spacing floor, §11).
  4. The window W opens at the wing’s answering sail and closes 6 ticks after the wing enters the band (≈ t 42; 6 is a start value at §11’s floor, range 6–10). Hinge: IF(fire drawn on the wing inside W) → L2 : L1.
    • A · the wing was answered. A reach turned to the wing. The hand drives L2, the inboard lane that reach turned from.
    • B · the wing was not answered. The flank is quiet, and the unanswered wing has just shown it. The hand drives L1, the wing’s own lane.
    • Lag 1, then the hand drives from the staging cell (≈ 8–10 ticks), so it commits at ≈ t 51–53, ≥12 after the wing’s entry, and lands inside the backstop with 17 ticks to spare. The wing withdraws from its raid by ≈ t 54, the great hull creeps back to its standoff after the hand commits, and a docked Runner does not withdraw (Ships §7).
  5. The great hull holds its station, bow on the hottest cell, drinking fire, until the hand commits. Then it resumes its lineage policy and creeps back to its standoff to shell.
Signature
  • First time. The biggest ship on the board comes closer than big ships come and sits under your fire. A handful of raiders dart at a flank. Behind the great ship, one formation stops and does nothing. Then it goes, suddenly, and it goes where you are not.
  • Returning tell. A Leviathan inside the band (it never comes that close on its own), and a formation parked one cell behind it with its bow between two lanes. It has not chosen. The wing is the question: whether you fire on it decides which lane the hand takes.
Captains’ independence
  • The great hull is fanatical: it does not rout, and it will hold. If the Piercer sinks it before the hand commits, its HOLD-UNTIL simply ends. The hinge is unaffected.
  • The wing is skittish. If it is struck hard in its first dart it Wavers and sags aft, but that fire fell inside W (which opened at its answering sail), so the hinge still reads A. The fleet never sends its hand down a lane it has just seen defended.
  • The kept hand. A Runner balks if its chosen lane went SCALDING during W, and takes its fallback: the softest COLD coast.
  • An Ashwood heart (a Runner or Provocateur flag) launches reserve roles at offset 0 (§7). Its hand is not kept: it drives the habit-keyed lane, L2, the moment the great hull draws fire, without waiting for the wing. This is the rash grain’s characteristic error, and it is legible as a reserve that never stops.
  • Flag sunk mid-stratagem (Cam 26, so the clock fallback applies). The wing wakes at the tick the carving predicted for first fire, three ticks late (§4). The hinge takes its foretold branch at W’s close: both branches carry the payload, so the foretold one is the habit-keyed lane, L2 (§4 clause (b)). A decapitated Great Hull drives the inboard lane a beat after the hour, whether or not you fired on the wing.
Counters
  • B · Crack the great hull and stay put. Piercer on the Leviathan (the reason it exists, Cannon §11), with a Powder Store. Keep your group on the sector’s lanes and do not re-task to the wing: it is a raid, so answer it with Standard or Suppressor, and a Jammer to make it linger where guns can land. Put Interdictor + Breaker on the wall behind both lanes, because the hand must come to that wall whichever lane it takes.
  • C · Lepanto’s own answer. Keep a hidden battery silent until the kept hand commits. Whichever lane it opens down, it opens into guns that have not spoken.
  • C · Steer the hand. Your fire on the wing, inside the window, chooses the lane: fire and the hand takes the inboard lane; hold and it takes the wing’s. Put it where your alpha waits. With Command Time, pick up the flank group as the wing’s sail blanches; the hour slows and you choose the tick.
  • C · Open the hand early. It sits motionless at the staging cell for ≈ 20 ticks. A deep-centred AoR over that cell reaches it (≈35% leak to the core, ≈0.8 cores per passage, Cannon §5), or a Marksman takes its riders.
If it fails
  • Grading. Succeeded if the hand lands. Failed if the hand is eliminated or routed before its commit point. Both branches carry the payload and the wing is a strike, not a lure, so neither the hinge nor §5’s void-errand grade makes it Countered.
  • What it learned. A hand shot on a lane the carving believed COLD is a fresh H1 observation, and the fleet counts that the quarter holds its fire. Ask the Silent Water becomes a candidate next sortie. If the Leviathan was cracked, next sortie’s FIT drops.
  • By grain.
    • Heartoak (−1, +1 on the same approach) re-carves it on the same road.
    • Ironbark (Failed → +1) carves it heavier: a shared convoy (two Runner cores) becomes the hand.
    • Willow (−5) leaves the line for the Sounding.
    • Blackthorn turns on the silent battery once it has glinted, with a Hunt aimed at the lane where it lost the hand.
The Carving

The great hull first, and let it drink the speaking stone’s fire. The swift to the flank. Keep the hand closed until the swift are answered or are not; then open it where the reach is not.

Real-world root

Lepanto, 1571: the Venetian galleasses towed ahead of the Holy League’s line to break up the Ottoman array before contact, and Santa Cruz’s reserve, which plugged the gaps as they opened (research §3.11). Here the attacker holds the reserve.

E4-04

When the Stone Gives

“When the stone gives, the ram.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Siege · the Mute SquareP4 / 1–22, no hinge (3 with H11’s look)H6 blocks it; H11 adds a look
Era & debut

E4 · the Joined Tide · Cam 24, battle 4 (the Breacher debuted at Cam 23, the new troops at Cam 24 battle 2), in the threshold’s E3 grammar. It spans several sorties: it rides an objective The Same Stone has already worked

Grows from

E3-02 The Same Stone

Grows into

T-03 The Roots Beneath the Wall

The growth

This is The Same Stone with a ram waiting for the stone to give, and the host waiting for the ram.

Intent

“The stone we have struck for two days is thin. Strike it again. When it gives, the ram; when the ram is in, the host.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions.
    • A wall section with hits recorded in ≥2 sorties (The Same Stone’s persistent objective), and no mend observed there (a breach re-seen as wall, the only mend the fleet can see, §6).
    • A Bombard line and a Breacher wedge sail.
    • 2 links in budget.
  • Gain conditions.
    1. E3-02 was carved on this section this battle (LINE +1).
    2. A Runner convoy with troops sails (the host).
    3. The ram’s straight lane to the section has a station within one cell of it: the fear edge on that line, read once at carving, so the ram can wait close.
    4. The section’s fear is no worse than HOT, so the Bombards can stand.
  • Ghosts. The carving is cut against the wall the fleet remembers. The fleet reads blows, never HP (Fleet Memory §1; §19 LAW · THE LOOK IS A PRESENCE), so a section healed in Build before it ever opened is a stale positive it cannot revise: its ledger keeps the blows, and no shell or sightline shows it whole. Only a breach walled over can be seen mended.
  • Habit. H6 (Mender) blocks it. With H6 active, any section observed mended fails the precondition, and the Heartwood carves The Same Stone on a fresh section instead (§6). H11 (Kill-Box) adds a look: with H11 active the ram holds at the mouth of the breach HOLD-UNTIL(seen(the ring behind)) while the siege’s lead hull, or a Recon if one sails, looks through the gap. The ram goes in only after the look, and the host after the ram, so a kill-box meets a ram it has watched come, not a ram and a host in one pour. It is the carving’s third link, and the first link a decapitation removes.
  • Grains. Ironbark +3 (Bombard flags: the stubborn heart), Yew +1.
Composition
PartFormationCore or screenRoleContactsDecision-bearing
The siegeBombard line (2 shields, line abreast) on the sectioncore (breach)siege1yes
The ramBreacher wedge (3 shields), holding at its station on its own straight lane to the section (read once at carving)core (breach)strike1yes
The hostRunner convoy carrying the sortie’s troops (Sapper or Swarm stacks, a Standard-bearer; Pioneers where the ground is fragile)core (land)cargo1, plus 1 stack ashoreyes (the stack)
  • C 4: once the Runner beaches it never moves again (Ships §7) and becomes glanceable, so the stack is the decision. Decision-bearing 3–4.
  • Links: the ram AFTER(breach observed at the section) (1). The host AFTER(ram commits) (2), carved so the host beaches ≥12 ticks after the ram commits (§11). Both links have latest ticks.
  • “Breach observed” means a fleet hull or troop sees the section open (LOS or a troop touch revises the Contact). On your fort the breach fires its sun-ray (Fleet Memory §8), so you see the exact tick the fleet knows.
  • Leaves thin: the other roads get nothing. The ram idles at the fear edge until the stone gives.
Choreography
  • Sorties n−2 and n−1. The Same Stone works the section with Bombards and Sappers, and the ledger accumulates its hits.
  • Build/Deploy before sortie n. You may mend it. The fleet never sees a mend on a wall it has not breached: it reads blows, not HP.
  • Sortie n.
    1. t 0. The siege and the ram sail at offset 0 on the section’s road, the ram on its own straight line. The host sails at offset 0 on the lowest-fear lane to the coast beside the section and holds at its fear edge.
    2. t ≈ 20. The ram stops at its station, wedge closed, bow-on to the section.
    3. t ≈ 23. The Bombards come abreast at standoff and shell the section: the Siege’s root sign.
    4. Breach at t_b. This depends on the section’s remaining hits; three Bombards abreast breach live (Build §11). Lag 1, then the ram’s answering sail. It drives straight at the breach (≈ 8–13 ticks) and nothing distracts it (Ships §6).
    5. The ram commits. Lag 1, then the host drives in and beaches beside the breach ≥12 ticks later. Its troops come through as one: the Standard-bearer tightens their re-path batching, so the landing follows one weakest-run A* path instead of leaving a 5-cell ruin (Build §11). Pioneers bridge fragile ground at the breach.
    6. Latest ticks. If the stone has not given by t 38 (start value; range 34–42), the ram drives at the section anyway, a ram at a whole wall, and commits at ≈ t 46–51. The host follows ≥12 ticks behind and beaches at ≈ t 58–63, inside the 70-tick backstop. The latest tick is set so that ram commit + 12 + the host’s run ends by t 66 on every board; greybox confirms it (§3).
Signature
  • First time. The same wall again, and the same line of ships abreast on it. Off to one side a wedge sits bow-on at the edge of your reach and does not move while the wall is shelled. When the wall opens, the wedge is already coming, and the transports are behind it.
  • Returning tell. The Siege’s root sign (the same stone, line abreast, sortie after sortie) plus a closed wedge parked at the fear edge with its bow on the worked section. It is full and forward, which is how you know it is not a refusal.
Captains’ independence
  • The siege (Bombard, middling resolve). Heavy fire makes it Waver and sag aft, and the breach comes late or never. The ram then drives at its latest tick.
  • The ram is fear-immune and unbreakable. Only Breaker’s knockback buys time against it, and the Interdictor fires at the hull nearest the wall (Cannon §11).
  • The host. The Runner balks if the coast cell beside the breach went SCALDING. It takes the softest COLD coast, and its troops land away from the breach, piecemeal.
  • Flag sunk, Cam 24 (threshold). The links break. The ram drives at the section on its own policy (a straight line at one wall, its objective), and the host takes the softest COLD coast. The sequence is lost: stone, ram and host arrive when they arrive.
  • Flag sunk, from Cam 25. Clock links. The ram drives at the tick the carving predicted for the breach, three ticks late, breach or not, and the host at the predicted commit plus its gap, late by the same (§4).
Counters
  • B · The breakthrough kit. Interdictor + Breaker at the section (they arrived with the Breacher, Campaign §6). Stonewright on the section in Build, twice over. Splasher at the breach for the troop stack.
  • C · Mend it and let it believe. Heal the section in Build before it ever opens. The fleet reads blows, not HP, so it carves against the damage it remembers; its Bombards spend the sortie re-cracking a wall that is whole again, and the ram waits for a breach that comes late or never. At its latest tick it drives into a mended section under Interdictor + Breaker. The fleet never learns why: a mend on an unbreached wall is invisible, so H6 counts nothing and the lie can be told again.
  • C · Let the stone give into a second ring. Leave the section and make the courtyard behind it a kill-box of inner guns (overcommit-punish, Fleet Memory §6). The ram and the host pour into it in carved order, one at a time. Twice sprung, it teaches H11, and from then the ram looks into the ring before it goes: move the box, or let the look see an empty ring and fill it in the next Build.
  • C · Break the line, not the ram. The Bombards are cheap and they stand at standoff. Kill the line before the breach (Standard, Suppressor), and the ram idles at the fear edge until its latest tick, exposed to a deep-centred AoR.
If it fails
  • Grading. Succeeded if the breach is made and the ram commits. Failed if the ram and the host are eliminated before their commit points.
  • What it learned. A mend on a section it never breached teaches it nothing: the ledger keeps its blows and H6 counts no mend (§6), so a healed section keeps drawing the Siege line until it opens. H6 counts only a breach re-seen as wall. A ram killed at the section writes SCALDING there. Hulls or a troop stack lost inside a breached ring are an H11 count.
  • By grain.
    • Ironbark (Failed → +1, prefers the next form) cannot reach T-03, so it comes again at the same stone with every Bombard in the queue.
    • Yew (Countered 0) waits a sortie with Let the Fire Work on the section and returns.
    • Blackthorn (+2 where it lost most) comes back to the same section.
    • Willow (−5) leaves the Siege line for the Sounding.
The Carving

The stone we have struck two days, strike it the third. The ram waits at the edge of the dread. When the stone gives, the ram; when the ram is in, the host.

Real-world root

Constantinople, 1453: the final assault of 29 May went in through the stretch of land wall the great guns had broken over weeks; and Rochester, 1215, where King John’s miners brought down the corner of the keep (research §3.8).

E4-05

Along the Shore, Home by the Far Road

“Draw them along the shore; come home by the far road.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Lure · the Turned SternP3–4 / 2–33, one a hinge, so it needs the Cam 25 budget and leaves the book after one decapitationH4, H3
Era & debut

E4 · the Joined Tide · Cam 25, battle 4 (after Three Roads, One Hour)

Grows from

E3-03 Feigned Flight

Grows into

T-05 The Burning Retreat

The growth

This is Feigned Flight grown long and turned sideways. The line does not run home. It runs along your coast, and your reach runs after it.

Intent

“Their reach follows what it strikes. Give it ground one sector at a time along the shore, and keep the line. When it has followed into the second, come home by the far road into the arc it left.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions.
    • H4 active.
    • ≥2 live approaches with adjacent coast sectors.
    • A lure-capable formation sails: a Bombard line, or a Provocateur wolfpack. A strike sails: a Corsair wolfpack.
    • Station 2, the cell one fear cell along the coast from the lure’s first station, is believed COLD.
    • 3 links in budget.
  • Gain conditions.
    1. Station 1’s arc holds a wall or castle Contact: something to come home to.
    2. A cargo sails to follow the strike home: a Runner convoy, or a Skyfall.
    3. The last sortie’s re-task crawled along the coast in the lure’s direction (the fleet has seen your reach follow sideways).
    4. E3-03 was carved this battle (LINE +1).
  • Habit. H4 (+1), H3 (+1: they strike first and far, so they will fire on the lure at station 1).
  • Grains. Twistbough +2 (Corsair, Wraith and Mimic flags), Blackthorn +1.
Composition
PartFormationRoleContactsDecision-bearing
The lineBombard line (or a Provocateur wolfpack, from Cam 7)lure1yes
The raidersCorsair wolfpack, holding at the fear edge of station 1’s road (“the far road”)strike (obeys the hinge)1yes
The bearersRunner convoy at the same edge (or a Skyfall, whose bombers are released on the hinge)cargo (obeys the hinge)1 (+1 cloud)yes
  • Links: the line’s slide AFTER(fire drawn at station 1) (1). The hinge at station 2, shared by all three parts (2). The line HOLD-UNTIL(strike commits) at station 2, in branch A (3).
  • Leaves thin: the line fights alone. It is a real siege at each station, but no one is coming to help it.
Choreography
  1. t 0. The line sails at offset 0 to station 1, its standoff on the first sector. It cannot wait: this is the longest carving of the era. The raiders and the bearers sail at offset 0 on station 1’s road and stop at its fear edge, bow-on to station 1’s arc.
  2. t ≈ 23–24. The line engages at station 1 and draws fire.
  3. The slide. On the first fire at station 1, lag 1, then the line’s answering sail: the Feigned Flight’s turn, taken at the first volley. It gives ground sideways, one fear cell along the coast (≈ 13 ticks at 1 grid/tick), holding line abreast. That is the Lure’s root sign: a turn that keeps its shape, where a true rout scatters and runs for spawn (Ships §4). If you never fire on it at station 1, it slides anyway at its latest tick, station arrival + 6 (start value; range 4–10).
  4. t ≈ 38. Station 2. The line engages again. W = 6 ticks (start value at §11’s floor; range 6–10). Hinge: IF(a re-task toward station 2 is observed inside W) → A : B. The event is H4’s own, read live (§4): fire on the line at station 2 arriving from the bearing that struck it at station 1. Station 2 was believed COLD, outside that bearing’s reach, so fire from that bearing there means the reach that held station 1 has walked after the line. It is a read of where your reach went, not of whether you fired.
    • A · the reach followed. The raiders and the bearers drive down the far road into station 1’s arc. The Corsairs, faster than 1 grid/tick, commit at ≈ t 50–52; the bearers at ≈ t 53–58. The line holds station 2 until the raiders commit.
    • B · no one followed. No fire came, or it came from another bearing: station 1’s arc is still watched. The line turns back in at station 2 (Feigned Flight’s turn) and works the station-2 sector as a real siege. The raiders and the bearers hold as a pin, and at their latest tick take their fallbacks: the Corsair raids where fear is lowest, and the Runner beelines the softest COLD coast.
  5. The tick budget. The raiders are home from their raid by ≈ t 64 and the line, released when they commit, by ≈ t 64; the bearers dock and do not withdraw (Ships §7). Everything clears the 70-tick backstop with ≈ 6 to spare.
Signature
  • First time. A line of ships you are beating turns away. It does not turn home. It moves along your coast in perfect order, and you follow it with your guns. Far behind where it started, two formations sit at the edge of your reach and do not move. When your guns have moved, they do.
  • Returning tell. The Turned Stern: a formation giving ground in its shape. What names this form is the direction (sideways, along the shore, one sector at a time), and the formations waiting at the fear edge of the road it left.
Captains’ independence
  • The line. A Bombard line under heavy fire can genuinely Waver, and its slide becomes a sag aft that loosens. If it breaks at station 1 there is no station 2, the hinge sees no walk there, and the branch is B. A Provocateur line is fanatical and never breaks.
  • The raiders are skittish. If fire reaches them while they wait (your reach never left), they jink and may Waver before the hinge. The far road visibly fails before it begins.
  • The bearers. The Runner balks if station 1’s arc went SCALDING during the sortie.
  • Flag sunk mid-stratagem (from Cam 25). The slide keeps its predicted tick, station 1 arrival + 1, three ticks late (§4). The hinge takes its foretold branch, A (the far road carries the payload, §4 clause (b)), at W’s close, late by the same. A decapitated Kalka always comes home by the far road, a beat after the hour, into your station-1 group if it never left.
Counters
  • B · Each group keeps its arc. Answer the line at each station with the group whose arc it is in, and never re-task after it. The fire at station 2 then comes from another bearing: no walk is observed, the hinge reads B, and the line fights a real siege at station 2 alone while the far road sits as a pin and comes late, on its fallbacks, into a station-1 arc that is still covered. This is Kalka’s answer: do not pursue. Suppressor and Interdictor on station 1’s coast make any homecoming expensive.
  • C · Follow with fire from a group that already reaches both. Answer station 2 from the group that fired at station 1, without re-tasking it: a group whose arc already covers both stations. The fire comes from station 1’s bearing, the fleet reads a walk that never happened, and the raiders and the bearers come home by the far road into guns that never left.
  • C · Burn the far road first. The raiders and the bearers sit motionless at the fear edge for ≈ 30 ticks. Deep-centre an AoR there early, and the cell they wait in is no longer COLD.
  • C · Keep station 2 silent. Hold the group that covers it. The branch is B: the line turns back in and fights a real siege at station 2 alone, and the far road never wakes.
If it fails
  • Grading. Countered if B: no walk was observed at station 2, the branch this entry names Countered (§5). Failed if the raiders and the bearers are eliminated before their commit points. Succeeded if the bearers land or the raiders reach their arc.
  • What it learned. An A-branch homecoming shot on station 1’s arc means that arc was still covered. H4 is contradicted, and two contradictions deactivate it. A silent B branch confirms station 2 as quiet water, and the next sortie’s lanes will use it. A B branch answered from another bearing shows each arc keeping its own, which also contradicts H4.
  • By grain.
    • Twistbough (Failed −3; Countered −2) moves to the Feint and Mask lines. It would not repeat this even if it had succeeded (Succeeded −2).
    • Blackthorn (+2 where it lost most) turns on the arc that shot its raiders, with a Hunt once a gun there glints.
    • Ironbark (Failed → +1) carves it again heavier: a Bombard line lures, and a shared convoy comes home.
    • Willow (−5) drops it.
The Carving

Give them the shore a sector at a time, and keep your line. When the reach has walked to follow into the second, the far road: the swift and the bearers home to the first.

Real-world root

The Kalka River, 1223: Jebe and Subutai drew the Rus’ and Cuman host after them through a nine-day retreat, strung it out, and turned on it at the river (research §3.6).

E4-06

Sea Below, Sky Above

“When they strike the water, fall from the sky.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Bleed · the BreathP3 / 1–21H8 selects the form: the reversed form is chosen at carving, not live
Era & debut

E4 · the Joined Tide · Cam 26, battle 3

Grows from

E3-07 Between Their Breaths · E2-04 Noise in the East

Grows into

T-06 The Slow Rot

The growth

Between Their Breaths timed the reload, the gap a group cannot help. This carving times the switch, the gap a group opens by choice.

Intent

“The guns that strike our hulls in the water carry sea-shot. Give them hulls to strike. When they strike, send the sky to the house behind those guns, in the hour the hulls touch shore, so that whatever they load, one blow lands.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions.
    • A Skyfall sails (a Skyfall pair, battlegroup or mixed convoy).
    • A sea formation able to press a coast sails: a Runner convoy, else a Corsair wolfpack.
    • A castle Contact lies within bomber reach behind that coast.
    • 1 link in budget.
  • Gain conditions.
    1. Last sortie’s fear on the sea blow’s road came from a single bearing, so one group is believed to cover both the coast and the castle behind it.
    2. The castle has recorded hits (a softened target).
    3. E3-07 or E2-04 was carved this battle (LINE +1).
    4. The Skyfall is fully screened (the carrier can wait at the back line unharmed).
  • Habit. H8 (Sky-Ready) selects the form. Inactive: normal form, sea first. Active: reversed form, sky first (+1).
  • Grains. Yew +2 (Skyfall and Sower flags: the patient heart holds its deck shut until you fire).
Composition
PartFormationRoleContactsDecision-bearing
The sea blowRunner convoy (column), or a Corsair wolfpacklure: a real landing or raid, sent to draw sea fire at coast X1yes
The carrierSkyfall (ring or testudo: 4 shields + 1 rider) at the back linestrike1yes
The cloudits bomber cloud, after releasestrike1yes
  • Link: the Skyfall AFTER(sea fire drawn at X) plus a carved release offset (1). Latest tick: if no sea fire has come by the tick that still lets the cloud meet the sea blow’s commit, it launches anyway.
  • Target castle = the castle nearest the bearing the sea fire at X came from (the Effect channel’s per-shell bearing, Fleet Memory §3; ties: §14). The bombers go to the house beside the guns that fired, which is the house that group most likely covers. This carving reads no loaded domain; the general Skyfall seam still has to close in Ships (§24).
  • Leaves thin: the sea blow sails without the carrier’s riders, and the carrier’s screen is all it has at the back.
Choreography
  • Normal form (H8 inactive).
    1. t 0. The sea blow sails at offset 0 on the lowest-fear lane to coast X. The Skyfall holds at the back line with its deck shut (Skyfall: very slow, never approaches, Ships §5).
    2. t ≈ 24. The sea blow enters the band and draws sea fire at X. Lag 1, then the Skyfall’s answering sail, and its launch wind-up: the deck opens, the Skyfall’s own telegraph (Ships §5).
    3. Release is carved so the cloud reaches the target castle within ±2 ticks of the sea blow’s commit at X (≈ t 39 for a Runner’s full passage). The Admiral knows its own bombers’ speed. ±2 is a start value (range ±1–4), settled in greybox so the dilemma bites a single-group defender and never a split one.
    4. Law check. The deck opens ≥12 ticks before the cloud arrives: the 8-count switch (Cannon §6-B) plus a reload. A group that switches on the telegraph is loaded in time. What it cannot be is loaded for both. That is a dilemma, not simultaneity: one costed move answers it, and Deploy answers it for free (§11; Fight §6).
  • Reversed form (H8 active: bombers hit within 4 ticks of release two sorties running, §6).
    1. The Skyfall launches at offset 0, and the cloud draws the Air-ready group’s fire.
    2. The sea blow holds at the fear edge AFTER(bombers released), then drives so it commits about one switch-count (≈ 8 ticks) after the cloud arrives. The group that answered the sky must reload for the sea.
Signature
  • First time. Ships come at your coast and you shoot them. The great carrier at the back, which had done nothing, opens its deck. Bombers reach the castle behind your firing guns just as the ships reach the beach.
  • Returning tell. The Breath: one sea formation goes in alone while the carrier waits deck-shut at the back line. The deck opens on an answering sail one tick after your first shell lands on the sea blow. The launch is waiting for your fire. In the reversed form the deck opens first, and a column sits at the fear edge until the bombers are loosed.
Captains’ independence
  • The carrier is patient and rarely wavers. Launching exposes it (Ships §8). A carrier sunk before launch is a large tempo and resolve win.
  • The sea blow. A Runner balks at SCALDING. A Corsair is skittish: hit hard, it may turn out before it has drawn much fire, and the launch then waits for its latest tick.
  • The bombers approach fear-aware on flak, with a deterministic ±1 crazy-Ivan jitter (Ships §8).
  • Flag sunk mid-stratagem (Cam 26). The launch becomes a clock. The deck opens at the tick the carving predicted for sea fire, three ticks late (§4), fired upon or not, and the target is the castle the carving predicted. The deck opens a beat after the hour, and the bombers’ ±2 meeting with the landing is lost.
Counters
  • B · Split the domains at Deploy. Load one group Air over the castles behind the coast and one group Sea on the coast. No group has to choose. Add Saturation for the cloud: a cloud does not stack, and Saturation is the wide answer (Cannon §11).
  • C · Choose its target. The cloud goes to the castle nearest the guns that fired on X. Answer X from the group beside your best-covered castle, or beside an Air-loaded group, and the bombers fly into your flak.
  • C · Switch at the deck, not at the bombers. The deck-open is the telegraph. A switch begun then completes its 8-count well before the cloud arrives. Leave the landing to a Splasher and the inner ring.
  • C · Sink the carrier before the deck opens. It is slow and sits at the back: a deep-centred AoR, a Piercer through a Fleet Carrier, or a Marksman on its rider.
  • C · Hold the sea fire. The launch waits for your first shell on X. Hold until the landing commits, then alpha it. The cloud arrives a flight-time later, after the landing is already broken.
If it fails
  • Grading. Succeeded if bombers are released over the castle (§5) or the landing lands. Failed if the carrier sinks before launch and the sea blow is eliminated before its commit point.
  • What it learned. Bombers hit within 4 ticks of release are an H8 observation; two sorties of it make Sky-Ready active, and the next carving is the reversed form. A cloud shot down over the target castle writes air fear there, so the next launch picks another castle.
  • By grain.
    • Yew (Countered 0: it waits and retries later) carves a Patience form for a sortie (Let the Fire Work, Spent or Waiting) and returns to this.
    • Willow (−5) turns to the Sounding.
    • Twistbough moves to a Mask form.
    • Silverbark (Countered −4) drops it once your domains are seen split.
The Carving

Send hulls to the water. When the speaking stone strikes them, open the deck, and let the sky fall on the house behind that stone in the hour the hulls touch shore.

The reversed form, as it is told: They watch the sky. Fall first; and when their stone looks up, the hulls to the water. In each, one of Halyna begins and the other finishes.

Real-world root

Midway, 1942: Nagumo, his reserve re-arming for a second strike on the island, learned of the American carriers and had to choose between two loads on one re-arming cycle. The dive bombers arrived as the torpedo squadrons had drawn his fighters down to the sea (research §3.16).

E4-07

Borrow the Road

“Pass the quiet gun; know it; take it going home.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Hunt · the SparkP3–4 / 1–22, no hingeH1
Era & debut

E4 · the Joined Tide · Cam 24, battle 3, in the threshold’s E3 grammar

Grows from

E3-05 Make an Example

Grows into

E5-06 The Prize Too Easy

The growth

Make an Example punished the gun that spoke. Borrow the Road punishes the gun that hoped silence would hide it. From here on, silence protects only a gun the wood cannot see. “Going home” is the hunters’ road, not the convoy’s: a Runner never leaves the beach (Ships §7), so the hunt is released after the landing commits.

Intent

“Their quiet quarter hides a gun, and they believe silence hides it. Take the bearers past it on the way in, with eyes that see, and do not turn. Let the bearers land. Then the hunters take it.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions.
    • H1 active: a quarter that holds its fire.
    • A lane to a believed-soft coast passes that quarter within Recon sight range (Recon has the fleet’s long sight, Ships §5).
    • A convoy with a Recon rider sails, or a Recon picket escorts one. A Reaver pack sails.
    • 2 links in budget.
  • Gain conditions.
    1. The quarter holds no glinted gun yet. The gun is suspected, not known: the quarter sits in twilight on your fort (Fleet Memory §8).
    2. The lane passes the quarter’s fear edge without entering HOT, so the convoy is not the question.
    3. The last sortie’s Reavers destroyed the gun they hunted (hunting pays).
    4. E3-05 was carved this battle (LINE +1).
  • Habit. H1 (+1).
  • Grains. Blackthorn +3 (Reaver flags: the vengeful heart), Ashwood +1.
Composition
PartFormationCore or screenRoleContactsDecision-bearing
The transitRunner convoy (column behind a Bulwark) with a Recon rider: the eyescore (land) + shields + ridercargo1, plus 1 stack ashoreyes
The huntersReaver pack (2–4 hulls, scatter), trailing the convoy a cell astern at the fear edgewolfpackhunt1yes
(optional)a 4th formation—idle / pin1no
  • Links: the hunters AFTER(X glinted) (1) and HOLD-UNTIL(cargo commits) (2). They break for X when both are true. Their trailing distance is carved so they reach X ≥12 ticks after the convoy commits (§11).
  • Leaves thin: the Reavers are not screening the transit, and the transit’s escort is all it has.
Choreography
  1. t 0. The convoy sails at offset 0 on a lane that skirts the quarter at Recon sight range. The hunters follow a cell astern.
  2. t ≈ 12–24. The convoy passes the quarter. The Recon’s sight writes Contact on any exposed gun in range (LOS feeds Contact, Fleet Memory §2). On your fort that gun jumps from twilight to noon: it is glinted without having fired. Nothing turns toward it. If the gun fires on the convoy it glints by flash instead: the same result, sooner.
  3. t ≈ 39. The convoy commits to the coast (a Runner commits to the touch), and its troops land.
  4. Cargo committed and X glinted. Lag 1, then the hunters’ answering sail. They break from the convoy’s wake and drive at X, keeping their fix in sight and ignoring walls and castles (Ships §5), arriving ≈ t 51.
  5. If nothing glints, the hunters take their fallback: the freshest fix in the ledger, else pin.
Signature
  • First time. A column of transports sails right past one of your quiet guns, close enough to see it, and does nothing. On your own fort map that gun lights up in full colour as they pass. A pack of hunters trails the column and does not turn. The column lands. Then the hunters turn, and they come for the gun you thought was hidden.
  • Returning tell. A Reaver pack that does not bend when your gun glints: the Hunt’s root sign, held back. It shadows the transit instead. The glint on your fort, from a gun that never fired, is the other half of the tell.
Captains’ independence
  • The hunters keep station in the convoy’s wake until released. If the convoy’s cores die before committing, the HOLD-UNTIL can never be met. They take their latest tick and come for X anyway, late. Once released they over-commit on a juicy fix.
  • The transit. The Runner balks if its lane went SCALDING. If its cores die, its shields fork (revenge wolfpack or retreat) and the Recon rider, skittish, runs for spawn.
  • Flag sunk, Cam 24 (threshold). The links break, and the hunters act on local beliefs at once. They bend for X the moment it glints, before the landing: the crude Strike the Flash (E1-05) comes back. The plan loses its patience.
  • Flag sunk, from Cam 25. Clock links. The hunters break at the predicted commit tick, three ticks late (§4), whether or not the convoy has landed.
Counters
  • B · Camouflage or enclose the quiet quarter’s guns. Walls break sightlines (Fleet Memory §5). A gun the Recon cannot see cannot be glinted by sight, and a gun behind walls does not glint by flash. Then silence works again. Or cover X with a second group’s AoR and a Suppressor, so the pack meets fire from guns it has not fixed.
  • C · Kill the knower. The Recon rider is the eyes. The Marksman ignores screens and deletes the highest-value target (Cannon §5). Take the Recon before the column reaches sight range of your quarter, and the Runners’ short sight passes X in the dark.
  • C · Speak, since you are seen. X will be known either way. Fire on the column as it passes and make the knowledge cost them, then relocate X in the next Deploy so the Contact becomes a ghost (bait-and-relocate, Fleet Memory §6).
  • C · Meet the hunters on their schedule. Their strike comes after the landing, from the column’s wake, on a short lane you can read. Pre-task a group’s reach onto that lane and hold its alpha for the pack.
If it fails
  • Grading. Succeeded if X is destroyed or the landing lands. Failed if the convoy’s cores and the hunters are eliminated before their commit points. Countered if nothing glints: the hunt’s errand never began (Victory §4b).
  • What it learned. If walls or Camouflage hid X, the quarter stays twilight and H1 persists: the fleet cannot see what it suspects. Next sortie it asks instead, and Ask the Silent Water becomes a candidate. If X was relocated after its glint, H5 (Relocator) counts it.
  • By grain.
    • Blackthorn (+2 where it lost most) returns to X’s quarter with Make an Example.
    • Ashwood (Failed −2, Hunt +1) carves it again if the FIT holds.
    • Willow (−5) asks the water instead.
    • Twistbough moves on.
The Carving

Take the bearers past the speaking stone that keeps silence, near enough to see it, and do not turn. When the bearers are ashore and the stone is known, the hunters take it on the road home.

Real-world root

The Thirty-Six Stratagems #24: in 655 BC Jin borrowed passage through Yu to strike Guo, and took Yu on the way home (research §1).

E4-08

Thick and Hollow

“Look heavy where you are light.”
LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Mask · the Smoothed CutP3–4 / 22, no hingeH7, H9, H10
Era & debut

E4 · the Joined Tide · Cam 26 (CDE, no lineage debut), battle 4

Grows from

E2-08 Grey Before the Sails

Grows into

E5-05 Straw, Then Men · E5-07 The First Ship Carries (it borrows the draught seam)

The growth

Grey Before the Sails hid the landing in smoke. Here the mask is the formation itself. It is also Cheapest First’s logic (E1-04) at convoy scale: the thick convoy is the plum.

Intent

“They kill our bearers first, and they judge a bearer by its escort. Send the thickest where their hunters wait, laden with little. Hold the thin one, laden, at the edge of the dread. When their hunters have begun, the thin one to the soft shore.”

Chosen when
  • Preconditions.
    • H7 active (Core-Hunter: they kill our bearers first).
    • Two convoys sail whose thickness and load run opposite ways. One is visibly thick (the most hulls in its screen, riders included) and carries the lighter troop load. The other is visibly thin (shields lost and not refilled, but above the refusal threshold, Victory §4a) and carries the heavier load. The Admiral never moves hulls or riders (§5 law). The pair must exist in the queue, authored or made by the player’s own shield-stripping (§5 step 4; §24).
    • A believed-soft coast lies on a second road.
    • 2 links in budget.
  • Gain conditions.
    1. The thick convoy carries a Chirurgeon-Fleet or Quartermaster rider, so it can soak (heal-pulse, shield-beam).
    2. Last sortie’s cores died in one fear area, to one Effect bearing, so the core-hunter’s ground and bearing are known (§4; §6 H7). That area is the lure’s objective.
    3. The thin convoy’s road is COLD.
    4. E2-08 was carved this battle (LINE +1).
  • Losses so far. The thin convoy is thin because the player made it so: its shields died last sortie, and the nerve route was not finished.
  • Habit. H7 (+1). H9 (+1, Patient Shore): with H9 active the thin’s latest tick is re-cut to the thick’s predicted engagement from the core-hunter’s bearing, plus the lag (§6), so holding fire on the thick no longer keeps the thin at the edge. H10 (+1, Standard-Hunter): when the command convoy is the thin one, the standard rides where the core-hunter does not look (§5 step 4).
  • Grains. Twistbough +2 (the cunning heart). Silverbark reads H7 twice. Ironbark −2 and Heartoak −2: the stubborn and the proud will not mask.
Composition
PartFormationCore or screenRoleContactsDecision-bearing
The thickthe thick-and-light convoy, e.g. a shared Runner convoy with Quartermaster and Chirurgeon riders1–2 cores + shields + riderslure, objective draw-fire on the core-hunter’s ground1yes
The thinthe thin-and-laden Runner convoy, holding at the fear edge of the soft roadcore + 2–3 shieldscargo1, plus 1 stack ashoreyes
(optional)a wolfpack on a third roadwolfpackpin1no
  • Links: the thin AFTER(thick draws fire from the core-hunter's bearing) (1), with its latest tick carved so it still commits ≥10 ticks inside the backstop. The event is a target choice, not merely fire: the bearing the fleet’s cores died to last sortie (§4). The thick HOLD-UNTIL(thin commits) (2).
  • Leaves thin: the thin convoy, literally. The thick convoy is a real convoy with a real core and a light load, and the fleet accepts losing it.
Choreography
  1. t 0. The thick convoy sails at offset 0 on a lane toward the fear area where the fleet’s cores died last sortie: the ground it believes your core-hunters cover. The thin convoy sails at offset 0 on the soft road and stops at its fear edge.
  2. t ≈ 23. The thick convoy enters the band and draws the core-hunter’s fire (deep-centred AoRs, the Marksman). Its Chirurgeon heals and its Quartermaster shields and smokes. It keeps coming slowly and holds its station in the fire.
  3. Fire on the thick from the core-hunter’s bearing. Lag 1, then the thin convoy’s answering sail. Fire from any other bearing is not the event: the fleet is waiting to see its hunter take the bait. It drives for the soft coast (≈ 8–16 ticks) and commits at ≈ t 35–40, ≥12 after the thick’s first fire.
  4. The thin commits. The thick is released to its lineage policy: it beelines the softest COLD coast and lands its light load if it can.
Signature
  • First time. The biggest, best-guarded convoy you have yet seen comes straight at the guns you use for killing bearers. Your guns go to work on it, and hulls inside it glow and mend. Meanwhile a thin, battered column on the far road, which you had written off, runs for the beach.
  • Returning tell. The Smoothed Cut: a thick formation riding high, and on another road a thin one riding low, waiting at the fear edge. Two further seams show in the thick one. Its riders never step into your fire; only its shields interpose (Ships §9: a rider-heavy formation looks thick and is not). And it rides high because it is light.
Captains’ independence
  • The thick convoy’s riders are skittish. When its cores are struck hard they sag aft (Wavering), and the thick formation visibly thins under fire. The Rivenmen call it the blossoms falling.
  • The thin convoy sailed above its refusal threshold. Refusal is judged at the sortie boundary, so losing a shield in transit does not turn it back. It balks if its road went SCALDING.
  • Orphan fork. If the thick convoy’s last core dies, its shields turn wolfpack (revenge, Committed) or retreat, and its riders run.
  • Flag sunk mid-stratagem (Cam 26). Clock links. The thin convoy drives at the predicted tick (the thick’s predicted first fire plus lag), three ticks late (§4), whether or not the thick drew fire. The thick holds until the thin’s predicted commit.
Counters
  • B · Cover every coast. The thin convoy runs for the soft coast, so do not leave one: Standard and Splasher at the beach, Interdictor at the wall. Saturation with a Spotter over both convoys.
  • B · The fire is not wasted. A Marksman on the thick convoy still kills its Quartermaster and Chirurgeon, the back-line targets the Marksman exists for (Cannon §11).
  • C · Read the draught. A laden Runner rides low and the thick convoy rides high. Put the core-hunting on the thin column.
  • C · Answer the thick from another bearing. The thin convoy waits for your core-hunter to open on the thick. Keep the core-hunting group off it, and let a group on another bearing, or plain Standard fire from any group but that one, deal with the thick on its way in. That fire is not the thin’s event, so the thin waits at the fear edge to its latest tick and comes alone and late into the alpha your core-hunter held for it. With H9 active its latest tick is re-cut, and it comes at the hour it expected your hunter to fire: be on its road already.
If it fails
  • Grading. Countered if the thick draws no fire (the lure’s errand is void). Failed if the thin convoy’s cores die before its commit point. Succeeded if the thin lands.
  • What it learned. The order in which its hulls sank is counted again for H7 (§6). If the thin’s cores died first while the thick drew little fire, H7 stands but the bait has been read. If the thick’s riders died first, the fleet learns that you hunt its support, not its bearers.
  • By grain.
    • Twistbough (Succeeded −2, Failed −3) never repeats it and turns to the Lure or Feint lines.
    • Silverbark (Countered −4) drops it fastest once the draught has been read.
    • Ironbark and Heartoak almost never carve it.
The Carving

Put blossom on the bare bough and send it where they kill our bearers. Hold the laden one thin at the edge of the dread; when the stone that kills our bearers strikes the blossom, the thin one to the soft shore.

Halyna tell it by turns, one beginning and the other finishing.

Real-world root

The Thirty-Six Stratagems #29, Deck the tree with false blossoms: in the Romance of the Three Kingdoms, Zhang Fei at Changban has horsemen drag branches to raise dust like a host (research §1). This is joined to Ships §9’s own free deception: a rider-heavy formation looks thick and is not.

21E5 · THE TIDE THAT LEARNED DECEIT (Rhenthae)

Cam 27–31 · and THE LAST TIDE (Eirthae) · Cam 32 · decoys, the long carving, chain hinges, half-life, 4 habits · “the wood does not lie; it was told to make us believe one”

The growing. Rhenthae, “stone-tide”, is the wood grown after the groves had known a whole war of Rivenmen feints. Every battery that held its fire and then sprang, every gun moved after it glinted, every breach left looking shut, went home through the roots (fiction_hooks §3). The wood still cannot lie (§4; fiction_hooks §4). So it carves orders that are each true and together mislead: a real prize sent to be seen, real ships sailing like the young wood, straw that is honestly straw until it is honestly men. “The wood does not lie. It was told to make us believe a lie, and it obeyed.”

How organised it is. As organised as the fleet ever gets below the Thrones. Everything from the Joined Tide stays: three links, WITH across three roads, anticipation, clock fallback on decapitation, and a 1-tick propagation lag while the flag lives (§4, §13). What this Tide adds is aim: each carving is cut against a habit the fleet has counted in you (§6). The Joined Tide learned to act as one. This Tide learns to act on the player.

What the player is learning to read: capability, not display. Every false show has a seam, and every seam is something present on screen, never something missing (§9a; Victory §4a):

  • Projections never jink when shells fall near them and never leave a wreck. One shell on any copy dispels them all (Ships §6).
  • A laden hull rides low. An empty one rides high.
  • A withdrawal that keeps its spacing is carved. A rout scatters (Ships §4).
  • The wood cannot hide its waking. An answering sail on a ship that has nothing to wake to (§4) is this Tide’s loudest seam.
  • A prize is thin only because the fleet really lost its shields.

The dispatch changes meaning without changing its law (§9c red line). It still reports what the fleet learned and never what it will do. But in this Tide, what the fleet learned is what it aims at. “They marked that the north holds its fire” becomes a warning to build against in Deploy. Or it is a habit the player fed on purpose (§6: over-fitting is the feature).

Budget and roster.

DialValueSource
Contacts, board-wide6–8. Reached either by payload sorties (3–4 contacts, all decision-bearing, 19–24 hulls) or by preparation sorties (6–8 wolfpacks)Victory §4b
Per E5 carvingAll seven are clever (P): ≤4 contacts and ≤4 decision-bearing, carved on payload sorties only. Preparation sorties carve the older W forms wolfpacks can fill, or no stratagem (§5 step 1)§11
Approaches3 liveVictory §3
Links3 per carving, ≤1 of them a hinge (§4 LAW · COUNTING LINKS). From Cam 29 the hinge may be a chain§4, §13
Propagation lag1 tick while the flag lives; clock links after decapitation§4
Spacing / hinge window≥12 ticks between linked arrivals one group must answer. Hinge windows ≥6 ticks§11
Habits active3 at Cam 27–28; 4 from Cam 29§6
LineagesThe whole sea roster except the boss-only Herald: Bombard, Recon, Runner (with Swarm, Sapper, Aegis, Linebreaker, Infiltrator, Standard-bearer and Pioneer troops), Skyfall and bombers, Quartermaster, Bulwark, Sower, Reaver, Provocateur, Breacher, Corsair, common Leviathan, Wraith, Chirurgeon-Fleet. Plus the Mimic from Cam 27: solo, never shoots, outside the purpose denominatorShips §5–§9; Campaign §5

The ramp inside the Tide.

CamLayersWhat turnsCarving debut
27ABCDThe Mimic sails alone. The Heartwood gives it only idle, so it lies in its own lineage manner (disguise or projection), uncommanded. The player learns its seams in isolation. C turns, so no capability—
28ABCENothing in the Admiral (C turns)E5-06 The Prize Too Easy. It needs only E3 machinery, and nothing in it is false
29ABDEThe deceit family (below)E5-02 Come as the Young Wood Came, the first decoy (one sortie) · E5-05 Straw, Then Men, on a later battle, the first long carving, where the Heartwood takes command of the Mimic
30ACDENo Layer B, so no lineage debuts: every hull on the board is one the player has met (§13 LAW · BREADTH ENTERS WITH LAYER B)E5-04 The Fourth Time: a new patience carried by known hulls · E5-07 The First Ship Carries, on a later battle: the fleet’s answer to a shore that lets the first ship pass
31BCDE—E5-03 Fortitude, which combines decoy, long carving and feint
32ABCDEThe Last Tide (C turns, so no capability)E5-01 The Closing Hand: the Flood’s last form, thirty-one campaigns after its first word

There is one debut per battle, never on a campaign’s first battle, and never on the battle that introduces the Mimic (§13). The calendar is §16a; CALL-OUT 19 asks Jack to confirm it.

The deceit family (Cam 29). §13 names the family and why its parts are one idea. This section specifies them. E5-02 carries it first, on a battle of its own; the long carving, which no single sortie can show, is carried by E5-05 on a later battle (§13).

  1. The decoy role. A decoy is a formation that is not what it looks like (§3): projections, real ships in false dress, or a routine. A decoy is decision-bearing (§11), and every decoy names its seam.
  2. The long carving (multi-sortie conditioning): one stratagem over several sorties, with a standing part re-carved identically at every boundary, stage tests read at the boundary, break rules and a cash-in (§4, Long carvings). While a lie stands, the sortie’s own carving must fit ≤3 decision-bearing roles.
  3. Chain hinges. A hinge’s second branch may be a whole older stratagem, carved in full before launch (§4, Chain hinges). Taking the chain grades the parent Countered, and the chain is graded on its own objective. So every late carving carries an ancestor inside it, and the ancestor’s old counter still answers it. Each entry below names its chain.
  4. Half-life. A stratagem Countered twice is retired for the battle (§5 step 3; the Q-ship decay, research §3.14).
  5. The fourth habit (§6).
LAW · THE WOOD CANNOT LIE; A CARVING MAY MISLEAD

Every part of an E5 carving is a true order, executed as carved. The deception lies in how the parts combine and in what they aim at. Every false show carries a seam that the player can see without aiming. The Mimic may lie about ships, never about the exposure glint: it provokes real glints and never forges one (Fleet Memory §8; Jack’s ruling, CALL-OUT 35). Apart from the Mimic, only the decoy role may make a shape lie, and only with its seam (Ships §9: a shape never lies “by accident”).

RED LINE · A SEAM NEVER LIES

A hull that does not jink under a near miss is always a projection. A hull that rides low is always laden. A formation that keeps its spacing going aft is always under a carving. Seams render true sim state and are never authored per board. A player who trusts a seam must never be punished for trusting it.

Decapitation in this Tide.

  • Links become clock links, three ticks late and a tick apart by spawn order, and a hinge becomes a clock too: when its window closes it takes its foretold branch, whatever was observed (§4). A chain hinge foretells its parent’s own branch, never the chain (§4 clause (a)): the chain is the fleet’s second thought. Kill the heart, and the fleet keeps the hour, late, and the first intent, but loses the second thought.
  • A long carving survives the standard’s first fall, unless the successor’s grain breaks it (§4).
  • The home clause. If the standard falls a second time in the same sortie, every formation of the running carving withdraws in shape to spawn (the Lure root) instead of following its lineage policy. Retreat is still not safe passage (Victory §4). The clause is a fallback value (§3), not new machinery. It ships with the Cam 29 family and is first known at Cam 32 (CALL-OUT 26).

The Last Tide (Cam 32, Eirthae). Cam 32 turns piece-layer C, so it unlocks nothing (§13). It is the eldest wood that ever sailed: the whole book of 38 carvings, four habits at capacity, every E5 hinge in its chained form, and one debut, The Closing Hand. That debut is the Flood’s last form, thirty-one campaigns after “To the shore.”

What changes at Cam 32 is the Knowing. From Cam 32 Halyna know and tell the whole carving, including the branch the fleet did not take and each formation’s fallback, home clause included. It is still only this battle’s carving, so it explains and never forecasts (§9b). This is The Plan That Carved Its Own Defeat (Legends V.7, Part III): the first enemy plan for losing that the Rivenmen knew.

Recognition in this Tide.

  • The Knowing is fluent and names the carving’s aim: “Cut against: …” (§9b). The Carving lines below are what Halyna knew from the wreck-wood and told together: one of them touches the grain, what one knows the other knows, and one begins each line and the other finishes it, with Seren setting it down (fiction_hooks §7).
  • Each Carving’s second line is the chain. Until Cam 32 it is known only when the fleet took it.
  • Partial live readings begin in this Tide. A hull of the running carving that sinks mid-sortie floats its line’s root sign, which names the family and never the form (§9b).
  • Every carving keeps its line’s root sign (§9a), so the returning player’s question is always which Flood, Feint or Mask this is.
  • Every hinge window below is ≥6 ticks wide. In Command Time each window is a tick the player picks on purpose, never a reflex (§12). Stable doctrine, sensitive detail (§8): the carving chosen rarely changes, but which branch it takes turns on a single tick.
E5-01

The Closing Hand

“Give them the middle; take the sides; close the hand.”

The middle shells, takes your fire and gives ground in good order. Two hands wait, still, at the fear edge of each flank, then close one finger at a time: the stronger flank’s guns, the weaker flank’s castle, and a landing behind the weaker flank. If the middle draws no fire, the landing floods the middle instead.

LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Flood · the Wave (capstone)P4 / 33, one the chain hinge (chain: E2-01)H2, H4
Era & debut

E5 · the Last Tide · Cam 32 (CALL-OUT 19)

Grows from

E4-01 Three Roads, One Hour · E3-03 Feigned Flight

Grows into

Thaesaen’s Judgement (the Leviathan’s), carved whole at the Thrones (At the Throne, below)

The growth

This is Three Roads, One Hour with its middle road turned into a Feigned Flight. The three roads now wait on the lure, not on the clock.

Intent

Give them the fight they want in the middle. Every gun that follows the bending middle is a gun no longer guarding the sides. When their weight is in, take the sides, and land behind them while they look the wrong way.

Chosen when
  • Preconditions:
    • 3 live approaches, on a payload sortie.
    • Sailing: a Bombard line (or a Leviathan pair), a Corsair wolfpack, a willing Skyfall convoy, and a willing Runner convoy. Never a Breacher for the landing: the hinge chooses its road (§24 Ships §6).
    • No long carving standing, because this carving needs all four decision-bearing slots.
  • FIT (+1 each):
    • H2 active on the middle approach (“they guard the middle road”).
    • H4 active (“their reach moves when pressed”).
    • A believed rear face: a COLD or WARM coast cell behind the weaker flank, whose lane passes no SCALDING cell.
    • H8 not active.
  • HABIT: H2, H4.
  • GRAIN: Flood weights are Ashwood +3, Ironbark +2, Heartoak +2, Willow −2, Twistbough −2, Yew −1. The proud and the rash carve it. The cunning prefer a lie that never shows its face.
  • Worked score. A Heartoak flag, H2 and H4 active, a believed rear face, H8 inactive, and Three Roads already carved this battle: FIT 4 + GRAIN 2 + HABIT 2 + LINE 1 = 9. Under that heart, no Feint scores near it (Feint −2).
Composition

C 4, A 3, all four decision-bearing while they wait. Kind P. 3 links per branch, one of them the chain hinge (§4 LAW · COUNTING LINKS): the hinge and the sky hand’s WITH(44) in both, then the landing’s AFTER(bombers released) in the close, or E2-01’s cover hold in the chain. About 16–18 hulls, under the 24-hull cap.

  • The live count never passes four. The sea hand is a raid of one pass: it darts at 32, strikes, and withdraws in shape, so from about t 40 it is leaving and glanceable (§11). When the sky hand releases at 44, the live decisions are the middle, the carrier, its cloud and the waiting landing: four. The cloud is cleared or spent before the landing commits, and the stack ashore comes ≥12 ticks after the cloud.
  • The bending middle: lure, carrying the hinge. A Bombard line on the middle approach: 1 core and 2 shields, line abreast. It is a real siege line, and it really shells.
  • The sea hand: strike. A Corsair wolfpack of 2–4 hulls, with an errand and no core. It waits at the fear edge of the flank the fleet believes stronger.
  • The sky hand: strike. A Skyfall convoy (core, 4 shields and 1 rider, in a ring). It waits at the fear edge of the flank believed weaker.
  • The landing: cargo. A Runner convoy behind its Bulwark lead, lying one cell behind the sky hand. Its aspect faces the weaker flank’s hottest adjacent cell.
  • Cores are the Bombard, the Skyfall and the Runners. The shields and the Bulwark lead are screens.
  • The bomber cloud and the troop stack arrive only as products of the sky hand and the landing, each ≥12 ticks after the arrival before it (§11). The flag rides in whichever convoy the queue gave it, and counts as 0 contacts while screened (Victory §3).
Choreography

(one sortie; ticks from launch; lag 1).

  1. t 0. All four leave at offset 0 on straight lanes: the Wave. Each hand sails to its fear-edge cell (the last lane cell before its flank’s first HOT cell) and stops, at about t 12–18. The landing stops one cell behind the sky hand, in COLD water.
  2. t ≈23. The middle enters the engagement band (5 launch + 18 reading, Fight §3) and shells its wall.
  3. After the middle draws fire, plus one reload (4 ticks). The middle bends. Bending is its lure role, not a link. It turns aft holding its line and backs off about one cell per 8 ticks, still shelling. That is slow enough to stay inside the reach that is firing on it (Salamis, backing water).
  4. Hinge: IF(fire drawn on the middle in [26, 32]) → close : through the middle. The chain is E2-01. The window opens one reload after the middle reaches the band and is 6 ticks wide (§11). These are starting values, ±3, settled per board from its lane geometry.
  5. Close (the foretold branch: the parent’s own, §4 clause (a)). The hand closes one finger at a time. Every arrival a single group might have to answer comes ≥12 ticks after the last (the spacing law).
    • At 32, on the hinge’s own waking: the sea hand darts at the stronger flank’s freshest glinted gun, or at the edge of that flank’s HOT cell if no gun is glinted, makes its one pass, and withdraws in shape by about t 40 (Corsair: hit-and-run, Ships §6). This pins that flank’s group without staying on its board.
    • WITH(44): the sky hand opens its deck and releases on the weaker flank’s castle. That flank’s group must now take Air.
    • AFTER(bombers released) + 12: the landing commits to the rear-face coast behind the weaker flank, at about t 56–60 and inside the 70-tick backstop. It arrives while that group is loaded Air or reloading, which is E4-06 inherited. The weaker flank goes last, after a re-tasker’s reach has walked toward the middle, which is E4-01’s rule.
  6. Through the middle (the chain). E2-01 is carved whole on the middle approach, both of its parts filled by this carving’s own formations:
    • The middle stops bending and becomes E2-01’s cover: a Bombard line stationed at the band’s edge, shelling the wall nearest the crossing, HOLD-UNTIL(cargo committed).
    • At 32, the landing becomes E2-01’s column, its Bulwark in the van (E2-01’s default shape, backfilling the lead), and runs a pre-carved lane through the middle’s band to the coast behind it at full speed. It commits at about t 52–60.
    • The sea hand darts at the middle’s guns at 32, ≥12 ticks ahead of the column, and withdraws in shape by about t 40.
    • The sky hand keeps its hour and its castle. From 44 the live decisions are the cover, the column, the carrier and its cloud: four.
Signature
  • First time. All three roads empty at once. The middle comes on like any siege line, takes your fire, then backs away in good order, still in line and still shooting. On each flank a formation sits dead still at the edge of your reach, with a convoy lying behind one of them. Then the flanks go, one after the other, and troops come ashore behind the guns you had turned toward the middle.
  • Opening tell. The Wave, but two of the three roads stop at the fear edge and do not come on. A Flood that stops is this Flood. To confirm, watch the middle’s withdrawal: it keeps its spacing (the Turned Stern). A middle hull sunk mid-sortie floats the Wave, not the Turned Stern, so the retreat belongs to a Flood.
Captains’ independence
  • A hand that is shelled while it waits. If its fear-edge cell turns HOT, it balks: it slides along the cell edge and loosens. At its hour it takes its fallback, its lineage policy toward its own objective. The hand closes one finger short.
  • The middle has real nerve. If it loses hulls fast while it bends, it Wavers into a true retreat, or Breaks and scatters. The scatter tells you the bending has become flight. The hinge still reads fire drawn.
  • The landing’s shields backfill under fire (Ships §9). Stripped below half its screen, it Wavers and gives up the landing for its fallback.
  • If the flag is sunk mid-carving, links and hinge become clocks, three ticks late and a tick apart (§4). The hinge takes its parent’s branch, the close (§4 clause (a)): the hand closes at about 35 whatever the middle drew, raggedly, into flank groups that may never have moved. A second fall in the sortie sends the carving home.
Counters
  • B · Refuse the middle’s battle (the Fabian answer). Every group keeps its own arc. No re-task follows the bending line. The hands close on guns that are still there. Then:
    • split domains at Deploy, so the weaker flank’s group stays Sea and a pre-loaded Air group elsewhere takes the bombers;
    • Spotter over the fear edges, which puts the waiting hands inside reach;
    • Saturation on the bomber cloud;
    • Splasher and Interdictor at the rear-face coast.
  • C · Win the cavalry first (Zama). The sky hand sits still at the fear edge for about 25 ticks. Deep-centre an AoR on it, or use a Piercer, before 44, and the hand closes with one finger.
  • C · Hold the middle silent. With no fire on the middle in [26, 32], the chain runs the landing into the middle, into a silent group. The old answer to E2-01 still works: hold, then alpha the beaching column, with a Splasher on the beach.
  • C · Decapitate before 26. The hinge becomes a clock, and the hand closes into groups you never moved.
  • C · Relocate the glinted flank guns in Deploy. The sea hand then drives at ghosts (Fleet Memory §4).
  • One tick. A middle group that first fires at 31 closes the hand. One that holds until 33 brings the flood. Command Time makes that a chosen tick (§12).
If it fails
  • Failed when the middle, the Skyfall and the Runners (the parts that carry its cores) are all eliminated or routed before they commit. Countered when the chain is taken; the chain’s E2-01 is then graded on its own.
  • What it learns:
    • fear on the fear-edge cells, if a hand was shelled there;
    • the glints of the flank guns;
    • an H2 count if the middle drew the most fire;
    • after the chain, that the middle can hold its fire. H1 may now hold on the middle, which opens Ask the Silent Water there.
  • By grain:
    • Heartoak: +1 to closing the same hand on the same roads.
    • Ironbark: carves it again (sunk cost).
    • Ashwood: −2, and its reserve launches at offset 0, so the next Flood comes all at once, as Three Roads with no waiting.
    • Willow and Silverbark: turn away.
  • Countered twice, it is retired (half-life).
At the Throne: Thaesaen carves it whole

(§22).

  • The hand on one bearing. The hand closes across flanks (§22 T-rule 4). The centre Bombard line yields in shape. The sea wing (Corsairs) waits at the left flank’s fear edge and the air wing (Skyfall) at the right.
  • Heartoak never turns aside, so the Throne is never the yielding centre. It goes under. Thaesaen’s submersion falls on the Judgement’s hinge. It rises behind the rear face whose cells are believed coldest, and the archived tidal wave pushes that face’s walls one grid outward. The risen Throne is the part that lands behind the rear faces.
  • Twist · the tide keeps one hour. Both wings close WITH the rising tick.
  • Tell. The sea draws back from that rear face ≥12 ticks before Thaesaen rises (a presence, and the spacing floor).
  • Added counter. Interdictor + Breaker on the rear faces: the risen Throne is the enemy nearest your wall.
  • Contacts. Centre, two wings and the Throne: 4.
  • The Name (T-rule 9), known from the Throne’s own heart after it falls: Thaesaen, the tide-heart, beneath the harbour of Eldhythe: for our warnings, carved in stone to plead for us, which they hung over their trade-tables as ornament, and priced (Legends III.2).
The Carving

Bend at the heart as the bough bends; stand at the edges as the roots stand. When they lean upon the bending, close the hand.
