If none lean, go through the heart. — Cut against: they guard the middle road; their reach follows the pressing.

Real-world root

Cannae, 216 BC: Hannibal’s yielding crescent and double envelopment. Its answer, winning the cavalry arm first, came at Zama in 202 BC.

E5-02

Come as the Young Wood Came

“Come as the young wood came.”

A late fleet comes on the way the Hasty Tide did: a lone scout straight in, loose ships behind it, nobody waiting. It is fishing for the answers that beat the Hasty Tide, which were early fire from open guns. It turns one cell short, and the Reavers waiting at the fear edge take every gun that spoke. If nothing speaks, the young wood goes ashore for real.

LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Sounding · the Lone Stroke (capstone)P3–4 / 2–32, one the chain hinge (chain: E1-01, with E1-02 and E1-03)H3, H5
Era & debut

E5 · the Tide that Learned Deceit · Cam 29, the first decoy

Grows from

E4-02 Ask the Silent Water · the Hasty Tide’s words as its dress: E1-03 Go Until Struck, E1-01 Landfall, E1-02 Break the First Wall, E1-04 Cheapest First

Grows into

Naelthar’s Judgement (the Storm King’s), carved whole at the Thrones (At the Throne, below)

The growth

This is Ask the Silent Water wearing the young wood’s face: the stimulus test, dressed as the blunder the player learned to punish in Cam 1–6.

Intent

They beat the green wood by striking early from open walls. Send them the green wood. Every gun that answers as it answered then is a gun we will know, and take, in the same hour.

Chosen when
  • Preconditions:
    • A Reaver pack and a Recon sail.
    • A Bombard line or a Runner convoy is free to wear the dress (one or both).
    • Within reach of a live approach lies a HOT cell with no glinted gun inside it: a battery the fleet believes in but has never fixed.
    • Payload sortie.
  • FIT (+1 each):
    • H3 active.
    • H5 active (“they move the guns we find”). So the hunt happens within the hour, before any Deploy can move the gun.
    • The HOT cell’s believed centre lies inside the engagement band.
    • A WARM-or-colder fear-edge cell for the Reavers adjoins the HOT cell.
  • HABIT: H3 and H5. H1 is added when the HOT cell is the silent quarter, to make it speak early.
  • GRAIN: Sounding: Willow +3. It is the cautious heart’s boldest look. Silverbark doubles its HABIT. Twistbough scores it 0: the lie is in the dress, not in the plan.
Composition

C 3–4, A 2–3. Kind P. 2 links, one of them the chain hinge: the hinge, and the hunters’ AFTER(new glint), which the Corsair hears too, at +12 (§4 LAW · COUNTING LINKS).

  • The lone stroke: decoy. A Recon in E1 dress (Go Until Struck). It runs straight in until struck, then swerves.
  • The green line and/or the green landing: decoy, carrying the hinge.
    • The green line is a Bombard line in dress (Break the First Wall).
    • The green landing is a Runner convoy in dress (Landfall with Cheapest First: the cheapest hulls lead).
    • Both run loose and straight, with no aspect lock. The cores are real, and the Runners are laden. Here the decoy role means “not what it looks like”, not “not real”.
  • The hunters: hunt. A Reaver pack that sits motionless at the fear edge beside the HOT cell.
  • The hidden knife: strike, optional. A Corsair wolfpack, used only when a single dressed formation sails, so that C stays ≤4.
  • The dress is E1 behaviour done on purpose, using the late fleet’s voluntary looseness (Ships §9, formation quality). The shape lies about its cognition tier under the decoy licence, and carries its seam.
Choreography

(one sortie; lag 1).

  1. t 0. The lone stroke leaves alone, straight at the HOT cell: the Lone Stroke, ≥6 ticks ahead of anything else.
  2. t 6. The dressed formations leave together, straight and loose, for the wall and coast nearest the HOT cell, and wait for nobody after that. The Reavers leave for their fear-edge cell and stop there, at about t 18.
  3. t ≈18–26. The dressed formations come into reach. Any exposed gun that fires writes a glint (Fleet Memory §5).
  4. AFTER(new glint) + 1. The Reavers bend to it, keeping their fix in sight and ignoring walls (Ships §6). AFTER(new glint) + 12: the Corsair takes the second glint, or the first if there is only one.
  5. Hinge: IF(new glint in [20, 26]) → turn short : Landfall. The chain is E1-01, with E1-02 for a dressed Bombard line and E1-03 for the lone stroke. Tick 26 is when the dressed formations reach their turning cell, the last cell before the HOT cell’s believed centre.
    • Turn short (the foretold branch: a chain hinge foretells its parent’s own, §4 clause (a)). When the glint’s news arrives, the dressed formations turn one cell short and withdraw in shape.
    • Landfall (the chain). At 26 they bend toward the least-feared coast and go ashore. A dressed Bombard line takes the first wall it touched. The lone stroke runs on until something hits it. These are the E1 carvings, whole, and nothing in them waits.
    • Either way, the dressed sails blanch once, when the hinge resolves. The young wood had no answering sails.
  6. The hunt resolves inside the sortie. The glints are an hour old, so no Deploy can move the gun in time.
Signature
  • First time. It looks like Cam 1: a lone scout running straight in, then a loose line and a loose landing driving for your hottest water, nobody waiting for anybody. You answer it the way you answered then. It turns just short, in good order, and hunters come for every open gun that fired.
  • Opening tell. The Lone Stroke goes out first. But the ships behind it are loose, in a war where nothing is loose any more, and a Reaver pack sits still at the fear edge, which the Hasty Tide never had. The Sounding that comes on loose is this Sounding. The seams follow:
    • the “hasty” sails blanch;
    • the withdrawal keeps its spacing (the Hasty Tide scattered, E1-07);
    • a dressed hull sunk mid-sortie floats the Lone Stroke, not the Wave.
Captains’ independence
  • The dressed hulls have real nerve. A dressed formation that loses hulls fast in the reading band can Waver or Break before its turn. Then the dress comes true, and it scatters in a real Hasty rout.
  • A dressed formation balks early if its turning cell turns SCALDING before it arrives, turning two cells short instead of one.
  • Reavers rage on a juicy fix and over-commit (Ships §6). A pack whose gun is re-enclosed voids its errand and leaves the queue (Victory §4b).
  • If the flag is sunk, the hinge is a clock and takes its parent’s branch (§4 clause (a)): the dressed formations turn short at 29 (26, three ticks late) whatever glinted. The Reavers go at the predicted hour, late by the same, for the freshest glint the ledger holds, which may be last sortie’s ghost.
Counters
  • B · Answer the late war as the late war. Fire enclosed guns only, weapons-free.
    • Nothing glints, so the Reavers have no fix.
    • The chain sends the green wood ashore into enclosed fire. Meet it as in Cam 1: Standard and Splasher at the coast.
    • Camouflage any exposed gun on that face.
  • C · Judge capability, not display. Loose ships plus a still Reaver pack means this carving. Hold the exposed guns and deep-centre an AoR on the Reavers where they wait, from about t 18. Then meet the dressed ships as you like.
  • C · Feed the hunters. Let one exposed gun on a face you can spare speak first. The Reavers bend to it, into a box of enclosed guns.
  • One tick. An exposed gun that first speaks at 27 is too late to turn the dressed formations. You get both halves: the green wood lands, and the Reavers still bend to the gun.
If it fails
  • Countered when the chain is taken (nothing glinted). Failed when every dressed core and the Reavers fall before they commit.
  • What it learns: any glints; the fear the dress drew; and one contradiction of H3 if the player did not strike early (two in a row deactivate it, §6).
  • By grain:
    • Willow (Countered −3): turns to look another way, with Sounding and Mass or Ask the Silent Water.
    • Blackthorn: +2 toward the cell where the Reavers died.
    • Twistbough: never twice.
    • Silverbark: −4.
  • Countered twice, it is retired: the young wood is not answered here any more.
At the Throne: Naelthar carves it whole

(§22).

  • The young wood. Loose E1-dress formations blunder in piecemeal and turn one cell short. Their sails still blanch when their links wake (§4 LAW · THE WOOD CANNOT HIDE ITS WAKING), and that anachronism is still the seam.
  • Twist · all at once, and the Throne is the hunter. Ashwood is rash: every part leaves at offset 0, so Naelthar’s whole Judgement is in the reading band together. The lone stroke still leads by speed, as the Hasty Tide’s did (§17 E1-03). The blunderers’ own straggle keeps the arrivals ≥12 ticks apart. The Throne’s lightning falls AFTER(glint) on the glinted gun inside the fear cell that has written the most Effect on it (§22 T-rule 7). Reavers take the rest.
  • Lightning never falls from inside the thundercloud, because nothing fires while hidden.
  • The archived “cannons at half range against the flying boss” is dropped: a stat nerf that no channel explains (§24).
  • The Name (T-rule 9), known from the Throne’s own heart after it falls: Naelthar, blood of the sky, on the crags of Highreach: for the cranes of our felled kin that lifted their stones into the sky that was ours (Legends III.2).
The Carving

Come as the green wood came: loud, alone and straight. Stop one breath short of the fire, and let the hunters wait upon the flashing.

If none flash, go ashore as the green wood went. — Cut against: they strike first and far; they move the guns we find.

Real-world root

Stratagem #27, Feign madness but keep your balance (假痴不癲).

E5-03

Fortitude

“Let them see the host where it is not, and keep them seeing.”

On the road you already guard, a phantom host takes station, a brawler goads your guns and smoke lands on the shore. Meanwhile the real landing waits in the grey on another road and comes in once your guns have spoken. The host stays for sorties afterwards, promising a second landing.

LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Feint · the Two Mouths (third form)P, a long carving4 / 2 (3 if the cargo’s best lane lies on a third road)3, one the chain hinge (chain: E1-06-form)H2, H4
Era & debut

E5 · the Tide that Learned Deceit · Cam 31 (CALL-OUT 19)

Grows from

E3-06 Open Roads, Hidden March · E2-08 Grey Before the Sails

Grows into

T-04 The Road Under the Sand (Senneir)

The growth

This is Open Roads, Hidden March with its credible siege replaced by a host that is not there, and one that outlasts the real landing. The smoke of Grey Before the Sails now promises sails that never come.

Intent

They already guard the east road; they believe our strength is there. Show them that strength. Land in the west while they watch the east, and keep showing it, so that a hand of theirs stays in the east for sorties after. Fortitude invents no belief. It feeds one the player already holds, as the 1944 deception fed the belief in Calais.

Chosen when
  • Preconditions:
    • A Mimic sails, with a demonstrator: a Provocateur wolfpack, or a Bombard line.
    • A willing cargo core: a Runner convoy, or a Skyfall standing in.
    • ≥2 live approaches, and a lane for the cargo to approach B whose summed fear is lower than any lane on A.
    • No other long carving standing.
  • FIT (+1 each):
    • H2 active on A.
    • A Sower sails.
    • B’s lane holds no HOT cell.
    • H4 active.
  • HABIT: H2, H4.
  • GRAIN: Feint: Twistbough +3 (the Mimic’s own grain, §7); Heartoak −2. A Heartoak flag will not carve it, and a Heartoak successor will not keep it: the long carving breaks.
Composition

C 4, A 2 (3 if the cargo’s best lane lies on a third road). Three decision-bearing. Kind P. 3 links, one of them the chain hinge: WITH(22), the hinge, and the host’s HOLD-UNTIL(sortie end).

  • The host: decoy, the standing part. A solo Mimic projecting a false convoy around itself (Mirror-Host, Ships §6): copies of the fleet’s own Runner column, arranged in mirror symmetry about the Mimic. While it holds that shape it counts as one contact.
  • The demonstration: lure, carrying the hinge. A Provocateur wolfpack, the lone durable pusher, working laterally along A’s band edge one cell in front of the host, between it and the fort: real hulls interposing for the phantoms. It is the host’s screen, and it makes the test shot cost something the fleet has planned for (Counters).
  • The grey: pin (glanceable). A Sower Sowing Line that smokes A’s coast and peels off. Once its field is laid its errand voids and it leaves the queue (Victory §4b).
  • The landing: cargo. A Runner convoy behind its Bulwark lead, with its aspect toward B’s hottest adjacent cell.
Choreography, across sorties
  • Stage 1: the landing sortie. The cash-in comes first, then the persistence. Ticks from launch:
    1. t 0. The host sails to A’s fear edge and holds there, drifting sideways in mirror symmetry. The landing sails to a COLD fringe cell on B, its grey, and waits there in plain sight.
    2. t ≈18. The demonstration works along A’s band edge. It moves sideways and never commits forward: the Two Mouths.
    3. WITH(22). The grey darts in, smokes A’s coast and peels off. No sails follow the smoke.
    4. Hinge: IF(fire drawn on A's host, demonstration or grey in [24, 30]) → land on B : wait in the grey. The chain is E1-06’s form. The window opens as the demonstration first comes into reach and is 6 ticks wide.
    5. Land on B (the foretold branch: the parent’s own, §4 clause (a)). The landing’s sails blanch and it drives from B’s fringe to B’s coast, about 20 grids, committing at about t 52. That is ≥12 ticks after A drew fire, so a planned walk by one group still fits (spacing law). After the landing, the host holds on A until the sortie ends (HOLD-UNTIL(sortie end)).
    6. Wait in the grey (the chain). This is E1-06’s form: its QUIET wait, on a Runner convoy its Hasty-Tide filter never took (§16c). The landing stays in B’s fringe and drifts in only once 4 ticks have passed with no new fear written anywhere on its lane, up to the backstop. E1-06’s old answer, the hold-feint, still answers it.
  • Stages 2–3: the promise.
    • The host stands alone on A, the standing part: same cell, same hour, as if a second landing were gathering. The rest of the sortie carves its own stratagem with ≤3 decision-bearing roles, elsewhere.
    • Stage test: no shell lands on the host.
    • The promise lasts 2 sorties. That is a starting value with a range of 1–3, settled so that at least half of first-time greybox testers keep a group on A through at least one Deploy after the real landing. That is the deception’s measurable bite.
Signature
  • First time. A great host gathers on the road you always guard. Smoke lands on that shore, and a brawler goads your guns there. The host never comes in. Then a landing comes out of the grey on the other road. The next sortie the host is back where it was, and still it does not come.
  • Opening tell. The Two Mouths: one formation working sideways, never committing, while another road lies quiet with a convoy waiting at its fringe. Which Feint shows you a whole host? This one. The seams follow:
    • the host’s hulls do not jink when shells fall near them, and nothing it loses leaves a wreck;
    • its copies drift in mirror symmetry;
    • its smoke lands with no sails behind it.
Captains’ independence
  • The demonstration is fanatical. The Provocateur pushes into the reach of exposed guns on its own judgment. If nothing ever fires on it, its errand voids and it leaves the queue.
  • The Mimic is a trickster. At the first shell on any copy, every copy winks out and the Mimic flees (Ships §6). The promise dies with the illusion.
  • The landing balks if its grey fringe cell turns HOT while it waits. It slides, loosens and takes its fallback, the Runner baseline: beeline for the softest COLD coast.
  • If the flag is sunk, the hinge is a clock and takes its parent’s branch (§4 clause (a)): the landing goes at 33 (30, three ticks late) whether A drew fire or not. The standing host survives unless the successor’s grain breaks the long carving. Kill a Twistbough flag and leave a Bulwark, Breacher or Leviathan to take the standard (Heartoak, Feint −2), and the host is abandoned at the next boundary.
Counters
  • B · A group on each road, and B’s group stays on B. Weapons-free, the landing meets fire. The host costs you nothing, because it never comes in. Also:
    • a Flare over A shows the host for what it is (§24: pending Ships §6’s ruling on whether light dispels a projection);
    • Splasher and Interdictor at B’s coast.
  • C · Ask what the host cannot do alone. This is E3-06’s answer, grown. The host brings no wrecks and no jinks, and its smoke brings no sails. Do not fire on the demonstration. Its errand voids, the hinge takes the chain, and the landing sits in B’s grey. Then deep-centre an AoR on that fringe, or hold silent until it drifts in, and alpha it.
  • C · The test shot, at its price. One shell on any copy dispels the host (Ships §6). But the goad paces in front of the host, so a shell reaches a copy only past it: a Marksman’s (it ignores screens, Cannon §5), or a reach re-tasked onto the host’s flank. Inside the window either is fire drawn on A, so the test shot that dispels the host also releases the landing on B, which is exactly what the fleet planned; from an exposed gun it glints as well, and the goad turns to push that gun. Fired before the window, or in the promise sorties when the host stands alone, the test shot is free and ends the promise (Countered).
  • C · Choose which heart you leave them. Decapitate a Twistbough flag and leave a Heartoak successor. The long carving breaks.
  • Deploy. The promise sorties hand you two Deploys. The player who has read the host moves A’s group; the one who has not keeps paying for it.
If it fails
  • Countered when the chain is taken (A drew no fire), or when the host is dispelled during the promise. Failed when the landing is eliminated or routed before it commits.
  • What it learns:
    • fear on A and B;
    • one contradiction of H2 if A drew no fire;
    • if the host stood unshot through its promise, that A is a road no gun answers. It goes on Straw, Then Men’s map as a silent road, so the phantom becomes the straw through believed state, with no hinge.
  • By grain:
    • Twistbough: Succeeded −2, never the same lie twice, so it turns to Straw, Then Men on the same road, or to E3-06. Failed −3.
    • Willow: −5 when Failed, −3 when Countered.
    • Silverbark: −4.
  • Countered twice, it is retired.
The Carving

Raise a host of mist upon the road they guard, and let the grey promise sails. Go ashore by the other road; then leave the mist standing, that they may watch for a second tide.

If the guarded road is not struck, wait in the grey. — Cut against: they guard the east road; their reach walks toward noise.

Real-world root

Operation Fortitude, 1944: FUSAG, Quicksilver, Taxable and Glimmer. The phantom held the German 15th Army at Calais for weeks after the real landing.

E5-04

The Fourth Time

“Do the same thing thrice; the fourth time, mean it.”

For three sorties, one convoy runs out empty to the same far water, idles there while its eye looks at your coast, and leaves for home at the same hour. On the fourth it rides laden. Another road opens twelve ticks before its hour, its eye looks once more, and if your coast is as it was, the routine becomes the landing. If the coast has changed, it goes back to waiting in the grey.

LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Patience · the Knot (fourth form)P, a long carving2 (3 with the pull’s bomber cloud) / 22, one the chain hinge (chain: E1-06-form)H2, H4; H1 is a gate
Era & debut

E5 · the Tide that Learned Deceit · Cam 30 (CALL-OUT 19)

Grows from

E3-08 Spent or Waiting · E1-06 Wait in the Grey

Grows into

T-07 The Long Winter (Eirlenth)

The growth

This is Wait in the Grey carved three times over, until the waiting itself is what the Rivenmen stop watching. Spent or Waiting looked at a silent gun to learn whether it was waiting. This carving gives the Rivenmen a waiting of the fleet’s own to watch, until they stop looking, and keeps its own eye open the whole time.

Intent

Let them see the same ships at the same grey water, leaving at the same hour, until the sight means nothing. Look at their shore each time. On the fourth, if their shore is as it was, go on at that hour, while another road pulls their reach away.

Chosen when
  • Preconditions:
    • A willing Runner convoy with a Recon rider: the eye the routine keeps.
    • A far fringe cell F on approach R: the last COLD cell of the reading band, with a lane from F to a coast cell that holds no HOT cell, and R’s coast within the rider’s sight from F.
    • H1 not active on R’s quarter. A quarter that springs silent batteries would spring on the fourth run.
    • No other long carving standing.
    • ≥4 formations besides the convoy still in the queue. That is a starting value with a range of 3–5, settled so the fourth run lands before the battle’s last third in at least 70% of greybox battles.
  • FIT (+1 each):
    • H2 active on an approach other than R.
    • H4 active.
    • A Skyfall or Corsair sails, for the fourth run’s pull.
    • E3-08 or E1-06 was carved this battle (LINE +1).
  • HABIT: H2, H4.
  • GRAIN: Patience: Yew +3, Willow +2, Blackthorn −2, Ashwood −2. A Runner, Provocateur or Reaver successor breaks it.
  • Cam 30 (ACDE) has no Layer B, so no lineage debuts there (§13 LAW · BREADTH ENTERS WITH LAYER B). Every part is a hull the player already knows: a Runner convoy, Bulwark shields, a Recon rider, and a Skyfall or Corsair for the pull. The campaign that brings no new ships brings a new mind, and that makes its debut the cleanest read in the Tide.
Composition
  • The routine sorties: one decoy, the standing part. A Runner convoy behind its Bulwark lead, with 4 shields and a Recon rider, unladen, so it rides high (the carving sails a transport unladen: §24 Ships §7). It is 1 decision-bearing contact, and nothing else in the sortie belongs to this carving. Any rider already aboard stays aboard on every run, because the routine must look the same each time.
  • The fourth sortie:
    • the routine becomes cargo, laden, so it rides low;
    • the pull, a pin, is a Skyfall launch or a Corsair dart on another approach.
  • C 2 (3 with the pull’s bomber cloud), A 2, one to two decision-bearing. Kind P. 2 links, one of them the chain hinge: WITH(T_r − 12) and the hinge.
Choreography
  • Sorties n−3 to n−1: the routine.
    • The convoy leaves at offset 0 on the same lane to F, arrives at about t 14 and holds there: the Knot. At the routine hour T_r it turns for home in shape.
    • Same lane, same cell, same hour, same shape, every time.
    • Its Recon rider looks from F on every run and writes what it sees. R’s coast is therefore re-read every sortie: it is the one coast in the battle where the player cannot keep a stale negative, and the last run’s reading is the one the fourth run compares against.
    • T_r starts at 30, with a range of 24–34. F sits about 20 grids out from the coast and sea speed is 1 grid/s, so a run that leaves at T_r must commit by about t 52, before the natural sortie ends at 54 (Fight §3).
    • Stage test: no fire drawn on the convoy, and its lane no hotter than WARM at the boundary. A run that fails the test breaks the carving (Countered).
    • “Thrice” is in the carving’s words. If greybox shows two routine runs are enough, the Carving becomes “twice; the third time”.
  • Sortie n: the fourth. It is identical until T_r − 12. Then:
    1. WITH(T_r − 12): the pull opens its deck or darts on another approach. It is a real threat, and it comes 12 ticks before the turn, so a re-tasker’s reach is already crawling away at T_r (the spacing floor, §11).
    2. Hinge: IF(seen(R's coast) contradicts the ledger the last run wrote, in [T_r − 6, T_r]) → wait in the grey : land. A contradiction is a gun Contact seen on R’s coast where the last run saw none, a gun gone from where it stood, or a glint written there since the last run. The event is a look, not fire: it reads what you did in Deploy. The window is the rider’s last look from F before the hour, 6 ticks wide (§11).
    3. At T_r the hinge resolves and the convoy’s sails blanch on either branch, which on a routine they never did (§4: a hinge wakes its formations both ways).
    4. Land (the foretold branch: a chain hinge foretells its parent’s own, §4 clause (a)). The coast is as it was. The convoy turns for the coast instead of home and commits at about T_r + 22, around t 52.
    5. Wait in the grey (the chain). The coast has changed: the Rivenmen are ready. This is E1-06’s form, its QUIET wait on a Runner convoy its Hasty-Tide filter never took (§16c). The convoy stays at F and drifts in only once 4 ticks have passed with no new fear written on its lane, up to the backstop. E1-06’s old answer, the hold-feint, still answers it.
Signature
  • First time. The same convoy parks at the same far water every sortie, its little scout drifting at the edge of sight as if idly watching your coast, and leaves at the same moment. You stop watching it. On the fourth sortie it comes in.
  • Opening tell. The Knot: a formation holding in a COLD fringe and launching late, at the same cell and the same hour, sortie after sortie. The Patience that repeats itself is this one. The seams on the fourth run:
    • it rides low from the moment it leaves the launch zone;
    • another road opens twelve ticks before its hour;
    • at its hour its sails blanch, which they never did before, because a routine has nothing to wake to.
Captains’ independence
  • Idling costs nerve. Three idle sorties drain resolve through “no progress” (Ships §4). A routine convoy that has sagged to Wavering by the fourth gives up the landing for its fallback, the routine, without waiting on the hinge. The lie is not free, and it cannot run forever.
  • A convoy whose fringe cell turns HOT (the player’s reach arrives at F) balks and takes its fallback. That run counts as a broken routine.
  • The rider is a skittish Recon. Fired on at F, it swerves: the run has drawn fire and fails its stage test.
  • If the flag is sunk on the fourth run, the hinge is a clock and takes its parent’s branch, land, at T_r + 3 (§4). The convoy lands into whatever is waiting, and can no longer turn back. During the routine sorties there are no links to lose. The routine goes on unless the successor’s grain breaks it (Ashwood or Blackthorn).
Counters
  • B · Keep R’s coast answered, in the open, from the first run. Every sortie, a group whose AoR covers R’s coast cells, weapons-free, with an Interdictor and a Splasher at the beach. The rider sees the same guns every run, so nothing changes, and the fourth run lands into them. Deploy makes this cheap: you get three Deploys to build the answer.
  • C · Change the coast where the eye can see it. In the last Deploy before the fourth run, move an exposed gun onto R’s coast, or glint one there in the sortie before. The look sees the coast changed, the hinge takes the chain, and the convoy waits in the grey at F: hold-feint it (E1-06’s answer), or shell it at F where it idles.
  • C · Change the coast where the eye cannot. Enclose the new battery instead. The look sees wall, as it always has, reads the coast unchanged, and the laden run turns in on the hour into guns it never saw. The fleet’s one re-read coast is only as good as its eye.
  • C · Audit the routine. Check the ride height, and watch for the answering sail at the hour. Command Time makes T_r a tick you choose: re-task onto its lane then, or hold silent until it commits and alpha it on the beach.
  • C · Break it early. Extend a group’s reach (Spotter or a re-task) and shell the convoy at F on its second or third run. The routine is broken, and the fleet has spent its sorties for nothing.
If it fails
  • Countered when a routine run breaks, or when the fourth run’s look finds the coast changed and the chain is taken. Failed when the cargo is sunk or routed before it commits.
  • What it learns: the fear on R’s lane, the Recon rider’s contacts, and the H2 and H4 counts. A gun seen gone from R’s coast is an H5 count.
  • By grain:
    • Yew: Countered counts 0. It waits and starts a routine again later, perhaps on another road.
    • Willow: −3.
    • Silverbark: −4.
  • Countered twice, it is retired: the Q-ship decay.
The Carving

Go thrice to the same grey water, look at their shore, and thrice come home at the same hour. The fourth time, if their shore is as it was, go on.

If their shore has changed, wait in the grey as the green wood waited. — Cut against: they guard the west road; their reach moves when pressed.

Halyna tell it by turns, one beginning and the other finishing.

Real-world root

Stratagem #1, Deceive the heavens to cross the sea (瞞天過海), and the Gallipoli “silent stunts” of 1915.

E5-05

Straw, Then Men

“Sail straw down every road; where no fire wakes, send the landing.”

The Mimic runs a column of copies down a different road every sortie, the least-feared road it has not yet run, and turns at the surf. Every open gun that fires on the straw is glinted for free, and every road the straw runs unanswered goes on the fleet’s map. Then real Runners, in the same silhouette, come down a road the straw found silent, while the straw runs another.

LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Mask · the Smoothed Cut (third form)P, a long carving3 / 2 on the men sortie2, one the chain hinge (chain: E2-01-form)H7, H3
Era & debut

E5 · the Tide that Learned Deceit · Cam 29, on a later battle than E5-02’s: the first long carving, and the first time the Heartwood commands the Mimic

Grows from

E4-08 Thick and Hollow

Grows into

T-08 The Mirror Host (Neivaere): the fleet maps you, then mirrors you

The growth

This is Thick and Hollow taken two steps further. The false thickness becomes false ships, and the false ships become a chart of the Rivenmen’s silences: straw sent down every road to learn which one no gun answers.

Intent

Their fire costs us nothing when it falls on straw, and every open gun that fires on straw is a gun we will know. Send straw down every road. Where no fire wakes, the road is ours: send the landing in the shape of the straw. This is Zhang Xun’s arrows turned around: the fleet harvests glints from the shooting, and a map from the silence.

Chosen when
  • Preconditions:
    • A Mimic sails; on the men sortie a Runner convoy sails to be the men.
    • ≥2 live approaches, each with a lane from spawn to a coast: roads to map.
    • No other long carving standing.
  • FIT (+1 each):
    • H7 active. Straw shaped like bearers draws a core-hunter’s fire early, which is the harvest.
    • H3 active.
    • A road’s lane crosses a HOT or WARM cell with no glinted gun inside it. The harvest is richest where guns are believed in but not yet fixed.
    • A Corsair sails, for the men’s pull.
  • HABIT: H7, H3.
  • GRAIN: Mask: Twistbough +2 (the Mimic’s grain), Ironbark −2, Heartoak −2. The heavy hearts will not keep it.
Composition
  • The straw sorties: the straw is a decoy and the standing part. A solo Mimic projects copies of the fleet’s own Runner column around itself (Mirror-Host, Ships §6). While it holds that mirror shape it counts as one decision-bearing contact. Its standing rule (§4) is the map: each sortie it runs the least-feared lane to a coast that it has not yet run this battle (ties: §14). The Mimic works alone, never in formation with the fleet (Ships §6).
  • The men sortie: C 3, A 2, two decision-bearing. Kind P. 2 links, one of them the chain hinge (WITH(t_band − 12) and the hinge).
    • The straw sails again, down its next unrun road, or, if every road has been run, down the road that answered it least.
    • The men, cargo: a real, laden Runner convoy behind its Bulwark lead, in the very column silhouette the straw has been copying, down the most recent road the straw ran unanswered (ties: the lower fear). It is its own formation on its own road; nothing real sails inside a projection.
    • The pull, pin: a Corsair wolfpack on another approach.
Choreography
  • Straw sorties (the map).
    • The Mimic leaves at a fixed offset, 6, on its road, runs at column pace to the coast, turns at the surf without beaching (a promise never kept, Ships §6), and drifts back out.
    • If a shell lands on any copy, every copy winks out and the Mimic flees. If the gun that fired was exposed, the Mimic’s own sight writes its glint. The Mimic lies about ships, never the glint: it provokes a real one. That road goes on the map as answered.
    • A run that finishes unshot marks its road silent.
    • Stage test. Straw that is shot is still paying, in glints and fear bearings, so being shot does not break the carving. It breaks after 3 fruitless runs in a row: shot, with no new glint written. That is a starting value with a range of 2–4, and it means the player is answering the straw from enclosed guns. It also breaks when every road it can run has been answered, if the Mimic is lost, or if the grain turns against it.
  • The men sortie comes at the first boundary where the map holds ≥2 roads, at least one of them silent (start value; range 2–3 roads mapped; the habit law’s “seen twice”, §6).
    1. The straw and the men both leave at the usual offset, 6, each on its road, at straw pace.
    2. WITH(t_band − 12): the Corsair darts on another approach. t_band is when the men cross into the band, about the offset + 18.
    3. Hinge: IF(fire drawn on the men in [t_band, t_band + 6]) → run : land at straw pace. The chain is E2-01’s form: the men break into full speed as a column behind the Bulwark van, backfilling the lead (E2-01’s default shape), with no cover line, the one part of E2-01 it does not carry (§16c).
    4. Land at straw pace (the foretold branch, §4 clause (a)): the men beach at about t 44–50.
Signature
  • First time. A column runs a road, turns at the surf and never lands. The next sortie it runs a different road. Your guns empty into it, and it vanishes at the first hit. You stop bothering with it. Then one sortie two columns come, on two roads, and one of them lands, on the road you had left quiet.
  • Opening tell. The Smoothed Cut: silhouettes that do not jink when shells fall near them, a new road every sortie. A copy that is “sunk” floats nothing, and that absence is itself the Mask. On the men sortie:
    • two columns on two roads, where there had been one;
    • near misses make one column’s hulls jink (real hulls crazy-Ivan, Ships §4);
    • those hulls ride low;
    • another road opens twelve ticks before the column enters the band.
Captains’ independence
  • The Mimic is skittish. It flees once exposed, and every flight costs a straw run.
  • On the men run, the Runners are real. A struck Runner jinks and its shields backfill. Once found, the column drives on through the chain. Stripped below half its screen, it Wavers and gives up the landing for its fallback.
  • If the flag is sunk on the men run, the hinge takes its parent’s branch: the men land at straw pace, three ticks later than carved (§4), slow and already found. If the standard passes to an Ironbark or Heartoak heart, the straw carving breaks at the next boundary.
Counters
  • B · Answer every road, from enclosed guns. Keep a weapons-free enclosed battery on every road the straw can run. Every run is answered, no glint is harvested and no road goes silent on its map. After three fruitless runs the Heartwood gives it up. Saturation dispels projections widely and cheaply.
  • C · Choose the silent road. Leave one road silent on purpose, over a hold-feint: a silent enclosed battery (Fleet Memory §6). The straw marks it silent, and the men come down it into your held alpha, Interdictor and Splasher at the beach.
  • C · Two columns, one shell each. On the men sortie, the first shell on each column tells straw from men: straw vanishes, men jink and stay. Then alpha the men.
  • C · Kill the Mimic. It is solo, soft and never shoots, so a Marksman or Piercer can take it. No straw, no map, no men.
If it fails
  • Countered when the men are found (the chain is taken) or the straw is given up. Failed when the men are sunk or routed before they commit.
  • What it learns: the harvested glints, the map of answered and silent roads, and the fear on each road.
  • By grain: Twistbough: Succeeded −2 (never the same straw twice) and Failed −3.
  • Countered twice, it is retired.
The Carving

Sail straw down every road. Where no fire wakes, send the landing in the shape of the straw.

If the fire wakes, run. — Cut against: they kill our bearers first; they strike first and far.

Halyna tell it by turns, one beginning and the other finishing.

Real-world root

Stratagem #7, Create something from nothing (無中生有): Zhang Xun’s straw men at Yongqiu, 756.

E5-06

The Prize Too Easy

“Sail the prize past their reach, and let them hunger.”

A carrier that has really lost most of its shields crawls along one cell outside the reach of the guns that have been hunting your bearers. If that reach walks out to take it, Reavers and a Leviathan strike the arc it left. If it stays home, the carrier does what carriers do, and bombs the castle behind it.

LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Hunt · the Spark (fifth form)P3–4 / 22, one a hinge (no chain)H7, H4
Era & debut

E5 · the Tide that Learned Deceit · Cam 28 (CALL-OUT 19). It is the Tide’s first carving and the gentlest lie in the book, because nothing in it is false

Grows from

E4-07 Borrow the Road

Grows into

T-02 The Thrown Stone (Rhenvael)

The growth

Borrow the Road glinted a gun by sailing past it. This carving glints nothing: it moves a reach. It parades something just outside the reach of the guns that hunt its bearers, something those guns cannot bear to let go, and when the reach walks out for it, the hunters strike where the reach stood. It asks the player to decide where a group goes, not whether a gun fires.

Intent

They kill our bearers first, and their hunters’ reach moves when pressed. Give that reach something it cannot bear to let pass, just beyond it: a carrier thinly screened, its losses real. If the reach walks out to take it, strike where the reach stood. If it holds, the carrier does what carriers do.

Chosen when
  • Preconditions:
    • H7 active, so the core-hunter’s ground is known: the fear area and the Effect bearing the fleet’s cores died to (§4; §6 H7).
    • A thin, high-value core sails: a Skyfall at ≤2 of its 4 shields, but still above its refusal threshold. At default intensity a standoff core refuses only at 0 shields (Victory §4a). The Admiral cannot strip shields or override refusal, so the prize is always a real loss the fleet has already taken, and this is a late-battle carving by construction.
    • A Reaver pack sails.
    • Payload sortie.
  • FIT (+1 each):
    • H4 active.
    • A Leviathan pair sails.
    • The core-hunter’s home arc holds a glinted gun or a wall Contact: something to strike where the reach stood.
    • Bombers can reach a castle behind that arc.
  • HABIT: H7, H4.
  • GRAIN: Hunt: Blackthorn +3, Ashwood +1. Silverbark reads it hardest, because its HABIT counts double.
Composition

C 3–4, A 2, three decision-bearing, plus the bomber cloud on the launch branch. Kind P. 2 links: the hinge, obeyed by the prize and both hunters (1), and the Leviathan’s AFTER(Reavers commit), carved 12 ticks behind them (2). This carving has no chain; it needs only E3 machinery.

  • The prize: cargo, carrying the hinge: a real carrier with a real deck, sent to be seen, so §5’s void-errand grade for a lure does not apply to it. The thin Skyfall convoy (≤2 shields plus its rider). It is slow, and plainly under-screened. Its parade station is carved: the cells one cell outside the core-hunter’s believed reach, on the bearing it guards, within about one cell of the Reavers’ commit point. A carrier leaves the back line only where a carving names its station, and never closes past it (§24 Ships §5; CALL-OUT 37).
  • The hunters: hunt. A Reaver pack at the fear edge of the core-hunter’s home arc, bows on it.
  • The long gun: hunt. A Leviathan pair creeping to a standoff just outside the longest reach it has been hit from (Grand Battery, Ships §6), facing the same arc.
  • Optional: a wolfpack pin elsewhere.
Choreography

(one sortie; lag 1).

  1. t 0. The prize leaves at offset 0 at Skyfall pace for its parade station. The Reaver pack sails to the fear edge of the core-hunter’s home arc and stops. The Leviathan creeps to its standoff.
  2. t ≈22–30. The prize parades along its station, deck closed, slow, one cell outside the reach that has been killing the fleet’s bearers.
  3. Hinge: IF(a re-task toward the prize is observed in [22, 30]) → strike where it stood : launch. The event is H4’s own, read live (§4): fire on the prize, or on its parade cells, from the core-hunter’s bearing. The prize sails outside that bearing’s believed reach, so fire from it there means the reach walked. The window is 8 ticks (start value; range 6–10), the parade’s length.
  4. Strike where it stood. Lag 1, then the Reavers’ sails blanch and they drive into the core-hunter’s home arc for its freshest glinted gun (ties: §14), else its wall Contact. They commit Δ = max(12, d + 4 + s) after the walk was observed, where d is the distance from the prize’s cell to their commit point (§20 LAW · THE THIRD ROAD WIDENS EVERY GAP): about 21 ticks, so by ≈ t 51, and a planned walk back still fits. The Leviathan opens on the arc’s wall 12 ticks after the Reavers commit. The prize turns aft in shape, its work done. The Reavers are home by ≈ t 63.
  5. Launch (the foretold branch: it carries the payload, §4 clause (b)). At 30 the prize opens its deck and releases on the castle behind the core-hunter’s arc. Bomber turnaround is about 4 ticks (Ships §8). The hunters hunt any gun that glints against the bombers, by their lineage policy, which needs no extra link.
Signature
  • First time. A carrier with almost no escort crawls along just outside the reach of the guns that have been killing your bearers, as though nobody had warned it. Your hunting group cannot resist it. The moment that group’s reach walks out to take it, hunters come in behind, where the reach used to be.
  • Opening tell. The Spark: a Reaver pack waiting still at the fear edge, bows on one arc, the arc your core-hunters guard. A carrier where carriers never come, off the back line, under-screened and slow, one cell outside that arc. The Hunt whose bait is a carrier is this one. A hunter sunk mid-sortie floats the Spark.
Captains’ independence
  • The prize is patient but thin. It keeps parading under fire until its resolve band falls. When it Wavers, it turns aft early and the launch branch may never come.
  • Reavers rage on a juicy fix and over-commit. The Leviathan is fanatical and holds its standoff.
  • If the flag is sunk, the hinge is a clock and takes its foretold branch: the prize launches at 33 (30, three ticks late, §4). The hunters hunt only what glints.
Counters
  • B · Let the prize pass, and keep every reach home. The Fabian answer (E5-01): no walk, no strike where it stood. Meet the launch with a group loaded Air over the castle behind (split domains in Deploy), and Saturation on the bomber cloud. Camouflage the core-hunting group, so its guns give the Reavers no glints.
  • C · Take the prize from where you stand. The prize is real: a thin Skyfall is a core. Kill it with an enclosed Marksman in a group off the core-hunter’s bearing whose reach already holds the parade cells. No walk is observed and no glint is written: you bank a core kill and a resolve blow, while the hunters wait for a walk that never comes.
  • C · Feed it a false walk. Let the core-hunting group’s own long reach take the prize without re-tasking (a Marksman, or a Spotter-grown AoR). The fire comes from the core-hunter’s bearing, the fleet reads a walk that never happened, and its Reavers strike an arc that never emptied.
  • C · Walk on purpose, from an arc covered twice. Re-task the core-hunting group toward the prize from an arc a second group also covers. The hunters strike where the reach stood and find the second group waiting.
If it fails
  • Succeeded when the hunters destroy a gun in the arc the reach left, or bombers are released over the castle (§5). Countered when the prize dies to fire from another bearing before it launches: the bait was taken, but not with the walk it was cut for. Failed when the prize, its only core, is sunk or routed before its parade station, and the hunters before their commit points.
  • What it learns:
    • H4 strengthens if the walk was observed, and is contradicted if the prize was answered from another bearing;
    • an H7 count if the prize drew the core-hunter’s fire;
    • any new glints.
  • By grain:
    • Blackthorn: +2 toward the arc where it lost the most.
    • Ashwood: −2.
    • Willow: −5 when Failed, −3 when Countered.
  • Countered twice, it is retired, in the Q-ship decay (research §3.14).
The Carving

Sail the prize past their reach, and let them hunger. Where the hunger walks, strike where it stood.

If they do not hunger, the prize is a prize: open the deck. — Cut against: they kill our bearers first; their reach moves when pressed.

Halyna tell it by turns, one beginning and the other finishing.

Real-world root

Stratagem #15, Lure the tiger down from the mountain (調虎離山), with #31’s beauty trap (美人計) as the bait: the tiger is strong only on its own mountain.

E5-07

The First Ship Carries

“They let the first wood pass; let the first wood bear.”

A lone small ship goes first into the water that killed most, as bricks always have, while the bearers wait at the edge of your fire. But this brick rides low: it is a thin Runner, laden, and a shore that lets the first ship pass lets it land. If you shoot it, it turns out like a brick, and the bearers at the edge come in on your reload.

LineKindC / ALinksHabit
Bleed · the Breath (fourth form)P2–3 / 1–22, one the chain hinge (chain: E2-06)H9
Era & debut

E5 · the Tide that Learned Deceit · Cam 30, on a later battle than E5-04’s debut (CALL-OUT 19, 40)

Grows from

E2-06 Toss a Brick (reach-back: the brick, carved as the bearer) · E4-08 Thick and Hollow (the draught seam)

Grows into

T-06 The Slow Rot (with H9 active, the Herald’s brick carries)

The growth

This is Toss a Brick with the brick and the bearer exchanged. The Second Tide spent a worthless hull to buy a reload for the landing. The Rivenmen learned that the first ship is never the one that matters, and let it pass. So the wood sends the thing that matters first, in the brick’s place and at the brick’s pace, and keeps the brick’s old trick, whole, for a shore that shoots.

Intent

They let the first wood pass, and keep their fire for the wood that follows. Let the first wood bear. If they strike it, let it be the brick after all, and send the bearers in the breath.

Chosen when
  • Preconditions:
    • H9 active: twice the fleet has seen its first lone hull pass unshot while a later part was fired on (§6).
    • A thin Runner convoy sails: one core and 1–2 shields, above its refusal threshold (Victory §4a), laden. It is thin because the player made it so; the Admiral never strips shields.
    • A second cargo convoy sails, to wait at the fear edge.
    • The fear cell where the fleet lost the most hulls last sortie lies on the thin convoy’s shortest lane, with a COLD or WARM coast behind it.
    • Payload sortie.
  • FIT (+1 each):
    • That cell sank ≥2 hulls last sortie (E2-06’s own gain 1).
    • Last sortie’s first fire on that road fell on a later part, not the first: the patient shore, seen on this road.
    • The waiting convoy is at full screen.
    • E2-06 or E4-08 was carved this battle (LINE +1).
  • HABIT: H9 (+1).
  • GRAIN: Bleed: Yew +2. Silverbark doubles its HABIT.
Composition

C 2–3, A 1–2, two decision-bearing (and the stack ashore). Kind P. 2 links, one of them the chain hinge: the hinge, and the chain’s own HOLD-UNTIL(fire drawn on the first ship).

  • The first ship: decoy, carrying the hinge. The thin, laden Runner convoy in the brick’s dress: its 1–2 shields lead it in file, cheapest first (E1-04), so from the fear edge it reads as a worn pack going in alone. Real core, real troops: the decoy role means “not what it looks like”, not “not real” (§21 LAW).
  • The bearers: cargo. A full Runner convoy holding at the fear edge of the killing cell, bows in, laden: E2-06’s cargo, where every patient shore expects the landing to be.
  • Optional: a wolfpack pin elsewhere.
  • The bearers’ latest tick is carved by this entry, not re-cut by H9: the first ship’s predicted commit + 12, the spacing floor (§11). This carving is the fleet’s answer to the patient shore.
Choreography

(one sortie; lag 1).

  1. t 0. Both leave. The bearers stop at the fear edge of the killing cell (about t 12–15, p ≈ 14).
  2. t ≈ 15–17. The first ship goes on alone into the killing cell: the Breath, as every brick has.
  3. Hinge: IF(fire drawn on the first ship in [its entry into the killing cell, +8]) → be the brick : bear. The chain is E2-06, carved whole.
    • Bear (the foretold branch: a chain hinge foretells its parent’s own, §4 clause (a)). It was let pass. At the window’s close the first ship’s sails blanch, and it crosses the cell at its pace, commits at about t 28–30 and beaches. Its stack comes ashore. The bearers hold to their latest tick (≈ t 42), then go in grey, and commit at about t 52–55, ≥12 ticks behind the first ship’s stack. The group that saved its volley for them must now choose between the stack ashore and the column at sea, which is E4-06’s switch, inherited.
    • Be the brick (the chain, E2-06 whole). It was struck. The first ship does what a brick does: it jinks and runs back out through the same water, still drawing fire as it flees. The bearers’ HOLD-UNTIL(fire drawn on the first ship) is already met, so lag 1 later their sails blanch and they drive through the cell inside your reload (E2-06), committing about 14 ticks after the first shell. The first ship is fleeing, not arriving, so the spacing law holds.
  4. Every part is ashore or home inside the backstop: the latest commit is ≈ t 55, and docked Runners do not withdraw (Ships §7).
Signature
  • First time. A small ship runs alone into the water where you sank the most ships last time, and the convoy behind waits at the edge of your fire, as you have seen a dozen times. You let the little one pass, as you have learned to. It does not turn. It lands.
  • Opening tell. The Breath: a lone hull goes in first while the rest wait at the fear edge. The Bleed whose brick rides low is this one. The seams:
    • the first ship rides low; a brick rides high (§9a; E4-08’s draught);
    • its file ends in a transport’s silhouette, placed last by cost (E1-04’s order);
    • at the window’s close its sails blanch, and a brick has nothing to wake to.
Captains’ independence
  • The first ship is a Runner with a thin screen. Its shields backfill and jink; stripped of them, it Wavers and turns out early, which reads as a brick’s flight.
  • The bearers balk at a SCALDING cell, like E2-06’s cargo: scald the cell while the first ship is inside it and the chain’s landing turns away.
  • If the flag is sunk, the hinge is a clock and takes its parent’s branch, bear, three ticks late (§4). The first ship lands, and the bearers come at their latest tick.
Counters
  • B · Price every lone hull by its draught. A lone hull that rides low is a bearer: shoot it. Keep two groups on the killing cell so one reload never empties it (Toss a Brick’s blunt answer, which the chain still meets), with a Splasher at the coast behind.
  • C · Split the fire (E2-06’s answer, carried by the chain). One group takes the first ship, the other holds for the bearers. The chain then has no reload to use.
  • C · Vary your patience. Shoot the first lone hull one sortie in two. H9 never reaches its second count, and this carving is never a candidate.
  • C · Let it land into a box. The first ship’s beach is the COLD coast behind the killing cell. A Splasher and a held Standard there take the stack, and the bearers come ≥12 ticks later into a group that has not had to switch.
If it fails
  • Succeeded when the first ship’s stack is ashore (bear) or the bearers land (the chain). Countered when the chain is taken: the first ship was struck. Failed when both cores are sunk or routed before they commit.
  • What it learns: the fear on the killing cell; any glints; and, if the first ship was struck first, one contradiction of H9 (two in a row deactivate it, §6).
  • By grain:
    • Yew: Countered counts 0. It waits, and sends the first ship again later.
    • Willow: −3 when Countered.
    • Silverbark: −4.
  • Countered twice, it is retired.
The Carving

They let the first wood pass. Let the first wood bear.

If the first is struck, be the brick, and let the bearers go in the breath. — Cut against: they let the first ship pass.

Halyna tell it by turns, one beginning and the other finishing.

Real-world root

St Nazaire, 28 March 1942: HMS Campbeltown, an obsolete destroyer disguised as a German torpedo boat, led the raid up the Loire and rammed the dock gate. The old ship was the weapon, and its hidden charge went off hours later.

22THE THRONES (Naelsaen / Nael’saen)

Boss Finale · eight Judgements, the rings, fanatical nerve that still plans from fear

It rides §3–§13 of this doc. Fiction from fiction_hooks §2, §5, §9. Lineage and chassis facts from Ships §4, §6, §9. Sizing from Victory §3 (boss row) and Deploy. Set-pieces from the archived boss table (GDD_Removed, Boss Mechanics), re-expressed through perceived channels only (CALL-OUT 9).

The era

The growing. The Thrones never sailed in the Fall (fiction_hooks §2). They are the ten eldest pillars of the fog, grown into moving thrones and seated in the ten fallen havens, and the Rivenmen go out to them. Here the Law of the Rings ends. A sapling held a sign, forty rings a phrase, a life of men a sentence. A pillar that held up the sky holds a judgement: one carving that the whole of its wood was grown to make, cut by a people spending the last of their air on it.

How organised it is. Completely. The book is cumulative, so a Throne can carve all 46 carvings, with E5’s half-life and chain hinges. Each Throne adds four things that no Tide had (§13):

  1. A master line, worth +3 on top of its grain, so its ordinary sorties rehearse that line’s history (T-rule 3).
  2. A visible build-up: three rings, three groans, then the Judgement (T-rule 2).
  3. A bespoke coordination twist: one rule of the root-bond no other fleet has, named in each entry.
  4. Fanatical nerve that still plans its lanes from fear (T-rule 6).

Links 3, ≤1 of them a hinge, whose branches may be whole stratagems (§4). Link lag is 1 tick while the Throne lives, 0 where a twist says so, and +3 with clock links once it falls (§4). The Shore-Reading holds 6 active habits (§6).

What the player is learning to read. No new sign. Every root sign learned since the Hasty Tide comes back (§9a). A Throne battle is one line told from root to capstone. The rehearsal sorties show the line’s older forms, the rings count down, and the Judgement is the capstone, with a seam the player has been learning to find since Cam 1. The reading question changes. It is no longer “which carving?” (each Throne has one Judgement, and the rings announce it). It becomes “where, on which tick, and cut against which of my habits?” This is §8 at its sharpest: the doctrine is announced three sorties ahead, and the detail lives on a tick.

Lineages available. All sixteen sea lineages, every troop lineage, and bombers. New here:

  • the Herald, boss-only, as Vaelress’s chassis (Ships §6);
  • the five boss chassis (Ships §4): Leviathan, Bulwark, Herald, Mimic and Chirurgeon. The Chirurgeon appears as riders only (CALL-OUT 33).

Budget (Victory §3 boss row; Deploy):

DialValue
MapCompact 4/4 (all four castles required, plus the Throne’s hull at 0)
Live approaches1, split into three flanks (T-rule 4)
Contacts4–6 on one bearing; ≤4 decision-bearing, always
Hulls16–24; mean formation 4; pool 40–50
Player1–2 groups; guns 6 → 12
Sorties3–5 (Victory) against 8–12 (GDD); the rings work for both (CALL-OUT 29)

Every Judgement’s own roles field ≤4 contacts (T-rule 5).

The Throne rules

These nine rules hold for every Throne. Each entry below overrides only what it names.

T-rule 1 · The Throne is the flag, and the grain is its own. Each Throne is a rare promoted flagship on a boss chassis, dialled to maximum intensity, with a bespoke set-piece (Ships §4). It carries the standard for the whole battle, and its grain is fixed (§7, fiction_hooks §9). No successor carves its Judgement.

T-rule 2 · LAW · THREE RINGS, THEN THE JUDGEMENT
  • At the first tick of the reading band of every sortie in which it does not judge, the Throne groans once, and a pale ring closes in its bark. The ring count shows on the Throne in Build and Deploy as well: a presence on the hull, never a label.
  • Sortie 1 brings the first groan and one ring. Sortie 2 brings the second groan and two rings. On sortie 3 the Throne groans a third time, and the Judgement falls with it.
  • After any Judgement, succeeded or not, the rings fall away and the count starts again. In a long battle the Judgement falls on sorties 3, 6 and 9.
  • Withheld. If the Judgement’s preconditions fail in believed state at the boundary where it is due, the Throne still groans the third groan, but it does not judge. The third ring stays open and the Throne carves a rehearsal instead. It judges at the first boundary where the preconditions hold. The held groan is a presence the player hears.
  • Cracking the heart. Once per battle, driving the Throne below its phase threshold before its third groan clears the rings to zero, and the cracked heart must grow them again (CALL-OUT 30). Starting value 50% of the Throne’s hull. That is the archive’s only boss threshold (the Magma Titan’s “armour weakens below 50%”). Range 40–60%. It is settled in greybox so that a blunt-kit player who focuses the Throne from sortie 1 crosses it before the third groan in about one attempt in three.
  • The rings count sortie boundaries. They read no wall-clock and no RNG, and the Judgement’s timing is fully known a whole Build ahead.

T-rule 3 · Rehearsals. In groan sorties the Throne carves by the ordinary selection rule (§5): its grain’s weights plus +3 on its master line. Its groan sorties therefore speak its own line’s older forms. The player hears the root before the capstone, and each Throne battle teaches its line in the order Introduce → Isolate → Combine, inside the one battle. Some Judgements need setting up across sorties: T-03’s root, T-04’s host, T-06’s rot and T-07’s routine. These standing parts are carved into every groan sortie as a fixed part, beside whatever the rehearsal is.

T-rule 4 · One bearing, three flanks. The Throne boards have one live approach. The Enemy Overlay divides it into three flanks (left, centre and right), each a band of fear-cell columns at least one cell wide. At the Thrones, any precondition, role or habit that names an approach reads a flank instead:

  • a Feint’s “≥2 live approaches” becomes ≥2 flanks;
  • H2 Favoured Bearing counts the flank that carries the most fear mass.

The spacing law is unchanged, and it bites harder here. With 1–2 groups, nearly every arrival is answered by the same group, so every pair of linked decision-bearing arrivals is carved ≥12 ticks apart (§11).

T-rule 5 · Narrow Judgements; the held host. Every Judgement is clever: it carries a hinge, ≥2 links, or a lure or decoy. Its own roles therefore field ≤4 contacts (§11). The authored C of a Throne sortie is 4–6. Any formation beyond the Judgement’s roles takes the held host part, which is §11’s held host at Throne scale:

  • a wolfpack waits at the launch zone’s forward edge to L_max (18 ticks, §3), then sails as pin on the lowest-fear lane, with no link and no root sign. It is glanceable mass (§11);
  • a core-bearing formation is decision-bearing even as a pin (§11), so it is held at the forward edge until the Judgement’s live decision-bearing count falls below four (a head sunk, a part routed or withdrawn), and goes home unengaged if that has not happened by t24 (§11).

When a held host exists, its forward-edge presence is part of the Judgement’s signature. It means “the Throne is judging now.” It waits full-screened and bow-on, never in the zone’s depth, so it never reads as a refusal (§15 test 8).

T-rule 6 · LAW · FANATICAL NERVE STILL PLANS FROM FEAR (§2 LAW · INTENSITY TOUCHES NERVE, NEVER THE PLAN; §13; brief -6)
  • The Throne pins its fleet at fanatical. On the lane no hull balks, sags aft or cascades. Refusal follows the fanatical row only: approach cores refuse at 0 shields, and standoff cores never refuse (Victory §4a).
  • In the carving, nothing changes. Lanes, flanks and objectives are chosen from believed fear exactly as §3 and §5 say. The fear haze predicts every lane, and a hold-feint written in Build or Deploy still routes a Judgement.
  • A Throne cannot be frightened off a lane. It can be lied to about which lane is safe.
  • So captains’ independence at the Thrones lives in the lineage policies: grid paths inside a cell, interposition and backfill, crazy-Ivan jinks, which fix a Reaver fixes on, how a Corsair hugs a seam, and the orphan revenge swap. Every deviation is still one of the visible events (§2 law). Balks and sags simply do not happen while the Throne stands. For Vaelress they do not happen while its standard stands.

T-rule 7 · Every set-piece names its channel (CALL-OUT 9). A Judgement is a carving, not a script. Everything it aims at is believed state.

ThroneArchived set-pieceWhat it aims by, re-expressed
ThaesaenSubmerges every 2 sorties; resurfaces “near a random castle”; the tidal wave pushes walls 1 grid outRises behind the rear face whose cells are believed coldest (fear grid). The wave is geometry and aims at nothing
Leavaren3 heads; regrows 5% per sortie if undamagedOwn state: each head’s own hits taken
VaelressPoison cloud rots walls; spawns mini-troopsThe wall section seen mended most (Contact revisions: H6). The plague’s face is chosen by which rotted stone is seen to give first (LOS). The Jam-Hull jams only a tower the Contact ledger holds (a glinted tower, LOS)
RalensaenRoots grow through walls 1 grid per sortie; hides as a treeThe first wall its Bombards touched (Contact). It keeps that wall for as long as the root lives
RhenvaelBoulder throw; climbs for range; cave stealth; stationary 3 ticks when throwingThrows only at glinted guns (Contact). It stands off at the farthest cell it has ever been struck in (its own Effect record). It exits by the cave nearest that cell
EsthaerLava trail; armour weakens below 50%; sinks into lava and rises from a flowOwn movement and own state. It rises from its own trail
SenneirBurrows; erupts under a castle; vulnerable 2 ticks afterThe castle whose surrounding cells are believed coldest (summed fear, §3 weights)
EirlenthIce breath freezes walls (no repair next Build); blizzard stealthThe wall Contact at the end of its own routine lane. The blizzard follows an authored cadence
NaeltharLightning hits “the highest-HP cannon” (this reads truth)The glinted gun inside the fear cell that has written the most Effect on it (Contact ∩ Effect)
NeivaereBecomes a crystal; ricochet beamThe disguise is own state. The beam hits glinted guns only, and only after the disguise has ended

Every archived stealth window keeps its archived cadence: every 15–25 ticks, lasting 5–8, with no fire while hidden. It is authored per Throne and deterministic.

T-rule 8 · When a Throne falls.

  • The Throne’s hull at 0 is half the win. The other half is 4/4 enclosed (GDD Stage 6). The battle goes on until both hold, or until the fleet Retreats or is Annihilated.
  • If it falls mid-Judgement, the links become clock links (§4): the hulls keep the hour the heart foretold, three ticks late and a tick apart, but not the reason. An open hinge takes its foretold branch when its window closes (§4; CALL-OUT 32). The one exception is Eirlenth, whose clock is the blizzard’s, not the heart’s (T-07).
  • Succession. The most command-capable survivor takes the standard, unbuffed. It carves in its own lineage’s grain, and intensity drops to that lineage’s resting point, so balks and sags come back. The master line and the Judgement die with the Throne. The Contact ledger, the fear grid and all six habits persist.
  • Three Thrones have no single heart. Leavaren sinks when its last head sinks. For Neivaere, only the real crystal counts. Vaelress’s standard is a target separate from its hull.

T-rule 9 · The Knowing at the Thrones. After a Throne battle is won, Halyna know the Throne’s own wreck-wood and tell it together, and Seren sets it down (§9b; fiction_hooks §7). They tell:

  • its true name;
  • the haven it holds;
  • its Judgement;
  • the wrong it was grown to avenge.

If the battle is lost, they know the Judgement from the wreck that hurt the fort most, and the name stays untold until that haven is retaken. Partial live readings go on as in E5: a sunk hull of the Judgement floats its line’s root sign for the rest of the sortie. After the tenth Throne comes the green sliver, which both of them touch in the same moment, one hand each, though one hand has been enough since the first winter (fiction_hooks §7; Legends VI.1).

The ten Thrones at a glance (true names, havens, grains, chassis, rehearsal weights, Judgements and twists) are the roster of §23b.

The two Thrones that carve an older form whole

The Flood and the Sounding are the grammar every other carving is written in, so their last forms matured first (§16c; CALL-OUT 10). Thaesaen carves E5-01 The Closing Hand whole, and Naelthar carves E5-02 Come as the Young Wood Came whole. What changes at Throne scale is given in those entries, under At the Throne (§21). Both keep every T-rule: the rings count down to them, their rehearsals speak their lines’ older forms, and they read the one bearing as flanks.

The eight Judgements

Beats refer to the standard sortie (Fight §3): launch zone 5 ticks, reading band 18, engagement band 16 grids, withdrawal 12, backstop 70. The Throne boards are compact and their passage is shorter, so every window below is carved as an offset from a visible event, never as an absolute tick. B marks a blunt counter and C a clever one (§10).

T-01

The Heads That Grow

“Strike one head; two will answer.”
LineKindContactsLinksCut against
Screen · the Bar Before (capstone: E1-04 → E2-03 → E3-04 → E4-03 → T-01)P1 in the reading band → 4 in the engagement band, on one bearinga standing flow at lag 0, and one hingeH7 (H3 deepens it)
Throne

Leavaren (Lea’varen, “bearer of new branches”), the Rivenmen’s Hydra · seated in Tidesmeet (River) · Ironbark · three heads on the Leviathan boss chassis, screened by Bulwarks · master of the Bar Before (Screen).

Era & debut

The Thrones · the Leavaren battle, whenever the player takes the River Throne in the Finale

Grows from

E4-03 The Great Hull and the Kept Hand

Grows into

— (the Screen capstone)

The growth

This is the Kept Hand grown three heads. The great hull that soaked the first volleys is now three great hulls from one root. The reserve that committed once, at its hinge, now goes wherever the axe falls, as often as it falls.

Intent

“They kill the bearers first. Give them three bearers and one shield that follows the axe. Whichever head they choose, the whole tree leans to shield it, and the heads they did not choose grow back. Make every blow cost them the whole wood.”

Chosen when

Forced by the third groan (T-rule 2).

  • Preconditions (otherwise withheld): ≥2 heads afloat, and a wall Contact on the bearing.
  • Detail, from believed state.
    • The heads work the wall with the most accumulated hits (Contact ledger), by the lowest summed-fear lane.
    • The kept hand’s first station is behind the centre head. If Leavaren has judged before in this battle, it is behind the head struck hardest last time (Ironbark sunk cost).
  • Cut against H7 Core-Hunter, which is the whole design. With H3 Early Fire active, the tree stays shut one cell deeper, so the first volley falls on shields.
  • Rehearsals. Ironbark (Siege +3, Flood +2, Screen +1) plus the master +3 make every groan sortie a Screen or Siege form: Cold Shadow and The Great Hull and the Kept Hand with a head as the great hull, and The Same Stone on the wall it means to judge.
  • Only Leavaren carves it.
Composition

One bearing. The fan spans all three flanks.

PartHullsCore / screenRoleContacts: reading band → engagement band
The tree3 heads, each a third of Leavaren’s hull · a shared screen of 9 Bulwarks (Ships §9: 4 × 3^0.775 ≈ 9) · 2 Quartermaster riders (Signals Ship)3 cores · 9 shields · 2 riderssiege (the heads shell the wall) + screen1 → 3
The kept hand3 of the 9 Bulwarks and both Quartermasters, split from the tree at the commit pointscreensreserve, carrying the twist0 → 1

Decision-bearing: 1 in the reading band and 4 in the engagement band (three heads and the kept hand). A fifth or sixth formation is the held host (T-rule 5). A wolfpack sails as a glanceable pin at L_max. A Runner convoy is core-bearing, so it is held at the forward edge until a head sinks, and lands on the softest coast only then; if no head has sunk by t24 it goes home and sails first next sortie (§11).

Twist · the root that hears
  • The kept hand’s link to the heads fires at lag 0, and it is standing: it re-fires every time a different head takes the lead in hits this sortie. Each head’s own damage record is own state.
  • The Quartermasters carry the twist (Signals Ship speeds propagation, Ships §6).
  • With both Quartermasters sunk, the link falls back to the ordinary kind. It fires once, at the 1-tick lag, and never again that sortie.
Choreography

Across the battle.

  • The groan sorties rehearse (above) while Leavaren counts which head the Rivenmen strike, and in what order.
  • Regrowth. At every sortie boundary, groan or Judgement, each head that took no hit that sortie regrows 5% of Leavaren’s whole hull.
    • Source: the archived Hydra value. Range 3–8%.
    • Settled so that a player who strikes all three heads each sortie never loses ground, and one who strikes a single head loses about a tenth of the Throne per sortie.
    • A sunk head never regrows.

The Judgement sortie.

  1. The tree leaves its seat as one convoy: three lobes of shields bow-on, tight, aspect locked on the hottest cell ahead. It is the Bar Before, three times over. It takes the lowest-fear lane and does not balk.
  2. The opening. At the commit point the heads fan half a cell apart across the three flanks, each keeping 2 shields. The kept hand holds half a cell behind the centre head. One contact becomes four.
  3. The flow. The kept hand sails AFTER(a head leads in hits) to that head and backfills its screen (Ships §9 reallocation).
    • Each flow shows the answering sail on the kept hand and on the head it joins. On a Throne the bark itself blanches.
    • A flow to an outer head takes about 6 ticks: half of a 13-grid cell at 1 grid per tick (Victory §3).
  4. The hinge. IF(a head sinks in the engagement band) → Two will answer : the fan holds to the withdrawal.
    • Two will answer: within 6 ticks the survivors close into one tree around the kept hand. The sunk head’s shields are released to them (Ships §9 surplus rule), making a two-core screen of 7. The tree tightens and presses the same wall.
  5. Withdrawal in order. Unstruck heads regrow at the boundary.
Grading
  • Succeeded: a breach in the judged wall.
  • Countered: the kept hand lost both Quartermasters before its second flow, or every head was struck and no breach came.
  • Failed: two heads sank.
Signature
  • First time. One enormous shieldwall in three lobes comes bow-on and tight, then opens at the edge of your fire into three great heads. A knot of shields sits behind the middle one. Whenever a head takes a beating, the knot slides to it, its sails going pale. At the next countdown, the heads you left alone put out new leaf.
  • Returning tell. The three-lobed Bar Before in the reading band. Then, at the first volley, the kept hand’s sails blanch together with the struck head’s. That means the Quartermasters are riding and the hand will flow again.
Captains’ independence
  • The heads are fanatical: none balks, sags or routs (T-rule 6).
  • Each head picks its own grid path and keeps its own two shields in interposition. Its screen backfills from within before the kept hand arrives.
  • The kept hand’s Bulwarks jink under fire and re-form.
  • Flag sunk. Leavaren has no single heart. The standard lives in the root and passes from head to head, and the Throne sinks only when its last head sinks (T-rule 8).
  • A sunk head’s shields do not take the revenge swap. The surplus rule releases them to the survivors.
Counters
  • B · Prune evenly. Centre the AoR on the centre head and grow it with a Spotter until its reach takes all three. Use Saturation for the fanned line and a Piercer on any head whose screen has thinned. With every head struck every sortie there is no regrowth, and one kept hand cannot shield three heads. It is slow and sure and needs no timing.
  • B · Hold the wall. Put a Stonewright on the face the heads shell and build a second ring behind it, then make that face’s cells SCALDING with two groups’ fire. The heads chose their lane from fear at the boundary, but once on it they never balk (§2 LAW · INTENSITY TOUCHES NERVE, NEVER THE PLAN), so they grind on stone inside a kill-zone they did not believe in when they came: the meat-grinder a fanatical fleet walks into (Ships §4).
  • C · Cut the root’s ears, then choose. The kept hand’s station is known a whole Build ahead: behind the centre head, or behind the head you struck hardest last time. Centre a Marksman’s reach there, so the Quartermasters are the highest-value hulls it can see (Cannon §5 target personality). With both gone, the hand flows once. Draw that one flow with an opening volley on the west head. Then spend the held alpha on the east head, on the tick the hand commits west (hold-fire → weapons-free; in Command Time that tick is a choice, not a reflex, and the second order falls inside the first’s post-commit dwell or on the announced commit tick, §12). The east head stands with two shields.
  • C · Bring them down together. Sinking one head early closes the other two into a 7-shield tree. So wear all three low, and finish them inside one engagement band. The close takes 6 ticks, and a half-closed tree is two heads in the open.
If it fails
  • Leavaren’s reaction (Ironbark). Failed → +1, preferring the line’s next form. The capstone has none, so it comes heavier on the same road. The rings reset, and at the next third groan it judges again on the same bearing and the same wall, with the kept hand starting behind the head you struck hardest last time: it expects the same axe. The rehearsals in between turn to the Mute Square on that wall.
  • What it has learned.
    • the order in which the Rivenmen strike heads (H7, and its own damage record);
    • every cell that sank a head (fear).
  • The exploit. Its stubbornness is the opening. The kept hand’s first station can be read off the last Judgement.
The Carving

Known from the root-wood of a sunk head by Rhyna, whose palms were on it, and by Halvard as she knew it; Seren set it down as they told it:

Three crowns, one root. Where the axe bites, lean all the wood; the crown unbitten, let it leaf.

The Name

Leavaren, bearer of new branches, rooted in Tidesmeet: for the pillars they floated down the river to be their bridges.

Real-world root

Lepanto, 1571. Santa Cruz’s reserve plugged the Christian line twice, first on the left, then against Uluç Ali. A reserve that can see plugs every gap; a blind one plugs one.

T-02

The Thrown Stone

“Every gun that speaks, the mountain answers.”
LineKindContactsLinksCut against
Hunt · the Spark (capstone: E1-05 → E2-05 → E3-05 → E4-07 → E5-06 → T-02)P4 on one bearing3, one a hingeH3, H1 (H5 turns on verification)
Throne

Rhenvael (Rhen’vael, “stone-grain”), the Rivenmen’s Colossus · seated in Carnhold (Mountain) · Blackthorn · Leviathan boss chassis (Grand Battery) · master of the Spark (Hunt).

Era & debut

The Thrones · the Rhenvael battle

Grows from

E5-06 The Prize Too Easy

Grows into

— (the Hunt capstone)

The growth

This is the Prize Too Easy with the prize and the hunter grown into one hull. The mountain is the prize every gun wants to shoot, it stands where no gun it knows can reach, and every gun that shoots at it is found and broken.

Intent

“They hide their guns behind stone and keep them quiet. We cannot find a gun that does not speak, so make speaking cost a gun and make silence cost the wall. If they speak, the mountain answers. If they hold, the mountain walks.”

Chosen when

Forced by the third groan.

  • Preconditions: none beyond a standoff cell, which always exists. It is never withheld, because silence changes its branch, not whether it falls.
  • Detail, from believed state.
    • Standoff: one cell beyond the farthest cell Rhenvael has ever been struck in (its own Effect record: Grand Battery, Ships §6). High ground is preferred where the board has it. Before it has ever been struck, it stops at the inner edge of the reading band.
    • Throw order:
      1. the glinted gun in the cell where the fleet lost the most hulls last sortie (Blackthorn’s +2);
      2. then the freshest glint;
      3. ties go to the lower cell index.
    • With H5 Relocator active, only glints re-verified by LOS this sortie are thrown at. It has learned that you move guns.
  • Cut against H3 Early Fire (throws begin at the reading band’s edge) and H1 Silent Quarter (the Walk goes along the silent flank, daring the hold-feint that hid there before).
  • Rehearsals. Hunt +6: Make an Example, Borrow the Road, The Prize Too Easy. The mountain rehearses the Spark.
Composition

One bearing, centre flank.

PartHullsCore / screenRoleContacts
The mountainRhenvael + 2 Bulwark shields (command convoy)core · shieldshunt + siege1
The eyesa Recon pair holding station together (it never pickets here)wolfpackprobe: keeps glints fresh by LOS1
First packReaver wolfpack (3)wolfpackhunt, the second target1
Second packReaver wolfpack (3)wolfpackhunt, the third target1

Four contacts, all decision-bearing. The two packs split fixes across exposed guns (Reaver Pack, Ships §6), so the stone and the packs never share a target, and a pack’s errand is not voided by the stone.

Twist · every glint, at once

Every glint any hull writes reaches the mountain and both packs at lag 0. It is the fleet-wide fix-sharing a Reaver Warlord gives its packs (Ships §6), given to this Throne as its twist: Rhenvael is no Reaver, and no other Leviathan shares fixes. Nothing that speaks is heard late.

Choreography

Across the battle. The groan sorties rehearse Hunt forms. Each one teaches Rhenvael a longer reach: every hit from farther out moves its next standoff farther out. Cave stealth runs every sortie on its archived cadence, exiting by the cave nearest the standoff.

The Judgement sortie.
