  1. The mountain leaves its seat and stops at its standoff: exactly one cell past the farthest place it was ever struck, and never nearer. The eyes run to the fear edge. The packs idle at the fear edge behind them (the Spark).
  2. The hinge. IF(≥1 gun glinted by 6 ticks after the reading band ends) → the Thrown Stone : the Walk.
  3. The Thrown Stone.
    • The mountain throws at glinted guns in order: a 2×2 impact (archived).
    • The cycle. Each throw has a 3-tick wind-up (archived: stationary 3 ticks), during which the mountain stands still and its two shields part to clear the arc. It has no interposition then. As each stone leaves, the mountain turns its head onto its next target, so every wind-up is announced a full cycle ahead. Starting cycle: 9 ticks per throw (3 wind-up + 6 recovery, the slow end of the Leviathan’s “glacial fire rate”). Range 7–12. Settled so testers can count the cadence by ear by the second stone.
    • The first pack sails AFTER(the second glint). The second pack sails AFTER(the third glint), no sooner than 12 ticks behind the first.
  4. The Walk. The mountain leaves its standoff and closes on the wall with the most accumulated hits, fanatical, into whatever reach is there. The packs wait at the fear edge AFTER(glint).
Grading
  • Succeeded: a thrown-at or hunted gun destroyed.
  • Countered: the Walk was taken and the mountain was struck inside a reach it had never seen.
  • Failed: both packs gone before their commit points and no gun destroyed.
Signature
  • First time. A mountain far out beyond your guns stops at the edge of the farthest place you ever hit it, and comes no closer. Each time one of your open guns fires, the mountain goes still, leans back, and a stone comes down on that gun. Small packs of hunters bend toward whichever gun the stone did not take. If nobody speaks, the mountain walks in.
  • Returning tell. The standoff stop: it halts one cell past your known reach, never nearer. Plus a Reaver pack idling at the fear edge. The Spark at mountain scale.
Captains’ independence
  • The packs fixate. Each keeps its fix in sight and chooses its own path through the seams. A pack whose gun is re-enclosed mid-run loses its errand and goes (Victory §4b).
  • The eyes skirt at sight range.
  • Flag sunk. If the mountain sinks mid-Judgement, glint sharing ends: fixes travel slow and local (+3 ticks), and the packs keep the fixes they hold.
Counters
  • B · Say nothing. Enclose every gun and Camouflage every exposed group. No glint means no stone, and the hinge takes the Walk: a fanatical capital walking into enclosed guns. Grind it with Piercer and Powder Store. No timing.
  • B · Move what it found. Relocate every glinted gun in Deploy. The stones fall on ghosts (stale positives) until H5 turns on verification. After that, sink the eyes and relocation works again.
  • C · Hide your longest arm. Never let it be struck from your longest reach: keep one group silent, or grow its AoR with a Spotter after the last time it was seen. Its standoff then falls inside that reach. Feed it one glint from a gun you will relocate next Deploy. When it winds up, alpha the 3-tick wind-up from the group it does not know (Piercer; hold-fire → weapons-free on the wind-up tick). The window is short but announced: the throw follows a glint the player chose to create, and the mountain turns its head onto the gun it will throw at next as soon as the previous stone leaves, a full cycle (9 ticks) before the wind-up and 12 before it ends (a presence; see The cycle). So the wind-up tick is known ≥12 ticks ahead, and the held alpha is placed before it comes, not in reaction to it. In Command Time it is a chosen tick.
  • C · Blind the verifier. With H5 active, sink the Recon pair early. Unverified glints are then left alone, and every gun you moved is safe.
If it fails
  • Rhenvael’s reaction (Blackthorn). Failed → −3, but +2 to any carving aimed at the cell where it lost most hulls last sortie. Between Judgements it hunts that cell, and its next Judgement’s first stone goes there. That is predictable a Build ahead: leave a ghost there.
  • What it has learned.
    • the farthest cell it was struck in, so its standoff ratchets out;
    • who speaks first (H3);
    • who moves guns (H5).
The Carving

Known from a stone-scarred plank of the mountain’s own flank by Halvard, whose hands were on the wood; Rhyna knew it as he knew it:

Stand past the farthest fire. Whatever speaks, answer with the mountain; whatever keeps silent, walk to it.

The Name

Rhenvael, stone-grain, seated in Carnhold: for the smithies on the cairn-mountain, where the iron of the felling was beaten out.

Real-world root

Counter-battery doctrine: Bruchmüller’s 1918 fire plans put a counter-battery phase on every battery that flash- and sound-ranging had located. Translated: the guns here are static, so the throw hunts the glint, not the gun.

T-03

The Roots Beneath the Wall

“Go under what you cannot go through.”
LineKindContactsLinksCut against
Siege · the Mute Square (capstone: E1-02 → E3-02 → E4-04 → T-03)P4 on one bearing3, one a hingeH6 (H3 moves the face out a cell; H11 does not move it)
Throne

Ralensaen (Ra’lensaen, “root-heart”, a pillar walking home), the Rivenmen’s Ancient Treant · seated in Holtward (Forest) · Ironbark · Bulwark boss chassis · master of the Mute Square (Siege).

Era & debut

The Thrones · the Ralensaen battle

Grows from

E4-04 When the Stone Gives

Grows into

— (the Siege capstone)

The growth

This is When the Stone Gives with the sapping carried underground. The wall worked over sorties is now worked from beneath. The Breacher that waited for the stone to crack now waits for the ground to open. And the first wall the young wood fixed on at Cam 1 is the wall the eldest wood goes under.

Intent

“They mend what we break. Shot is wasted on a mended wall, and no mending reaches below the stone. Go under the first wall we touched, a grid each morrow. When the ground gives, the ram.”

Chosen when

Forced by the third groan.

  • Preconditions (otherwise withheld): the root alive, and ≥1 wall grid undermined.
  • The root’s wall is fixed at the first groan: the first wall the Bombard line touches (Contact). That is E1-02’s fixation, grown patient. Ironbark never moves it while the root lives.
  • Cut against H6 Mender: mending does not reach below. With H3 active, the Bombards stand off one cell farther. H11 (Kill-Box), if active, does not move it: Ironbark counts the kill-box and comes anyway, because it never leaves the root’s wall (its characteristic error, and the exploit below).
  • Rehearsals. Siege +6: The Same Stone and When the Stone Gives on the root’s wall. The Mute Square every sortie.
Composition

One bearing, the root’s flank.

PartHullsCore / screenRoleContacts
The TreantRalensaen, standing as a tree among the treescoresiege (the root)1 (via the heave; 1 in body once it moves)
The faceBombard line + 2 shields, line abreastcore · shieldssiege on the root’s wall, from the surface1
The ramBreacher wedge + 3 shieldscore · shieldsstrike, loitering1
The handsRunner convoy carrying Sapper and Pioneer troopscore · shieldscargo, loitering1

Four decision-bearing.

Twist · the root speaks through the ground
  • The surfacing is a clock that every hull keeps (WITH).
  • Troops ashore on the root’s line are linked to the Treant at lag 0, so the Sappers and Pioneers never straggle.
Choreography

Across the battle (the standing part).

  • Groan 1. The Bombards touch a wall, and that wall is the root’s. The root runs from the Treant under open ground at 1 grid per tick. That is the sea-speed analogue (Victory §3), a feel value in the range 0.5–1, settled so the heave is visible for at least half a reading band. It reaches the foot of the wall. Where it runs, the ground heaves.
  • At each later sortie boundary, the root advances 1 grid beneath the wall (the archived rate) and undermines each grid it passes under.
  • Mending restores a grid’s stone but not the ground under it: an undermined grid is still undermined.

The Judgement sortie.

  1. The face comes in line abreast on the root’s wall. The ram and the hands wait visibly at the launch zone’s forward edge, full-screened and bow-on (§3 LAW · A HOLD IS A PRESENCE; §15 test 8). The ram’s lane is its own straight line to the root’s wall, fixed at carving (§24 Ships §6).
  2. The surfacing tick is carved at the face’s commit tick + 6.
    • WITH(t_surface): every undermined grid collapses. The breach is as wide as the root: starting 2 grids (the Sapper’s 2×2 detonation, Ships §7), range 1–3.
    • The root’s head breaks the ground inside.
  3. The hinge. IF(breach observed within 6 ticks of t_surface) → the Ram : the Mute Square.
    • The Ram: the ram drives the breach, and the hands land Sappers and Pioneers 12 ticks behind it. The Pioneers bridge the rubble; the Sappers go for the next ring.
    • The Mute Square: the face keeps shelling the undermined face from outside, and the ram drives its one straight lane at the unopened wall, a ram at a whole wall, because a Breacher only commits (Ships §6). The hands stay at the forward edge, full and forward, never thin: they are held, not refusing (§11), and sail first next sortie.
  4. A wall thickened past the root’s head does not open. The surfacing breaks only the grids above the root.
Grading
  • Succeeded: the Ram branch reaches the second ring, or the breach holds to the withdrawal.
  • Countered: the Mute Square.
  • Failed: the Treant sinks, or the ram and hands are eliminated before commit.
Signature
  • First time. A heave in the ground crawls from the forest toward one wall and stops at its foot. Every assault shells that same wall. A wedge sits motionless in the launch zone. The ground under the wall humps a little further each time. One tree in the wood does not move in the wind.
  • Returning tell. The heave line and the Mute Square together. The heave points home: it runs from the tree that does not sway.
Captains’ independence
  • The face’s Bombards jink and hold their spacing.
  • The Sappers chain on the nearest wall. The Pioneers choose which rubble to bridge.
  • The ram re-forms under fire (Breacher wedge).
  • Flag sunk. The Treant holds the root. Sunk, the root dies where it lies: no more grids are undermined, but grids already undermined stay so for the battle. The ram and the hands still keep the carved surfacing hour and drive at a wall that may not have opened: the uncanny scene of fiction_hooks §5.
Counters
  • B · Thicken the wall. Every Build, add one grid on the root’s line, on the inside. The root makes one grid per sortie, so it never surfaces through. Or build a second ring behind. Stonewright cannot save the ground; Build can. Interdictor + Breaker at the heave’s end: the ram is the enemy nearest your wall, and the Breaker shoves it back into the rubble. Splasher on the troop clump. No timing.
  • B · The kill-box. Leave the root’s wall as it is and make the ring behind it a kill-box (overcommit-punish, Fleet Memory §6). The Ram pours the wedge and the troops into it. Hold fire until the ram commits.
  • C · Follow the heave home. Flare the forest where the heave begins, at the tree that does not sway, and deep-centre an AoR on it. It cannot fire while it stands as a tree (T-rule 7). Sink it and the root dies with it, and so does the Judgement.
If it fails
  • Ralensaen’s reaction (Ironbark). Failed → +1: it comes again, heavier, under the same wall, and the root keeps growing. Sunk cost: it never switches walls while the root lives. That is the exploit: the kill-box is a Build-phase certainty.
  • What it has learned.
    • which grids were thickened (Contact);
    • H6.
The Carving

Known from a root-end dug out of the breach by Rhyna, whose palms were on the grain; Halvard, still in the breach, knew it as she did:

Where the stone will not open, go beneath it. Root by root, morrow by morrow; when the ground gives, the ram.

The Name

Ralensaen, root-heart, walking home to Holtward: for the keep they roofed with our felled kin.

Real-world root

Tyre, 332 BC, where Alexander built a mole of about 800 m over about seven months. Rochester, 1215, where the mine under the south-east corner was fired with the fat of forty pigs.

T-04

The Road Under the Sand

“Show them the host; come up beneath the stone.”
LineKindContactsLinksCut against
Feint · the Two Mouths (capstone: E2-04 → E3-06 → E5-03 → T-04)P4 on one bearing2, one a hinge, beside a standing hostH4 (H2 picks the host’s flank)
Throne

Senneir (Sen’neir, “the deep-beneath”), the Rivenmen’s Sandworm · seated in Sandreach (Desert) · Willow · Leviathan boss chassis · master of the Two Mouths (Feint).

Era & debut

The Thrones · the Senneir battle

Grows from

E5-03 Fortitude

Grows into

— (the Feint capstone)

The growth

This is Fortitude with its second road dug downward. On one bearing the Feint line has no other approach, so the Worm makes one under the sand. The host above is Fortitude’s phantom main effort, and it still promises a second landing after the real one has come.

Intent

“They will guard where the host points. Show them the host, and let them look. Beneath the looking, go. Rise under the castle they watch least, and look once more before rising, for the willow bends before it breaks.”

Chosen when

Forced by the third groan.

  • Preconditions (otherwise withheld): ≥2 flanks with a castle behind them, and the host’s parts afloat (a Mimic and a Provocateur).
  • Detail, from believed state. Two castles are carved:
    • A, the castle whose surrounding cells are believed coldest (summed fear at §3 weights);
    • B, the next coldest.
    • Ties go to the castle farther from the host’s flank, then the lower index.
  • Cut against H4 Re-tasker: the host draws the reach, and the Worm rises under what the reach left. With H2 active (the favoured flank), the host goes to that flank.
  • Rehearsals. Willow (Sounding +3, Patience +2) plus the master Feint +3: The Sounding and Ask the Silent Water map which castle’s cells stay cold. Noise in the East and Open Roads run on flanks (T-rule 4).
Composition

One bearing: the host on one flank, the Worm beneath.

PartHullsCore / screenRoleContacts
The hostMimic mirror-host projecting a landing forcesolodecoy1
The goadProvocateur wolfpackwolfpacklure: pushes the flank’s exposed guns1
The WormSenneir, burrowedcorestrike beneath1
The landingRunner convoy with a Sower ridercore · shields · ridercargo, loitering1

Four decision-bearing.

Twist · the road beneath

Bosses cross the DMZ (GDD). The Worm crosses under everything: walls, the DMZ and the bearing’s fear. Its burrow is the second road the one-bearing map lacks, and no other hull can take it.

Choreography

Across the battle (the standing part). The host appears on the same flank from the first groan and persists: Fortitude’s phantom main effort, two sorties deep before the Judgement. Where Senneir lies burrowed at its seat, the sand stirs.

The Judgement sortie.

  1. The host and the goad demonstrate on their flank: lateral, never closing (the Two Mouths). The goad pushes the exposed guns there.
  2. The Worm charges from its seat toward A, and the fissure draws itself across the sand.
    • Fairness line. The fissure points at its castle ≥12 ticks before the Worm rises (the spacing floor). The Worm’s pace under the sand is carved to hold that.
  3. The hinge. IF(the host draws fire that writes new fear on A's cells, within a 6-tick window) → the fissure bends for B : it holds for A. The bent fissure again points ≥12 ticks ahead.
  4. The Worm erupts under its castle, damaging the castle and its walls, and is vulnerable for 2 ticks (archived).
  5. The landing sails AFTER(eruption observed), arriving ≥12 ticks behind the eruption. Its Sower smokes the last cell.
  6. The host keeps demonstrating until the withdrawal. It is still promising a second landing.
Grading
  • Succeeded: the eruption breaks the castle’s enclosure.
  • Countered: the host drew no fire and the eruption met a covered castle.
  • Failed: the Worm was driven off before rising, or the landing was eliminated before commit.
Signature
  • First time. A great host moves back and forth on one flank, sortie after sortie, and never quite comes in. Then a line of broken sand runs across the desert toward one of your castles, bends once, and the ground under that castle bursts. Ships come for the hole it leaves.
  • Returning tell. The Two Mouths: a lateral host that never commits. The host’s hulls do not jink when shells land near them (E5-03’s seam). And where the Worm lies, the sand stirs.
Captains’ independence
  • The goad chooses which exposed gun to push.
  • The projections keep in character, never in formation discipline, drifting in mirror symmetry (Ships §6).
  • The Sower picks its smoke cell.
  • Flag sunk. If the Worm is sunk before rising, the landing still sails on the carved eruption hour, into a castle that never opened (clock links).
Counters
  • B · Leave no cold castle. Cover every castle, which at 4/4 is required anyway, and grow reach with a Spotter. Keep the group’s AoR home: do not re-task to the host. Interdictor + Breaker at the castles’ inner faces: the risen Worm is the enemy nearest your wall. No timing.
  • C · Answer the fissure, not the host. The fissure’s heading, and so the rise tick, are known ≥12 ticks ahead (the fairness line). Pre-task an AoR onto its end, and alpha the Worm’s 2-tick vulnerability after the eruption with a held volley placed before the rise, not in reaction to it (hold-fire → weapons-free on the rise tick, chosen in Command Time).
  • C · Test the host. One test shot at the host: projections die together (Ships §6). Then ignore that flank and keep the reach on the castles.
If it fails
  • Senneir’s reaction (Willow). Failed → −5; Countered → −3. It switches at once: the next cycle’s rehearsals turn to Sounding forms that probe the castles, and its next Judgement starts from a castle whose cover it has not yet seen.
  • What it has learned.
    • which castle’s cells turned hot when the host was fired on (H4);
    • which flank was guarded (H2).
The Carving

Known from a length of rib broken out of the sand, both of them kneeling, though only her hands were on it:

Show them the host and let them look. Go beneath the looking; rise where no eye is.

The Name

Senneir, the deep-beneath, under Sandreach: for the deep galleries of the canyon citadel, shored with our felled kin.

Real-world root

Constantinople, 1453. On the night of 22 April, Mehmed II had 70–80 ships hauled about a mile overland on greased rollers into the Golden Horn, behind the boom.

T-05

The Burning Retreat

“Burn as you go; let them chase the fire.”
LineKindContactsLinksCut against
Lure · the Turned Stern (capstone: E1-07 → E3-03 → E4-05 → T-05)P4 on one bearing2–3, one a hingeH4, H3 (H5 bends the trail)
Throne

Esthaer (Es’thaer, “the burning”), the Rivenmen’s Magma Titan · seated in Emberhythe (Volcanic) · Twistbough · Leviathan boss chassis · master of the Turned Stern (Lure).

Era & debut

The Thrones · the Esthaer battle

Grows from

E4-05 Along the Shore, Home by the Far Road

Grows into

— (the Lure capstone)

The growth

This is the long feigned flight with the heart itself as the bait. At Kalka a column gave up sector after sector. Here the Throne sheds its own crust, a true weakness, because the wood cannot lie. It walks away burning, and comes home up out of its own fire.

Intent

“They follow a wound they can reach, with every gun that has the range, and every gun that follows is seen. Bare the soft side and walk away along the shore. When their reach has followed, rise where it left.”

Chosen when

Forced by the third groan.

  • Preconditions (otherwise withheld): a shore lane along ≥2 flanks, and the waiting Corsairs afloat.
  • Detail, from believed state.
    • It turns toward the flank believed to hold the most exposed guns (Contact), where pursuit will glint.
    • It rises at the start of its own trail: the turning cell, which is the arc the pursuing reach leaves.
  • Cut against H4 Re-tasker and H3 Early Fire. With H5 Relocator active, the trail hugs the shore in front of the glinted guns.
  • Rehearsals. Twistbough (Feint +3, Mask +2, Lure +2) plus the master +3: Feigned Flight, Along the Shore, and a Mask or Feint form. Never the same one twice (Succeeded → −2).
Composition

One bearing, centre and one side flank.

PartHullsCore / screenRoleContacts
The TitanEsthaer + Bulwark shieldwallcore · shieldslure: the only Throne that baits with its own heart1
The waitingCorsair wolfpack at the centre’s far fear edgewolfpackstrike1
The returnBreacher wedge (or Runner convoy); a Breacher’s lane is its own straight line to the turning cell’s wall, fixed at carvingcore · shieldsstrike/cargo, loitering1
The burnersSower wolfpack (Sapper-Ship)wolfpacklays fire on the shore behind the side flank’s guns1

Four decision-bearing.

Twist · the heart as bait
  • The flag takes the lure role. No other Throne and no other Tide ever spends its heart as the bait.
  • Its trail is its road home. The archived “sinks into lava, erupts from a lava flow” becomes a return along its own fire.
  • Where the trail and the Sowers’ fire touch the shore, those grids are area denial until the next Build. No gun can be hauled across them in the coming Deploy (Deploy §3), so the guns it found tend to stay where it found them.
Choreography

Across the battle. The rehearsals vary, never repeating. Esthaer counts which guns pursue and which reaches move.

The Judgement sortie.

  1. The Titan comes in on the centre and takes fire. Its crust glows at the seams from the reading band: the promise. The waiting Corsairs sit still at the centre’s far fear edge.
  2. The turn. At its turning cell (the first engagement-band cell) it sheds its crust and withdraws sideways along the shore toward the side flank, aft and lateral, in shape (the Turned Stern). It trails lava on every grid it crosses.
    • The shed crust. While the crust is shed, the Titan takes +50% damage. It is a true weakness. The value is anchored to the archived “armour weakens”; range +25–75%; settled so that pursuit with fire costs the Titan real hull. The bait must be worth taking.
  3. The burners lay fire on the shore behind the side flank’s exposed guns.
  4. The hinge. IF(pursuit writes ≥1 new glint, or new fear on cells the Titan passed as COLD (the H4 event), within 8 ticks of the turn) → the Return : the Stand.
    • The Return: the Titan sinks into its trail (archived stealth, 5–8 ticks) and rises at the trail’s start, the turning cell. The Corsairs go WITH the rising. The wedge lands 12 ticks behind them. The rise comes ≥12 ticks after the turn.
    • The Stand: nobody chased, so the flight was real enough. The Titan turns back in on the side flank and burns the wall there, and the Corsairs join it. The hinge decides only when the wedge drives, never where: in the Stand it drives its same straight lane at its latest tick, alone (§24 Ships §6).
Grading
  • Succeeded: the Return lands on the abandoned arc.
  • Countered: the Stand.
  • Failed: the Corsairs and the wedge were eliminated before commit.
Signature
  • First time. A burning giant cracks open, backs away along the shore too orderly, and trails fire. A pack of raiders sits perfectly still at the edge of the fear where the giant turned. Then the giant goes down into its own fire and comes up where it started, with the raiders on either side of it.
  • Returning tell. In the reading band: glowing seams on the Titan’s crust, and a still pack at the far fear edge. At the turning cell: the Turned Stern, a retreat that keeps its spacing (a true rout scatters, Ships §4).
Captains’ independence
  • The shieldwall holds interposition on the retreat and jinks under fire.
  • The Corsairs choose their own seam in.
  • The burners choose which shore grids to fire.
  • Flag sunk. If the Titan sinks mid-retreat, which is likelier than for any other Throne because it is bared, the Corsairs and the wedge keep the carved rising hour and strike an empty arc (clock links). The lava cools at the next Build as usual.
Counters
  • B · Do not follow. Each group keeps its arc and fires only what enters it. Suppressor on the centre, which is the return lane. The shed crust is a gift only to guns already on it.
  • B · Pursue with stone. Pursue only with enclosed guns (Camouflage the group). That fire writes no glint. Without a re-task there is no H4 event either, and the hinge takes the Stand: a straight fight on the side flank.
  • C · Take the bait with fire, never with a re-task. Keep the AoR centred on the turning cell. The trail’s start is visible, and that is where it rises. Let the retreat pass through your reach’s edge. When the Titan rises, crust still shed, alpha it (hold-fire → weapons-free on the rise tick). The lure becomes the kill.
  • C · Two groups. One pursues with enclosed fire. The other holds silent on the centre (a hold-feint). The Corsairs and the rising Titan come up into a silent group.
If it fails
  • Esthaer’s reaction (Twistbough). Failed → −3; Succeeded → −2 (“never twice”). Either way the next Judgement mirrors itself: it retreats toward the other flank, and the rehearsals between switch to Mask and Feint forms.
  • What it has learned.
    • which guns pursued (glints);
    • whose reach moved (H4);
    • which glinted guns could not be moved (H5, since the lava held them).
The Carving

Known from a charred keel-end hauled out of the cooling shore by Halvard, whose hands were on the wood; Rhyna knew it as he knew it, and the knowing burned them both:

Bare the soft side and walk away burning. Where they leave to follow, rise.

The Name

Esthaer, the burning, in Emberhythe: for the forges that ate our felled kin for charcoal.

Real-world root

The Kalka River, 1223: Jebe and Subutai’s nine-day feigned retreat strung out a coalition claimed at 80,000. Salamis, 480 BC: the Greek line backing water before it turned to ram.

T-06

The Slow Rot

“Rot the wall; wait for the mending to fail.”
LineKindContactsLinksCut against
Bleed · the Breath (capstone: E2-06 → E3-07 → E4-06 → E5-07 → T-06)P4 on one bearing3, one a hinge, beside the standing rotH6, H3 (H9 makes the brick carry)
Throne

Vaelress (Vael’ress, “the grain that rots”), the Rivenmen’s Plague Herald · seated in Fenholm (Swamp) · Yew · Herald boss chassis, with Chirurgeon and Quartermaster riders · master of the Breath (Bleed).

Era & debut

The Thrones · the Vaelress battle

Grows from

E4-06 Sea Below, Sky Above · E5-07 The First Ship Carries (its brick, with H9 active)

Grows into

— (the Bleed capstone)

The growth

This is the Bleed line’s last timing, and it times the Rivenmen’s hands instead of their guns. The brick spent one volley. The breath was timed to the reload. Sea and sky were timed to the domain switch. The first ship bore what the brick only pretended to. Now the rot is timed to the mending, and the strike comes in the breath between volleys.

Intent

“They mend what we break. So rot what they mend, in more places than their hands can reach, and count the hands. Come in when the rot outruns them, between two of their breaths. Hold the standard high, so no hull turns back before the stone gives.”

Chosen when

Forced by the third groan.

  • Preconditions (otherwise withheld): two wall sections under rot, and the standard or the Herald afloat.
  • Detail, from believed state.
    • S is the section seen mended most (H6, from Contact revisions).
    • S′ is the next.
  • Cut against H6 Mender and H3 Early Fire. The rot on the walls is what makes the Rivenmen menders: the Herald teaches you to mend, then counts your mending. It counts only what it can see: a rotted breach walled over in Build (§6 H6). A Stonewright’s mending inside the Fight is invisible to it, and simply slows the rot (true HP).
  • With H9 active (they let the first ship pass), the brick is E5-07’s first ship: a thin, laden Runner in the brick’s dress, and the plague’s stack comes ashore from it if you let it pass, sent to the face the hinge chooses.
  • Rehearsals. Yew (Patience +3, Bleed +2) plus the master +3: Toss a Brick, Between Their Breaths, Let the Fire Work.
Composition

One bearing.

PartHullsCore / screenRoleContacts
The HeraldVaelress + its standard · Bulwark shieldwall · 2 Chirurgeon riders (heal-pulse) · 1 Quartermaster Jam-Hull (Ships §6 Specials)core · shields · riderssiege (the rot on S)1
The rot-sowersSower wolfpackwolfpacksiege (the rot on S′)1
The brickone cheap Bombard, alone; with H9 active, a thin laden Runner (E5-07), whose landed stack is the plague—lure (decoy with H9)1
The plaguea Swarm stack of mini-troops rising from the rot (archived set-piece)troop stackstrike1

Four decision-bearing.

Twist · the standard
  • While the standard stands, every hull is Herald-pinned: never refuses, never routs, never cascades (Victory §4a, Herald-pinned row).
  • The Herald re-anchors on the standard, not on a successor (Ships §4).
  • Break the standard and nerve drops to default for the battle: balks, sags and cascades return.
Choreography

Across the battle (the standing part). Every sortie, the Herald lays its cloud on the section seen mended most. The rot eats wall grids through the Fight at a rate that outruns one Stonewright Post’s mending:

  • starting value 1.5× one Post’s mending (the archived rate is 1% per tick in the cloud);
  • range 1.2–2×;
  • settled so that one Post loses a section in two sorties, never in one, and two Posts hold it.

The Rivenmen wall over, in Build, what the rot has broken, and the fleet counts each breach it sees walled over (H6).

The Judgement sortie.

  1. The Herald clouds S. The rot-sowers cloud S′. That is two rotting faces, and more rot than one pair of hands can mend.
  2. The brick goes in alone on S’s face, draws the volley, and turns (the Breath).
  3. The hinge. IF(a breach is observed on S′ before the brick's volley + 6) → the plague rises at S′ : the plague rises at S. The window runs from the rot’s first tick to 6 ticks after the brick draws the volley (≥6 ticks, §11). Either way it goes to the face whose mending is losing, and the fleet reads losing the only way it can: by which rotted stone is seen to give first. The event is a breach observed, a Contact revision by LOS. It never reads a mend, which the fleet cannot see.
  4. The plague rises AFTER(volley observed on the brick) + the reload offset read from the cadence of incoming hits (E3-07), at its chosen face.
  5. WITH(the plague's rising), the Jam-Hull shuts one Stonewright Post for a few ticks: the Post the Contact ledger holds on that face (a tower glinted by LOS, Fleet Memory §4), else the nearest tower Contact the ledger holds, else nothing. A Post seated where no sightline reaches it, in an enclosed courtyard, is never in the ledger and is never jammed. The channel is Contact.
Grading
  • Succeeded: the plague breaks through a rotted face.
  • Countered: the brick drew no fire, or the plague met a loaded volley.
  • Failed: the plague is cleared at the wall and both faces hold.
Signature
  • First time. Green rot spreads on exactly the walls you mended last. A lone cheap hull runs in, takes your volley and turns away. Then, in the lull before your guns fire again, things crawl up out of the rot. The ships around the banner never flinch.
  • Returning tell. The Breath: a lone brick with the rest waiting at the fear edge. Rot on your freshest mending. Hulls that stiffen and stop skirting near the banner (Ships §6, Herald tell).
Captains’ independence
  • No hull turns back while the standard stands.
  • The Chirurgeons heal whichever hull is hurt most near them.
  • The brick picks its own turn cell.
  • The plague stack picks its own grid path on A* (Ships §7).
  • If the standard breaks, the skittish Chirurgeons and Jam-Hull peel away first. They are the first visible deviations of the battle.
  • Flag sunk. If the Herald sinks, the rot stops spreading, and the plague still rises on its carved breath (clock links).
Counters
  • B · Mend two-deep, out of sight. Stack two Stonewright Posts on each rotting face. A jammed #1 hands off to its backup, which is Ships §6’s answer to the Jam-Hull (“tower-resilience stacks”), and a Post seated in an enclosed courtyard is never in the ledger, so it is never jammed. Rebuild rotted sections in Build and double the ring behind them (triage the pieces where the rot is). Splasher on the plague stack. No timing.
  • B · Price the brick. Hold fire on lone cheap hulls. The plague’s link then wakes at its latest tick, into a loaded volley. Once the Herald has counted H9 it answers this: the brick rides low and carries (E5-07), and the plague’s latest tick is re-cut to its predicted breath (§6). Price the brick by its draught instead.
  • C · Break the standard. Place a Marksman whose reach holds the Herald. The standard is the highest-value target in reach (Cannon §5). Nerve drops to default; the Chirurgeons and the Jam-Hull break first, and the rest of the fleet can be bluffed again.
  • C · A false breath, or choose which stone gives. Stagger fire inside the group so the cadence it reads is false (E3-07’s answer). Or choose the face yourself: put both Posts on S and leave S′ to the rot, so S′ is the stone seen to give first and the hinge sends the plague where your alpha is waiting. The fleet cannot see who mends where; it sees only which wall opens.
If it fails
  • Vaelress’s reaction (Yew). Failed → −3; Countered → 0. It waits and tries again. The next cycle rots the same faces deeper, not faster.
  • What it has learned.
    • the order of mending (H6);
    • the reload cadence (Effect timing).
The Carving

Known from a rotten plank that would not stop weeping sap, her palms on it and Halvard knowing it as she did; she would not let go until it was told:

Lay the rot where the stone is mended, and count their hands. When the rot outruns the hands, go in between two breaths.

The Name

Vaelress, the grain that rots, in Fenholm: for the town they stood on our pillars, driven into the fen because our wood would not rot.

Real-world root

Actium, 31 BC. Agrippa seized Methone and cut the supply line first. Antony, wasted by disease and desertion, came to battle with about 230–250 heavy ships of the 500 he could not man.

T-07

The Long Winter

“Wait in the white; strike the stone that cannot mend.”
LineKindContactsLinksCut against
Patience · the Knot (capstone: E1-06 → E2-07 → E3-08 → E5-04 → T-07)P3 on one bearing3, one a hinge, every one on the blizzard’s clock, beside the standing routinethe routine’s own count of runs unfired (H6 picks the frozen wall)
Throne

Eirlenth (Eir’lenth, “the long winter”), the Rivenmen’s Frost Wyrm · seated in Rimewatch (Frozen) · Yew · Bulwark boss chassis · master of the Knot (Patience).

Era & debut

The Thrones · the Eirlenth battle

Grows from

E5-04 The Fourth Time

Grows into

— (the Patience capstone)

The growth

This is the Fourth Time made shorter by the frost. The Tide that Learned Deceit conditioned three times because every wall could be mended between times. The Wyrm freezes a wall that cannot be mended, so twice is enough, and the third going means it.

Intent

“They trust what always happens, and they mend what breaks. Make a routine they learn to ignore, and whiten the stone at its end so no hand can mend it. On the third going, under the white, land.”

Chosen when

Forced by the third groan.

  • Preconditions (otherwise withheld): a frozen section, the routine convoy afloat, and the routine’s stage count at 2: two runs in a row unfired, E5-04’s stage test (§21) kept as the standing part’s count.
  • Detail, from believed state.
    • The routine’s fringe cell is the COLDest fringe cell on a side flank.
    • Its lane is the lowest-fear lane from that cell.
    • F is the wall Contact at the lane’s end.
  • Cut against the conditioning itself, counted by the long carving’s own stage test: runs unfired (E5-04). It is not a Shore-Reading habit: an unfired routine produces no H1 event, since H1 counts only a sprung hold-feint (§6). A run that draws fire resets the count to 0, and the Judgement is withheld until two unfired runs stand again (T-rule 2: the third ring stays open). With H6 active, F is the section seen mended most, if it lies within one cell of the lane’s end.
  • Rehearsals. Patience +6: the groan sorties are the routine (the standing part), beside a Patience or Bleed form.
Composition

One bearing, a side flank.

PartHullsCore / screenRoleContacts
The WyrmEirlenth + 2 Bulwark shieldscore · shieldssiege: breath, then strike on F1 while seen
The routineRunner convoy + 4 Bulwark shields + 1 Quartermaster ridercore · shields · riderdecoy in groan sorties (a conditioned routine, E5); cargo in the Judgement1
The white huntersWraith wolfpack, loitering at the fog edgewolfpackstrike, branch A only1

Three decision-bearing.

Twist · the hour in the white
  • The blizzard is the Wyrm’s own clock (archived cadence, authored per board). Every link in the Judgement is WITH the blizzard’s onset, so it is already a clock link.
  • Sinking the Wyrm mid-Judgement changes nothing. The blizzard is weather, not the heart’s clock, so §4’s decapitation lateness does not touch it. It is the only Throne whose Judgement survives its own death intact.
Choreography

Across the battle (the standing part).

  • Groan 1. Run one. The routine loiters at its fringe cell and leaves at tick T (offset ≥12: the Knot). It runs its lane toward F, turns off one cell short of the engagement band, and goes home in shape.
  • Groan 2. Run two: the same cell and the same T. At the close of its last visible window, if the count stands at two unfired runs, the Wyrm breathes ice on F. F is frozen and cannot be mended in the coming Build (archived: frozen for 1 sortie). A run that drew fire resets the count, and the routine runs again from one.

The Judgement sortie.

  1. Run three. The routine rides low (laden), and it leaves at its hour T on a link carved on the blizzard’s clock, so its sails blanch as it goes, which a routine’s never do (E5-04’s seams).
  2. The sky whitens 3 ticks before every blizzard: a presence the player learns from sortie 1. The value is a feel number, range 2–5, settled so the whitening is noticed without Command Time.
  3. The Wyrm strikes F WITH(t_blizzard).
  4. The hinge. IF(the routine draws fire before its old turn-off cell, in the 6 ticks before it) → keep the lie : mean it.
    • Keep the lie: the routine turns off as it always did, and the white hunters go through F 12 ticks after the Wyrm.
    • Mean it: the routine does not turn. It lands at F 12 ticks after the Wyrm.
Grading
  • Succeeded: a landing or raid through F.
  • Countered: keep the lie, with the Wraiths driven off.
  • Failed: F held and the landing was eliminated before commit.
Signature
  • First time. The same convoy waits at the same corner of the fog and leaves at the same moment, twice, and turns away short both times. The long white beast breathes on one wall, and your masons cannot touch it. On the third time the snow comes in, the beast is at that wall, and the convoy does not turn.
  • Returning tell. The Knot: a routine that loiters and launches late from the same fringe cell. A frozen wall at the end of its lane. On the real run it rides low, and its sails blanch as it leaves.
Captains’ independence
  • The routine’s Bulwarks hold interposition and jink.
  • The Wraiths hug the fog edge and choose their own cells (Ships §6: barely there, never invisible).
  • Flag sunk. The Wyrm’s death changes nothing in the Judgement sortie (the twist). The hinge, if still open, closes on mean it: decapitating the Wyrm mid-Judgement makes the landing certain. Before the third groan, sinking it ends the Judgement.
Counters
  • B · Depth behind the frost. A frozen face cannot be mended, so spend the Build’s pieces on a second ring behind F (triage). Keep coverage on the routine lane, and never fully discount a routine (E5-04’s blunt answer). Flare in the blizzard shows the Wraiths and the Wyrm.
  • C · Break the conditioning. Fire on the routine on one of its runs. The stage count resets, and the Judgement is withheld: the Wyrm groans its third groan and does not judge, and rehearses until two unfired runs stand again (T-rule 2). Fire on one run in two and it never judges at all; the price is a group spent watching a routine every sortie. If you let it reach the Judgement and fire on the laden run before its old turn-off cell, the hinge keeps the lie, and the Wraiths come alone into a warned group.
  • C · Wait on the known stone. F is known a whole Build ahead: it is the only frozen section. Hold one group silent on it and spring the alpha when the laden routine passes its old turn-off cell. Read the ride height and the blanching sail; the blizzard’s hour is authored and learnable.
If it fails
  • Eirlenth’s reaction (Yew). Countered → 0; Failed → −3. It waits. The next cycle’s routine starts from a different fringe cell, because the old one is known.
  • What it has learned.
    • whether you fired on the routine (its own stage count);
    • how deep you built behind F (Contact).
The Carving

Known by a fire on the shingle from a frost-split strake that thawed as she held it; he knew it as she knew it:

Go the same road at the same hour until their eyes slide past. Whiten the stone no hand can mend; on the third going, land.

The Name

Eirlenth, the long winter, on the mere of Rimewatch: for the great hall on the ice, roofed with our felled kin, where they feasted warm through the long nights.

Real-world root

The Gallipoli evacuation, December 1915 to January 1916. Weeks of “silent stunts” conditioned the Ottoman line to quiet trenches, and 35,268 men left Anzac and Suvla with no deaths from enemy fire.

T-08

The Mirror Host

“Show them themselves.”
LineKindContactsLinksCut against
Mask · the Smoothed Cut (capstone: E2-08 → E4-08 → E5-05 → T-08)P≤4 on one bearingthe worn form’s own, plus the chain hingeyour highest-count habit, chaining to your second
Throne

Neivaere (Nei’vaere, “the silvered, the mirror”), the Rivenmen’s Crystal Lich · seated beneath Glasspire (Crystal) · Silverbark · Mimic boss chassis (the Impostor, Ships §6) · master of the Smoothed Cut (Mask).

Era & debut

The Thrones · the Neivaere battle

Grows from

E5-05 Straw, Then Men

Grows into

— (the Mask capstone)

The growth

This is Straw, Then Men turned inward. The straw mapped which roads the Rivenmen leave silent; the Lich has mapped what the Rivenmen’s hands love to do. It sends their own favourite answer back at them, played by a host with a mirror of real wood beside it, from a Throne standing as one crystal among many.

Intent

“Count what their hands love to do. Wear the carving that answers it, and let a mirror of it walk beside, so they must choose which to believe. Stand among the stones as a stone. Do it to them before they do it.”

Chosen when

Forced by the third groan.

  • Preconditions (otherwise withheld): ≥2 active habits, and hulls afloat to sail the mirror: a formation the queue sends that can take the shape of the real form’s bearing part, unladen (§24 Ships §7).
  • Its form is the Shore-Reading. The Lich wears the form keyed to the player’s highest-count habit. Branch B is keyed to the second-highest. Both are carved before launch, making a chain hinge (E5).
Top habitThe form it wears (trimmed to ≤2 decision-bearing parts)The answer the habit predicts
H1 Silent QuarterE4-02 Ask the Silent Waterthe quarter keeps silence as the probe lingers
H2 Favoured FlankE5-03 Fortitude, on flanksthe reach guards the flank the host threatens
H3 Early FireE5-02 Come as the Young Wood Camefire early and far on the blunderers
H4 Re-taskerE4-05 Along the Shore, Home by the Far Roadthe reach crawls after the retreat
H5 RelocatorE4-07 Borrow the Roadthe glinted gun is left where it was seen
H6 MenderE4-04 When the Stone Givesthe mended section is trusted
H7 Core-HunterE5-05 Straw, Then Menfire goes to the bearers
H8 Sky-ReadyE4-06 Sea Below, Sky Above, reversedthe sky is covered and the sea is not
H9 Patient ShoreE5-07 The First Ship Carriesthe first lone hull is let pass
H10 Standard-HunterE4-08 Thick and Hollowthe first fire goes to the thickest escort, where the standard is believed to ride
H11 Kill-BoxE3-02 The Same Stone, on the sister stonethe ring behind the struck stone is manned, and the sister’s is not
  • Ties go to the lower habit number (§6).
  • Rehearsals. Silverbark (all ±0; HABIT ×2) plus the master Mask +3: Grey Before the Sails, Thick and Hollow, Straw, Then Men, with habit-keyed forms rising fast.
Composition

One bearing. The mirror stands across the bearing’s centre line from the real form.

PartHullsCore / screenRoleContacts
The LichNeivaere, standing as one crystal among the board’s crystalscore (flag)command; hidden1
The mirrorone real formation from the queue, sailing the mirror image of the real form’s bearing part across the centre line, unladen, so it rides high. It wakes AFTER(its twin wakes), so its sails blanch one tick after its twin’sper formdecoy1
The real formthe formations the queue sends, in the parts of the chosen formper formper form≤2

≤4 decision-bearing. The mirror’s AFTER is one link; the worn form is trimmed to fit the rest of the budget beside the chain hinge.

Twist · reads with both hands
  • Six habits are active, the HABIT term is doubled (Silverbark), and the Judgement’s form comes from the Shore-Reading, not the book’s score.
  • The Lich-crystal never fires while it stands disguised (T-rule 7). Leaving the crystal to strike ends the disguise: the wood cannot strike and hide at once.
Choreography

Across the battle. The rehearsals are Mask forms. Between them the Lich counts, and the lookouts’ dispatch reports each habit as it crosses into active (§9c).

The Judgement sortie.

  1. The real form sails in its parts. The mirror sails its mirror image across the centre line, a tick behind its twin at every waking. The Lich stands among the crystals.
  2. The chain hinge. IF(the answer the top habit predicts is observed, within a 6-tick window at the form's own hinge moment) → press the top form : switch to the second habit's form. Both branches are whole carvings, carved before launch.
  3. The Lich’s beam, if it leaves the crystal to strike, falls only on glinted guns (Contact).
Grading

As for the form it wears.

Signature
  • First time. A host comes on that does exactly what you would have done, twice over. Its bearer has a twin across the middle. A field of crystals stands on the shore, and one of them turns, slowly, to face whichever of your groups fired last.
  • Returning tell. The Smoothed Cut: a twin that rides high while its partner rides low, and whose sails blanch a tick after its partner’s (the draught and the late sail: real wood cannot hide its load or its waking). And the one crystal that turns toward the last group that fired.
Captains’ independence
  • The real form’s captains are the form’s captains, with their own jinks, backfill and fixes.
  • The mirror’s captains are real: they jink, backfill and hold the mirror line by their carving, and they wake a tick behind their twin.
  • Flag sunk. Only the real crystal counts. Sink it and both forms keep their hour, three ticks late and a tick apart (clock links, §4), and the mirror goes on answering its twin.
Counters
  • B · Treat every silhouette as real, because every one is. Cover the bearing evenly with Spotter-grown reach, and answer both twins; the mirror is real wood and can be sunk like any other. Saturation across the host costs both.
  • C · Read the draught and the late sail. The twin that rides high and blanches a tick after its partner is the mirror: it carries nothing. Put the alpha on the one that rides low and blanched first. It is a form you have met before.
  • C · Change your habit. In the two groan sorties, play against your own top habit twice, so a habit you have already abandoned leads the count. The Lich cuts its Judgement against a Rivenmen who no longer exists (§6: over-fitting is the feature).
  • C · Find the crystal that turns. Flare it and deep-centre an AoR on it. It cannot fire while it hides.
If it fails
  • Neivaere’s reaction (Silverbark). Countered → −4: it learns fastest. The counter that beat it is itself counted, as a habit event, so the next Judgement is cut against the habit you used to beat this one. It never changes its grain; it bends least.
  • What it has learned. All six habits.
The Carving

Known from a shard of silver-barked wood that showed each of them the other’s face, his palms on it and hers at rest, for what he knew she knew:

Count what their hands love; do it to them before they do it. Stand among the stones as a stone.

The Name

Neivaere, the silvered, the mirror, beneath fallen Glasspire: for the spire that threw the naked sun into our veil.

Real-world root

The 33rd of the Thirty-Six Stratagems, let the enemy’s own spy sow discord. Garbo fed the Germans a network of 27 fictional sub-agents, and Fortitude’s radio war (Quicksilver) made the enemy’s most trusted channel carry the lie.

23PERSONALITIES — THE HEARTS, THE THRONES AND THE TIDES

Jack’s second test asks for “another human running the enemy”. A human opponent is a person before they are a plan: you prepare for someone (research §1.13; the AoE2 .per personalities, Advance Wars COs). The Rivenkeep enemy has three kinds of someone. The heart (the grain) is who is carving this battle. The Throne is who waits at the end of the war. The Tide is how far the wood has grown. The numbers live in §7, §13 and §22; this section is the character sheet the player learns them by.

23a · The nine hearts

A heart is set by the first Standard-Bearer’s lineage and passes to the successor’s on field promotion (§7). It is legible before it is felt: the flag icon names the lineage from sortie 1, and the failure reaction shows it from sortie 2.

The hearth saying is the whole of it: “Choose which heart you leave them” (fiction_hooks §5). The anchor hierarchy and the next-in-line pennant make the choice visible before the kill (§7).

23b · The ten Thrones

The ten eldest hearts, each the master of one line (§16b), each with a grain of its own. The beast-names are the Rivenmen’s fear-names; the Knowings reveal the true names one by one as each haven is retaken (fiction_hooks §9; CALL-OUT 12). Rehearsal weights are grain + master (§22 T-rule 3).

Each Throne is an opponent the player prepares for across a whole battle: the rings count down to its Judgement three sorties ahead (§22 T-rule 2), its rehearsals speak its line from the root, and its grain decides how it answers a Judgement that failed. Its grief is known only at the end (the Names, §22; CALL-OUT 34).

23c · The Tides’ characteristic errors

Each Tide errs in a nameable way, and each error is the lesson the player farms in that stretch of the war (research P18; §2).

TideIts errorWhat the player farmsWhere it shows
Hasty (Cam 1–6)It repeats what fails. Each formation has one word and cannot change itPredict the word from the haze; sink the hull to change it§17: the first words, carved once
Second (Cam 7–14)It straggles. Its one link arrives 3 ticks late, and a part whose partner never acts goes anyway at its hourRead who waits on whom; deny the event and the waiter comes alone§18: one goes, one waits
Remembering (Cam 15–22)It believes what it has measured: its blows on a stone, the breath of a battery, the silence an eye came back through, the silent stone its eye saw standing or goneFeed it a walled-over breach, a slow breath, a silence, or a battery its eye reads as wall, and it builds a sentence on it§19: the look and the hinge
Joined (Cam 23–26)It carves for where your reach will be, from the walks it has counted, and it keeps the hour when the heart is dead, late and out of stepA reach that does not walk, or a walk it has not counted§20: one tick, many sails
Deceit (Cam 27–31)It aims at the habits it has counted in you, including your patience, and it believes you will keep themTeach it a false habit, then spring the other side; vary what you repeat; read every seam§21: cut against
Last (Cam 32)It plans for losing, and so it carves its own retreatThe whole plan is known and told afterwards, including the branch not taken§21: the Last Tide
Thrones (Finale)It announces its Judgement three sorties ahead and cannot be frightened off a lane, only lied to about which lane is safePrepare for the Judgement in Build; lie to its fear map§22: the rings and T-rule 6

24INTEGRATION DEBTS TO OTHER DOCS

What this doc needs from its siblings, doc by doc. Each debt names the entry that raised it. A debt marked BLOCKS must be paid before the named carving ships.

Fleet Memory
  • Build/Deploy Is Simultaneous Planning: “computes its next-sortie plan” points here (§3–§5).
  • §12 ledger gains the §14 rows. New registers, each channel-derived and holding no new information: the Shore-Reading (§6, H1–H11), the breath per fear cell, kept as the most recent measurement (E3-07), the farthest cell struck (T-02), the blow count per wall section (E2-07, E3-02). One global tie-break for every selection: lower cell index, then lower queue index (§14).
  • §7 propagation: the link lag reads the batching model (§4): the era’s base, −1 while a Recon relays, +1 while the net is thin, +3 once the flag sinks. So sinking the Recons and thinning the fleet both slow the plan, not only the soldiers.
  • §7 flagship: amended, pending Jack (CALL-OUT 39). Fleet Memory says the flag’s death means “coordination collapses” and the fleet “can no longer act as one plan”. This doc keeps that before Cam 25 (links break) and changes it from Cam 25: pending links become clocks that fire three ticks late and a tick apart by spawn order, so the plan survives raggedly. The ratchet and the home clause are unchanged (§4).
  • Taken unchanged: the three channels, fear decay, “Contact revised only by contradiction”, the propagation net and its batching, the legibility law, and “the plan must stay legible” (the guardrail this doc is built to satisfy).
Ships
  • §4: field promotion also passes the grain (§7), and the next-in-line anchor carries a pennant.
  • §4: “fanatical” is split into its nerve effect and its route effect (§2 LAW · INTENSITY TOUCHES NERVE, NEVER THE PLAN; CALL-OUT 17). Every intensity plans lanes from believed fear; fanatical nerve never balks, sags or cascades, and holds its lane through fear written in the sortie, which is the kill-zone Ships §4 means by “ignores the fear grid”. Bluffable balks at HOT, default at SCALDING.

  • §5 (Skyfall): a carrier leaves the back line only to a station a carving names, and never closes past it (E5-06’s parade station). Pending Jack (CALL-OUT 37).
  • §6 (Mimic): pending Jack (CALL-OUT 35). The proposal strikes “forges exposure-glints” and the forged fear-haze: the Mimic lies about ships and never the glint (§9a). Until ruled, Ships §6 stands as written and every carving here that uses the Mimic already obeys the stricter rail.

Its lane is always its own straight line to one wall, fixed at carving; no hinge ever chooses its road (E4-03 and E5-01 bar it from hinge-chosen parts); once it drives it never routes by fear. So its timing can be read and its road cannot be steered, and Ships §6’s “you can’t bait it” still holds. Pending Jack (CALL-OUT 36).

A ruling on whether light dispels a projection (only E5-03’s Flare clause depends on it; cut the clause if not).
  • §7: a carving may sail a transport unladen (E5-04’s routine, T-07’s routine, T-08’s mirror). Fleet Memory redeploys troops fresh from surviving transports every sortie; this adds that a carving may choose to embark none, which the draught render then shows.

The dressed looseness of E5-02 is the late fleet’s voluntary formation looseness.
