25CALL-OUTs FOR JACK

Numbers 1–17 concern the framework (§0–§15), 18–34 the tree and the catalogue (§16–§22), and 35–40 the rulings that change a sibling doc or the book’s shape. Each gives a recommendation; the doc is written as if the recommendation is accepted.

CALL-OUT 1 · Revise Why §5 (required by Why §8, not optional)
  • Current text: “The player is not dueling a live, adapting opponent; they are learning and exploiting a legible fixed mind”, and “puzzle depth… not adversarial depth.”
  • Proposed replacement, in your words:

    “The ships should feel like there is another human running the enemy — working together but also independent. The fleet is a fixed, deterministic mind: it commits to a strategy between sorties from what it has seen, answers the player’s habits, and grows sharper across the war. Within a battle the player is dueling an opponent that adapts to them; across replays that opponent answers the same play the same way. So the depth is adversarial inside the battle and a puzzle across retries. The player does not know exactly what the ships are doing at first, but recognises the strategy and can counter it.”

  • It keeps §5’s honesty (the board is solvable) and drops the claim that nothing answers the player. The renewability paragraph stays, because variety of minds between boards is still the engine, and the Admiral adds variety of plans within one.
CALL-OUT 2 · Hours per day: RESOLVED (Jack, 2026-09-26: 24 hours; the racing hour is the intended urgency.)

A siege day has 24 hours of 60 minutes and passes in 18 real s, so an hour goes by in 0.75 s, as Build §2 already shows, and Build’s day display stays as it is. The sim tick stays 1 real s at the siege pace, so 1 tick = 1⅓ game-hours, and every tick count in this doc (the crawl, the reloads, the carvings, the backstop) stands. Under command an hour takes 18 real s (1/24 speed) and the minutes tick every 0.3 s (§12). The earlier recommendation of an 18-hour day is withdrawn.

CALL-OUT 3 · What “starting an order” means for Command Time

Recommend touch-down on a group. Alternatives: drag-start, or commit. Touch-down makes study free, and that is the intent (vision test 3). The risk is a slack late war (§12 CALL-OUT). If greybox shows slack, move the trigger to drag-start. Do not add a budget.

CALL-OUT 4 · Command Time ramp values

0.75 s down, a 1.0 s post-commit dwell at the floor, and 1.5 s up are feel numbers. The dwell exists so a player who planned two orders can place the second without racing the ramp: touching another group inside it keeps time at 1/24. Accept them as starting values, to be settled in greybox (§12)?

CALL-OUT 5 · Admiral spawn choice

May the Admiral pick among the spawns authored for a formation? Recommend yes, ≤2 per formation, within ≤3 live approaches. Without it, Feint and Flood lines can only vary lanes, not roads.

CALL-OUT 6 · Launch offset window

L_max = 18 ticks (range 10–24), which also sets every hold’s latest tick (§3). Accept?

CALL-OUT 7 · Habits are per battle, never across battles

Recommend per battle (§6: carrying them would break “two people play EXACTLY the same → same outcome”). The wood’s war-long memory is told by the fiction and delivered by the authored era.

CALL-OUT 8 · Half-life from E5 only

Early fleets repeat what fails, which is the Ender’s Game crudeness. Accept?

CALL-OUT 9 · Mystarch blindness

Several archived set-pieces read truth: the Storm King “strikes the highest-HP cannon”, and the Frost Wyrm and Sandworm target true states. Rule: every set-piece names its input channel. §22 T-rule 7 gives all ten re-expressions (for example, Naelthar strikes the glinted gun inside the fear cell that has written the most Effect on it). Accept them for the boss pass?

CALL-OUT 10 · The Thrones hold 8 Judgements for 10 Mystarchs

Thaesaen and Naelthar carve the Flood and Sounding capstones (E5-01, E5-02) whole. This keeps every tier inside your 6–8 band (7 / 8 / 8 / 8 / 7 / 8 = 46). The alternative is ten bespoke Judgements, with the Thrones at 10.

CALL-OUT 11 · Lineage debuts, and the rule that places them (Campaign §5 gap)
  • The rule (§13 LAW · BREADTH ENTERS WITH LAYER B): every lineage after Cam 1’s baseline debuts on a campaign carrying Layer B (Cam 3, 7, 11–13, 17–19, 23–25, 27–29, 31, 32), one per battle. A campaign without B debuts no lineage and fields every lineage already met, so “basic enemies” means no new hull, not a smaller roster.
  • The calendar under it: Recon Cam 2 · Reaver, Sapper and Bulwark Cam 3 (B’s isolation campaign; with Camouflage and Piercer) · Skyfall and Provocateur Cam 7 · Sower Cam 9 (with Flare) · Corsair and Quartermaster Cam 11 (with Jammer and Marksman) · common Leviathan Cam 13 · Chirurgeon-Fleet, Aegis and Linebreaker Cam 17 · Infiltrator Cam 18 · Wraith Cam 19 · Breacher Cam 23 · Standard-bearer and Pioneer Cam 24 · Mimic Cam 27.
  • Moved to obey the rule: Sapper (was Cam 4, C), Bulwark (was Cam 5, D: weather’s isolation), Provocateur (was Cam 8, AC), Aegis and Linebreaker (were Cam 15, CE), Wraith (was Cam 20, ACD).
  • Two exceptions to rule on, both base lineages: the Recon at Cam 2 (A), the fleet’s eyes, which bring no hull the player must answer with new kit and without which the Sounding root has no carrier before Cam 3; and the Sower at Cam 9 (AD), whose smoke and fire are the fleet’s own weather and arrive with D. The strict alternative moves the Recon to Cam 3 (E1-03 with it) and the Sower to Cam 7 (E2-07 then needs a later slot).
  • Tower proposals: the Powder Store at Cam 13 (with the common Leviathan, as output begins to matter) and the Rally Flag at Cam 15 (as verb-speed begins to decide hinges), so every counter’s kit is scheduled (Campaign §6).
  • Victory §4b’s Cam 1–6 wolfpack still needs a lineage (§24 Victory).
  • Accept, or re-sequence?
CALL-OUT 12 · Name collisions

The Leviathan and Herald are both lineages and Mystarchs (story_bible A11). The fiction gives each Mystarch a true name; the lineage names stay working handles. Rename the lineages, or keep beast-names as the Rivenmen’s fear-names?

CALL-OUT 13 · An assist mode

Brief -1’s “intercepted orders” (showing the carving in Deploy) is rejected for the default game (§9c). Keep it as an explicitly labelled assist that is off the leaderboard (the SC2 “Cheater” precedent, research §1.6), or drop it?

CALL-OUT 14 · Late live readings (§9b)

Sinking a stratagem’s hull mid-sortie reveals its line’s root sign, from E5 on. Accept, or keep every reveal in the post-battle Knowing?

CALL-OUT 15 · The Joined Tide

This inserts a Tide the story bible lacks. The bible’s “Remembering” ran to Cam 26; here Remembering is 15–22 and the Joined Tide is 23–26, matching A = 3. Its theme is the fleet learning to act as one: the dark mirror of the Aetherbond. Keep its name, Aelthae, with the Ael’thar echo (fiction_hooks §12 Q4)?

CALL-OUT 16 · The Admiral draws no random numbers

Variety comes from authoring and from the player. Accept? This is stricter than the fleet-stream rule.

CALL-OUT 17 · Split “fanatical” (brief -6)

Fanatics ignore fear on the approach but still plan lanes from it. The doc now adopts it for every intensity and every era (§2 LAW · INTENSITY TOUCHES NERVE, NEVER THE PLAN): the carving always reads the fear haze; intensity decides only which balks the captains make (bluffable at HOT, default at SCALDING, fanatical never). At the Thrones it is T-rule 6 (“a Throne cannot be frightened off a lane; it can be lied to about which lane is safe”). It amends Ships §4’s “ignores the fear grid” to its route effect (§24). Recommend: accept.

CALL-OUT 18 · Carved once (§17 LAW · GREEN WOOD TAKES ONE CUT)

An earlier draft re-scored every formation at every boundary and made the young wood repeat itself through Greenwood’s Failed −0. This draft makes the repeat structural instead: a glyph is cut once and cannot be re-cut. That is the Law of the Rings taken literally. It makes the Hasty Tide the most predictable fleet in the war, and it gives the Second Tide a jump in kind the player can see: from Cam 9 the Heartwood re-carves every hull for every morrow, and a formation that comes back saying something new is the first sign that the wood over there is thinking. The law holds through Cam 8, and the re-carving arrives at Cam 9 with the rest of the Second Tide’s family (§13). Recommend: accept. The alternative lets E1 formations change their word as fear moves, which blurs the line between crude and organised that you asked for.

CALL-OUT 19 · The debut calendar (§16a)
  • Ask the Silent Water at Cam 24, not 25: it needs only the E3 grammar, and it leaves Cam 25 to the capability and the two carvings that use it (E4-01, E4-05). It also gives Cam 24 a theme: the fleet stops trusting your silence and brings a ram to the breach.
  • The Tide that Learned Deceit re-sequenced: The Prize Too Easy at 28 (E3 machinery only; nothing in it is false), Come as the Young Wood Came and Straw, Then Men at 29 (the bundle, one debut per battle), The Fourth Time at 30 (basic hulls in a no-B campaign: the campaign that brings no new ships brings a new mind), Fortitude at 31 (it needs Layer-B lineages), and The Closing Hand at 32, so the Flood’s root (Cam 1) and its capstone bracket the war.
  • The Joined Tide’s capability is first met inside a known sentence: E3-04 re-cut WITH at Cam 25 battle 2, so E4-01 can debut at battle 3 (§13).
  • The First Ship Carries (E5-07) at Cam 30, on a later battle than The Fourth Time: both are carried by known hulls in the campaign with no Layer B, and E5-07 needs H9, which a battle of Cam 30 has had every chance to count.
  • Crowded campaigns with fallbacks: Cam 2 carries three debuts on separate battles (E1-07, the Recon, E1-03); if greybox says crowded, E1-07 moves to Cam 5 or 6, the E1 campaigns with no debut. Cam 3 now carries the Reaver, the Sapper, the Bulwark and E1-05, one per battle, because B’s isolation campaign is where breadth belongs; if crowded, E1-05 slides to Cam 4. Cam 11 carries the Corsair, Quartermaster, Jammer, Marksman, E2-01 and E2-05; E2-01 can slide to Cam 14 (it needs only the Runner and the Bulwark).
  • Accept?
CALL-OUT 20 · A waiting convoy and a refusing convoy (E2-02)

Both sit in the launch zone, and Victory §4a makes the refusal tell a presence. The entry gives the tells (a waiter is full-screened with a Recon out ahead and gone by t18; a refuser is thin and never leaves) and §15 test 8 measures them. Recommend: if testers cannot tell them apart by t8, the Sounding’s convoy waits at the launch zone’s forward edge instead.

CALL-OUT 21 · Lone hulls (§3 Parts)

The Sounding line’s probes and the Bleed line’s bricks are one-hull parts, and the formation law has no one-hull formation except the Mimic’s solo. Recommend: a rider or screen hull the carving detaches, or the lead hull of a dispersed Recon picket, counts as its own contact from the moment it leaves: Victory §3’s “formation breaking mid-approach: 1 → 1 per hull”, made deliberate. The alternative is a two-hull wolfpack whose lead runs a cell ahead. Either needs one line in Ships §9.

CALL-OUT 22 · The flinch

Two captain deviations appear from the Tide of Remembering: a holding formation that takes fire breaks its hold early, onto its colder branch or its fallback (E3-01), and lit Corsairs bolt from cover (E3-06). Recommend: classify both as balks, and widen the balk from “a lateral stall at a cell edge” to include “a hold broken by fire”. That keeps §2’s four visible deviations at four.

CALL-OUT 23 · Stratagem history from the Second Tide

Two homes were possible for the MEMORY term: the Remembering’s memory clause (Cam 16), or the grain itself, arriving whole at Cam 9 with the Second Tide’s family. Recommend the second: the grain’s whole temper arrives with the grain at Cam 9 (weights, MEMORY, the failure reaction, no immediate repeat of a Failed carving), as fiction_hooks §5 tells it (“the first Standard-Bearers with a temper”). Not at Cam 7, which opens the second approach and so turns nothing in the mind (§13). E3’s memory clause adds the look, the hinge and a history with branches (§13). And INERTIA does not apply after a Twistbough success, or its “never twice” is mostly cancelled (§5). Accept both?

CALL-OUT 24 · Scope of the spacing floor (§11)

E3-05’s convergence on one gun and E3-07’s strike inside a reload put arrivals on one group closer than 12 ticks, by design. Neither forces a costed verb: their blunt answers are made in Deploy, or are free fire-control. Recommend: the floor binds linked arrivals that demand a costed verb (a re-task or a domain switch) of the same group; and from Cam 23 it widens to Δ = max(12, d + 4 + s) wherever one group may answer two approaches, with a 4-tick slack s so that a planned walk back arrives a reload early and one tick of hesitation never decides it (§20). Accept?

CALL-OUT 25 · No wide form in the Joined Tide (§11, §20)

Every E4 carving is narrow and clever; preparation sorties in Cam 23–26 carve the book’s older W forms. A Joined-Tide W form would be a WITH wake across 6–8 wolfpacks, which is forced simultaneity by construction. The only lawful shape is one WITH tick for departure with arrivals spaced by Δ. Recommend: none. Accept?

CALL-OUT 26 · The home clause (§4, §21)

If the standard falls a second time in one sortie, the running carving withdraws to spawn in shape. It is “when the second falls, go home”, from the Last Tide Knowing, made real. It ships with the Cam 29 family and is first known at Cam 32. Accept it, or keep the Last Tide Knowing (Legends V.7, Part III) as fiction only?

CALL-OUT 27 · A successor’s grain can break a standing lie (§4)

, if the new heart weights that line at −2 or lower. Decapitation then decides which lies survive: kill the cunning heart and leave a proud one, and the host is abandoned. Accept?

CALL-OUT 28 · Last Tide Knowings show the branch not taken (§9b)

It is true, and it explains only this battle. Accept that it counts as explanation, not forecast?

CALL-OUT 29 · Boss sortie count

Victory and Deploy give 3–5 sorties per Throne battle; the GDD gives 8–12 (“~12 min • 10 sorties”). The rings work under either: the Judgement falls on sortie 3 and again every third sortie after. Recommend Victory’s 3–5. The Judgement then falls in the middle of the battle, with one or two sorties of consequence after it.

CALL-OUT 30 · Cracking the heart resets the rings; it does not cancel the Judgement (§22 T-rule 2)

An earlier draft said a Throne sunk to its phase threshold before the third groan never carves. That makes racing to the threshold dominant, and the Judgement is the Throne. Recommend reset, once per battle. In a 3–5 sortie battle a crack on sortie 2 still pushes the Judgement to sortie 5, so the race stays worth running.

CALL-OUT 31 · The Thrones are exempt from “one debut per battle, never with a new lineage” (§13)

Each Throne battle introduces its Throne, its chassis and its Judgement together, and Vaelress introduces the Herald as well. Recommend the exemption, because the unit of introduction here is the battle itself: the rehearsal sorties isolate the line before the Judgement combines it.

CALL-OUT 32 · The foretold branch (§4)

Three rules were possible for a hinge whose heart is dead: the payload branch, the branch the carving was cut for, or the otherwise branch (which T-03’s and T-05’s own flag-sunk behaviour contradicts). Recommend one rule in two clauses: (a) a chain hinge foretells its parent’s own branch, never the chain, because the chain is the fleet’s second thought and a dead heart has none (E5-01 closes, E5-02 turns short, E5-03, E5-04 and E5-05 land, E5-07 bears); (b) any other hinge foretells the branch that carries the payload, or where both do, the branch keyed to the habit the carving was cut against (E4-02’s flood, E4-03’s inboard lane, E4-05’s far road, E5-06’s launch). Under it a decapitated Eirlenth lands for certain, deliberately (T-07). Accept?

CALL-OUT 33 · Throne chassis

The Hydra’s heads ride the Leviathan boss chassis, as Ships §6 has it (an earlier draft said Bulwark). No doc assigns the Storm King a chassis; the Leviathan’s Battlecarrier expression is proposed. No Throne uses the Chirurgeon as its primary chassis: the Grand Chirurgeon is folded into Vaelress as riders. If you want a Chirurgeon Throne, Leavaren’s regrowth is its natural home, at the cost of moving the Hydra off Ships’ Leviathan. Accept?

CALL-OUT 34 · The Throne griefs and havens are proposals

Each Name ties a haven to the Mystholder harvest (Legend III) or to the warnings sold as ornament, as the Legends tell each grief (III.2, IV.1); Fenholm’s rot-proof pillars and Eldhythe’s priced warnings are the strongest. None is canon until you approve the havens’ working names (fiction_hooks §12 Q6) and the griefs.

CALL-OUT 35 · The Mimic never forges the exposure glint (Ships §6 redesign; §9a, §21)

Ships §6 defines the Mimic partly by what it forges: false Contact in the player’s read, “exposure-glints, fear-haze”. This doc keeps one hard rail instead. The Mimic lies about ships (projections, disguise) and never about the glint: it provokes real glints and never writes a false one (Fleet Memory §8). The reason is the glint’s job. It is the player’s only true read of what the fleet knows about their guns, and bait-and-relocate, enclosure and every Contact counter in the book stand on it. A forgeable glint makes them unreadable, and a seam that can lie breaks §21’s red line. The cost is real: a permanent lineage loses its forgeries. Recommend: adopt, and amend Ships §6. Until you rule, §24 marks the Ships item pending, and every carving here already obeys the stricter rail.

CALL-OUT 36 · Holding a Breacher (Ships §6; E4-01, E4-04, T-03, T-05)

Ships §6 makes the Breacher pure commit, a straight line at the wall, “you can’t bait it; you must physically stop it”. This doc parks it: the heave-to (E4-01), the ram at the fear edge (E4-04), the ram at the launch zone’s forward edge (T-03, T-05). Recommend the rule that keeps Ships’ promise: its lane is always its own straight line to one wall, fixed at carving; its station on that line is read once at carving; a link or a clock releases it; no hinge ever chooses its road (so E4-03’s kept hand and E5-01’s landing take Runners only); once it drives, it never routes by fear. Its timing can be read and its road cannot be steered. Accept, and add the line to Ships §6? The alternative is to hold it only on WITH clocks and drop E4-04’s breach-triggered ram.

CALL-OUT 37 · A carrier off the back line (Ships §5; E5-06)

Ships §5: the Skyfall “stays at the back line, never approaches”. The Prize Too Easy sails a thinly screened carrier to a carved station one cell outside the core-hunter’s believed reach and launches from there. Recommend: allow it only where a carving names the station, and never past it. The alternative prize is a thin Runner, which must cross into reach to land and so loses the “just outside” temptation that makes the carving a test of where the player’s reach goes.

CALL-OUT 38 · The eras are steps in kind (Campaign §4; Why §5; §13)

Campaign §4 says the enemy mind “does not step up in tiers. It creeps… No banner, no tier-up”, and that the named cognition apexes are “not discrete unlocks”; Why §5 asks for a creep “subtle in magnitude”. Your five periods and the Ender’s Game ordering ask for a mind that gets better in kind, visibly: the first answering sail, the first look, the first hour on three roads, the first lie told in true wood. Recommend: your periods supersede “no tiers” for plan-level capabilities only (links, hinges, the look, WITH, anticipation, decoys, long carvings, chain hinges), which turn on at Cam 9, 16, 25 and 29, each shown first by one carving and never bannered. Estimator quality (fear decay, propagation and its lag, the cognition apexes that are not plan-level) keeps creeping per battle as Campaign §4 says. Amend Campaign §4 and Why §5 to say so (§24).

CALL-OUT 39 · The hour kept raggedly (Fleet Memory §7; Ships §4; §4, §20)

Fleet Memory §7 and Ships §4: sink the flagship and “coordination collapses… the fleet can no longer act as one plan”. From Cam 25 this doc keeps a dead heart’s plan on its clocks, which reverses that. Recommend the compromise now in §4: before Cam 25 links break, as Fleet Memory is written; from Cam 25 they become clocks that fire late by the decapitated lag (+3) plus each formation’s batch index, so a shared WITH breaks into sails a tick apart and a walking reach gains at least three ticks. The kill still visibly disrupts the running sortie, and it still cuts the next plan (the ratchet). Amend Fleet Memory §7 and Ships §4 (§24). The alternative is Fleet Memory as written, every decapitation breaking every link in every era, which leaves the Joined Tide’s “hour that outlives the heart” and Kael’s fourth count (Legends V.5) without a mechanic.

CALL-OUT 40 · Three habits more, and two carvings changed (§6, §19, §21)

One player habit, never shooting the first ship, beat most of the book, and each waiting part’s latest tick then sent it in alone into loaded guns; a human opponent would have noticed in two sorties. Two more habits the book’s own counters teach, striking the standard first and building a kill-box behind the breach, were never counted. So:

  • H9 They let the first ship pass, H10 They strike at our standard, H11 Behind their wall, another join the register (§6), each with its dispatch line and its keyed forms. From Cam 16, H9 re-cuts every waiter’s latest tick to the tick its event was predicted (§6 LAW · THE PATIENT SHORE).
  • E3-08 Leave the Known Gun is replaced by Spent or Waiting, so the Patience line grows at its middle (the young wood’s wait, with a look); its H5 answer moves into the Hunt line.
  • E5-07 The First Ship Carries is added, the fleet’s answer to H9, so the Tide that Learned Deceit holds seven and the book forty-six.
  • Four hinges and links now read a decision other than “did you fire”: a walk (E4-05, E5-06), a target choice (E4-08) and a Deploy read (E5-04).

Recommend: accept.

Version History

VersionChanges
v0.3.0(2026-09-26) Brought into line with the revised Legends (v1.1.0) and Jack’s clock ruling (N4). Clock: a siege day has 24 hours and passes in 18 real s (0.75 s an hour); a sim tick stays 1 real s at the siege pace, so 1 tick = 1⅓ game-hours, and no tick count changes; the Command Time floor is 1/24 speed (an hour takes 18 s; the minutes tick every 0.3 s), with the ramp values unchanged; CALL-OUT 2 resolved. Knowing, not reading: the Reading of the Wood becomes the Knowing of the Wood (§9b), the Book of Readings the Book of Knowings (§9d), and every Carving line is told in the knowing form (only one of Halyna touches the wood; what one knows, the other knows), with the carved words kept; a root sign is still read by eye. Halyna: the pair is called by its one name, and its two-voice lines are unmarked, as the Legends’ two inks are; Seren is Seren Two-Inks. Names: name_map.md applied (Saenvael, Naelsaen, Thaesaen, Ralensaen, Vaelress, Neivaere, Leavaren, Eirlenth). No one aboard: the ship is the commander (§2), and E5-05’s carving ends “send the landing”. The Twelve: each battery is commanded by one of the Captain’s twelve True Men (§12).
v0.2.0(2026-09-25) Review pass. The fleet reads no HP anywhere (E4-04’s mend, T-06’s hinge and Jam-Hull); the ≤4 decision-bearing limit binds whole sorties (E3-05, E5-01, the held host of §11); the foretold branch is one two-clause rule (§4); decapitation from Cam 25 keeps the hour late and ragged; link lag reads the batching model; intensity touches nerve, never the plan (§2); Breacher, Leviathan, Mimic and Skyfall kept to their Ships entries or put to Jack (CALL-OUT 35–37); lineage debuts follow Layer B (§13); the Second Tide’s whole family turns on at Cam 9 and each era’s family is one idea; H9–H11 added with the patient-shore re-cut; Spent or Waiting replaces Leave the Known Gun; The First Ship Carries added (46); E5-05 maps roads, E5-06 tempts a walk, E5-04 reads a Deploy, T-08’s mirror is real wood; Command Time gains a post-commit dwell; tick budgets redone (E4-03, E4-04, E4-05); E4 Readings keep the wood’s lexicon and every Reading is given in two voices.
v0.1.0(2026-09-25) Doc created — the Admiral layer drafted from Jack’s vision.

How v0.1.0 was put together: a framework and six Tide catalogues, drafted separately and integrated here. What the integration settled:

  • One doc. The framework (§0–§15) and the five Tides plus the Thrones (§17–§22) are one document. The first ladder and growth-line table are replaced by the tree (§16) and the catalogue.
  • One vocabulary across the Tides. The fear edge and a hold’s latest tick (§3); link counting with the hinge inside the budget (§4), with E5’s counts restated and E5-01’s sea hand now driving on the hinge’s own waking; the foretold branch for a dead heart’s hinge (§4, CALL-OUT 32); stratagem history from the Second Tide (§5, CALL-OUT 23); grading on objective-bearing roles (§5); reduced forms under the decapitation ratchet (§4).
  • Reciprocal growth. Every grows from has its grows into (§16a): E3-01 → E4-01, E1-06 → E5-04, and E5-02’s Hasty-Tide dress now stand in both entries. Chains and dress are named in both entries they join (§16c).
  • Lifted from the era notes into the framework: the self-trigger field and parts (§3), pale and grey sails, seen(X), hazard laid, long carvings, chain hinges and the home clause (§4), the spacing floor’s scope and Δ (§11), the capability-debut law (§13), the new registers (§14), and two greybox tests (§15).
  • Aligned between the Tides and the Thrones. Thaesaen’s and Naelthar’s Throne-scale changes are folded into E5-01 and E5-02 (At the Throne); T-07 keeps E5-04’s seams (the sail that blanches at the hour, not a one-tick shift); the ten-Thrones roster moves to §23b.
  • Every era writer’s note for the integrator is resolved into §2–§15, the entries, §24 or §25. None remains in the catalogue.
